No console errors in any of it. The first pass is what caught the legend spacing.
- 28 new tests (20 in `net-worth.test.js`, 8 in `NetWorthChart.test.js`), 898 total, up from 870. `npm run lint`, `npm run check` (0 errors, same 2 pre-existing `<slot>` warnings) and `npm run build` all clean.

**Trade-offs / deviations from prompt:**
- **"The three scenario lines" are drawn in one hue, not three.** Read literally, the issue could mean three separately-coloured series. They are drawn as three distinct lines — solid realistic, two dashed edges — but sharing `--chart-2`, because they are one forecast under three assumptions and the shaded band between them is the point. A reader who wants the three figures side by side gets them from the legend (`3%–7%`), the caption and the forecast tab's scenario table. If the intent was genuinely three categorical colours, this is the line to change.
- **The chart is anchored on the latest snapshot and the horizon picker is invented UI.** Neither README.md nor the issue specifies a horizon for this chart; a fixed 5/10/20/30 list is a judgement call, as is defaulting to 10 years rather than `forecast.js`'s own 30-year default (at 30 years a short history is invisible). The forecast tab keeps the free-text horizon field.
- **Debts are carried flat through the projection**, because that is `forecast.js`'s documented convention (nothing in the data model says how a balance amortises), so the band's shape is really the holdings' shape with a constant subtracted. Inherited rather than introduced here, but it is visible on this chart in a way it wasn't in a summary table: a user with a mortgage they expect to pay down will see a band that doesn't reflect it.
- **No hover tooltip, no point markers, no auto-filled/recorded distinction and no accessible table fallback** — all #82, unchanged by this PR. The consequence is sharper now than it was for the tracked line alone: a reader can see the band's width but cannot read a specific month's low/mid/high off it. The `aria-label` therefore gained a second sentence — the final month's realistic figure and the pessimistic-to-optimistic range at that month — so the band's headline numbers exist in text somewhere, but it is still a summary, not the data.
- **Tailwind's utility layer still isn't reaching the page, and this PR still doesn't fix it.** `src/app.css` uses v3's `@tailwind` directives under Tailwind v4 (`@tailwindcss/postcss`), so most utilities never reach the served stylesheet — the app renders in serif with no padding. Flagged on #67, unchanged on `main`, and this PR adds two more inline-style workarounds rather than fixing it app-wide. It deserves its own issue before more UI lands on top of it.
- **The `.dark` values were verified by forcing the class in the scratch route**, because the app still has no dark-mode toggle — nothing in `+layout.svelte` ever sets `.dark`. Both new token values are correct and validated for when one lands; neither is reachable through the UI today.
- **`forecastBandSeries` takes the extremes across the scenarios, so `low`/`high` are pessimistic/optimistic only while the rates stay ordered** — which `scenarioGrowthRates` guarantees. That is `forecastBand`'s own existing behaviour, kept deliberately: the legend names them pessimistic and optimistic, which would be wrong if a caller ever handed in unordered rates, but a band that is always a band beats a legend that is always literal.
- `npm test` still isn't in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (flagged since #2; the app token lacks `workflows` permission), so all 898 tests run locally but not on the PR. `build`/`check`/`lint` remain the only CI gates.

## Net worth chart: point markers and accessible table fallback — 2026-08-07
<!-- METRICS:net-worth-chart-point-markers-and-accessible-table-fallback -->
- **Execution Duration:** __DURATION__ seconds
- **Model:** __MODEL__
- **Turns:** __TURNS__
- **Input Tokens:** __INPUT_TOKENS__
- **Output Tokens:** __OUTPUT_TOKENS__
- **Estimated Cost:** $__COST__ (from Claude Code's reported total_cost_usd)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable GPT-5-tier model:** $__GPT_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable Gemini-tier model:** $__GEMINI_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)

**Decisions:**
- **One `Circle` in data mode draws both marker shapes, exactly as the issue suggests.** `data={points}`, `cx`/`cy` as accessor functions, and `fill` as a function of the point (`hsl(var(--card))` when `auto_filled`, `hsl(var(--chart-1))` otherwise) — LayerChart resolves a function-valued `fill` per item, so there is one component and one pass over `points`, not a filled-points pass and a separate hollow-points pass. `stroke` stays `hsl(var(--chart-1))` on every marker regardless of shape, which is what makes the hollow ones read as rings rather than gaps in the line.
- **"Hollow" is `fill: hsl(var(--card))`, not `fill: none`.** The marker sits on top of the tracked `Spline`, so a genuinely transparent fill would let the line show through the middle of an auto-filled dot and blur the two shapes together at this radius (4px). Filling with the card's own surface colour instead punches a same-colour hole in the line, which is what actually reads as hollow — and it is why the token is `--card`, not `--background`: the chart sits inside a `Card`, and `--card`/`--background` are already independent tokens in `app.css` in case that ever changes. Confirmed in both themes by forcing `.dark` on a scratch route: the light hollow fill renders near-white, the dark one near-black, both matching their surface exactly.
- **The forecast band gets no markers, per the issue's ordering note.** `Circle`'s `data` is `points` (the tracked series) only, never `band` — a projected month is not a recorded one, and marking it would visually claim data that doesn't exist. This falls out of #82 landing after #81 rather than needing new logic: `points` and `band` were already two separate arrays.
- **The auto-filled count is a new exported helper, `autoFilledPointCount(points)` in `$lib/net-worth.js`, not a re-walk of `monthly_entries`.** It takes the same `points` array the chart already plotted and reduces on `auto_filled`, so the caption can never disagree with which markers actually rendered hollow — the failure mode the issue calls out by name. Four unit tests (`net-worth.test.js`): empty series, none auto-filled, a mixed run, and a fully auto-filled run.
- **The caption is conditional on `autoFilledCount > 0` and sits directly under the plot, inside the same `{#if hasPlot}` block as the `<Svg>`.** With nothing auto-filled there is no hollow shape on the chart to name, so a caption that always rendered would be explaining a marker that isn't there half the time. Wording states the count, the total, and spells out "hollow marker" explicitly, singular/plural handled (`1 ... was` vs `2 ... were`).
- **The `<details>` table fallback is gated on `points.length > 0` alone, not on `hasPlot`.** A single recorded month with the forecast switched off draws no `<Spline>` at all (#67's "a line needs two months" case) but is still one row of real data — the table can give a screen reader that month's figures even though the SVG has nothing to fall back from. This is a deliberate widening of "accessibility fallback for an SVG line" to "accessibility fallback for what's been recorded", on the view that a reader gets less, not more, if the one case with no line is also the one case with no table.
- **The table's columns are exactly the issue's list — month, investments, debts, net worth, auto-filled — read off `points`, with no forecast columns.** The scope note frames this as sitting "on top of the tracked history line," and the forecast band is a projection with its own numbers already spelled out in the caption paragraph above the chart; duplicating low/mid/high into a second table would be restating #81's numbers rather than falling back on #67/#82's.
- **`<th scope="row">` for the month cell, `<th scope="col">` for the header row, plain `<td>` for the four data cells.** A native table read by a screen reader needs both axes labelled to announce "Net worth, Mar 2026, £101,000" rather than three bare numbers in a row; `scope` is what does that without a second technology (no `aria-describedby` bookkeeping needed for a table this shape).
- **Every table/details declaration that matters for layout is inline as well as classed** (`border-collapse`, cell `padding`, `cursor: pointer` on the `<summary>`, the two `margin-top`s), matching every other load-bearing style already in this file for the reason given there: `app.css` still ships Tailwind v3's `@tailwind` directives under a v4 `@tailwindcss/postcss` pipeline, and #67/#81 both found that most utility classes don't reach the served stylesheet as a result. Worth a note here: the browser check below shows the page rendering with real spacing, borders and gaps regardless — gap/margin utilities of the same kind #67/#81 said were missing are visibly present in this run's screenshots. Not chased further (reproducing or fixing the discrepancy is outside this issue), but if a future issue picks up the long-flagged Tailwind ticket, it's worth re-checking whether it's actually still broken before spending time on it.
- **Driven for real in a browser**, continuing the pattern from #22–#29/#67/#81. `npm run dev` plus headless system Chromium (`puppeteer-core`, installed with `--no-save`) against a throwaway `/chartcheck` route (removed afterwards; `package.json` and the lockfile untouched) with a seven-month history carrying a five-month gap and two consecutive auto-filled months — confirmed 14 `<circle>` elements across a light and a forced-`.dark` copy of the chart (7 points × 2 charts), with exactly 4 of them (2 per chart) filled `hsl(var(--card))` and the rest `hsl(var(--chart-1))`; a cropped screenshot of the marker cluster shows the two hollow rings sitting visibly apart from the three filled dots; the caption read "2 of the 7 months shown were auto-filled..." in both themes; opening the `<details>` produced a seven-row table with the right month labels, £ figures and Yes/No column matching the seeded data exactly; and the dark-mode markers' computed `fill` resolved to the dark `--card`/`--chart-1` values, confirming the hollow/filled distinction survives the theme switch. No console or page errors in any of it.
- 10 new tests (4 in `net-worth.test.js` for `autoFilledPointCount`, 6 in `NetWorthChart.test.js` for the caption and table — the marker `<circle>` markup itself isn't assertable under `svelte/server`, see the trade-offs), 908 total, up from 898. `npm run lint`, `npm run check` (0 errors, same 2 pre-existing `<slot>` warnings) and `npm run build` all clean.

**Trade-offs / deviations from prompt:**
- **No component test asserts the `Circle` markup directly.** `svelte/server`'s `render` — the only test environment this repo has, per #67/#81's own header comment — gives SSR markup with no `ResizeObserver` and no container to measure, and LayerChart's `<Chart>` renders nothing at all under `<Svg>` until that measurement resolves; the existing tests already worked around the identical limitation for `Spline`/`Area` by never asserting on drawn paths. Two tests that asserted `<circle>` count and `fill` attributes were written first, both failed with an empty `<Chart>` output, and were removed rather than kept in a form that couldn't test what it claimed to. The marker *logic* (which points are hollow, in what count) is covered by `autoFilledPointCount`'s unit tests instead, and the actual SVG output was verified in a real browser as described above.
- **The table fallback renders even when `hasPlot` is false** (a single recorded month, forecast off) — see the decision above. If the intent was strictly "fallback for the chart, shown exactly when the chart is," this is a broadening; the trade-off is one more row of markup in a state that previously had none, in exchange for a screen-reader user getting the same figures a sighted user gets from the headline `£12,500` line above it.
- **The hollow marker's fill is a CSS variable, not `fill-opacity` or a pattern.** A patterned or dashed ring would be a second way to encode the same distinction and was not needed — `fill: hsl(var(--card))` plus a solid `stroke` already gives two visually distinct shapes (filled disc vs. outlined ring) without inventing a new visual language for one issue.
- **No `aria-hidden` or `role` was added to the `Circle` markers or the `<details>` table individually.** The chart's outer `role="img"`/`aria-label` (from #67/#81) already tells assistive tech to treat the whole plot area as one image and read the summary instead of walking its child SVG nodes, so the markers need no `aria-hidden` of their own; the table sits *outside* that `role="img"` container as ordinary markup, so it needs no ARIA at all beyond the `scope` attributes already on it.
- Still missing, and still #85's: the hover tooltip, the crosshair/point highlight on hover, and LayerChart's own chrome themed off this app's shadcn tokens. Nothing in this PR touches the `<Chart>` element's tooltip context or `app.css`.
- `npm test` still isn't in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (flagged since #2; the app token lacks `workflows` permission), so all 908 tests run locally but not on the PR. `build`/`check`/`lint` remain the only CI gates.

## Net worth chart: theme LayerChart's chrome off this app's design tokens — 2026-08-07
<!-- METRICS:net-worth-chart-theme-layerchart-chrome-off-design-tokens -->
- **Execution Duration:** __DURATION__ seconds
- **Model:** __MODEL__
- **Turns:** __TURNS__
- **Input Tokens:** __INPUT_TOKENS__
- **Output Tokens:** __OUTPUT_TOKENS__
- **Estimated Cost:** $__COST__ (from Claude Code's reported total_cost_usd)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable GPT-5-tier model:** $__GPT_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable Gemini-tier model:** $__GEMINI_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)

**Decisions:**
- **Took the failed session's findings as given, verified them against the installed package rather than re-deriving them.** The issue text already names the exact cause (missing `layerchart/core.css` import), the exact trap (`.lc-tooltip-root` portaled to `<body>`, not nestable under `.lc-root-container`), and the exact five properties (`--color-primary`, `--color-surface-content`, `--color-surface-100/200/300`). `node_modules/layerchart/dist/styles/*.css` confirmed all of it directly — the shadcn-svelte preset shows the right shape with the wrong values (bare tokens, not `hsl()`-wrapped), and the skeleton presets confirm the `light-dark(-100, -300)` tooltip background and the two-sibling-rule structure. No time spent re-discovering what the previous session already paid 100 turns to find.
- **`-100`/`-200`/`-300` all take the same value within each rule**, not just `-100` matching `-300` as the issue's finding 5 requires. This app has one surface per context (`--card` for the chart, `--popover` for the tooltip), not LayerChart's assumed three-step scale, so there's no third value to give `-200` that wouldn't be invented. `--color-surface-200` isn't read by anything in the installed `layerchart@2.1.0` version (`grep`-verified across `dist/components`) so this is currently inert, but the issue's own property list requires it declared, and declaring it as anything other than the other two would imply a distinction that doesn't exist in this app's token set.
- **`--color-primary` is the same token (`hsl(var(--primary))`) in both rules.** The issue's findings only call out `-content`/`-100`/`-200`/`-300` as differing between the card and popover surfaces; `--color-primary` drives the crosshair/highlight fill (`Highlight.base.svelte`), which is chart-identity colour, not surface colour, so there's no popover-vs-card case to make for it.
- **The `.lc-highlight-point` ring rule went in this issue, not deferred to #87**, per the issue's own instruction — nothing draws a highlight until #87 builds the hover layer, but the mapping is chrome and belongs with the rest of it.
- **`src/app.css.test.js` reads the stylesheet as text via `node:fs`** rather than rendering it and reading computed styles, matching the issue's own framing: every failure mode here is invisible in a passing render (a white-on-white tooltip still "renders"), so the test asserts the structural traps directly — the import string, `.lc-tooltip-root` as a top-level selector via a negative match against the nested form, all five properties present and `hsl(var(...))`-shaped, `-300` string-equal to `-100`, and `--stroke-color` present on `.lc-highlight-point`. `svelte-check` flagged the first draft's `String.match()` results as possibly-null (this repo's `jsconfig.json` type-checks plain `.js` test files); a small `declaredValue()` helper that throws on a missing property replaced the inline optional-chained assertions, which is also why the "is missing" custom failure message is gone — the thrown error names the property instead.
- **Verified for real in a browser**, on top of the text-level test, because the issue explicitly scopes the test to structure and leaves "does it look right" to a human. `npm run dev` plus headless system Chromium (`puppeteer-core`, installed with `--no-save`) against the real dashboard route, seeded via `localStorage` (the app's own persistence key, `uk-wealth-tracker:data`) with two monthly snapshots rather than a scratch route this time, since the existing `/` route already renders `NetWorthChart` and needed no throwaway page. Confirmed in both a light and a forced-`.dark` copy: `getComputedStyle` on `.lc-root-container` resolves all three inspected custom properties to the intended token's `hsl()` value in each mode; the y-axis gridlines render as a light `rgb(229,229,229)` on white and a dark `rgb(38,38,38)` on near-black (both visible, neither the library's unthemed default); the tick marks and axis label text swap from near-black to near-white between modes; and cropped screenshots of the chart in both themes show a readable axis, gridlines and labels with no white-on-white or invisible-chrome artifacts. No console or page errors. Screenshots and scratch scripts removed afterwards; `package.json` and the lockfile untouched.
- 7 new tests (`app.css.test.js`), 915 total, up from 908. `npm run lint`, `npm run check` (0 errors, same 2 pre-existing `<slot>` warnings) and `npm run build` all clean.

**Trade-offs / deviations from prompt:**
- **No changes to `NetWorthChart.svelte`, per the issue's own scope note.** The hover tooltip and crosshair that would make this mapping visible in normal use are #87's; today the only chrome this mapping is reachable through is the axis (rule, ticks, gridlines, label text) and, once #87 lands, the tooltip popover and highlight ring. Confirmed the axis path in the browser check above; the tooltip/highlight paths were confirmed structurally (the CSS variables resolve correctly on both selectors) but not visually, since nothing in the current component triggers them.
- **`--color-surface-200` is declared but currently unreachable in the installed LayerChart version.** Flagged as a decision above; noting it again here because it means one third of each rule's surface properties is unverifiable in a browser today — there's no component path in `layerchart@2.1.0` that reads it, so "does it look right" for that property specifically can't be checked until either LayerChart adds a consumer or a future version does.
- **The test's regexes assume single-line-ish declarations and one rule per selector** (`(?:^|\n)SELECTOR\s*\{([^}]*)\}` with no `s` flag beyond what `[^}]*` already spans across newlines). This is fine for the hand-written CSS this issue adds — each of the two rules is declared once, at the top level, with simple `property: value;` lines — but it isn't a general CSS parser: a duplicate `.lc-root-container` rule elsewhere in the file, or a nested rule containing an unmatched `}`, would confuse it. Acceptable for a file this small and hand-maintained; a real parser would be over-engineering for five properties across two selectors.
- `npm test` still isn't in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (flagged since #2; the app token lacks `workflows` permission), so all 915 tests run locally but not on the PR. `build`/`check`/`lint` remain the only CI gates.

## Net worth chart: hover tooltip and crosshair — 2026-08-07
<!-- METRICS:net-worth-chart-hover-tooltip-and-crosshair -->
- **Execution Duration:** __DURATION__ seconds
- **Model:** __MODEL__
- **Turns:** __TURNS__
- **Input Tokens:** __INPUT_TOKENS__
- **Output Tokens:** __OUTPUT_TOKENS__
- **Estimated Cost:** $__COST__ (from Claude Code's reported total_cost_usd)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable GPT-5-tier model:** $__GPT_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable Gemini-tier model:** $__GEMINI_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)

**Decisions:**
- **Took the previous session's `flatData` finding as given and verified it against the installed package before building on it.** `dist/states/chart.svelte.js`'s `flatData` is literally `[...data, ...everyMarkWithItsOwnData]`, `TooltipContext`'s `bisect-x` bisects exactly that array, and `HighlightState.points` switches to a per-series lookup the moment `ctx.tooltip.series.length > 0` — all three confirmed by reading the source, so the two-`<Chart>` split the issue prescribes is the right shape and not just the shape that happened to work in a browser once. No time spent re-deriving a diagnosis that already cost 100 turns.
- **The two charts are stacked in one `position: relative` wrapper and always both rendered**, even with the forecast switched off, rather than collapsing to a single chart when there's no band to draw. A conditional collapse would mean two code paths for the same picture — one where the axes belong to the interactive chart and one where they don't — and the interactive chart's `flatData` is the one thing this issue cannot afford to have vary. The lower chart therefore always owns the axes, the gridlines and (when there is one) the band; the upper always owns the tracked spline, the markers, the highlight and the tooltip. Confirmed in the browser that both `.lc-root-container` elements measure to the same pixel rect (`{x:9,y:596,w:1082,h:288}` for each), which is what makes the split invisible.
- **One frozen `CHART_PADDING` constant, shared by both `<Chart>` elements**, rather than the same object literal written twice. The two charts only line up if they reserve identical room for the axes; two literals would agree today and drift the first time one was edited. Same reasoning as `FORECAST_HORIZONS` being frozen above it.
- **`#82`'s `Circle` dropped its `data={points}` prop and now inherits the chart's.** `CircleState.dataMode` is true when *any* of `cx`/`cy`/`r` is a data prop, and this one passes `cx`/`cy` as accessor functions, so it stays in data mode and resolves against `chartDataArray(ctx.data)` — same markers, same hollow/filled logic, but `circleMarkInfo` now returns no `data`, so the tracked months are no longer duplicated into the array `bisect-x` searches. Verified: five markers still draw, and the hovered month is still correct at every one of them.
- **The tooltip's words are `netWorthTooltipReading(point)`, exported from a new `<script module>` block**, with the four `Intl` formatters and the four `format*` helpers moved up there with it. The function is what the tests can reach; the formatters had to follow because `formatMoney`/`formatMonth` are what it formats with, and they depend on no prop, so one set per app beats one per mounted chart. Nothing in the instance script changed as a result — module-scope bindings are in scope for it.
- **Net worth leads the rows, then investments, then debts**, per the issue: a reading that gave only the difference would make a £5,000 month look identical whether it is £5,000 of holdings or £305,000 against a £300,000 debt. That exact pair is the first unit test.
- **Only the net worth row carries a colour swatch** (`hsl(var(--chart-1))`, the tracked line's hue). Investments and debts are components of that figure, not series on the chart, and giving them dots would imply two more lines a reader could go looking for. `NetWorthTooltipRow.color` is optional for exactly this reason, and a test asserts precisely one row has it.
- **The auto-filled month is named in the heading as `Mar 2026 · auto-filled`**, using the same `·` separator the headline caption above the chart already uses. This is where #82's hollow marker gets said in words — a shape only means something to a reader who has already decoded the legend, and the tooltip is the one moment the chart is being asked directly about that month.
- **`netWorthTooltipReading` returns `null` for anything that is not a recorded month** — nullish in, but also any datum whose `net_worth` isn't a finite number. The two-`<Chart>` split means a band point should never reach it; this is the guard that keeps `£NaN` out of the UI if some future mark reintroduces the problem, and it is a unit test with a real band-shaped point in it.
- **`<Highlight lines points />` with no colour props at all.** LayerChart's own defaults already resolve the crosshair rule to `--color-surface-content` and the dot to `--color-primary` with a `--stroke-color` ring, and #85 mapped all three onto this app's tokens; overriding them here would have re-specified the same values in a second place. Confirmed in the browser that the dot computes to `rgb(23,23,23)` on `--primary` with a white ring in light mode and `rgb(250,250,250)` with a near-black ring in dark — i.e. #85's `.lc-highlight-point` rule is doing its job now that something finally draws one.
- **`<Tooltip.Root x="data">` with `y` left on the pointer**, and `aria-hidden="true"` on the portaled root. Snapping x puts the popover on the month it describes rather than a few pixels off it; leaving y on the pointer means a reading taken near the top of the plot doesn't cover the line it's about. The `aria-hidden` is because this sits inside a `role="img"` whose `aria-label` is the accessible route to the chart — the tooltip is a pointer affordance, can't be reached without hovering, and duplicating it into the accessibility tree would just put unlabelled figures next to the summary that already covers them. #82's table fallback is untouched and remains the full-data route.
- **Driven for real in a browser**, continuing the pattern from #22–#29/#67/#81/#82/#85. `npm run dev` plus headless system Chromium (`puppeteer-core`, installed with `--no-save` and removed afterwards; `package.json` and the lockfile untouched) against the real dashboard route seeded via `localStorage`, with a five-month history containing one auto-filled month, one zero-debt month and one underwater month. Confirmed: sweeping the pointer across the plot reads back `Jan 2026 → £90,000/£100,000/£10,000`, `Feb 2026`, `Mar 2026 · auto-filled`, `Apr 2026` with `Debts £0`, and `May 2026` with `Net worth -£50,000` — each snapping to its own month, with the crosshair rule and dot moving with it (`x1` 13 → 254 → 486 → 742 → 991); hovering out into the forecast parks the whole hover layer on May 2026 rather than following the pointer (crosshair pinned at the last recorded month, tooltip still reading it); the popover flips to the left of the crosshair near the right edge rather than running off the card; leaving the plot removes the tooltip, the rule and the dot entirely; and cropped screenshots in light and forced-`.dark` show a readable popover on the right surface in both. No console or page errors in any of it.
- **Updated the dashboard's own intro copy**, which still said "The chart's hover/marker layer lands in a later build" — true when #67 wrote it, wrong as of #82 and now doubly so. It reads "Hover a month to read its figures back."
- 8 new tests (`NetWorthChart.test.js`), 923 total, up from 915. `npm run lint`, `npm run check` (0 errors, same 2 pre-existing `<slot>` warnings) and `npm run build` all clean.

**Trade-offs / deviations from prompt:**
- **The UTC test re-imports the component under a fake time zone, which is heavier than the other tests here.** The issue asks for the UTC month name to be covered, but CI runs in UTC, so an assertion that a January point reads "Jan 2026" passes whether or not the formatter is pinned — it tests nothing. The test therefore sets `process.env.TZ='America/Los_Angeles'`, calls `vi.resetModules()` and `await`s a dynamic `import()` *inside* the `try`, so the module's formatters are constructed while the wrong zone is in effect. Mutation-checked: deleting `timeZone: 'UTC'` from `monthFormatter` fails it with `expected 'Dec 2025' to be 'Jan 2026'`, and restoring it passes. The cost is one test that knows about module registries and one that has to be `async`; the alternative was a test that would never have caught the bug it names.
- **No test asserts any tooltip or crosshair *markup*.** Same wall as #82's markers, one step further on: `<Chart>` renders nothing under `svelte/server`, and a `Tooltip.Root` with no pointer over it renders nothing even in a browser, so there is no element to assert against in either environment. The words are covered as a pure function and the drawn result was verified in a real browser, as described above. This is the reason the issue asked for `netWorthTooltipReading` to exist at all.
- **No tooltip rows for the forecast band**, which the issue lists as welcome but not required. Declined deliberately: the band is separate data on separate marks in a separate chart now, so a forecast row would have to be re-derived from the hovered x rather than read off the hovered point, and a row that can fall out of step with the shading drawn behind it is worse than no row. If it's wanted later, the honest way is to give the reading function the band as a second argument and have it look the month up — a change to this function's signature, not a change to the chart.
- **Two `<Chart>` elements mean two `ResizeObserver`s, two sets of scales and two SVG trees for one picture.** That is a real cost and it is paid on every dashboard render, including the common case where there is no forecast to separate out. It is what the issue's verified finding requires, and it buys a `bisect-x` search over one sorted array instead of four concatenated ones; but it is worth stating plainly that this is a workaround for how `flatData` and `<Highlight>` interact in `layerchart@2.1.0`, not an architecture anyone would choose from scratch.
- **The hover layer covers the plot area, so anything drawn there is no longer directly clickable.** Nothing in this chart is interactive today, so this costs nothing now — but a future issue that wants clickable markers will have to put them on the upper chart (where the `Circle` already is) rather than the lower one, or route the click through `onTooltipClick`. Noted here because the constraint isn't visible from either chart's markup alone.
- `npm test` still isn't in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (flagged since #2; the app token lacks `workflows` permission), so all 923 tests run locally but not on the PR. `build`/`check`/`lint` remain the only CI gates.

## Defined Benefit pension income calculation — 2026-08-07
<!-- METRICS:defined-benefit-pension-income-calculation -->
- **Execution Duration:** __DURATION__ seconds
- **Model:** __MODEL__
- **Turns:** __TURNS__
- **Input Tokens:** __INPUT_TOKENS__
- **Output Tokens:** __OUTPUT_TOKENS__
- **Estimated Cost:** $__COST__ (from Claude Code's reported total_cost_usd)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable GPT-5-tier model:** $__GPT_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable Gemini-tier model:** $__GEMINI_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)

**Decisions:**
- **No new stored fields.** The issue names exactly four inputs — accrual rate, years of service, expected salary, direct annual income — and `Pension` has held `db_accrual_rate`/`db_years`/`db_salary`/`db_annual_income` (nullable, normalised, range-validated) since #5. So this issue is entirely formula plus form: a new `$lib/defined-benefit.js`, the four inputs added to `PensionTracker`'s Defined Benefit branch in place of #29's forward-reference note, and a new `DefinedBenefitIncome` card under it. `model.js` and `types.js` are untouched, which also keeps the WealthR CSV import path (Phase 2) unaffected.
- **One rule for what counts as entered: a positive number.** `null`, `0` and negatives all read as "not recorded", for all four `db_*` fields. `types.js` says a stated income wins "when set", and strictly `0` is set — but a form cannot tell a blank box from a typed zero, no Defined Benefit scheme accrues at 0% or pays £0, and a stated `0` silently overriding a complete set of accrual inputs looks exactly like a bug rather than like arithmetic. Documented as convention (2) in the module header and pinned by its own test (`db_annual_income: 0` falls through to the formula). `asRecordedInput` is exported because `PensionTracker` writes the fields with it too, so the form and the formula agree on what "blank" means rather than each having a rule.
- **`accruedIncome` returns `null`, not `0`, when an input is missing.** "You haven't told me your salary yet" and "your pension is £0 a year" are different sentences and a zero can only say one of them. That distinction is what lets the tracker row say *No income yet* and the summary table say *Needs accrual rate and pensionable salary* — naming the specific fields, off `missingInputs`, rather than showing a confident £0.
- **Accrual is stored as a percent and asked for as both.** `db_accrual_rate` holds `1.6667`, because that is what the arithmetic wants, but no scheme member has ever been told they are in a "1.6667% scheme" — they are in a 1/60th. So the form has a fraction select beside the percentage box, and the percentage is the single source of truth: picking 1/80th writes `1.25`, and typing `1.25` derives the select back to 1/80th. The "is this rate that fraction" tolerance (0.005 percentage points, half the last place a 2dp rate carries) lives once, in `accrualFractionLabel`, and the component compares *labels* rather than restating it — so a stored `1.67` still reads as 1/60th while `1.9%`, which is nobody's fraction, stays a percentage. `COMMON_ACCRUAL_DENOMINATORS` is the seven real ones (1/45, 1/49 LGPS, 1/54 NHS 2015, 1/57 Teachers', 1/60, 1/75 USS, 1/80), with proper ordinal suffixes so a future 1/43rd doesn't render as "1/43th".
- **`projectDefinedBenefit` exists because "years of service" is the field this feature gets wrong.** A benefit statement quotes service *to date*; a retirement projection needs service *at retirement*, and quietly reading one as the other understates a mid-career pension by half. The form's help text states the convention, and the card's "If you keep accruing" panel does the sum — further years plus optional salary growth — rather than leaving it as mental arithmetic. A stated income comes back untouched with `basis: 'stated'`: there is nothing in it to project from, and inventing a growth rate for someone else's scheme figure would be worse than declining.
- **The projection is per-scheme, with a picker, not applied across the whole list.** You accrue in the job you are in while deferred schemes sit still, so one shared "another eight years" figure spread over every scheme would overstate the total. The picker only renders when there is more than one scheme on the accrual route, and the controls are component-local — "what if I stayed another eight years" is a question being asked, not a fact about the scheme, and there is no field to persist it in.
- **`capitalEquivalent` goes through `fire.js`'s own `fireNumber`.** "What is my DB pension worth as a pot" and "what pot do I need for this income" are the same arithmetic, so they are the same function — £25,000 a year at 4% is the £625,000 the FIRE tab would quote for the same target. It is labelled as a comparison, not a transfer value: a real CETV comes from the scheme's actuary on assumptions this app has no access to, and an inflation-linked income for life is worth rather more than a pot drawn at a fixed rate. The import direction is one-way (`defined-benefit.js` → `fire.js`), matching `salary-sacrifice.js` → `tax.js`.
- **A statement and an accrual calculation that disagree are both reported.** The statement wins (convention 3), but `routeDifference` is carried and the card names the gap when it is £1 or more, because a scheme that says £14,000 against inputs that come to £10,000 almost always means a mistyped salary, not a generous employer.
- **Every omission is written down rather than approximated.** Early retirement reduction (~4%/yr), late retirement uplift, revaluation in deferment, CPI indexation in payment, the automatic 3/80ths lump sum, commutation at ~12:1, spouse's pension, GMP/State Pension integration, and income tax — none has a field in `Pension` to hold it, so none is guessed at. They are listed in the module header and again in the card's own disclaimer, so a reader sees the same list whether they are reading the code or the screen. CARE gets a specific caveat: a real career-average pension is the sum of each year's own revalued slice, and one salary figure can only stand in for the career-average revalued salary.
- **Driven for real in a browser**, continuing the pattern from #22–#29/#67/#81/#82/#85/#87. `npm run dev` plus headless system Chromium (`puppeteer-core`, installed with `--no-save` and removed afterwards; `package.json` and the lockfile untouched) against the real `/pensions` route, seeded via `localStorage` with five pots — a DC pot, a full accrual scheme, a statement-only CARE scheme, a half-filled scheme and one where the two routes disagree. Confirmed: the tracker rows read `£18,750/yr · £1,563/mo · 1/60th × 25 yrs × £45,000`, `£6,250/yr · taken from your statement` and `No income yet — …`; the card totals `£39,000/yr`, `3 of 4 schemes costed` and `£975,010` at 4%; the disagreement warning names £14,000 against £10,000; choosing a Defined Benefit type swaps the pot-value fields for the four `db_*` inputs and back; picking 1/80th writes `1.25` and picking 1/60th writes `1.6667` with the select derived back to 60; typing `1.9` drops the select to "Other" and `1.25` snaps it to 1/80th; **Edit** round-trips all four fields into the form; adding a scheme through the form persists it and it survives a reload; and the projection reads `would pay £31,353 a year — £2,613 a month — on 33 years of service and a salary of £57,005 by then, up £12,603 a year on what it is worth today`, dropping the salary clause cleanly when growth is 0. No console or page errors in any of it. The first draft of that projection sentence built its punctuation out of `{#if}` blocks and left a space before the comma whenever a clause dropped out; it is now one derived string (`projectionTail`) for that reason.
- 61 new tests (45 in `defined-benefit.test.js`, 14 in `DefinedBenefitIncome.test.js`, 2 added to `PensionTracker.test.js`), 984 total, up from 923. `npm run lint`, `npm run check` (0 errors, same 2 pre-existing `<slot>` warnings) and `npm run build` all clean.

**Trade-offs / deviations from prompt:**
- **The Defined Benefit half of the add/edit form has no server-rendered test.** It only renders once a Defined Benefit type is chosen in the select, which is an interaction, and `svelte/server`'s `render` covers the initial render only — so the fraction select and the four inputs are unreachable from `PensionTracker.test.js`, exactly as the add/edit/remove logic already was. The arithmetic behind them is covered directly in `defined-benefit.test.js` and the controls themselves were driven in a real browser, as above; the test file's header now says which half is out of reach and why.
- **Display rounding makes the totals look like they don't multiply.** Money is formatted with `maximumFractionDigits: 0` throughout this app, so a scheme stored as `1.6667%` (rather than exactly 100/60) shows `£18,750` but is really £18,750.38 — and the capital equivalent then reads `£975,010` where a reader doing 25 × £39,000 in their head expects £975,000. The underlying figures are consistent to the penny and this is the same convention `SalarySacrifice` and the tracker rows already use, so it is left alone rather than special-cased here.
- **Defined Benefit income does not yet reach any other tab.** It is not netted off the FIRE magic number, not added to the forecast, and not in the retirement income stream builder — that last one is #33's explicit scope ("DB, annuity, SIPP drawdown, ISA withdrawals, GIA dividends, State Pension"), and `definedBenefitTotals` is shaped to be the thing #33 calls. Deliberately not started here.
- **Found, and left alone, an app-wide styling problem.** `src/app.css` uses Tailwind v3's `@tailwind base/components/utilities` directives against Tailwind v4.3.3, and the built stylesheet contains no preflight at all — no `box-sizing`, no margin reset, no `font-family`, so every page in this app renders in Times New Roman with browser-default spacing. Verified it is pre-existing and universal by screenshotting the untouched `/tax` route alongside `/pensions`: both are identically unstyled, and `grep box-sizing` on `build/_app/immutable/assets/0.*.css` returns nothing. Structure and layout of the new card are correct and match the surrounding components, but "does it look right" could only be checked structurally in this environment. Fixing it means changing the CSS baseline of the whole app, which is not something to do inside a Defined Benefit pension issue — it wants its own issue and its own visual review.
- **`db_years` is capped at 100 by `MAX_PENSIONABLE_YEARS`** rather than validated as an error, matching the range `model.js` already checks it against. A projection that would take total service past 100 clamps silently instead of reporting a problem — consistent with how `fire.js` clamps its own horizons, but it does mean a nonsense input produces a number rather than a complaint.
- `npm test` still isn't in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (flagged since #2; the app token lacks `workflows` permission), so all 984 tests run locally but not on the PR. `build`/`check`/`lint` remain the only CI gates.

## Pension tax relief display per pot — 2026-08-07
<!-- METRICS:pension-tax-relief-display-per-pot -->
- **Execution Duration:** __DURATION__ seconds
- **Model:** __MODEL__
- **Turns:** __TURNS__
- **Input Tokens:** __INPUT_TOKENS__
- **Output Tokens:** __OUTPUT_TOKENS__
- **Estimated Cost:** $__COST__ (from Claude Code's reported total_cost_usd)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable GPT-5-tier model:** $__GPT_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable Gemini-tier model:** $__GEMINI_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)

**Decisions:**
- **`contribution_pct` is read as the net, pre-relief amount — what actually leaves take-home pay — not the grossed-up figure that lands in the pot.** This is the one genuinely ambiguous call the issue leaves open: `Pension.contribution_pct`'s own doc comment just says "own contribution as a percentage of salary". Read as gross, there is nothing for a "tax relief" feature to add; read as net, grossing it up (`grossUpContribution`, £80 → £100) is exactly what makes basic-rate relief visible, and it matches how gov.uk itself describes a workplace minimum ("you pay 4%, get 1% tax relief, for 5% total"). Documented as convention (1) in `$lib/pension-relief.js`'s header specifically so it can be revisited if a future issue reveals it should have gone the other way.
- **Relief is assessed at one flat marginal rate per pot (`tax.js`'s own `marginalTaxRate` on the whole salary), not sliced band-by-band the way `salary-sacrifice.js`'s `sacrificeSlices` treats a sacrifice.** A personal contribution doesn't reduce the salary the way a sacrifice does, and slicing it would double-count relief already folded into the 60% taper band's own marginal rate. The simplification is named as convention (2) rather than silently narrowing the issue's "20% basic, 40% higher" wording to only those two numbers — the real marginal rate is used throughout, so a Scottish taxpayer correctly sees 19–48% and someone in the £100k–£125,140 taper correctly sees the 60% rate as their relief rate too, one flat figure per pot either way.
- **Only `dc_workplace` and `sipp` pots get a relief row; Lifetime ISA pots are carried separately with their own note, not folded into the same table.** A LISA's 25% top-up is a government bonus on savings, not income tax relief on earnings — it doesn't vary with the member's tax rate and can't be topped up via Self Assessment, so giving it a "marginal rate" column would misstate how it works. `isReliefEligible`/`isLifetimeIsa` split the three relevant `PENSION_POT_TYPES` members cleanly; Defined Benefit and State pots (no contribution field at all) are dropped the same way `defined-benefit.js` drops a non-Defined-Benefit pot handed to it.
- **New module, not folded into `salary-sacrifice.js` or `tax.js` despite reusing `marginalTaxRate`.** Relief-at-source grossing-up is a distinct mechanic from either — it isn't a reduction in taxable income (that's sacrifice's job) and it isn't a band calculation on its own (that's `tax.js`'s). `$lib/pension-relief.js` imports from `tax.js` only, the same one-directional shape `salary-sacrifice.js` and `defined-benefit.js` already have, so nothing downstream of it needs to know it exists.
- **`PensionTaxRelief.svelte` reads `pensions` and `profile` without binding**, seeded from the store the same read-only way `TaxCalculator` seeds from `profile.gross_salary`/`profile.tax_region` — there is nothing to type into this card itself; every figure falls out of a pot's own contribution percentage (already entered in `PensionTracker` above it) and the profile's salary/region (already entered on the Tax tab).
- **What this deliberately does not model**, written once in the module header and once in the card's own disclaimer rather than left implicit: the £3,600 minimum relief that grosses up even a non-earner's contribution; the tapered and money-purchase annual allowances `salary-sacrifice.js` also leaves out; employer contributions (never the employee's income, so never personally relieved); and net-pay-arrangement workplace schemes, where relief at every rate is already automatic because the contribution never leaves gross pay — this module always assumes relief-at-source, the SIPP-standard case and the one that actually needs a "claim extra via Self Assessment" note.
- **Driven for real in a browser**, continuing the pattern from #22–#29/#67/#81/#82/#85/#87/#90. `npm run dev` plus headless system Chromium (`puppeteer-core`, installed with `--no-save` and removed afterwards; `package.json` and the lockfile untouched) against the real `/pensions` route, seeded via `localStorage` with an £80,000 salary and a DC Workplace pot (5%), a SIPP (10%), a LISA and a Defined Benefit scheme. Confirmed: the card reads `£12,000/yr` contributed across 2 eligible pots, `£3,000 automatic, £3,000 to claim`, `£15,000/yr lands in your pots`; each pot's row shows `marginal rate 40%` and the correct £4,000/£1,000/£5,000/£1,000 and £8,000/£2,000/£10,000/£2,000 splits; the Self Assessment prompt names "2 pots"; the LISA note reads "1 Lifetime ISA pot is not shown above… 25% government bonus…"; and the Defined Benefit note explains that pot's income is worked out separately, above. No console or page errors.
- 32 new tests (23 in `pension-relief.test.js`, 9 in `PensionTaxRelief.test.js`), 1016 total, up from 984. `npm run lint`, `npm run check` (0 errors, same 2 pre-existing `<slot>` warnings) and `npm run build` all clean.

**Trade-offs / deviations from prompt:**
- **README.md's "20% basic, 40% higher" is illustrative, not a hard cutoff — the actual marginal rate can be anywhere `tax.js` allows** (19–48% in Scotland, 60%/67.5% inside the personal allowance taper). Narrowing the calculation to literally two numbers would have been simpler but wrong the moment a Scottish or high-earning profile used the card; using `marginalTaxRate` throughout keeps the display correct everywhere `tax.js` already is, at the cost of the card sometimes showing a rate the issue text never mentions.
- **The net/gross reading of `contribution_pct` (convention (1) above) is a genuine judgement call, not something the data model or README.md settles.** If a future issue reveals the field is meant to already be the grossed-up, land-in-the-pot figure — which is how some workplace pension portals quote it — this module's `grossUpContribution` would need inverting rather than removing; flagged here rather than picked silently.
- **Employer contributions are named in the disclaimer but never shown, even though `PensionTracker` already collects `employer_pct`.** README.md's line is about the *member's* relief specifically ("claim extra via Self Assessment" is not something you do for pay you never received), and showing an employer figure alongside without a relief number of its own risked implying it does get relief. Left out rather than added as an unearned-looking zero.
- **No interaction test for the relief table beyond initial render** — same wall as every other pension card: `svelte/server`'s `render` covers first paint only, and this component has no local state or inputs to interact with in the first place (everything derives from props), so that ceiling costs nothing here. Confirmed by driving the real card in a browser, as above.
- `npm test` still isn't in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (flagged since #2; the app token lacks `workflows` permission), so all 1016 tests run locally but not on the PR. `build`/`check`/`lint` remain the only CI gates.

## Dividend income planner — 2026-08-07
<!-- METRICS:dividend-income-planner -->
- **Execution Duration:** __DURATION__ seconds
- **Model:** __MODEL__
- **Turns:** __TURNS__
- **Input Tokens:** __INPUT_TOKENS__
- **Output Tokens:** __OUTPUT_TOKENS__
- **Estimated Cost:** $__COST__ (from Claude Code's reported total_cost_usd)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable GPT-5-tier model:** $__GPT_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable Gemini-tier model:** $__GEMINI_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)

**Decisions:**
- **The building/income phase split is modelled as `fire.js`'s accumulation/drawdown split's dividend-specific twin, around one age slider — not two independent panels.** `$lib/dividends.js`'s `dividendIncomePlan` projects the portfolio (`projectDividendPortfolio`) from now to the switch age with every holding compounding under its own recorded `strategy` (DRIP reinvests, income pays out), then hands the holdings as they stand at that date to `dividendIncomePhase`, which asks what every one of them would pay if it took its dividend as income from that point on — regardless of what strategy it was originally recorded with. That "regardless" is the deliberate reading of README.md's "income phase": the age slider is a plan-wide switch, not a per-holding override, and a holding's own `strategy` field (set in `DividendTracker`) only governs what happens to it *before* the switch.
- **Income-phase income never depletes, on purpose.** Unlike `fire.js`'s `portfolioRunway`, which spends the pot down and can hit zero, the income phase here only ever draws the yield — the pot itself is carried forward flat. This is a real simplification (no capital growth is modelled either, so "flat" is exactly true rather than an approximation), but it matches what "income phase" means for a dividend portfolio specifically: the point of the strategy is that the capital is never touched, so there is no runway calculation to make.
- **Annual yield compounds via `auto-invest.js`'s own `monthlyGrowthRate`** (the geometric monthly equivalent of an annual rate), not a flat ÷12 — the same conversion `forecast.js` and `fire.js` already use for growth rates, so a DRIP holding's twelve-month compounding lands back on exactly its stated annual yield rather than drifting from it. This is also what makes the DRIP-vs-income comparison in `dividends.test.js` meaningful: twelve identical monthly coupons off a value that never grows add up to *less* than the amount a reinvested holding ends the year worth more by, which is the whole point of the two strategies existing.
- **Tax is out of scope, deliberately and visibly.** README.md's fuller Dividend Income Planner section also asks for the £500/yr dividend allowance and 10.75%/35.75% GIA rates, but issue #35 ("UK dividend allowance + GIA tax rates") is a separate, later milestone build for exactly that. Every figure here is gross; `dividendPortfolioSummary`'s `sheltered`/`unsheltered` split (by `TAX_SHELTERED_WRAPPERS`) exists only so #35 has a slice to key its calculation off, not because this module applies any tax itself — called out in both the module header and the planner card's own disclaimer, the same "not modelled" pattern `pension-relief.js` and `salary-sacrifice.js` use for their own deferred scope.
- **`DividendTracker.svelte`/`DividendIncomePlanner.svelte` follow `PensionTracker.svelte`/`PensionTaxRelief.svelte`'s shapes exactly**: `dividends[]` sits flat on `AppData` (already true since issue #2's data model), one component owns add/edit/remove against it with `bind:dividends`, the other reads `dividends`/`profile` without binding and does the maths. No activity log, for the same reason pensions have none — `ACTIVITY_LOG_ENTITY_TYPES` covers `investment`/`debt` only (issue #14's scope) and was never widened.
- **Driven for real in a browser**, continuing the pattern from #22–#33/#90. `npm run build` + `vite preview` against the real `/dividends` route, driven with headless system Chromium via `playwright-core` installed in a scratch directory outside the repo (`package.json`/lockfile untouched, removed afterwards). Added a £50,000 Stocks & Shares ISA holding at 4% yield with a £200/mo contribution, set age 40 → switch age 55: the tracker showed "£50,000 of value paying £2,000/yr", the building-phase table compounded to £138,978 by age 55 (£52,978 reinvested, £36,000 contributed), and the income phase correctly re-based off that grown figure — £5,559/yr, £463/mo, accumulating £166,774 drawn by age 85 with the pot itself never falling. No console or page errors. (The unstyled Tailwind rendering in this preview environment is pre-existing — confirmed identical on `/pensions`, not something this change introduced.)
- 37 new tests (26 in `dividends.test.js`, 6 in `DividendTracker.test.js`, 5 in `DividendIncomePlanner.test.js`), 1053 total, up from 1016. `npm run lint`, `npm run check` (0 errors, same 2 pre-existing `<slot>` warnings) and `npm run build` all clean.

**Trade-offs / deviations from prompt:**
- **The age slider is a single "switch to income" boundary, not two separately-steerable ages.** README.md just says "with an age slider" (singular), and `FireCalculator.svelte`'s own retirement-age slider is the closest existing precedent — one age doing double duty as "when building stops" and "when income starts" reads as the simpler, more literal match than inventing a second slider the issue never asks for.
- **No per-holding override in the income phase** — once switched, every holding pays income at its own yield regardless of its `strategy` field, including a holding still marked `drip`. An alternative reading (only holdings marked `income` ever pay income, DRIP holdings keep reinvesting forever) would make the age slider inert for an all-DRIP portfolio, which is a worse fit for "income phase" as a plan-wide life stage than as a per-holding switch nobody asked for.
- **Capital growth is not modelled, matching `forecast.js`'s separation of concerns rather than reusing its growth-rate machinery.** A holding's value here only moves from its own yield and its contribution schedule; folding in `profile.growth_rate` or a per-holding price-return assumption would have made this tab disagree with the net worth dashboard about the same holding's future value, which seemed worse than leaving price growth out entirely and saying so in the disclaimer.
- **`DividendIncomePlanner`'s building/income tables are sampled and rendered as HTML tables, not plotted** — the same choice `FireCalculator.svelte` and `CompoundingPanel.svelte` already made for their own projections; LayerChart is wired into the net worth dashboard (#67/#81/#82/#85/#87) but not yet into any forecast-style panel elsewhere in the app, and this issue's own text does not ask for a chart specifically.
- **No interaction test beyond initial render for either new component** — the same wall as every pension card: `svelte/server`'s `render` covers first paint only. `DividendTracker`'s add/edit/remove logic is left to manual verification (mirroring `PensionTracker`, which has the identical gap); `DividendIncomePlanner` has no local state to interact with beyond the age inputs, both confirmed working by driving the real page in a browser, as above.
- `npm test` still isn't in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (flagged since #2; the app token lacks `workflows` permission), so all 1053 tests run locally but not on the PR. `build`/`check`/`lint` remain the only CI gates.

## UK dividend allowance + GIA tax rates — 2026-08-07
<!-- METRICS:uk-dividend-allowance-gia-tax-rates -->
- **Execution Duration:** __DURATION__ seconds
- **Model:** __MODEL__
- **Turns:** __TURNS__
- **Input Tokens:** __INPUT_TOKENS__
- **Output Tokens:** __OUTPUT_TOKENS__
- **Estimated Cost:** $__COST__ (from Claude Code's reported total_cost_usd)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable GPT-5-tier model:** $__GPT_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable Gemini-tier model:** $__GEMINI_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)

**Decisions:**
- **New module `$lib/dividend-tax.js`, not an extension of `dividends.js` or `tax.js`.** #34 built the dividend planner and its header already named this build as the thing that would tax it, leaving `dividendPortfolioSummary`'s `sheltered`/`unsheltered` split as the hook. Keeping the tax in its own module preserves that separation — `dividends.js` still returns gross figures and knows nothing about wrappers beyond splitting on them, and `dividend-tax.js` imports from `enums.js`/`tax.js`/`dividends.js` with nothing going the other way, the same one-directional shape `pension-relief.js` has over `tax.js`. The three deferral notes #34 wrote into `dividends.js` ("issue #35's job") now name the module instead of the issue number.
- **The £500 allowance is modelled as a nil-rate band, not an exemption — it uses up basic/higher rate band space.** This is the single detail most likely to be got wrong: on a £50,000 salary the taxable income is £37,430, only £270 short of the £37,700 basic rate limit, so the £500 allowance straddles the boundary and *every* taxed pound of a £10,000 dividend lands at 35.75%, not just the pounds above £37,700. Implemented by walking the band ladder over the interval `[taxableOther + allowanceUsed, taxableOther + chargeableDividend)` rather than by subtracting £500 and taxing the remainder, and pinned by tests at both boundaries (`£45,000` splits 4,770/4,730 across ordinary/upper; `£120,000` splits 4,640/4,860 across upper/additional).
- **Dividend rates are UK-wide, so `profile.tax_region` deliberately has no effect here — and the £37,700/£125,140 limits are restated as this module's own constants rather than imported from `tax.js`'s ladder.** Scotland sets rates and bands for non-savings, non-dividend income only; a Scottish taxpayer's dividends meet the same three rates against the same UK limits. Deriving the basic rate limit from `bandsFor(region)` would have been wrong for exactly the region that most needs it right — `SCOTLAND_BANDS` has no £37,700 boundary at all. Written up as convention (2) in the module header, tested, and stated in the card's own opening paragraph, since "why doesn't the region selector change this" is the obvious question a Scottish user will have.
- **The additional rate (39.35%) is carried even though README.md names only the basic and higher rates.** README.md's "10.75% basic, 35.75% higher" is the pair that moved in April 2026; the additional rate still exists and was left alone. Matching the spec literally would have taxed a £150,000 income at the upper rate, so the constant is there with a comment explaining why it is not in the spec text — the same judgement `pension-relief.js` made about README.md's "20% basic, 40% higher".
- **The personal allowance is allocated to other income first, then dividends, and the taper is assessed on total income including dividends.** Both are conventions (4) and (5) in the header. The first is the allocation that is never worse for the taxpayer and is what every UK calculator shows — it is what makes £13,070 (£12,570 + £500) of dividends tax-free for someone with no other income. The second matters because dividends are part of adjusted net income: a £95,000 salary plus £20,000 of dividends tapers the allowance to £5,070 even though the salary alone would have kept all of it.
- **Per-holding tax is an apportionment, and the card says so.** Dividend tax is charged on total dividend income against one set of bands, so no individual holding has a rate of its own — a second GIA holding can push the first one's income into the upper rate. Each taxable holding carries its pro-rata share of the total by gross income; sheltered holdings carry zero rather than a share of a bill they did not cause.
- **`DividendTaxSummary.svelte` sits between the tracker and the planner, and owns one input: the taxpayer's other taxable income.** Everything else it needs — each holding's wrapper — `DividendTracker` already collects. The other-income field is seeded from `profile.gross_salary` (the same read-only seeding `PensionTaxRelief` and `DividendIncomePlanner` do) but is editable and not written back, because the income a dividend portfolio is taxed against is often not today's salary: someone asking about the income phase is asking about a year in which the salary has stopped. The card shows the sheltered/taxable split, allowance used and headroom, a rate-by-rate ladder (personal allowance and dividend allowance as 0% rows above the three statutory rates, the shape `TaxCalculator` already uses), a holding-by-holding table, and what the ISA/SIPP shelter is worth this year against an all-GIA counterfactual.
- **Driven for real in a browser**, continuing the pattern from #22–#34/#90. `npm run build` + `vite preview` against the real `/dividends` route, driven with headless system Chromium via `playwright-core` installed in a scratch directory outside the repo (`package.json`/lockfile untouched, removed afterwards). Seeded a Scottish profile on £60,000 with three holdings — a £50,000 Stocks & Shares ISA at 4%, a £60,000 GIA at 4% and £20,000 unwrapped at 3%. Confirmed: £2,000/yr sheltered against £3,000/yr taxable, £500 allowance fully used, £893.75 all at the upper rate (correctly the *UK* upper rate despite the Scottish region), apportioned £715.00/£178.75 across the two taxable holdings, and "saves £715 of tax this year". Retyping the other income to £30,000 moved the whole £2,500 to the ordinary rate (£268.75, £215.00/£53.75 apportioned) and to £0 put £3,000 under the personal allowance row with "£10,070.00 more in dividends before any dividend tax is due" and the shelter correctly reported as worth nothing yet. No console or page errors.
- 59 new tests (46 in `dividend-tax.test.js`, 13 in `DividendTaxSummary.test.js`), 1112 total, up from 1053. `npm run lint`, `npm run check` (0 errors, same 2 pre-existing `<slot>` warnings) and `npm run build` all clean.

**Trade-offs / deviations from prompt:**
- **The issue's fourth clause — "DRIP compounding projection vs income-taken chart" — was already built by #34, and is extended here rather than rebuilt.** #34's own journal entry records it delivering exactly that as sampled tables in `DividendIncomePlanner`, with tax explicitly deferred to this issue; re-implementing it would have duplicated a working panel. What changed is the disclaimer, which no longer says the allowance and rates "land in a later build (#35)" and instead points at the card above it and says why the projection itself stays gross.
- **The projection stays gross — tax is applied to today's portfolio only.** Taxing the building/income phases would mean asserting that a £500 allowance and these three rates hold for the thirty-plus years the projection covers, which is a much stronger claim than the tab makes anywhere else (the rates changed in April 2026, and the allowance has been cut from £5,000 to £500 since 2018). It would also need an "other income at age 67" assumption the app does not collect. Stated in both the card's disclaimer and the planner's; if a future issue wants a net projection, `dividendPortfolioTax` takes the figures it would need.
- **Reinvested dividends are taxed exactly like income-taken ones, which is correct — but the DRIP projection does not deduct that tax from what it reinvests.** A DRIP holding in a GIA is taxed on its dividend whether or not the cash is spent, so a real-world unsheltered DRIP compounds slightly slower than `dividends.js` shows. Netting it off inside the projection is the same forward-assumption problem as above, so it is not done; the point is worth an issue of its own if the unsheltered-DRIP case ever matters.
- **The knock-on cost of the personal allowance taper on *other* income is not included here.** Above £100,000 an extra pound of dividend also destroys 50p of allowance, which raises the tax on the salary underneath — that number is `tax.js`'s `takeHomeBreakdown`, and computing a second version of it here would put two different totals for the same salary on two different tabs. The card says so where the taper bites, and the module header lists it under "what this deliberately does not model". `marginalRate` is therefore the statutory dividend rate only.
- **No interaction test beyond initial render** — the same wall as every other card in this app: `svelte/server`'s `render` covers first paint only. The one input (other taxable income) is seeded from the profile, so every branch of the card is reachable from a server render by varying `profile.gross_salary`; retyping it was confirmed by driving the real page in a browser, as above.
- **The dividend allowance is not cross-checked against the ISA allowance tracker.** The card suggests moving GIA holdings into an ISA "subject to the £20,000 annual ISA allowance" as plain copy, without knowing what the user has already subscribed this year — the ISA allowance tracker is its own README.md feature and its own issue, and reading its state from here would couple two features that do not otherwise know about each other.
- `npm test` still isn't in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (flagged since #2; the app token lacks `workflows` permission), so all 1112 tests run locally but not on the PR. `build`/`check`/`lint` remain the only CI gates.

## Property tracker: types + core fields — 2026-08-07
<!-- METRICS:property-tracker-types-core-fields -->
- **Execution Duration:** __DURATION__ seconds
- **Model:** __MODEL__
- **Turns:** __TURNS__
- **Input Tokens:** __INPUT_TOKENS__
- **Output Tokens:** __OUTPUT_TOKENS__
- **Estimated Cost:** $__COST__ (from Claude Code's reported total_cost_usd)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable GPT-5-tier model:** $__GPT_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable Gemini-tier model:** $__GEMINI_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)

**Decisions:**
- **No new data-model work — `Property`, `PROPERTY_TYPES` and `MORTGAGE_TYPES` already existed complete** from #2's scaffold build, including `createProperty`, `normaliseProperty` and every field's `validateAppData` rule. This issue is purely `PropertyTracker.svelte` plus the `/property` route wiring it into the store, the same shape #29/#34 (`PensionTracker`/`DividendTracker`) already established for their own flat lists.
- **`mortgage_type` offers all four `MORTGAGE_TYPES` in the form select, not just the three the issue text names (fixed/tracker/SVR).** `none` was already on the data model for a property owned outright; leaving it out of the select would have made the tracker unable to represent a mortgage-free property without the user lying in the rate/payment fields, so the full enum is exposed the same way `DividendTracker` renders every `WRAPPERS`/`PAYOUT_FREQUENCIES` value regardless of which ones the source issue happened to name.
- **Scoped tightly to "types + core fields" and nothing else in the milestone.** `properties[]`'s typedef also carries `rental_income`, `running_costs`, `growth_rate` and `include_in_net_worth`, but `gh issue list --milestone "M6"` shows those are explicitly #37's ("Property equity + BTL cashflow calculations") and #38's ("Mortgage deal expiry reminder + equity growth projection") own scope, not #36's. `PropertyTracker`'s form only collects name, type, value, mortgage balance, monthly payment, interest rate, mortgage type and deal expiry — the exact seven-field list in the issue body — and does not compute or display equity, cashflow, yield or a deal-expiry warning. `createProperty`'s own defaults (0/0/3/true) cover the untouched fields for now; #37/#38 extend the same component rather than a new one when they land.
- **`PropertyTracker.svelte` follows `DividendTracker.svelte`'s shape exactly**: `properties[]` sits flat on `AppData` (already true since #2), one component owns add/edit/remove against it with `bind:properties`, a summary line sums two raw input fields (value, mortgage balance — deliberately not their difference, since equity is #37's job) rather than deriving anything. No activity log, for the same reason pensions and dividends have none — `ACTIVITY_LOG_ENTITY_TYPES` covers `investment`/`debt` only (#14's own scope) and was never widened.
- **Driven for real in a browser**, continuing the pattern from #22–#35/#90. `npm run build` + `vite preview` against the real `/property` route, driven with headless system Chromium via `playwright-core` installed in a scratch directory outside the repo (`package.json`/lockfile untouched, removed afterwards). Added a £350,000 buy-to-let with a £200,000 tracker mortgage at 4.75%, £1,200/mo payment and a 2027-03-31 deal expiry: the row showed "Buy to let · Tracker", "£350,000 value · £200,000 mortgage at 4.75% · £1,200/mo · deal expires 2027-03-31", and the summary "1 property recorded, totalling £350,000 of value with £200,000 outstanding mortgage". Edit pre-filled every field correctly, a rename round-tripped, and removing it returned to the empty state. No console or page errors.
- 7 new tests (in `PropertyTracker.test.js`), 1119 total, up from 1112. `npm run lint`, `npm run check` (0 errors, same 2 pre-existing `<slot>` warnings) and `npm run build` all clean.

**Trade-offs / deviations from prompt:**
- **All form fields are always shown regardless of `mortgage_type`.** A property with `mortgage_type: 'none'` still has visible (if zero) interest rate and monthly payment inputs, rather than the form hiding them conditionally. No existing tracker in this codebase conditionally shows/hides fields based on another field's value (`PensionTracker` comes closest, deriving a select from a number rather than hiding inputs), so adding that behaviour here would have been a new pattern for a cosmetic gain; the row display already omits the "/mo" note when `monthly_payment` is zero.
- **No equity, BTL cashflow, gross yield or deal-expiry amber/red warning anywhere in this component**, even though `deal_expiry` is collected and displayed as a plain date. README.md's fuller "Property Tracker" section asks for all of these, but they are `gh issue list`-confirmed as #37/#38's own scope; adding them here would duplicate work those issues are specifically tracked to do, the same restraint #34's journal entry showed leaving dividend tax to #35.
- **No interaction test beyond initial render** — the same wall as every other tracker in this app: `svelte/server`'s `render` covers first paint only. Add/edit/remove is left to manual verification (mirroring `DividendTracker`/`PensionTracker`, neither of which tests that part either), confirmed working by driving the real page in a browser, as above.
- `npm test` still isn't in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (flagged since #2; the app token lacks `workflows` permission), so all 1119 tests run locally but not on the PR. `build`/`check`/`lint` remain the only CI gates.

## Property equity + BTL cashflow calculations — 2026-08-07
<!-- METRICS:property-equity-btl-cashflow-calculations -->
- **Execution Duration:** __DURATION__ seconds
- **Model:** __MODEL__
- **Turns:** __TURNS__
- **Input Tokens:** __INPUT_TOKENS__
- **Output Tokens:** __OUTPUT_TOKENS__
- **Estimated Cost:** $__COST__ (from Claude Code's reported total_cost_usd)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable GPT-5-tier model:** $__GPT_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable Gemini-tier model:** $__GEMINI_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)

**Decisions:**
- **New module `$lib/property.js`, not an extension of `debt.js` or `model.js`.** #36 already added every field this issue needs (`rental_income`, `running_costs`, `include_in_net_worth`) to the data model specifically so this build would not need one of its own — its journal entry names #37 as the reason those three fields exist unused. `property.js` follows `dividends.js`'s shape: pure functions in, plain numbers/objects out, `PropertyTracker.svelte` reads it but never computes any of this itself.
- **Net monthly cashflow includes the mortgage payment — `rental_income - running_costs - monthly_payment`, not the two-term sum `Property.running_costs`'s own doc comment seemed to imply ("the other half of net cashflow").** A two-term cashflow would ignore the single largest monthly outgoing a mortgaged buy-to-let has, which defeats the point of the number: a landlord asking "does this property pay for itself" needs the mortgage payment in the answer. `monthly_payment` already existed on the record for the equity calculation, so folding it into cashflow too needed no data-model change — just the read. Corrected the stale doc comment on `Property.running_costs` in `types.js` to state the three-term formula explicitly, so the next reader isn't misled the way this session briefly was.
- **Gross yield stays gross** — annualised `rental_income` against `value`, nothing subtracted. That is what "gross" means in the README.md term, and it is the ratio BTL investors actually quote when comparing properties of different sizes; subtracting costs into it would leave it neither a standard gross yield nor a net one with a name of its own.
- **Cashflow/yield are computed for any property with rent recorded, not gated by `type`.** README.md frames the calculation as "BTL:", and `rental_income`'s own doc comment says "Buy-to-let and holiday lets", but a primary residence with a lodger is a real case this module has no principled reason to refuse to add up — the UI (see below) still only shows the line when there is rent or running costs to show.
- **`include_in_net_worth` is a row-level instant toggle, not an add/edit form field** — the same pattern `DebtTracker.svelte` already uses for a debt's `exclude_from_net_worth`, rather than routing a boolean through the full form and an extra "Save changes" click for what is otherwise a one-click decision. `rental_income`/`running_costs`, by contrast, went into the form as two new always-visible fields, matching #36's own precedent of never conditionally showing/hiding fields by another field's value.
- **`propertyPortfolioSummary` splits the card's totals by the toggle**: `totalEquity` (every property) alongside `includedInNetWorth`/`excludedFromNetWorth` slices, so the summary line can say both "£320,000 equity" and, only when something is actually excluded, "£120,000 of that counts towards net worth (1 property excluded)" — mirroring `dividendPortfolioSummary`'s sheltered/unsheltered split in shape, not in what it splits on.
- **Driven for real in a browser**, continuing the pattern from #22–#36/#90. `npm run build` + `vite preview` against the real `/property` route, driven with headless system Chromium via `playwright-core` installed in a scratch directory outside the repo (`package.json`/lockfile untouched, removed afterwards). Added a £350,000 buy-to-let with a £200,000 tracker mortgage at 4.75%, £1,200/mo payment, £1,500/mo rent and £200/mo running costs: the row showed "£150,000 equity" and "£1,500/mo rent · £100/mo net cashflow · 5.14% gross yield". Unchecking "Include in net worth" added "(excluded from net worth)" to the row and updated a second two-property scenario's card summary from "£320,000 equity" to "£320,000 equity. £120,000 of that counts towards net worth (1 property excluded)"; re-checking it removed the note. Editing the property pre-filled the new rental income/running costs fields correctly. No console or page errors.
- 23 new tests (16 in `property.test.js`, 7 in `PropertyTracker.test.js`), 1142 total, up from 1119. `npm run lint`, `npm run check` (0 errors, same 2 pre-existing `<slot>` warnings) and `npm run build` all clean.

**Trade-offs / deviations from prompt:**
- **Property equity does not feed into the main net worth chart or total.** `net-worth.js` (issues #67–#87) sums only `monthly_entries[].investments`/`.debts`; no issue in the tracked backlog wires property or asset equity into that series, and inventing that integration here would be scope creep beyond "equity + BTL cashflow calculations". The toggle this issue asks for governs the property tab's own card total (`propertyPortfolioSummary`) today; a future cross-cutting issue would be the place to fold it into the dashboard-wide figure.
- **No deal expiry amber/red reminder or equity growth projection chart** — both explicitly #38's scope, and `growth_rate` remains on the record unused by anything this component does.
- **No activity log**, for the same reason pensions, dividends and #36's own property rows have none — `ACTIVITY_LOG_ENTITY_TYPES` covers `investment`/`debt` only and was never widened.
- **No interaction test beyond initial render**, plus one render-diff test asserting the `include_in_net_worth` checkbox's `checked` attribute — the same wall as every other tracker in this app: `svelte/server`'s `render` covers first paint only. Add/edit/remove and the toggle's actual click handler are left to manual verification, confirmed working by driving the real page in a browser, as above.
- `npm test` still isn't in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (flagged since #2; the app token lacks `workflows` permission), so all 1142 tests run locally but not on the PR. `build`/`check`/`lint` remain the only CI gates.

## Mortgage deal expiry reminder + equity growth projection — 2026-08-07
<!-- METRICS:mortgage-deal-expiry-equity-growth-projection -->
- **Execution Duration:** __DURATION__ seconds
- **Model:** __MODEL__
- **Turns:** __TURNS__
- **Input Tokens:** __INPUT_TOKENS__
- **Output Tokens:** __OUTPUT_TOKENS__
- **Estimated Cost:** $__COST__ (from Claude Code's reported total_cost_usd)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable GPT-5-tier model:** $__GPT_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)
