- **Ballpark cost, comparable Gemini-tier model:** $__GEMINI_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)

**Decisions:**
- **`$lib/property.js` grows two more exports rather than a new module** — `dealExpiryStatus` and `propertyEquityProjection` are both readings of a single `Property`, the same shape `propertyEquity`/`propertyCashflow`/`propertyGrossYield` already are, and #37's own journal entry left `growth_rate` on the record specifically for this issue's projection. A `property-projection.js` split would have separated two functions that both exist to answer "what does this property's mortgage do over time" from the three that already do the "what does it do today" half of the same question.
- **`dealExpiryStatus` takes `now` as an explicit argument rather than reading the clock itself**, the one function in this module that is not a pure snapshot of its input otherwise. `PropertyTracker.svelte` mirrors this with its own `now` prop (defaulting to `new Date()`), so a server-rendered test can assert the exact amber/red boundary — "expires in 90 days" vs. "in 91 days" — without the answer silently changing depending on what day CI happens to run. Both dates compare at UTC midnight, `net-worth.js`'s own convention for month boundaries, so "expires today" reads the same regardless of the reader's timezone.
- **The mortgage amortises in the projection — it does not just carry forward unchanged.** `forecast.js` documents carrying a bare `Debt` forward unchanged as deliberate ("nothing in the data model says how a balance amortises… interest rate and monthly payment live on `Property`, not `Debt`") — but a `Property` *is* the record with those two fields, which is exactly the information a standard reducing-balance schedule needs. `propertyEquityProjection` runs one monthly step per point (interest accrues at `interest_rate / 12`, `monthly_payment` is deducted, floored at zero), which is also why it walks month-by-month internally even though it only returns one point per year — annual-only steps would double-count a year's interest against a single payment figure. Where `monthly_payment` is `0` (no data, or `mortgage_type: 'none'`), the balance is carried forward unchanged instead, falling back to `forecast.js`'s own convention rather than inventing a schedule from nothing.
- **A payment too small to cover its own interest is not special-cased — the balance grows.** A real interest-only mortgage whose payment has been set below the interest due behaves exactly this way (negative amortisation), and hiding it behind a floor would make the projection lie about a property that is quietly getting more indebted, not less.
- **Value growth reuses `auto-invest.js`'s geometric-monthly convention** (`(1 + growth_rate/100)^(1/12) - 1`, compounded twelve times, rather than `growth_rate / 12`) even though the projection only samples once a year — kept monthly so the same per-month walk drives both the value line and the mortgage amortisation, rather than growing value annually and amortising monthly on two different clocks that would drift against each other over 30 years.
- **One chart, not one per property.** `PropertyEquityChart.svelte` takes a single `property` prop; `PropertyTracker.svelte` adds a `<select>` beneath the list (defaulting to the first property, kept in sync via `$effect` if the selected one is edited or removed) rather than rendering a chart per row. The same reasoning `NetWorthChart` gives for one dashboard chart instead of one per holding applies more sharply here: a portfolio of even five or six properties would put five or six 30-year charts on screen for the one anybody is actually looking at at a time.
- **`PropertyEquityChart.svelte` needs only one `<Chart>`, unlike `NetWorthChart`'s two-chart split.** That split exists because the forecast band is a *second* data array with its own shape (`low`/`mid`/`high`), which breaks `bisect-x`/`<Highlight>` if it shares a chart with the tracked line. Here `propertyEquityProjection` returns one array with three accessors (`value`/`mortgageBalance`/`equity`) already on every point, so `bisect-x`'s `flatData` is exactly the series being drawn — no second array to trip over.
- **The mortgage balance line is coloured with `--destructive`, not a third chart hue.** `app.css` only defines `--chart-1`/`--chart-2` (`NetWorthChart`'s own two series); a shrinking debt is the one line here that reads correctly as "the bad one going down is good news" in a warning colour, so it borrows the token the app already uses for warnings rather than adding a `--chart-3` token for one component.
- **Added a `growth_rate` field to the add/edit form.** `createProperty` has defaulted it to 3% since #36, but nothing before this issue let the user change it, and a projection whose one real assumption cannot be edited is a chart nobody can make say anything but the same default 3%. Placed at the end of the form, after deal expiry, since it is new to this issue rather than part of #36's original seven-field list.
- **The deal-expiry reminder's colour is given inline (`style="color: ..."`) as well as by a `data-deal-status` attribute**, the same double-up `NetWorthChart`'s legend swatches use and for the same reason: `app.css` still ships Tailwind v3's `@tailwind` directives under Tailwind v4, so utility classes are inert app-wide (flagged since #67), and a warning that silently renders in the body text colour would defeat the one thing this feature exists to do. Confirmed in a real browser below that the amber (`#b45309`) and red (`hsl(var(--destructive))`) both compute to their intended `rgb()` values rather than falling back to inherited grey.
- **Driven for real in a browser**, continuing the pattern from #22–#37/#96. `npm run build` + `vite preview` against the real `/property` route, driven with headless system Chromium via `playwright-core` installed in a scratch directory outside the repo (`package.json`/lockfile untouched, removed afterwards). Added a £350,000 buy-to-let with a £200,000 tracker mortgage at 4.75%, £1,200/mo payment and a deal expiring 30 days out: the row showed "⚠ mortgage deal expires in 30 days" computing to `rgb(180, 83, 9)`. A second property with a 2020-01-01 deal expiry showed "⚠ mortgage deal expired 2410 days ago" computing to `rgb(239, 68, 68)`. The chart select listed both properties by name, switching it re-titled the chart card and re-drew the lines; the equity/value/mortgage-balance lines, legend, axis and "Show as a table" fallback all rendered (14 `<svg>` elements on the page), and the mortgage line visibly flattened to zero around year 22–23 in the screenshot, matching the amortisation maths. Editing a property round-tripped both the new growth-rate field and the existing deal-expiry date correctly. No console or page errors.
- 26 new tests (18 in `property.test.js`, 8 in `PropertyTracker.test.js`), 1168 total, up from 1142. `npm run lint`, `npm run check` (0 errors, same 2 pre-existing `<slot>` warnings) and `npm run build` all clean.

**Trade-offs / deviations from prompt:**
- **No activity log entries for a deal-expiry reminder firing or a projection being viewed** — neither is a data mutation, and `ACTIVITY_LOG_ENTITY_TYPES` covers `investment`/`debt` only regardless; consistent with every other property-tab decision on this so far.
- **The projection assumes today's interest rate and monthly payment hold for the entire horizon** — no re-fix at the next deal expiry, no rate-rise scenario. README.md asks for "equity growth projection", not a rate-scenario tool, and `forecast.js`'s own three-scenario machinery is scoped to investments/debts inside `monthly_entries`, not properties; folding property mortgages into that scenario system is a materially bigger change than this issue's own scope. The chart's disclaimer says so directly ("assumes today's rate and payment continue unchanged for the whole horizon").
- **The projection is not wired into the main net worth forecast** (`forecast.js`/`NetWorthChart`), for the same reason #37's own equity toggle isn't: no tracked issue asks for property/asset projections to feed the dashboard-wide forecast, and inventing that integration here would be scope creep past "equity growth projection chart (30-year)" — which the issue text scopes to the property tab specifically.
- **No `PropertyEquityChart` unit test beyond what `PropertyTracker.test.js` exercises through its parent** — the same wall as `NetWorthChart.svelte`, which also has no dedicated test file: `svelte/server`'s `render` is what `PropertyTracker.test.js` already uses, and asserting the chart's own heading/select-driven swap through the parent covers the same first-paint ground a standalone file would, without a second render harness for one child component. The module-block `propertyProjectionTooltipReading` function (mirroring `netWorthTooltipReading`'s own testable-without-a-pointer shape) is exported but, like its `NetWorthChart` counterpart, not directly unit-tested here — left with the same manual-verification note as the SVG lines it labels.
- **No interaction test beyond initial render for the new form field, reminder or chart selector** — the same wall as every other tracker in this app: `svelte/server`'s `render` covers first paint only. The `<select>`'s actual change handler, and the add/edit form's new growth-rate field, are left to manual verification, confirmed working by driving the real page in a browser, as above.
- `npm test` still isn't in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (flagged since #2; the app token lacks `workflows` permission), so all 1168 tests run locally but not on the PR. `build`/`check`/`lint` remain the only CI gates.

## Browser-only storage backend (IndexedDB + localStorage fallback) as the default persistence mode — 2026-08-07
<!-- METRICS:browser-only-storage-backend-default-persistence-mode -->
- **Execution Duration:** __DURATION__ seconds
- **Model:** __MODEL__
- **Turns:** __TURNS__
- **Input Tokens:** __INPUT_TOKENS__
- **Output Tokens:** __OUTPUT_TOKENS__
- **Estimated Cost:** $__COST__ (from Claude Code's reported total_cost_usd)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable GPT-5-tier model:** $__GPT_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable Gemini-tier model:** $__GEMINI_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)

**Decisions:**
- **Three modules, not two: `browser-storage.js` (backend), `gist.js` (backend), `persistence.js` (the one API over both).** The issue asks for exactly this split and it earns itself immediately — each backend now knows nothing about modes or about the other, and `store.js` swapped one import for another without a single change to how it loads, saves or debounces. The `index.js` re-export needed narrowing to match: all three modules deliberately expose the same `loadAppData`/`saveAppData` names (that sameness *is* the "same load/save contract" the issue asks for), and `export *` silently drops names two starred modules both provide, so `persistence.js` is starred as the front door while the two backends are re-exported by name only.
- **The document is stored as a JSON string in IndexedDB, not as a structured-clone object.** IndexedDB would happily hold the object graph directly, but then the browser copy and the Gist copy would be different artefacts of the same document, and anything ever added to `AppData` that isn't structured-cloneable would fail at save time rather than at review time. A string keeps both modes byte-identical, which is what makes the JSON export/import bridge (#100) a straight copy in either direction.
- **`localStorage` reuses `gist.js`'s old `LOCAL_DATA_KEY` (`uk-wealth-tracker:data`) rather than a new key**, and a load that finds IndexedDB empty falls back to reading that key. Any browser already carrying data from a pre-#61 build therefore keeps it: the first load adopts the old copy and the next save promotes it into IndexedDB. A fresh key would have silently presented existing users with an empty app, with their real data still sitting in the origin one key over.
- **`BrowserStorageError` is reserved for "your data was not saved", never for "nothing stored yet" or "one backend was unavailable."** IndexedDB missing, refusing to open, throwing on `open()` (Firefox private windows), being blocked by another tab, or erroring mid-read all fall through to `localStorage` silently — those are environment facts, not failures. The error is thrown only when nothing accepted the write (quota exceeded, no storage at all) or a read failed with no fallback left. `store.js`'s `describeError` now passes both backends' own messages through verbatim and keeps its "Could not save/load: …" framing for anything else, so the sync banner says what actually went wrong.
- **`open()` resolving to `null` rather than rejecting** — every unavailability path funnels into one "is there a database?" answer that callers treat as "use `localStorage`", instead of five different rejection shapes each needing its own catch. `onblocked` resolves `null` too: a request blocked by another tab holding an older version open may never settle at all, and hanging every save on it would be worse than falling back.
- **Writes resolve on `transaction.oncomplete`, not `request.onsuccess`.** The request succeeding only means the put was accepted into the transaction; a caller that `await`s a save and then closes the tab would otherwise be racing a transaction that hasn't committed. This matters more here than usual because the store's whole write path is an 800ms debounce followed by exactly one save.
- **Gist mode stays the starting mode on a build that carries a token**, per the issue and README.md — configuring `VITE_GITHUB_TOKEN` is itself the opt-in, and a build with no token has browser-only as its *only* mode. The remembered choice (`uk-wealth-tracker:persistence-mode` in `localStorage`) overrides that default whenever the build can honour it, and a remembered `gist` on a build whose token has since gone quietly resolves back to `browser` rather than failing every save against a mode that no longer exists. `setPersistenceMode` throws `RangeError` for an unavailable mode rather than silently accepting it, since #100's UI should only ever offer `availablePersistenceModes()`.
- **`gist.js` now throws instead of falling back when called with no token.** Its `localStorage` half moved out wholesale, so "no token" is no longer a mode it has — it is a caller that bypassed `persistence.js`'s mode check. Silently doing nothing with the user's data there would be the worst of the three options.
- **Hand-rolled in-memory IndexedDB and `localStorage` stubs in the test file rather than adding `fake-indexeddb`.** `gist.test.js` already hand-rolls its `Storage` stand-in for the same reason (the `node` test environment has neither global), the module touches exactly one database, one store and one record, and the stub's fault injection — `open-throws`, `open-errors`, `blocked`, `transaction-throws`, `read-fails`, `write-fails` — is what makes each fallback path individually testable, which a real implementation would have made harder, not easier. Callbacks fire on a microtask, as the real API's do, so the promise wrappers are genuinely exercised.
- **Driven for real in a browser**, continuing the pattern from #22-#38/#98, since node tests can only ever exercise a stub of the API this issue is about. `npm run build` + `vite preview`, headless system Chromium via `playwright-core` in a scratch directory outside the repo (`package.json`/lockfile untouched, removed afterwards). Verified against the real dashboard: the header read "Saved to this browser only"; a fresh profile had no IndexedDB record and no error; adding a month wrote a 1.7KB JSON string to `uk-wealth-tracker` → `app-data` → `document` with `schema_version: 1` and left `localStorage` completely empty; the entry survived a reload. In a second context with `window.indexedDB` redefined to throw `SecurityError` (what a locked-down browser looks like), the same edit landed in `localStorage` under `uk-wealth-tracker:data` and also survived a reload. In a third, a document planted under the old `uk-wealth-tracker:data` key was adopted on load and promoted into IndexedDB alongside the next edit (`[2029, 2033]`). No console or page errors in any of the three.
- 42 tests added net (24 in `browser-storage.test.js`, 18 in `persistence.test.js`, 2 more in `store.test.js`, against the four `gist.test.js` local-fallback cases that this change retires), 1210 total, up from 1168. `npm run lint`, `npm run check` (0 errors, same 2 pre-existing `<slot>` warnings) and `npm run build` all clean.

**Trade-offs / deviations from prompt:**
- **CLAUDE.md was edited as well as README.md and `.env.example`.** The issue names only the latter two, but CLAUDE.md's architecture section described `lib/gist.js` as "the only persistence layer" and told future sessions to read and write through it — after this change that instruction points at the wrong module, and CLAUDE.md is loaded into every session in this repo. Left as a two-line correction (the file tree and the "key architectural point" sentence); nothing else in it was touched.
- **Switching mode does not move any data between backends.** `setPersistenceMode` remembers the choice and that is all — a user who switches from Gist to browser-only sees whatever that browser has stored, not their Gist's contents. Moving a document across is exactly what JSON export/import is for, and the issue puts `data-transfer.js` in #100; building an implicit copy-on-switch here would both duplicate that work and make the switch a destructive action in a session that has no UI to confirm it.
- **No "delete all browser data" function**, even though it is a three-line `IDBObjectStore.clear()` next to everything else here — #63 owns that action, and the issue's out-of-scope list names it explicitly.
- **`browserStorageBackend()` is asynchronous**, unlike `getPersistenceMode()`, so a template cannot show "IndexedDB" vs "localStorage" without awaiting. There is no synchronous honest answer: whether IndexedDB is usable is only knowable by trying to open it, and a synchronous `typeof indexedDB !== 'undefined'` would claim IndexedDB in exactly the private-browsing case where the open then throws. #100's UI can await it once on mount.
- **One record per origin, no multi-tab coordination.** Two tabs open on the app still last-write-wins, the same caveat `store.js` already documents for Gist mode — IndexedDB could support a `versionchange`/`storage`-event story, but nothing in Milestone 7 asks for it and this app is single-user by design.
- **`migrateAppData` and `normaliseAppData` are the same function today** (`model.js` has one schema version so far, and says so). The new backend calls `migrateAppData` where `gist.js` calls `normaliseAppData` — the seam the model module deliberately left for a version 2 — so the two paths will diverge correctly when there is a version 2, rather than both needing to be found and changed then.
- `npm test` still isn't in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (flagged since #2; the app token lacks `workflows` permission), so all 1210 tests run locally but not on the PR. `build`/`check`/`lint` remain the only CI gates.

## GitHub sign-in for Gist persistence mode — 2026-08-07
<!-- METRICS:github-sign-in-for-gist-persistence-mode -->
- **Execution Duration:** __DURATION__ seconds
- **Model:** __MODEL__
- **Turns:** __TURNS__
- **Input Tokens:** __INPUT_TOKENS__
- **Output Tokens:** __OUTPUT_TOKENS__
- **Estimated Cost:** $__COST__ (from Claude Code's reported total_cost_usd)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable GPT-5-tier model:** $__GPT_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable Gemini-tier model:** $__GEMINI_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)

**Decisions:**
- **A pasted personal access token, not the OAuth device flow — because a browser cannot complete the device flow at all.** GitHub's `https://github.com/login/device/code` and `/login/oauth/access_token` endpoints send no `Access-Control-Allow-Origin` header, so the code-for-token exchange has to happen on a server. This app is a static GitHub Pages build with no backend by design (DESIGN.md → Hosting), and a device flow would also need a registered OAuth app's client id configured *somewhere* — the same build-time configuration step this issue exists to delete. The issue anticipates exactly this and allows "at minimum a PAT-entry screen". Recorded in DESIGN.md as its own decision rather than only in this entry, since "why isn't there a Sign in with GitHub button" is the first question anyone will ask of this code.
- **Two modules, one direction: `github-auth.js` owns the credential and the identity, `gist.js` owns which Gist.** `github-auth.js` knows nothing about Gists; `gist.js` imports it and never the reverse. That leaves the two operations that genuinely span both halves — `connectGitHubAccount` and `disconnectGitHubAccount` — living in `gist.js`, which is the module that can see both a token and a Gist id without a cycle. The alternative (a `signOut` in `github-auth.js` that also cleared the Gist pointer) would have been a two-module import cycle for one function.
- **The build's `VITE_GITHUB_TOKEN` is demoted to a fallback rather than removed.** `getGitHubToken()` is "the signed-in token, else the build's", so every existing deployment keeps working untouched, while any browser that signs in overrides it locally. `gist.js` no longer reads the env var at all — it asks `github-auth.js` — which is what makes `isGistConfigured()` (and therefore Gist mode's very existence in `persistence.js`) become true the moment someone signs in, with no reload.
- **The Gist id precedence is flipped: a Gist chosen in the app now beats `VITE_GIST_ID`.** This mirrors `persistence.js`'s existing rule that a remembered choice beats the build's default — what the user picked in the app wins over what was compiled in — and without it, pasting a Gist id on a build that carries `VITE_GIST_ID` would have been a silent no-op. Safe for existing browsers: the id cache was only ever written by the create-if-missing path, which only ran when `VITE_GIST_ID` was *unset*, so no browser can be carrying both from an earlier build.
- **The Gist id now carries the login it belongs to (`uk-wealth-tracker:gist-owner`), and sign-in compares against *that*, not against the previously signed-in account.** The first cut compared the new account to `getStoredAccount()`, which passed its unit test and then failed in the browser: the realistic way two accounts share a device is sign out, *then* sign in as someone else — and signing out has already cleared the account record, so there was nothing left to compare against and the second account inherited the first's Gist pointer. GitHub answers 404 for every read and write of a Gist you don't own, which reads to a user as "my data has vanished" rather than "that Gist isn't yours". An id with *no* recorded owner (cached by a pre-#62 build, or created while running on a build token whose owner this app never asked about) is deliberately left alone — unknown is not the same as different, and clearing it on a guess would orphan real data.
- **Signing out drops the token but keeps the Gist pointer.** Deliberately asymmetrical with signing in: the token is the secret worth forgetting, the id is a bookmark, and forgetting it would mean signing back in as the same person created a second, empty Gist and silently orphaned the first. Nothing is deleted either way — deleting stored data is #63's job, and the panel says so in as many words.
- **An absent `X-OAuth-Scopes` header means "not stated", never "no access".** GitHub sends that header for classic tokens (so a token without `gist` is refused at sign-in, before anything is stored) but not for fine-grained ones, which carry per-resource permissions instead. Treating absence as failure would have refused every fine-grained token; treating it as success-with-a-caveat is honest, and the panel says so on screen. Verified in a real browser that the header is only readable because GitHub also sends `Access-Control-Expose-Headers` — a cross-origin response header is invisible to JS otherwise, which is why the "unknown scopes" branch is a real path and not defensive padding.
- **`redactToken` scrubs the token out of every error this module raises, and the panel never renders it back.** GitHub's own error bodies don't echo credentials, but a `fetch` `TypeError` can quote whatever it was handed, and "never logged" is only true if it is true of the unhappy paths. The entry field is `type="password"` with `autocomplete="off"` and is cleared the instant sign-in succeeds, so after that the component holds no secret at all — confirmed by asserting the token string appears nowhere in the rendered page after a full sign-in/sign-out cycle in a browser.
- **Signing in, signing out and re-pointing at a different Gist each re-hydrate the store.** All three change *which document is the live one*; without the re-hydrate, the in-memory document would still be the previous backend's and the next keystroke anywhere in the app would debounce-save it into the new one. Signing in also calls `setPersistenceMode('gist')` outright, since signing in *is* the opt-in per README.md — and signing out needs no matching call, because a remembered `gist` with no token resolves back to `browser` on its own (#61's fallback, now covered by a test that exercises exactly that sequence).
- **The connection is a Svelte store exported from `github-auth.js`, not from `store.js`.** It lives beside the functions that mutate it, so `signIn`/`signOut` can't drift out of sync with what the header is showing; putting it in `store.js` would have meant every mutation needing a paired update in a module that otherwise only ever holds the `AppData` document. It starts signed-out on both server and client (storage is unreadable while prerendering) and is refreshed from `onMount` — the same shape `persistence.js` already uses to stay SSR-safe.
- **`verifyGitHubToken` has its own small `fetch` rather than reusing `gist.js`'s `githubRequest`.** It is the only request made with a token the app hasn't accepted yet, and the only one that reads response *headers* — which `githubRequest` doesn't expose — and importing it would have been the cycle described above.
- **`/connect` is a route, but not one of `NAV_TABS`.** Those are README.md's feature tabs; this is a setup screen most people visit once. It is reached from a connection indicator in the nav shell's header instead, which is also what satisfies the issue's "the UI must make clear which GitHub account/Gist is currently connected" from *every* tab rather than only from the one page. #100's Settings tab is the obvious thing to absorb or link to it later.
- **Driven for real in a browser**, continuing the pattern from #22–#38/#98/#61, with `api.github.com` intercepted (no real token, and the point is the app's wiring rather than GitHub's behaviour). `npm run build` + `vite preview`, headless system Chromium via `playwright-core` in a scratch directory outside the repo (`package.json`/lockfile untouched, removed afterwards). Verified: the header read "Connect GitHub" and the panel "Not signed in" with empty storage; pasting a token and connecting sent exactly one `GET /user` with `Authorization: Bearer …`, stored the token and account, set the mode to `gist`, changed the header to "@octocat" and the dashboard's line to "Synced to your GitHub Gist"; a token whose scopes were `repo` was refused with the scope message and stored nothing; pasting `https://gist.github.com/octocat/aa11bb22cc33` into "Which Gist" extracted the id, re-hydrated from it and rendered a link to it; adding a month on the dashboard produced a real `POST /gists` with `"public": false`; signing out returned the header, the panel and the storage mode to signed-out while keeping the Gist id; and signing in as a second account after that sign-out cleared the first account's Gist pointer (the bug above, re-verified after the fix). No console messages and no page errors in any run.
- 68 tests added (33 in the new `github-auth.test.js`, 21 more in `gist.test.js`, 11 in the new `GitHubSignIn.test.js`, 3 more in `persistence.test.js`), 1278 total, up from 1210. `npm run lint`, `npm run check` (0 errors, same 2 pre-existing `<slot>` warnings) and `npm run build` all clean.

**Trade-offs / deviations from prompt:**
- **No "Sign in with GitHub" button, and no OAuth app.** The first decision above is the whole reason; the cost is that the user still has to visit GitHub, create a token and paste it, rather than approving a prompt. The panel links straight to a pre-scoped token page (`?scopes=gist&description=uk-wealth-tracker`) so the scope can't be got wrong, which is most of what the device flow would have bought.
- **The token is in `localStorage`, so an XSS on this app is a token disclosure.** No client-only storage avoids that; `sessionStorage` would only trade it for re-pasting the token every session, on a personal app used on the owner's own devices. The mitigations are the token's narrow `gist` scope (which reaches nothing else in the account) and revocation, both stated on the page rather than left implicit. Both modules' doc comments say this plainly rather than implying the storage is safe.
- **A revoked or expired token is only discovered on the next load or save**, where it surfaces as the existing `GistError` in the sync banner. There is no background re-validation and no refresh — refresh tokens don't exist for PATs, and polling `GET /user` to pre-empt a failure the next save would surface anyway is a network call per session for no new information.
- **A build-configured token's account is only shown if you ask.** The app has never presented that token to GitHub, so nobody knows whose it is without a call; the panel says so and offers a "Check whose token it is" button rather than making an unrequested API request on mount for a state most deployments aren't in.
- **The app cannot clear `VITE_GITHUB_TOKEN` or `VITE_GIST_ID`, only override them per browser** — they are compiled into the bundle. A deployment that wants to stop shipping a token has to rebuild without it, which the README now says.
- **No storage-mode switcher, no JSON export/import, no "delete all my data".** #100 owns the Settings tab and the mode switch; #63 owns the wipe. This page changes the mode only as a side effect of signing in or out, which is the opt-in the issue describes — building a general switcher here would duplicate #100's work.
- **Switching to Gist mode does not copy this browser's data into the Gist** (or the other way). Same position #61 took and for the same reason: moving a document between backends is JSON export/import's job (#100), and a silent copy-on-switch would make signing in a destructive action with no confirmation UI to sit behind.
- **`describeGistTarget()` returns an `owner` the UI doesn't render.** It exists for the mismatch rule above; showing "belongs to @x" beside a Gist that is, by construction, always the signed-in account's would be noise. It is on the return value rather than hidden so a future Settings tab doesn't have to re-derive it.
- **The gist-owner record is a usability safeguard, not a security boundary.** It stops the app pointing an account at a Gist it can't read; it is not what stops anyone reading a Gist, which is (still) only the secrecy of its id — README.md and DESIGN.md both already say so and now say it in the sign-in context too.
- **`GitHubSignIn.test.js` covers the initial render only**, the same wall every component test in this repo hits: `svelte/server`'s `render` has no click handling. The click paths are covered at module level in `github-auth.test.js`/`gist.test.js` and end-to-end by the browser drive above.
- **`normaliseGistId` validates shape, not existence** — `https://gist.github.com/octocat/` normalises to `octocat`, since nothing in a URL distinguishes a login from a short numeric Gist id. A wrong-but-well-formed id surfaces as the existing `GistError` on the next load, which is the same place a deleted Gist surfaces.
- `npm test` still isn't in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (flagged since #2; the app token lacks `workflows` permission), so all 1278 tests run locally but not on the PR. `build`/`check`/`lint` remain the only CI gates.

## Delete all stored data (per-user wipe), Gist mode — 2026-08-07
<!-- METRICS:delete-all-stored-data-per-user-wipe-gist-mode -->
- **Execution Duration:** __DURATION__ seconds
- **Model:** __MODEL__
- **Turns:** __TURNS__
- **Input Tokens:** __INPUT_TOKENS__
- **Output Tokens:** __OUTPUT_TOKENS__
- **Estimated Cost:** $__COST__ (from Claude Code's reported total_cost_usd)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable GPT-5-tier model:** $__GPT_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable Gemini-tier model:** $__GEMINI_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)

**Decisions:**
- **The Gist is deleted outright, not emptied — because a Gist's revision history outlives its content.** The obvious implementation is a `PATCH` setting the data file to `{}` or removing it, and it would have been wrong: every earlier revision of a Gist stays readable to anyone holding its id, and per DESIGN.md the id's secrecy is the *only* thing protecting a secret Gist's contents. An emptied Gist would have told the user their financial history was deleted while leaving all of it one click into GitHub's "Revisions" tab. `DELETE /gists/:id` is the only operation that takes the history with it, so that is what "delete all my data" means here. Recorded in DESIGN.md rather than only in this entry, since "why does it delete the whole Gist" is the first question anyone will ask of this code.
- **A Gist holding files this app never wrote is a different case, and is handled differently.** Someone who pointed the app at a Gist they also keep notes in would lose those notes to a `DELETE`. There, only `uk-wealth-tracker.json` is removed, the result carries `revisionsRemain: true`, and the panel says in as many words that earlier revisions still contain the data and that deleting the whole Gist on github.com is the way to be rid of those too. Honest and incomplete beats complete and destructive of things the user never asked to lose.
- **The action takes no Gist id — it cannot be aimed.** The issue asks for the wipe to be "provably scoped to the authenticated user's own Gist id, never an arbitrary/other id"; the strongest form of that is an API with no parameter for one. `deleteGistData()` reads the id from the same `getGistId()` the live sync uses, and the component never sees, holds or passes one. There is no call site that could pass the wrong id, so there is no need to trust that none does.
- **Three identities have to agree before anything is deleted:** the account this browser signed in as (the one the confirmation panel named on screen), the account the token authenticates as *right now* (`GET /user`, re-checked rather than trusted from sign-in time — a token can be replaced or re-scoped behind the app's back), and the account GitHub reports as the Gist's `owner`. Any disagreement throws with nothing deleted, naming who the Gist actually belongs to. The comparison prefers GitHub's numeric user id over `login`, so a renamed account still owns its Gist; `login` is the fallback for records with no usable id, compared case-insensitively.
- **Not available at all on a build-time `VITE_GITHUB_TOKEN`.** The app has never presented that token to GitHub, so nobody has verified whose account it reaches — and an irreversible operation is the wrong place to start guessing. `describeDeleteTarget().blocked` says so on screen and disables the button rather than hiding the section, so the answer to "where is delete my data" is an explanation rather than an absence. This is the concrete reason #62 had to land first.
- **Two-step confirmation with a typed phrase, and the destructive control does not exist until the first step.** Before arming, the panel renders no phrase field and no destructive button at all — there is nothing on screen a stray click can reach. Arming reveals a panel that lists exactly what will go (the Gist id, the account, this browser's copy) and requires `DELETE`, case-sensitive, before the button stops being disabled. It is deliberately *not* a form, so Enter submits nothing, and Cancel discards the typed phrase. The phrase and its matcher live in `persistence.js`, not in the component, so #100's Settings tab asks for the same phrase rather than inventing a second one users learn to click through.
- **A Gist-mode wipe clears this browser's copy too**, which is the issue's "and any local cache of it". Remote first, then local: if GitHub refuses, the throw leaves the local copy intact and the user can retry, rather than having destroyed the only copy they could still have exported. `browser-storage.js` clears IndexedDB *and* the `localStorage` fallback, and attempts both even when the first fails — a half-done wipe that leaves one copy behind is the one outcome worth avoiding, since the next load would adopt it and silently undo the deletion.
- **The local Gist pointer is forgotten after a successful wipe, but the sign-in is kept.** Leaving a pointer to a Gist the app would re-populate on the next keystroke is exactly the surprise the action exists to prevent; re-pointing at a Gist is one paste on the same page. Signing out, by contrast, is a separate button that already exists, and a wipe that also logged you out would make "start again from empty" needlessly harder. When the deleted id came from `VITE_GIST_ID`, which the app cannot unset, the result carries `buildIdRemains: true` and the panel says the build has to be rebuilt without it.
- **`store.js` cancels the pending debounced save before the wipe and suppresses saving across it.** The 800ms timer from the user's last edit would otherwise fire *after* the deletion and re-upload everything into the Gist that had just been deleted, and the reset to an empty document would itself schedule a save. Both are covered by tests that advance fake timers past the debounce and assert nothing was written. The reset is a plain `set`, not a re-hydrate: the backend was just emptied, so a load could only return the same empty document — and in Gist mode it would be a request against an id GitHub no longer has, i.e. an error banner for a wipe that worked.
- **Driven for real in a browser**, continuing the pattern from #22–#38/#98/#61/#62, with `api.github.com` intercepted. `npm run build` + `vite preview`, headless system Chromium via `playwright-core` in a scratch directory outside the repo (`package.json`/lockfile untouched, removed afterwards). 33 checks, all passing: in browser-only mode a real month was written to IndexedDB, the panel showed no destructive control until armed, `delete`/`DELETE ALL`/Enter all left the data intact, Cancel closed the panel having deleted nothing, and the confirmed wipe emptied both IndexedDB and `localStorage`, wrote nothing back over the following 1.5s, and left the dashboard reading "No monthly snapshot yet" after a reload. In Gist mode the confirmed wipe made exactly `GET /user`, `GET /gists/aa11bb22cc33`, `DELETE /gists/aa11bb22cc33` in that order, cleared the Gist pointer, kept the token and account, and left the header still reading "@octocat"; a Gist owned by `@ada` produced the refusal message with no `DELETE` or `PATCH` request at all and the pointer intact; and a Gist holding `notes.md` alongside our file was `PATCH`ed with `{"files":{"uk-wealth-tracker.json":null}}` and never deleted. No console messages and no page errors in any run.
- **The browser drive found a real bug, as it did in #62.** The panel read `describeDeleteTarget()` once on mount, so signing in a few centimetres above it on the same page left it still offering to delete "this browser's copy" while the app had switched to Gist mode. Unit tests could not have caught it — they render the component once, in one state. Fixed by re-reading the target whenever the `githubConnection` store changes, which is the one thing the sign-in panel already refreshes after every connect, disconnect and change of Gist.
- 50 tests added (15 in `gist.test.js`, 11 in `persistence.test.js`, 9 in `browser-storage.test.js`, 8 in the new `DeleteAllData.test.js`, 7 in `store.test.js`), 1328 total, up from 1278. `npm run lint`, `npm run check` (0 errors, same 2 pre-existing `<slot>` warnings) and `npm run build` all clean.

**Trade-offs / deviations from prompt:**
- **The wipe lives on the `/connect` page, not on a Settings tab.** #100 owns Settings, and it isn't built yet; the connect page is already reachable from every tab via the header indicator and is already where storage is explained, so it is where the action can be found today. The cost is that a browser-only user has to visit a page called "Connect GitHub" to delete local data — mitigated by the page's own copy now saying it is also where you delete the lot, and by #100 being the obvious place for this panel to move to.
- **`revisionsRemain` is reported, not solved.** For a multi-file Gist the app removes its file and tells the user the history survives; it does not offer to delete the whole Gist for them, which would destroy files this app never wrote. The GitHub API has no "delete one file's history" operation, so there is no third option.
- **A Gist emptied of our file can still be re-created by simply carrying on using the app.** The wipe resets the document and forgets the pointer, so the next save creates a *new* private Gist rather than refilling the old one — but it does create one. Anyone who wants to stop syncing entirely should sign out as well, which the panel says.
- **An in-flight save started before the wipe is not cancelled.** The debounce timer is cleared and saving is suppressed for the duration, but a `fetch` already awaiting inside `persist()` cannot be recalled — a sub-second window in which a save could land in a Gist about to be deleted, or (in the multi-file case) re-create the file just removed. Aborting it would mean threading an `AbortController` through the whole save path for a race that resolves itself on the next reload; noted rather than built.
- **Ownership is proved against GitHub's answer, not against the token's permissions.** A token with the `gist` scope can delete any Gist its account owns, and this app only ever asks GitHub which account owns *the one Gist it syncs with*. That is the "provably scoped" the issue asks for and it is enforced client-side; nothing stops a determined user from calling the GitHub API themselves with their own token, which is true of any browser-only app and not a boundary this code can create.
- **Other devices are told nothing.** A second browser signed into the same Gist keeps its own local copy until its next load, which then finds the Gist gone and shows a `GistError` in the sync banner. There is no push channel in a backend-less app; the confirmation panel says other devices will find it gone rather than implying they are wiped.
- **`DeleteAllData.test.js` covers the initial render only**, the same wall every component test in this repo hits: `svelte/server`'s `render` has no click handling, so "typing DELETE enables the button" is asserted in the browser drive above and via `isDeleteConfirmed` at module level, not in the component test.
- **No JSON export beside the delete button**, though the panel tells the user to export first. `data-transfer.js` is #100's, and stubbing an export here would be building that issue's work badly.
- `npm test` still isn't in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (flagged since #2; the app token lacks `workflows` permission), so all 1328 tests run locally but not on the PR. `build`/`check`/`lint` remain the only CI gates.

## JSON export/import + Settings tab (storage mode switch, data management UI) — 2026-08-07
<!-- METRICS:json-export-import-settings-tab-storage-mode-switch-data-management-ui -->
- **Execution Duration:** __DURATION__ seconds
- **Model:** __MODEL__
- **Turns:** __TURNS__
- **Input Tokens:** __INPUT_TOKENS__
- **Output Tokens:** __OUTPUT_TOKENS__
- **Estimated Cost:** $__COST__ (from Claude Code's reported total_cost_usd)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable GPT-5-tier model:** $__GPT_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable Gemini-tier model:** $__GEMINI_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)

**Decisions:**
- **An export carries an explicit `app: "uk-wealth-tracker"` marker, because `normaliseAppData` alone cannot tell "a real export" from "arbitrary JSON that happens to parse".** The issue asks import to "reject a file that is not a uk-wealth-tracker export" as a distinct check from data validation — but `normaliseAppData` defaults every missing field, so `{}` normalises into a perfectly valid empty document, and `{"hello":"world"}` fares no better. Without a marker, "not our export" is undetectable; with one, `parseImportDocument` rejects it before `migrateAppData`/`validateAppData` ever run, and says so rather than accepting a document that merely happens to be shaped right by accident.
- **The export document is the live `AppData` spread flat, plus `app` and `exported_at`, not a nested envelope.** The issue calls for "the same JSON shape both persistence modes already speak … carrying `schema_version` … plus an exported-at stamp" — a wrapper object (`{ meta, data: {...} }`) would have been a second shape to keep in sync with `types.js`. Importing is `migrateAppData` over the whole parsed object; the two extra keys are simply dropped by `normaliseAppData`'s "unknown top-level keys are dropped" rule, so the round trip needs no unwrapping step.
- **Import validates in two layers — the marker, then `migrateAppData` + `validateAppData` on what is left — and never mutates anything until both pass.** `parseImportDocument` is a pure function returning `{ ok: true, data }` or `{ ok: false, reason, message, errors }`; `DataManager.svelte` only calls `appData.set()` after the user confirms a result that was already `ok: true`. A file that fails either layer leaves the current document byte-identical, which is the issue's "all-or-nothing" requirement enforced by construction rather than by a try/catch around a partial write.
- **A file stamped with a newer `schema_version` than this build understands is refused, for free.** `normaliseAppData` already keeps a stored version that's greater than `SCHEMA_VERSION` rather than silently relabelling it, and `validateAppData` already flags `schema_version <= SCHEMA_VERSION` as an error — both written for the Gist-load path, before this issue existed. Running the same two functions for import means a document exported by a future build is rejected with `schema_version: must be …` in the error list rather than partially adopted, at no extra cost.
- **The Settings-tab mode switch does more than `setPersistenceMode` alone.** `persistence.js`'s own doc comment (written for #61/#62) says switching mode "does **not** move any data … moving it between them is JSON export/import's job (#100)" — true of the raw API, but the issue explicitly asks the Settings switch to "copy the data currently open into the mode being switched to" while leaving the mode being switched away from untouched. `DataManager.svelte` reconciles this by calling `setPersistenceMode` and then immediately `flushAppDataSync()` — the same store function `deleteAllAppData`/a "save now" action would use — which writes whatever `appData` currently holds into whichever backend the mode switch just made active. The old backend is never written to or deleted, so switching back later still finds what was there before.
- **With no usable GitHub token, the mode-switch buttons don't render at all — a sentence explains why instead.** `availablePersistenceModes()` already exists for exactly this ("browser-only is the only mode … say so rather than offering a switch that cannot work", per the issue). The panel checks `.length > 1` rather than trying to disable a `gist` button that would only fail with `setPersistenceMode`'s `RangeError` on click.
- **`data-transfer.js` needed a unique JSDoc typedef alias, and picked the wrong one on the first pass.** Following the existing local-`AppDataDoc`-alias convention (used by `browser-storage.js`/`gist.js`/`persistence.js` to avoid colliding with `model.js`'s own `AppData` typedef) produced a real `svelte-check` failure: `persistence.js` already claims `AppDataDoc` for itself, and both modules are wildcard-exported from `index.js`, so two same-named JSDoc typedefs became an "ambiguous re-export" error — the exact trap `store.js`'s doc comment already warns about, solved there by naming its own alias `StoreAppData` instead. Renamed to `TransferAppData`/`TransferValidationError` (the latter colliding with `model.js`'s own exported `ValidationError` typedef) and `npm run check` went back to 0 errors.
- **The Settings route follows the same hydrate-then-render shape as the tax/property tabs**, not a bespoke one: `+page.svelte` awaits `hydrateAppData()` behind a `ready` flag and hands off to `DataManager.svelte`, which reads and writes the `appData`/`syncState` stores directly rather than taking props — the same shape `DeleteAllData.svelte` already uses on the connect page, since both are "act on the whole document" panels rather than a form bound to one collection.
- **`settings` was added to `NAV_TABS` last**, after `estate`, per the issue's explicit "add the Settings tab to `NAV_TABS`" instruction — it is a data-management utility rather than one of README.md's ordered feature tabs, so it goes after them rather than interrupting their sequence.
- **Driven for real in a browser**, continuing the pattern from #22–#38/#98/#61/#62/#63, this time with no GitHub interception needed since the change is storage-mode/export/import rather than Gist wiring. `npm run dev` + headless Playwright Chromium installed to a scratch `/tmp` directory (`package.json`/lockfile untouched, removed afterwards). Verified: the header nav gained a working "Settings" link; the Settings page read "Storage mode right now: This browser only" and explained Gist sync wasn't available with no token configured, with no mode-switch buttons rendered; recording a month and clicking "Export data as JSON" produced a real file download (`uk-wealth-tracker-export-<date>.json`) containing the `app`/`exported_at` marker and the recorded month; feeding that file back through the file picker showed the confirm step with an accurate "1 monthly snapshot, 0 pensions, 0 properties, 0 assets, 0 dividend holdings" summary, and confirming replaced the store and showed the success notice; a non-JSON file, a valid-but-unmarked JSON file, and a marked file with an out-of-range field (`month: 13`) were each rejected before the confirm step, the last one listing `monthly_entries[0].month: must be a whole number from 1 to 12` inline. No console messages and no page errors in any run.
- 19 tests added (11 in the new `data-transfer.test.js`, 8 in the new `DataManager.test.js`), 1347 total, up from 1328. `npm run lint`, `npm run check` (0 errors, same 2 pre-existing `<slot>` warnings) and `npm run build` all clean.

**Trade-offs / deviations from prompt:**
- **The Gist-mode side of the storage-mode switch was not driven live in a browser.** This environment has no real GitHub token to sign in with, so the switch's actual copy-into-Gist behaviour rests on `flushAppDataSync`/`saveAppData` already being covered by `store.test.js`/`persistence.test.js`/`gist.test.js`, plus a `DataManager.test.js` render with a signed-in `localStorage` fixture confirming both mode buttons appear and the explanatory copy is correct. The browser-only half (the only mode this environment can actually reach) was driven end to end.
- **No dedicated "which Gist did the switch write to" confirmation beyond the existing sync banner.** `flushAppDataSync()` updates `syncState`, which the Settings panel reads for an error but does not otherwise surface Gist identity — that's already shown on the connect page (`GitHubSignIn.svelte`'s "Data Gist" line), and repeating it here would be a second source of truth for the same fact rather than new information.
- **Import's confirm-step summary counts records, not a diff against what's currently stored.** The issue asks for "a confirm step (it replaces everything)"; a full field-level diff between the current document and the pending import would be a second implementation of `validateAppData`'s comparison logic for a warning most users would skim past — the summary states record counts and the panel's copy says "replace everything" in as many words instead.
- **CSV/XLSX export and WealthR CSV import remain out of scope**, exactly as the issue lists them (secondary Phase 2 / #64 concerns) — `data-transfer.js` only speaks this app's own JSON shape.
- **`DataManager.test.js` covers the initial render only**, the same wall every component test in this repo hits: `svelte/server`'s `render` has no click handling, so the file-picker/confirm/switch-mode paths are asserted via the pure `data-transfer.test.js` unit tests plus the browser drive above, not inside the component test itself.
- `npm test` still isn't in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (flagged since #2; the app token lacks `workflows` permission), so all 1347 tests run locally but not on the PR. `build`/`check`/`lint` remain the only CI gates.

## ISA allowance tracker — 2026-08-07
<!-- METRICS:isa-allowance-tracker -->
- **Execution Duration:** __DURATION__ seconds
- **Model:** __MODEL__
- **Turns:** __TURNS__
- **Input Tokens:** __INPUT_TOKENS__
- **Output Tokens:** __OUTPUT_TOKENS__
- **Estimated Cost:** $__COST__ (from Claude Code's reported total_cost_usd)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable GPT-5-tier model:** $__GPT_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable Gemini-tier model:** $__GEMINI_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)

**Decisions:**
- **This is a from-scratch re-implementation, not a rebase of PR #79.** An earlier attempt at #28 (branch `isa-allowance-tracker-28`) had already been built and reviewed, but its branch point predated the following day's flurry of merges (dividend planner, property tracker, pension relief, Gist/browser-storage rework, GitHub sign-in, delete-all-data, JSON export/Settings) badly enough that a local dry-run merge produced real conflicts in application code, not just `docs/journal.md`. The owner closed it rather than risk a manual conflict resolution nobody had fully reviewed, and re-queued #28 against current `main`. Diffing the closed branch's `src/lib/isa.js`/`isa.test.js`/`IsaAllowanceTracker.svelte`/`.test.js` against `main` first showed every dependency they used — `enums.js`'s `ISA_WRAPPERS`/`WRAPPER_LABELS`, `fire.js`'s `monthlyEquivalentContribution`, `model.js`'s `createInvestment`, `types.js`'s `Investment` shape — was untouched by the intervening merges, so that arithmetic and its 36 tests carried over verbatim; only the wiring (`index.js`'s export list, the Tax page) needed rewriting against `main`'s current shape.
- **The five adult wrappers pool one £20,000 limit; the JISA has its own separate £9,000; the Lifetime ISA carries a second £4,000 cap *inside* the adult pool, not beside it.** This is the one piece of domain modelling the issue's one-line description doesn't spell out, and getting it wrong would misreport real allowance headroom: a saver who has put £17,000 into a Stocks & Shares ISA and wants to add £4,000 to a LISA has only £3,000 of *combined* room left, not £4,000 of separate LISA room, because the LISA's bonus-eligible £4,000 sits inside the same shared pot HMRC accounts against. `isaAllowanceSummary` returns each wrapper's own `remaining`/`overBy` as the *tighter* of whichever limits actually bind it, so the tracker's per-wrapper numbers stay correct even when the two limits disagree about which one is the binding constraint.
- **A wrapper's contribution field is seeded from the current recorded contribution pace, then owned by the session, not written back anywhere.** The data model has no field anywhere for "what has actually been paid into this ISA since 6 April" — `Investment.monthly_contribution` is a forward-looking pace, the same figure `fire.js`/`forecast.js` already project growth from. `isaContributionPace` annualises that pace per wrapper (reusing `fire.js`'s own `monthlyEquivalentContribution` so a quarterly contribution annualises identically to how the forecast tab already treats it) as a starting guess a user corrects to their real year-to-date figure, the same "seed from the store, then let the user own it" shape `TaxCalculator.svelte` already uses for salary and region. Nothing on the card is persisted, so it says so in as many words in the footnote.
- **Seeded from the latest recorded snapshot's holdings, read the same way the Forecast tab already reads them.** `Investment` records live nested inside `monthly_entries[]`, not as a flat top-level collection, so the Tax page now also hydrates `monthly_entries` from the store (read-only — it never writes them back, since the tracker only annualises a pace) and passes `forecast.js`'s existing `positionFromEntries(monthlyEntries)?.investments` down, the same helper `ForecastProjections.svelte` uses to find "the latest snapshot" rather than inventing a second way to pick it.
- **Help to Buy ISA is flagged `closedToNewAccounts` on every wrapper summary**, since it closed to new savers on 30 November 2019 but existing holders can still pay into one — the tracker still tracks it (an existing HTB holding still competes for the same shared adult allowance) but the card notes the wrapper itself can't be opened fresh.
- Reused `src/lib/isa.js`, `src/lib/isa.test.js` (36 tests), `src/components/IsaAllowanceTracker.svelte` and `src/components/IsaAllowanceTracker.test.js` (9 tests) essentially unchanged from the closed branch, confirmed compatible with current `main` by full re-run rather than assumed; added `isa.js` to `index.js`'s export list and wired `IsaAllowanceTracker` into the Tax tab, seeded from a newly-hydrated `monthlyEntries`. 1383 tests total, up from 1347. `npm run lint`, `npm run check` (0 errors, same 2 pre-existing `<slot>` warnings) and `npm run build` all clean.
- **Driven for real in a browser**, continuing the pattern from #22–#38/#98/#61/#62/#63/#100. `npm run build` + `vite preview`, headless system Chromium via `puppeteer-core` in a scratch `/tmp` directory (`package.json`/lockfile untouched, removed afterwards). Verified: the Tax tab's new card renders all six wrapper labels, the £20,000/£9,000/£4,000 limits and the Help to Buy "closed to new accounts" note on load with no recorded holdings; typing £12,000 into the Stocks & Shares ISA field live-recalculated the shared adult total to "£12,000 of £20,000" and "£8,000 remaining"; pushing the Lifetime ISA to £5,000 correctly flagged "£1,000 over" against its own sub-limit. No console messages or page errors in any run.

**Trade-offs / deviations from prompt:**
- **No live Gist-mode drive.** As in #100, this environment has no real GitHub token, so the browser verification above ran in browser-only mode; the store-hydration path it exercises (`hydrateAppData`/`appData`) is identical regardless of persistence mode and is already covered by `store.test.js`/`persistence.test.js`/`gist.test.js`.
- **The tax-year day-count and every limit are 2026/27-only, hard-coded the same way `tax.js`/`dividend-tax.js`/`pension-relief.js`/`student-loan.js` already are.** No tax-year selector exists anywhere in the app yet; adding one is a cross-cutting change affecting five modules at once, out of scope for this issue.
- **The Lifetime ISA's 25% government bonus and 25% early-withdrawal penalty are mentioned in the footnote but not modelled as figures.** The issue asks for limits, contributions and remaining allowance specifically; the bonus is a pension/retirement-income concern closer to #33's territory than an allowance tracker's.
- `npm test` still isn't in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (flagged since #2; the app token lacks `workflows` permission), so all 1383 tests run locally but not on the PR. `build`/`check`/`lint` remain the only CI gates.

## UK State Pension projection from NI qualifying years — 2026-08-07
<!-- METRICS:uk-state-pension-projection-from-ni-qualifying-years -->
- **Execution Duration:** __DURATION__ seconds
- **Model:** __MODEL__
- **Turns:** __TURNS__
- **Input Tokens:** __INPUT_TOKENS__
- **Output Tokens:** __OUTPUT_TOKENS__
- **Estimated Cost:** $__COST__ (from Claude Code's reported total_cost_usd)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable GPT-5-tier model:** $__GPT_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable Gemini-tier model:** $__GEMINI_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)

**Decisions:**
- **The 10-year minimum is implemented even though README.md only names the 35-year figure.** The functional spec says "35 years for full £241.30/week" and stops there, but the new State Pension has a second threshold underneath it that changes the answer entirely: fewer than 10 qualifying years pays *nothing at all*, not a small fraction. Interpolating thirty-fifths all the way down would tell someone with 6 years they were on course for £2,151 a year when the real figure is £0 — the one output of this feature it would be actively harmful to get wrong. `weeklyStatePension` therefore returns 0 below `MINIMUM_QUALIFYING_YEARS`, and the card has a dedicated branch that says so and prices the years it would take to cross the cliff.
- **Weekly is the stored constant; annual is the *rounded* weekly figure × 52.** DWP quotes and uprates the State Pension per week, so `FULL_STATE_PENSION_WEEKLY = 241.30` is the primary figure and everything else derives from it. Rounding the weekly figure to pence before annualising (rather than annualising the exact fraction) is deliberate: 20 years is £137.89 a week, and £137.89 × 52 = £7,170.28 is the number a user gets by multiplying what they can see on screen. The alternative differs by a couple of pence for no visible reason.
- **Zero is a real answer here, `null` means "not told yet" — the opposite of `defined-benefit.js`'s convention (2).** #30 treats a zero as a blank box, because no DB scheme accrues at 0% a year. National Insurance is different: a twenty-year-old genuinely has 0 qualifying years, and "you have 0 years, which pays £0 and needs 10 before it pays anything" is a useful thing to say. `asQualifyingYears` therefore keeps a recorded `0` and only returns `null` for a blank/unparseable input, and `StatePensionProjection.recorded` is what distinguishes the two on screen. Counts are floored (a qualifying year is a yes/no per tax year, so `12.7` is a typo) and clamped to the 0–60 range `model.js` already validates the `ni_*` fields against.
- **State Pension age is looked up from a three-band table, and every entry point takes an override.** The projection needs it for two things the counts alone can't give: when the income starts, and the ceiling on "further years expected" (a qualifying year takes a tax year to earn, so someone 27 years from State Pension age cannot earn 35 more). `STATE_PENSION_AGE_BANDS` implements the legislated timetable as at 2026/27 — 66 before 6 April 1960, 67 to 5 April 1977, 68 after, per the Pensions Act 2014 and 2007 — with both simplifications erring *later*: the phased 66→67 band (April 1960–March 1961) is given 67, and since `Profile` stores a birth month rather than a date, a birthday in a boundary month falls on the later side. The timetable is reviewed roughly every six years and has moved before, so `statePensionTiming` accepts an explicit override rather than presenting the table as fact.
- **One `type: 'state'` record in `pensions[]`, owned by the new card, filtered out of the old one.** `enums.js` has said since #29 that the State Pension "is derived from NI qualifying years instead, via a dedicated flow (#31)"; this is that flow. The record still lives in `pensions[]` rather than anywhere new — README.md's data model puts `ni_qualifying_years`/`ni_future_years` on `Pension`, and #33's retirement income stream builder needs to find it beside the DB schemes — but `PensionTracker` now filters it out of its pot list as well as out of its type select, since a row there would be four zeroes and an Edit button whose type select could only turn the State Pension into something it is not. `findStatePension` takes the first match: National Insurance is a fact about the person, and a second record would be double-counting a pension nobody gets twice.
- **The card writes on every keystroke and snaps the boxes back on `change`, with no Save button.** Two number inputs against a record that already exists is not a form, and the store's 800ms debounce means a per-keystroke write costs a re-render rather than a request. `commit()` is called from each input's own handler rather than from an `$effect`, because the write it makes is to `pensions`, which the projection reads back — an effect would re-run on its own output and spin. The handlers set the input state themselves instead of using `bind:value`, so ordering between the binding and the handler can't leave the committed value a keystroke behind. `normaliseInputs` on `change` snaps each box to what was actually stored, so typing `2835` shows 60 once focus leaves rather than a number the projection visibly isn't using.
- **`capitalEquivalent` reuses `fire.js`'s `fireNumber`, exactly as `defined-benefit.js` does** — so "what is my State Pension worth as a pot" and "what pot do I need for this income" stay one piece of arithmetic rather than two that could drift, and the State Pension lines up against DB income and DC pots on the same basis.
- **Driven for real in a browser**, continuing the pattern from #22–#38/#98/#61/#62/#63/#100/#28. `npm run build` + `vite preview`, headless system Chromium via `puppeteer-core` in a scratch `/tmp` directory (`package.json`/lockfile untouched, removed afterwards). Verified: the empty state; typing 20 + 10 producing £206.83/wk, "30 of 35", "5 years short" and the £358-a-year-per-year pricing live; the record appearing in IndexedDB with `type: "state"` and both `ni_*` fields set; the under-10 branch; an over-range `2835` clamping to 60 and the box snapping back on blur; a full page reload rehydrating both boxes and the whole projection from IndexedDB; the Pension pots card correctly showing "No pension pots recorded yet" with only the State Pension record present; and Remove clearing both the record and the boxes. No console messages and no page errors in any run.
- The browser drive caught a copy bug the SSR tests could not: the under-minimum sentence read "Another 2 years takes it from £0 to £0 a year", because `nextYearValue` is the value of *one* more year and one more year below the cliff is still £0. The sentence now names the income at 10 years (£3,585) and says the whole of it arrives with the year that crosses the line; `nextYearValue`'s own doc comment was corrected to match, and a test pins the £0-at-8-years behaviour that is actually correct.
- 59 tests added (43 in the new `state-pension.test.js`, 14 in the new `StatePensionProjection.test.js`, 2 in `PensionTracker.test.js` for the filtering), 1442 total, up from 1383. `npm run lint`, `npm run check` (0 errors, same 2 pre-existing `<slot>` warnings) and `npm run build` all clean.

**Trade-offs / deviations from prompt:**
- **The pre-2016 transitional "starting amount" is not modelled, and is the largest thing this deliberately gets wrong for older users.** Anyone with a National Insurance record from before 6 April 2016 gets the *higher* of the new 35ths rule and the old basic-plus-additional (SERPS/S2P) rules, with any excess kept as a protected payment on top of the full rate; anyone contracted out has a Contracted Out Pension Equivalent deduction pulling the other way and can need more than 35 years. Neither is derivable from a single count of qualifying years, neither has a field in `Pension` to hold it, and README.md specifies the 35ths rule — so 35ths is what this implements, with the module header and the card's footnote both saying plainly that the gov.uk forecast is the figure that overrides it.
- **No uprating: every figure is in 2026/27 pounds.** Same nominal-vs-real convention `fire.js` and `defined-benefit.js` already document. For a 30-year projection this understates the cash figure — the triple lock has beaten inflation in most recent years — which is the safe direction to be wrong in, and is stated in the footnote rather than silently assumed.
- **Deferral, voluntary Class 3 contributions and income tax are named in the footnote but not modelled.** Deferral (~5.8% a year) and Class 3 buy-back both need constants the app doesn't carry for 2026/27, and inventing a Class 3 weekly rate to price "buy your missing years" would be exactly the kind of made-up tax-year figure CLAUDE.md warns against. The card prices a missing year in *pension* terms (£358 a year for life) instead, which is derived from README.md's own £241.30 rather than from anything invented.
- **State Pension age is a supporting figure, not a headline, and there is no UI to override it.** `statePensionTiming` accepts an override and it is tested, but no control is wired up: the profile's date of birth drives it, and adding a fourth input to a two-input card for an assumption most users won't want to change belongs with a wider profile/assumptions UI rather than here.
- **`StatePensionProjection.test.js` covers the initial render only**, the same wall every component test in this repo hits — `svelte/server`'s `render` has no click or input handling. The interactive half (commit-on-keystroke, snap-back on blur, Remove, the persistence round trip) is covered by the browser drive above and by `state-pension.test.js`'s direct unit tests of the functions behind it.
- **Nothing feeds this into the FIRE/forecast tabs yet.** A State Pension starting at 67 changes the drawdown a pot has to support before and after that date, but combining income streams is #33's scope; this issue's deliverable is the projection itself, and `statePensionOutlook` returns it in the shape that work will need.
- `npm test` still isn't in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (flagged since #2; the app token lacks `workflows` permission), so all 1442 tests run locally but not on the PR. `build`/`check`/`lint` remain the only CI gates.
