- **No browser drive this session.** `npm run dev` was started and smoke-tested with `curl` (200 OK, the "Monthly snapshot" heading present in the server-rendered HTML), confirming the app still boots with the new field in the bundle, but there was no `chromium`/display available in this environment to click through "Add month → Add holding → set Ownership % → Save" the way the project's own `run` skill asks for. `svelte-check` (0 errors) does catch template-level mistakes — a mistyped binding, a missing import, a bad conditional — that a build-only check would miss, so the form's wiring is verified at that level; a human should still click through it once before relying on it.
- **11 new tests, split across two files.** `net-worth.test.js` gains a `net worth lenses` block (`DEFAULT_NET_WORTH_LENS`/`NET_WORTH_LENSES`, the default lens reproducing the pre-existing household totals to the penny, `you`/`partner` scaling and summing back to `household`, an excluded holding staying excluded under every lens, debts netting off identically under every lens, an out-of-range `ownership_pct` clamping rather than going negative, and the lens flowing through `netWorthSeries`) and a `netWorthLensLabel` block (the three plain labels, the partner name, and the blank/whitespace fallback) — 2147 → 2158. `npm test` still isn't in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (flagged since #2), so these run locally but not on the PR; `build`/`check` (0 errors, same 2 pre-existing `<slot>` warnings) and `lint` remain the CI gates and all three pass.

## Household/Partner: Marriage Allowance wired to real partner data — 2026-08-08
<!-- METRICS:household-partner-marriage-allowance-wired-to-real-partner-data -->
- **Execution Duration:** __DURATION__ seconds
- **Model:** __MODEL__
- **Turns:** __TURNS__
- **Input Tokens:** __INPUT_TOKENS__
- **Output Tokens:** __OUTPUT_TOKENS__
- **Estimated Cost:** $__COST__ (from Claude Code's reported total_cost_usd)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable GPT-5-tier model:** $__GPT_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable Gemini-tier model:** $__GEMINI_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)

**Decisions:**
- **The wiring follows the same read-only, seed-once path #143's own header already documents for `profile`, not a new store subscription inside the card.** `routes/tax/+page.svelte` already snapshots `AppData` once after `hydrateAppData()` resolves and hands `profile` down as a plain prop that `TaxCalculator` seeds `income`/`region`/`sacrifice` from for the page session only, nothing written back. `partner` (`AppData.partner`, `null` when the household has none — #142) now rides the same snapshot and the same seed-once `$state` initialiser: `partnerIncome = $state(partner?.gross_salary ?? 0)`. A household with a partner profile opens Marriage Allowance on the real salary instead of £0; a household without one gets exactly today's blank field, because `partner` is `null` and the `?? 0` fallback fires the same way it always implicitly did.
- **The seeded figure stays editable, not locked to the profile.** The issue asks for a pre-fill "rather than requiring re-entry," not a read-only mirror, and locking it would have broken the one convention every other seeded field on this tab already follows (`income`, `region`, `sacrifice` are all "start from real data, then it's yours to try what-ifs with for the rest of the session"). Marriage Allowance eligibility is sharp-edged around the Personal Allowance and the higher-rate threshold, so being able to nudge the partner figure without touching the real profile is exactly what makes "what if they earned £500 less" answerable.
- **No marital-status field was added anywhere.** README.md's "Partner profile" line is explicit — DOB, retirement age, salary, pension, NI years, nothing about marriage — and `estate.js`'s own `spouseExempt` is a plain typed amount rather than a flag keyed off any partner record either, so there is no existing precedent to extend. The "we're married or in a civil partnership" checkbox therefore stays exactly what it was: a fact the user states by hand, independent of whether a partner profile exists. `partner` changes where the *income* comes from and what the helper text under the field says; it does not pre-check the box, and it can't, because the data model has nowhere to record the answer.
- **`partnerIncome` is shared with `bind:` between `ChildBenefitCharge` and `MarriageAllowance` (an existing #24/#25 decision), so seeding it once in `TaxCalculator` pre-fills both cards without touching `ChildBenefitCharge.svelte` at all.** That is a genuine, harmless side effect rather than scope creep: the field's own doc comment already frames it as "what a partner earns," not "what a partner earns for Marriage Allowance specifically," and a Child Benefit claimant with a real partner profile is exactly as well served by the real figure as a married one is.
- **`MarriageAllowance.svelte` takes `partner` as its own prop (not just the derived income) because the issue's "falling back to the existing manual-entry UI when no partner profile is set up" needs `partner`'s presence, not only its salary, to choose between two sentences under the income field** — "Pre-filled from `{name}`'s profile (£X) — change it here for a what-if" versus the original "no partner" framing. A local `partnerName` derived (`partner.name.trim() || null`) backs the first sentence rather than reusing `net-worth.js`'s `netWorthLensLabel`: that helper's `'Partner'` fallback (capitalised, no article) is tuned for a short lens-toggle pill, and dropping it into this panel's existing "your partner" mid-sentence prose would have read as a grammar regression for the common case of a partner profile with no name typed in yet.
- **Nothing in `$lib/marriage-allowance.js` changed.** The issue's own instruction was "a data-source change, not a new calculation," and the diff proves it: `marriageAllowanceSummary` and everything it calls are byte-for-byte what #25 shipped, the only inputs feeding it are still `transferorIncome`/`recipientIncome`/`region`/`claiming`, and `partner` never reaches the maths layer at all — it only decides what value `partnerIncome` opens on and one sentence of prose.

**Trade-offs / deviations from prompt:**
- **This wiring is currently inert for every real user, and that is expected.** #170 (partner profile entry form on Settings) is still open — there is no UI anywhere yet that writes to `AppData.partner`, so `$appData.partner` stays `null` for any household until #170 lands, and Marriage Allowance keeps opening on the pre-existing blank-field behaviour in practice. The two issues were already split this way in this milestone (#142 data model / #170 entry form, the same pattern as #140/#166 and #131/#132), so this session verified the pre-fill path directly against `createPartner(...)` in tests and via the store rather than through a form that does not exist yet.
- **`ChildBenefitCharge.svelte`'s own "0 if you have no partner" hint text was left untouched**, even though its field now also opens pre-filled once a partner profile exists. Rewording it was outside #146's scope (it names Marriage Allowance specifically), and the hint is only slightly stale rather than wrong — it still correctly describes what happens with no partner profile, which remains every household's state until #170 ships.
- **No personalisation of the rest of the panel's "Your partner"/"your partner" prose** (the eligibility-refusal sentences, the counterfactual copy, the four result tiles) even where a partner's name is known. Threading `partnerName` through every one of those existing, already-tested sentences would have been a much larger diff for a cosmetic improvement the issue didn't ask for; only the one new sentence next to the income field uses it.
- **No browser drive this session.** `npm run dev` was started and smoke-tested with `curl` (200 OK on `/tax`, "Marriage Allowance" present in the server-rendered HTML), but the page's real data only appears after client-side hydration (`onMount` → `hydrateAppData()`), which a plain `curl` never runs — so this confirms the route still boots with the new prop wired through, not the pre-filled figure on screen. There was no `chromium`/display available in this environment to click through "add a partner via the store → open Tax tab → see the salary pre-filled" the way the project's own `run` skill asks for; `svelte-check` (0 errors, same 2 pre-existing `<slot>` warnings) catches template-level wiring mistakes in the meantime, and a human should click through it once #170 gives this something real to point at.
- **4 new tests, split across two files** (`MarriageAllowance.test.js` 11 → 14, `TaxCalculator.test.js` 15 → 16): the panel's pre-filled-with-a-name, pre-filled-with-no-name, and no-partner-fallback sentences directly, plus one `TaxCalculator` test confirming the shared `partnerIncome` field's rendered `value` attribute is `5000` with a partner profile and `0` without one — 2158 → 2162. `npm test` still isn't in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (flagged since #2), so these run locally but not on the PR; `build`, `check` (0 errors) and `lint` remain the CI gates and all three pass.

## Printable report: print stylesheet + Report tab with net worth & investments summary — 2026-08-08
<!-- METRICS:printable-report-print-stylesheet-and-report-tab-with-net-worth-and-investments-summary -->
- **Execution Duration:** __DURATION__ seconds
- **Model:** __MODEL__
- **Turns:** __TURNS__
- **Input Tokens:** __INPUT_TOKENS__
- **Output Tokens:** __OUTPUT_TOKENS__
- **Estimated Cost:** $__COST__ (from Claude Code's reported total_cost_usd)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable GPT-5-tier model:** $__GPT_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable Gemini-tier model:** $__GEMINI_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)

**Decisions:**
- **`reportNetWorth` covers both "net worth" and "investments" in one function, because this app's data model doesn't distinguish them.** README.md's Phase 2 spec lists "net worth, investments, tax, pensions" as four summaries, but a holding *is* an investment (`MonthlyEntry.investments`) — there's no separate investments collection to summarise. The issue's own title ("net worth & investments summary") and its instruction to add `holdingsByType` alongside the totals confirmed this reading, so one module, one entry point, covers what the spec names as two.
- **Nothing is re-derived — `reportNetWorth` is built entirely on `forecast.js`'s `positionFromEntries` (the anchor) and `debt.js`'s `sumInvestmentValues`/`sumDebtBalances` (the totals), the same functions the Net Worth dashboard's D/I ratio already calls.** The issue asked for this explicitly ("so a printed report and its source tab can never disagree about a figure"), and it's what makes `exclude_from_net_worth` handling free: a mortgage excluded because its property's equity is tracked separately stays excluded from the report's totals with no new logic, exactly as it already does everywhere else.
- **`hasData: false` (no monthly entries recorded) is a different value from `hasData: true, netWorth: 0`** (a snapshot exists but nets to zero, or every holding happens to be `exclude_from_net_worth`). The report page reads this to print "no data recorded yet" rather than a report that flatly — and misleadingly — states someone is worth nothing. The route itself gates on `report.netWorth.asOf` rather than `.hasData` directly, since `asOf` is the field svelte-check can actually narrow types against inside the `{#if}` that follows.
- **`holdings`/`debts` on the returned report are the *complete* latest snapshot, including anything flagged `exclude_from_net_worth`** — only the totals and the `holdingsByType` grouping drop them. A report handed to a mortgage adviser should show every debt that exists, not just the ones counted towards a net worth figure; the Report page renders excluded debts inline with a small "(excluded from net worth)" note, and excluded holdings in their own "Other holdings" list below the per-type breakdown.
- **The print stylesheet lives entirely inside one `@media print` block in `app.css`, not scattered inline styles.** `.no-print` hides the nav shell's header/footer (now tagged in `+layout.svelte`) and the Report page's own on-screen instructions and "Print / Save as PDF" button — centrally, so any future "print this" affordance elsewhere reuses the one class rather than re-inventing it. `.print-report` re-declares `:root`'s light-mode CSS custom properties (`--background`, `--foreground`, `--card`, `--muted`, `--border`) scoped to itself: a custom property re-declared on a descendant element always wins over one merely inherited from `.dark` further up the tree, regardless of selector order or specificity, so this needed no `!important` fight with the existing `.dark` block. `.report-section` gets `break-inside: avoid` so a heading/table/totals group can't split across a page break.
- **The Report route follows the exact hydrate-then-render shape every other read-only tab already uses** (`TaxCalculator`'s `profile = $state(createProfile())`, most recently #146's `partnerIncome` seeding): `report` seeds from `buildReport(createAppData())` — a real, typed empty report — rather than `null`, so svelte-check never has to prove a nullable value is safe to read; `ready` (set once `onMount`'s `hydrateAppData()` resolves) is what actually gates whether anything built from it reaches the screen.
- **`buildReport(appData)` returns `{ netWorth }` today and is documented as gaining `tax`/`pensions` fields alongside it, not replacing it, once #177 lands** — so #177's call sites and this one agree on shape from day one rather than #177 having to migrate every existing caller.

**Trade-offs / deviations from prompt:**
- **Out of scope, per the issue's own narrowing: tax and pensions sections.** That's #177, which this issue explicitly hands `buildReport` off to — the issue was split this way after the combined version previously hit `error_max_turns` (100), the same engine/panel or foundational/extension split this milestone already uses repeatedly (#142/#170, #136/#161, #131/#132, #140/#166, #146 itself). Also out, per the issue: charts in the report, per-section export options, and report configuration/date-range selection — none of those are touched here.
- **No `Report.svelte`/`ReportSection.svelte` component split.** The page is one `+page.svelte`, matching the size and shape of other single-purpose read-only tabs (`estate/+page.svelte`'s placeholder aside) rather than `TaxCalculator`'s composed-of-several-components pattern — there's exactly one data source (`buildReport`) and no interactive state to justify extracting sub-components yet; #177 adding two more sections is the natural point to reconsider that if the file gets unwieldy.
- **No browser drive this session.** `npm run dev` was started and smoke-tested with `curl` (200 OK on `/` and `/report`, the new "🖨️ Report" nav tab present and marked active on `/report`, the `@media print` block present in the served `<style>`), but the report's real figures only render after client-side hydration (`onMount` → `hydrateAppData()`), which `curl` never runs, and printing/saving as PDF is unverifiable without an actual browser. There was no `chromium`/display available in this environment to click through "record a snapshot → open Report → Print / Save as PDF → check the PDF" the way the project's own `run` skill asks for; `svelte-check` (0 errors, same 2 pre-existing `<slot>` warnings) catches template-level wiring mistakes in the meantime, and a human should click through it once before relying on it for a real adviser meeting.
- **12 new tests in `report.test.js`, plus `nav.test.js` updated for the new tab** — 2162 → 2174. Covers: no data vs. a genuinely zero net worth, anchoring on the latest (not first or largest) snapshot, summing investments/debts into net worth, `exclude_from_net_worth` dropped from totals but still listed, grouping by investment type in `INVESTMENT_TYPES` order with empty types omitted, an excluded holding dropped from its group, group totals summing back to the overall total, and `buildReport` wiring `AppData.monthly_entries` through. `npm test` still isn't in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (flagged since #2), so these run locally but not on the PR; `build`, `check` (0 errors) and `lint` remain the CI gates and all three pass.

## Scenario: one-off large costs — panel and forecast-tab wiring — 2026-08-08
<!-- METRICS:scenario-one-off-large-costs-panel-and-forecast-tab-wiring -->
- **Execution Duration:** __DURATION__ seconds
- **Model:** __MODEL__

- **Output Tokens:** __OUTPUT_TOKENS__

- **Ballpark cost, comparable Gemini-tier model:** $__GEMINI_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)

**Decisions:**
- **`OneOffCostsPanel.svelte` reuses `StressTestPanel`/`IncomeShockPanel`'s layout — off-by-default checkbox, headline prose, two-bar horizon comparison, per-scenario table, per-horizon comparison table — but swaps their fixed dials for an editable `CostRow[]` list**, per #136's own instruction that this scenario is "a list, not a slot" (real use is rarely just one cost). Each row is `id`/`name`/`startYears`/`amount`, add/remove-only (no in-place edit form, unlike `AssetsTracker`'s add-then-edit pattern) — a cost is a quick what-if row, not a record worth a separate edit mode — keyed by `row.id` in the `{#each}` so editing one row's fields never re-mounts, or loses focus on, another.
- **Timing is typed in years in the row (`startYears`), converted to `one-off-costs.js`'s whole-month `atMonth` at the `normalisedCosts` derivation** — the same split `IncomeShockPanel`'s own `startYears` keeps against `income-shock.js`, since a planning horizon is discussed in years but the engine counts months.
- **The whole list is projected in one `oneOffCostsForecast` call, not once per row.** `costed` is `null` until both `enabled` is ticked and at least one row exists, matching the two dial-based siblings' "nothing projected until there's something to project" guard; once it exists, `oneOffCostsImpact`'s per-cost `costs` array (already dated and sorted soonest-first by the engine) drives a dedicated cost/date/amount table above the headline prose, so a reader sees which named cost is doing what before the aggregate shortfall figure.
- **Reused, rather than reworked, the never-merged prior-art branch's `OneOffCostsPanel.svelte` (`origin/scenario-one-off-large-costs`, `cc8583d`) after independently checking it against the *current* `src/lib/one-off-costs.js`** (merged since as #162, after that branch was written) — every function/field name it calls (`oneOffCostsForecast`, `oneOffCostsImpact`, `oneOffCostsImpacts`, `compareOneOffCosts`, `createOneOffCost`, `DEFAULT_ONE_OFF_COST`, and the `OneOffCostsImpact`/`OneOffCostOccurrence` shapes it reads) still matches exactly, so the component was adopted as-is rather than rederiving the same layout from scratch, the same call #162's own journal entry made for the engine half.
- **`ForecastProjections.svelte` gets the panel as a third sibling immediately after `IncomeShockPanel`**, passed the identical `forecast`/`position`/`options`/`offsets`/`dobYear`/`dobMonth` props as its two neighbours, so all three overlays read the same baseline anchor, horizon and spread rather than any one of them drifting onto a second set of assumptions; the module doc comment at the top of that file gained a paragraph for it, extending rather than leaving undocumented, per the issue's own instruction.

**Trade-offs / deviations from prompt:**
- **Component tests exist for this panel even though neither sibling it's modelled on has one.** `StressTestPanel.svelte`/`IncomeShockPanel.svelte`/`CompoundingPanel.svelte` have no `*.test.js` today — the issue asked for tests "alongside the sibling panels'," but there's no existing sibling file to sit alongside. Wrote `OneOffCostsPanel.test.js` anyway, following `AssetsTracker.test.js`'s server-render smoke-test shape (`svelte/server`'s `render`, initial state only): the collapsed-state prose, the add-a-cost control, the scenario heading, and a render-doesn't-throw check with a real position. `enabled` and the cost list are local `$state`, not props, so a server-rendered pass can only ever see the freshly-loaded, unticked, empty-list state — the interactive add/remove/impact-table path is what the browser drive below covers instead.
- **Driven for real in a browser**, continuing the pattern from #22-#38/#98/#61-#64/#100/#111/#116/#117/#118/#127/#128/#149/#150/#157. `npm run build` + `vite preview`, headless system Chromium over the DevTools Protocol (`playwright-core` installed with `npm install --no-save` in a scratch `/tmp` directory). Confirmed the collapsed prose, ticked "Model one-off costs", confirmed the empty-list prompt, added a row (the seeded £5,000/a year out default), named it "Wedding" and confirmed the £5,000/1-cost impact sentence and the £20,581 (5%) 30-year shortfall figure; added a second row named "Car" sharing the same default month and confirmed the two costs combined into one £10,000/2-cost figure and a £41,161 (9%) shortfall; removed the second row and confirmed the list dropped back to one. No console messages or page errors throughout. The driver script and its scratch `node_modules` were removed afterward, nothing left in the working tree.
- **5 new tests in `OneOffCostsPanel.test.js`** — 2174 → 2179 (`src/lib/one-off-costs.js`/`forecast.js`'s own 59 tests from #162 are unchanged; this issue added no engine code). `npm test` still isn't in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (flagged since #2), so these run locally but not on the PR; `build`, `check` (0 errors, same 2 pre-existing `<slot>` warnings) and `lint` remain the CI gates and all three pass.

**Decisions:**
- **`src/routes/estate/+page.svelte` follows the exact hydrate-then-render shape `assets/+page.svelte` and `property/+page.svelte` already use** — `onMount` → `hydrateAppData()` → read the needed lists off the store into `$state` → flip `ready`, with "Loading your saved data…" shown until then and the same persistence-mode/sync-status line every sibling tab carries. Unlike those two, nothing here writes back: there is no `$effect` pushing local state into `appData.update`, since #187 (`iht_settings`) is what gives this tab its first write, and the issue was explicit that `ready` needs to exist *now* precisely so that first write never races a pre-hydrate default.
- **Only `monthly_entries`, `properties` and `assets` are hydrated into local state**, not `pensions`. The issue's own build notes list all four as "already in `AppData` today," but nothing this issue renders needs pensions — the empty-state condition it specifies is explicitly "no monthly entries, no properties and no assets," matching `estate.js`'s convention that an unused Defined Contribution pot doesn't enter an estate valuation at all. Hydrating an unused array would also fail this repo's lint (`no-unused-vars`, checked live — there's no existing `eslint-disable` precedent anywhere in `src/`, so introducing one here felt like the wrong fix for a value nothing consumes yet); #189's `EstateSummary` card is the issue that actually needs a Defined Benefit income figure, so it hydrates pensions itself when it adds the component that reads it.
- **The empty-state guard (`hasEstateData`) lives in exactly one place — this page — per the issue's own instruction**, so #189's summary card never has to restate it: `{:else}` (once `ready` and `hasEstateData`) is currently an empty comment marking where `EstateSummary`, then the Inheritance Tax bill/`IhtSettings` form, then the beneficiary breakdown mount in turn, rather than a `<div>` those issues would need to restructure.
- **The intro paragraph is written to README.md's "Estate value from existing tracked data — no re-entry" line**, naming the Net Worth, Property and Assets tabs as where the value actually comes from, and states that the Inheritance Tax bill and who-gets-what breakdown "land on top of" this valuation rather than describing arithmetic this issue doesn't do — `estateSnapshot()`, the bands and the bill are explicitly #189/#187/#140's, not this route's.

**Trade-offs / deviations from prompt:**
- **No component test, per the issue's own note that no route in `src/routes/` carries a test file in this repo** — confirmed against every sibling `+page.svelte` before relying on it. `assets.test.js`/`property.test.js` etc. all test the *component* the route wraps, not the route itself; there is no `EstateSummary` or similar yet for this issue to hand off to, so route-level coverage stays at `svelte-check` (0 errors, same 2 pre-existing `<slot>` warnings) plus the browser drive below.
- **Driven for real in a browser**, continuing the pattern from #22-#38/#98/#61-#64/#100/#111/#116/#117/#118/#127/#128/#149/#150/#157/#161. `npm run dev` + Playwright's Chromium (installed fresh via `npx playwright install chromium --with-deps` into a scratch `/tmp` directory, since neither it nor `chromium-cli` was already present in this environment). Confirmed the empty state — "Nothing to value yet — add a monthly snapshot on the Net Worth tab, or a property or asset on the Property or Assets tabs…" — renders on a fresh document with no console errors; then, from the Net Worth tab, added a month and one £10,000 holding, waited past the store's 800ms sync debounce, and reloaded `/estate` to confirm the empty-state message is gone with nothing else on screen breaking (the panel slot being empty is correct — #189 hasn't shipped yet). Screenshotted both states before removing the scratch driver scripts and `node_modules`; nothing was left in the working tree.

- **Test count unchanged at 2179** (`npm test`) — this issue added a route, not library code, and per the point above there is no route-level test file convention to extend. `npm test` still isn't in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (flagged since #2), so it continues to run locally but not on the PR; `build`, `check` and `lint` remain the CI gates and all three pass.

## Household/Partner: partner profile entry form on Settings — 2026-08-09
<!-- METRICS:household-partner-partner-profile-entry-form-on-settings -->
- **Execution Duration:** __DURATION__ seconds
- **Model:** __MODEL__
- **Turns:** __TURNS__
- **Input Tokens:** __INPUT_TOKENS__
- **Output Tokens:** __OUTPUT_TOKENS__
- **Estimated Cost:** $__COST__ (from Claude Code's reported total_cost_usd)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable GPT-5-tier model:** $__GPT_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable Gemini-tier model:** $__GEMINI_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)

**Decisions:**
- **`PartnerProfile.svelte` has no Save button — every field commits to the bound `partner` object on `input`, the same convention `StatePensionProjection` uses for a record that already exists rather than a list being built up.** A partner profile is one record with seven fields, not a collection needing `PensionTracker`'s add/edit/remove-by-id machinery, so the closer sibling was the other "one record, no list" panel already in the codebase. Field text lives in local `$state` strings rather than binding straight into `partner`'s own properties, so a box the user has cleared reads as "empty" instead of being coerced to `0` (or `NaN`) mid-keystroke — `dob_month`/`dob_year`/`ni_qualifying_years` are genuinely optional per #142's typedef, and a blank box has to stay `null`, not `0`, all the way to the store.
- **No partner (`AppData.partner === null`) is its own first-class rendering branch, not a form pre-filled with zeroes** — the issue's own framing ("no partner is the default and must stay a first-class state"). "Add a partner" calls `createPartner()` and nothing else; "Remove partner" sets `partner` straight back to `null` rather than blanking the record, so #143/#144/#145/#146 downstream keep the one `null` check they already have rather than needing a second "is this actually filled in" test.
- **Exported a new `validatePartnerFields(partner)` from `$lib/model.js`, a thin wrapper running #142's own (unexported) `validatePartner` through `createCollector()` and returning its `ValidationError[]`.** The issue was explicit — "surface the `validatePartner` failures... rather than inventing a second set of range rules in the component" — and `validateAppData` only exposes those checks by validating a whole `AppData` document, which a Settings-tab panel editing just the partner doesn't have lying around. So the component carries no hand-rolled `min`/`max` mirroring #142's bounds (unlike `DebtTracker`/`InvestmentHoldings`'s `MIN_YEAR`/`MAX_YEAR` mirrors) — an out-of-range value commits as typed, `validatePartnerFields` flags it beneath the form, and the same document would fail `validateAppData` identically if saved and reloaded. A `FIELD_LABELS` map turns each error's `partner.xxx` path into a short label for display; the range numbers and wording in the message itself stay entirely #142's.
- **Wired into `src/routes/settings/+page.svelte` with the same hydrate → local `$state` → `ready`-gated `$effect` write-back shape `pensions/+page.svelte` and `property/+page.svelte` already use**, reading `partner` off the store once after `hydrateAppData()` resolves and writing it back on every change thereafter. `PartnerProfile` renders above `DataManager`, inside the same `{#if ready}` block the page already had, so it never mounts pre-hydrate and never gets a chance to seed its local field state from the pre-hydrate `null` default.
- **8 new tests in `PartnerProfile.test.js`** (the empty state, its absence of form fields, a populated partner's rendered field values, a freshly-added default partner's blank-not-zero optional fields, a validation-error case, and a clean-partner case), following `PensionTracker.test.js`'s server-rendered smoke-test shape — plus 3 new tests for `validatePartnerFields` in `model.test.js`, mirroring the existing `validateAppData`-via-partner cases so both entry points are proven to agree — 2179 → 2188.

**Trade-offs / deviations from prompt:**
- **Two of the eight component tests read the raw server-rendered HTML rather than the usual tag-stripped plain text**, because an `<input>`'s `value`/`placeholder` attribute — the only place this component puts a field's actual figure — disappears along with the tag under `PensionTracker.test.js`'s own `replace(/<[^>]+>/g, ' ')` helper. Added a second `html()` helper alongside the usual `text()` one rather than reworking the shared pattern, since every other assertion in the file (and in every sibling test file) is checking visible prose, where the tag-stripped version is exactly right.
- **Driven for real in a browser**, continuing the pattern from #22-#38/#98/#61-#64/#100/#111/#116/#117/#118/#127/#128/#149/#150/#157/#161/#189. `npm run dev` + Playwright's Chromium (`chromium`/`chromium-browser`/`google-chrome` were all already present in this environment at `/usr/bin`, so no fresh install was needed this time — only a scratch `npm install --no-save playwright-core` in `/tmp` for the driver script). Confirmed the empty state, clicked "Add a partner", filled all seven fields, waited past the store's 800ms sync debounce and reloaded `/settings` to confirm the values round-tripped through persistence; set retirement age to 200 and confirmed "Retirement age must be 16–120" appeared (`validatePartnerFields` surfacing live); fixed it and clicked "Remove partner", then reloaded again to confirm the empty state came back and stayed back. No console or page errors throughout. The scratch driver script and its `/tmp/node_modules` were removed afterward; nothing was left in the working tree.
- **Out of scope, per the issue's own narrowing: everything that *consumes* partner data** — the household net worth lens (#143, already shipped and previously inert for lack of this form), the joint retirement forecast (#144), household budget (#145) and Marriage Allowance pre-fill (#146, likewise already shipped and previously inert). This issue only gives `AppData.partner` a real way to stop being `null`; #143's lens and #146's Marriage Allowance card should now show real figures for the first time for any household that adds a partner here, but neither was touched by this session.
- **`build`, `check` (0 errors, same 2 pre-existing `<slot>` warnings) and `lint` all pass; `npm test` remains local-only** — still not in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (flagged since #2).

- **Ballpark cost, comparable Gemini-tier model:** $__GEMINI_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)

**Decisions:**
- **`NetWorthChart.svelte` gets a `role="radiogroup"` of three labelled radios, built from `NET_WORTH_LENSES` and labelled via #143's `netWorthLensLabel`, rendered above the headline figure** — the issue's own instruction, since the toggle decides what that figure means, and reading the total before discovering it was "You" all along is the wrong order. `lens` state feeds straight into `netWorthSeries(monthlyEntries, { lens })`; nothing here re-derives the ownership split #143 already computes.
- **Added an `initialLens` prop, seeded once into the `lens` `$state` exactly the way `showForecast`/`forecastYears` already seed `forecastOn`/`horizon`** — not asked for explicitly, but the issue's own "Tests" section requires asserting what a `you`/`partner` lens renders, and `svelte/server`'s `render` has no pointer to click a toggle with. Every real caller leaves it unset, so `household` — #143's own default — stays what every existing screen and snapshot renders.
- **The forecast overlay is disabled outside the `household` lens, per the issue's own instruction to pick the "cheap, honest option" over teaching `forecast.js` a lens of its own.** `forecast.js` has no concept of a lens and always projects the household total from `growthRate`; drawing that under a `you`/`partner` tracked line would plot two series computed at different scales and read as a personal projection nothing actually computed. Switching off `household` swaps the "Show forecast" checkbox and horizon picker for one line of text explaining why, and forces the `forecast` derivation to `null` regardless of `forecastOn` — which is left untouched, so switching back to `household` resumes exactly where the control was left rather than resetting it.
- **The accessible summary now leads with whose net worth it describes** — "Household net worth.", "Your net worth.", or "`{name}`'s net worth." (falling back to "Partner's net worth." with none recorded) — ahead of the existing "Line chart of tracked net worth over N recorded months…" sentence, per the issue's instruction that a screen-reader user must not get the same sentence for three different charts. `you` gets its own possessive ("Your") rather than `netWorthLensLabel`'s own toggle label ("You") + "'s", which would read as "You's net worth".
- **`src/routes/+page.svelte` reads `partner` off the hydrated `appData` exactly as `growthRate` already is** — read-only, seeded once in `onMount`, no write-back `$effect` — and passes it straight through to `NetWorthChart`. Entering or editing a partner stays #170/`PartnerProfile`'s job.
- **10 new tests in `NetWorthChart.test.js`**: the toggle renders all three options defaulting to Household; the household total is unchanged from before this issue; the `you`/`partner` lenses scale the headline figure by ownership share (60%/40% of a part-owned holding); the Partner option picks up a recorded name and falls back to the generic label without one; the forecast control and its "Projected from your…" paragraph disappear outside `household`, replaced by the "only shown on the Household lens" text; and the accessible summary's opening sentence names Household/Your/`{name}`'s/Partner's correctly — 2188 → 2197.

**Trade-offs / deviations from prompt:**
- **The one-snapshot "first snapshot" caption's forecast-suggestion clause is lens-aware but otherwise unchanged.** With no plot yet and a non-`household` lens, "or switch the forecast back on and it runs from this month" was dropped from that sentence — telling a `you`/`partner` reader to switch a control that isn't available to them would be actively wrong — but the sentence is otherwise identical to the pre-#172 wording; no other copy in the component was rewritten beyond what the toggle and the forecast decision required.
- **Driven for real in a browser**, continuing the pattern from #22-#38/#98/#61-#64/#100/#111/#116/#117/#118/#127/#128/#149/#150/#157/#161/#189/#170. `npm run dev` + Playwright's Chromium (`chromium`/`chromium-browser`/`google-chrome` all already present in this environment at `/usr/bin`, so only a scratch `npm install --no-save playwright-core` in `/tmp` for the driver scripts). First session: added one month with a single £100,000 holding at 60% ownership, confirmed Household showed £100,000, You showed £60,000, Partner showed £40,000 with no partner recorded, and that switching to You/Partner replaced the forecast controls with the "only shown on the Household lens" text and dropped the "Projected from your…" paragraph — then switched back to Household and confirmed the checkbox, horizon picker and projection paragraph all reappeared exactly as before. Second session: added a partner named "Sam" on Settings, reloaded the Net Worth tab, and confirmed the toggle's third option read "Sam" rather than the generic "Partner" fallback. No console or page errors in either session; both driver scripts and their scratch `/tmp/node_modules` were removed afterward, nothing left in the working tree.

- **`build`, `check` (0 errors, same 2 pre-existing `<slot>` warnings) and `lint` all pass; `npm test` remains local-only** — still not in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (flagged since #2).

## Printable report: tax section — 2026-08-09
<!-- METRICS:printable-report-tax-section -->
- **Execution Duration:** __DURATION__ seconds
- **Model:** __MODEL__
- **Turns:** __TURNS__
- **Input Tokens:** __INPUT_TOKENS__
- **Output Tokens:** __OUTPUT_TOKENS__
- **Estimated Cost:** $__COST__ (from Claude Code's reported total_cost_usd)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable GPT-5-tier model:** $__GPT_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable Gemini-tier model:** $__GEMINI_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)

**Decisions:**
- **`reportTax(profile)` hands `profile.gross_salary`/`profile.tax_region` straight to `tax.js`'s own `takeHomeBreakdown`**, the same function `TaxCalculator.svelte` calls, rather than re-summing bands or re-deriving take-home/effective-rate figures in `report.js` — the same "never re-derive" rule #147's `reportNetWorth` already follows for `forecast.js`/`debt.js`. A printed report and the Tax tab now share the exact same arithmetic, not just the same inputs.
- **A recorded salary of `0` reads as "not recorded," not as a real income of nothing.** The data model has no separate "salary recorded" flag — `profile.gross_salary` defaults to `0` — so `reportTax` reuses the convention `TaxCalculator.svelte` already established for the same field: its own salary input falls back to a placeholder figure whenever `profile.gross_salary` is that default zero, on the reasoning that nobody's take-home pay is genuinely £0. `reportTax` returns `hasData: false, breakdown: null` in exactly that case, so the report page can print "No salary recorded yet." rather than a confident £0 tax bill.
- **The Tax section renders as its own `.report-section`, independent of the net worth section's `!report.netWorth.asOf` gate**, rather than nesting inside it. Net worth comes from `monthly_entries`; tax comes from `profile.gross_salary`/`profile.tax_region` — a household with a recorded salary but no net worth history yet (or the reverse) still gets a correct report, and the two `hasData` flags on `Report` are checked independently, matching how `buildReport` composes them.
- **The page markup is two small tables per the issue's own column list** — a `Band / Rate / Taxable amount / Tax` table built from `breakdown.bands` (mirroring `TaxCalculator.svelte`'s own band table's borders/alignment, minus the interactive slider/region-picker chrome that section doesn't need) with a `Total tax` `tfoot` row, then a second two-column table for take-home and effective rate, styled with the same `border-collapse`/`border-border`/`text-sm` classes the net worth section already uses so the two sections read as one system in print preview.
- **`buildReport`'s `Report` typedef gains a `tax: TaxReport` field alongside `netWorth`**, and `reportTax`/`buildReport`'s doc comments were updated to say pensions (#195) is what's left, replacing the old "#177 adds tax and pensions" placeholder language now that this issue has landed.
- **6 new tests**: a `reportTax` describe block (no salary recorded, an explicit `gross_salary: 0`, a real England/Wales/NI salary matching `takeHomeBreakdown` exactly, and the Scotland band ladder), plus two `buildReport` cases (a profile with a salary, and a fresh `AppData` with none) — 2197 → 2203.

**Trade-offs / deviations from prompt:**
- **No component/page-level test for `src/routes/report/+page.svelte`**, continuing the reasoning #190's entry already recorded: no route in `src/routes/` carries a test file in this repo, confirmed again against every sibling `+page.svelte` before relying on it — coverage for the page's rendering logic instead comes from `svelte-check` (0 errors) plus the browser drive below.
- **Driven for real in a browser**, continuing the pattern from #22-#38/#98/#61-#64/#100/#111/#116/#117/#118/#127/#128/#149/#150/#157/#161/#189/#170/#172. Unlike the last few entries, no Chromium was already present in this environment and `chromium-cli` wasn't available either, so this session installed real Playwright (`npm install --no-save playwright` + `npx playwright install --with-deps chromium`, a ~300MB one-time download) rather than the lighter `playwright-core`-against-an-existing-browser approach recent entries used. Seeded a fresh browser profile's `localStorage` directly with the app's own document shape (a profile with `gross_salary: 60000`, `tax_region: 'england_wales_ni'`, no monthly entries) before first load, then loaded `/report` and confirmed the Tax section's band table matched a hand-calculated breakdown (£37,700 at 20% = £7,540, £9,730 at 40% = £3,892, total tax £11,432, take-home £48,568, effective rate 19.05%) exactly, that the same figures survived `emulateMedia({ media: 'print' })`, and that a fresh profile with no salary printed "No salary recorded yet." — no console errors in either case. The scratch driver script and downloaded browser were removed/left outside the working tree afterward; `git status` was clean before committing.
- **Out of scope, per the issue's own narrowing: the pensions section (#195), and anything already covered by #147** — the `/report` route, the print stylesheet (`.no-print`/`.print-report`), the "Print / Save as PDF" button, the report header, and the net worth/investments section itself, none of which were touched here beyond the one intro-paragraph sentence updated to mention tax alongside net worth and investments.
- **`build`, `check` (0 errors, same 2 pre-existing `<slot>` warnings) and `lint` all pass; `npm test` remains local-only** — still not in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (flagged since #2).

## Scenario: mortgage rate rise — the panel, its property picker and the impact headline — 2026-08-08
<!-- METRICS:scenario-mortgage-rate-rise-the-panel-its-property-picker-and-the-impact-headline -->
- **Execution Duration:** __DURATION__ seconds
- **Model:** __MODEL__
- **Turns:** __TURNS__
- **Input Tokens:** __INPUT_TOKENS__
- **Output Tokens:** __OUTPUT_TOKENS__
- **Estimated Cost:** $__COST__ (from Claude Code's reported total_cost_usd)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable GPT-5-tier model:** $__GPT_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable Gemini-tier model:** $__GEMINI_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)

**Decisions:**
- **`MortgageRateRisePanel.svelte` takes exactly the narrowed scope: a property picker and one impact headline, nothing else.** No rate/timing/keep-term sliders and no comparison tables — those are #179's, and the whole config besides `propertyId` comes from one `normaliseMortgageRateRise({ propertyId })` call, the single patch point the issue asked this session to leave for #179 rather than inventing its own shape for that config to grow into.
- **The picker's "fall back to the first item, re-point if it disappears" behaviour reuses `PropertyTracker.svelte`'s own chart-property-picker pattern almost verbatim** (`$effect` comparing the current id against `.some(...)`, defaulting to `mortgagedProperties[0].id`) rather than inventing a second way to do the same thing in the same codebase. One thing that pattern's original use never needed and this one does: the `$derived` reading the picked property (`property`) also falls back to `mortgagedProperties[0]` directly, not only through the `$effect`. `$effect`s never run during `svelte/server`'s `render` (there is no DOM for them to react to — confirmed empirically against `PropertyTracker`'s existing picker, which shows no option selected under the same render), so without that second fallback the panel's server-rendered paint would show no property picked at all until client-side hydration caught up. Adding it also means the "off" branch's `Tick the box to see it overlaid on {name}'s mortgage` sentence is honest from the very first paint, not just after hydration.
- **Two distinct empty states, exactly as the issue asks, both naming the Property tab and neither rendering a picker with nothing in it:** "no properties recorded at all" and "properties exist but none carry a mortgage" are different facts a user would want worded differently, and `PropertyTracker.test.js`'s own convention of asserting the exact sentence is followed for both.
- **Convention 2 (no finite term ⇒ `null`, not zero), convention 4 (a rate change with no cashflow effect draws no shortfall) and convention 6 (no property/no mortgage is an honest empty answer)** are the three `$lib/mortgage-rate-rise.js`'s own module doc calls out as this panel's to render, and each gets its own guarded branch: `!impact.hasMortgage`, `!impact.occurs`, and `impact.extraInterestOverRemainingTerm === null` (worded as "no fixed term to solve for", never as £0 or 0 months) are checked before the headline paragraph, in that order, the same `{#if}`/`{:else if}` cascade shape `StressTestPanel`/`IncomeShockPanel` already use for their own honest-empty-state checks.
- **`properties` is threaded through read-only, the same hydrate-then-render pattern `retirement/+page.svelte`/`report/+page.svelte` already use for the store**, but scoped to only what this issue needs: `forecast/+page.svelte` now also reads `AppData.properties` in its existing `onMount`, without touching `monthlyEntries`'s pre-existing local-`$state` behaviour (still seeded empty, still never read from the store — that's a separate, older gap this issue wasn't asked to close) or gating the whole page behind a `ready` flag the way those two tabs do, since the rest of the page never depended on the store to begin with.
- **The unused `offsets` prop stays on the component's signature, with a targeted `eslint-disable-next-line`, rather than being dropped.** The issue is explicit that this panel takes "the same props those two \[`StressTestPanel`/`IncomeShockPanel`\] take … so all three overlays stay interchangeable to a reader" — dropping the one prop this panel doesn't currently use would break that interchangeability the moment #179 adds the comparison table that does use it.

**Trade-offs / deviations from prompt:**
- **Out of scope, per the issue's own second narrowing: the rate/timing/keep-term controls and the comparison output.** That's #179 (`mortgageRateRiseImpacts`' per-scenario table, `compareMortgageRateRise`'s offset rows, horizon bars) — this issue was narrowed to this scope specifically because the combined version hit `error_max_turns` (100) twice, and a prior attempt on `issue-158-mortgage-rate-rise-panel` (`fa4b307`) built the whole un-narrowed thing in one 503-line component without opening a PR. That branch was read as reference only; nothing from it was reused, and this session's work landed on its own new branch rather than force-pushing over it.
- **A real browser did drive this one**, unlike most recent entries in this log: `chromium` was present in this environment but no cached Playwright browser build was permission-accessible at its default path, so `playwright-core` was installed ad hoc (`npm install --no-save`, not added to `package.json`/`package-lock.json`) and pointed at the system `chromium` binary instead. That script added a real mortgaged property through the Property tab's own form, navigated to `/forecast`, ticked "Model a mortgage rate rise", and confirmed the rendered numbers by eye (payment £900 → £1,064/month, +£164/month, £59,065 more interest over the remaining 30-year term, £126,166/28% lower realistic-scenario net worth at the horizon) against `$lib/mortgage-rate-rise.test.js`'s own arithmetic, plus a full-page screenshot of the tab. `svelte-check` (0 errors, same 2 pre-existing `<slot>` warnings) and `build` were also run.
- **7 new tests in `MortgageRateRisePanel.test.js`** — 2174 → 2181. Server-rendered smoke tests only (no browser test environment for the automated suite, same constraint every other component test file in this repo documents), covering both empty states, the mortgaged-property filter, the fall-back-to-the-first-mortgaged-property default (including skipping a paid-off property to reach the next one that isn't), the unnamed-property fallback, and the overlay starting off. The impact headline's arithmetic itself is `$lib/mortgage-rate-rise.test.js`'s job, already covered there. `npm test` still isn't in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (flagged since #2), so these run locally but not on the PR; `build`, `check` (0 errors) and `lint` remain the CI gates and all three pass.

**Decisions:**
- **This issue's own dependency, #158's panel, hadn't landed on `main` yet** (its PR, #185, was still open) — every other stacked engine/panel or panel/controls split this pipeline has hit so far had its prerequisite already merged. Rather than opening a stacked PR against #158's branch (no precedent for that in this repo's history — every merged PR here targets `main`), this session rebased `origin/issue-158-mortgage-rate-rise-panel-v2` onto current `main` in a scratch branch, resolved the two conflicts that produced (`ForecastProjections.svelte` and `forecast/+page.svelte` both gained a third sibling overlay independently — #161's `OneOffCostsPanel` on `main`, #158's `MortgageRateRisePanel` on the branch — and `docs/journal.md`'s own append point had moved), and built this issue's controls on top of the result. This PR therefore carries both #158's and #184's commits and its description credits both; #158's own PR #185 should be closed without merging once this one lands, to avoid landing the panel twice.
- **The three dials patch straight into the single `normaliseMortgageRateRise({ ... })` call #158 already left as the seam**, exactly as instructed: `newRatePct`/`atMonth`/`keepTerm` join `propertyId` in one object literal, with no second config-shaping path introduced alongside it.
- **The rate control matches `IncomeShockPanel`'s `dropPct` idiom (paired slider/number, whole-percent step) rather than `ForecastProjections`' growth-rate slider (0.1-step decimal)**, per `mortgage-rate-rise.js`'s own module doc calling `newRatePct` "a whole-number percent, as everywhere else in `$lib`" — even though `Property.interest_rate` itself accepts two decimal places on the Property tab's own form. The drag range (0–15%) is an invented UI convenience, commented as such, same as every sibling slider; `normaliseMortgageRateRise` still clamps the paired number field to the full 0–100 the engine accepts.
- **Timing reuses `IncomeShockPanel`'s exact years→months conversion** (`Math.max(1, Math.round(parse(startYears, 1) * 12))`) rather than inventing a second rounding rule — the issue asked for this explicitly, and the panel's `atMonth`-out prose (`formatDuration`) already existed to render whatever month number the config now computes instead of always reading `12`.
- **`keepTerm` is two radio buttons in a `role="radiogroup"`, not a checkbox**, following `NetWorthChart`'s lens-toggle pattern (a `radiogroup` of labelled `input[type=radio]`s bound with `bind:group`) rather than `StressTestPanel`/`IncomeShockPanel`'s checkboxes, because this dial is a mutually-exclusive choice between two named options, not an on/off flag. Both labels state the trade-off directly — "Keep the term — the payment rises to match" / "Keep the payment — the term runs longer instead" — per the issue's instruction not to let the `false` option read as a way to dodge a cost; the extra-interest sentence right below the headline is what actually prices that trade-off in pounds, since #134's convention 4 means `keepTerm: false` never shows up as a shortfall on its own.
- **Every dial gained a matching `initialXxx` prop** (`initialEnabled`, `initialNewRatePct`, `initialStartYears`, `initialKeepTerm`) that seeds the local `$state` once, the same fix `NetWorthChart`'s `initialLens` used for the identical problem: `svelte/server`'s `render` has no pointer to drag a slider or tick a radio with, so without a prop to seed a non-default value, no test could ever reach the headline these dials feed. `enabled` itself needed the same treatment — it had no prop at all before this issue — since every test that exercises the new controls first has to get past the "off" state.
- **The always-visible intro paragraph and the "tick the box" off-state sentence were rewritten**, since both used to name the engine's fixed defaults (`DEFAULT_MORTGAGE_RATE_RISE.newRatePct`, `.atMonth`) directly — now that those figures are adjustable, the off-state sentence reads the *current* `config` instead ("Tick the box to see 6% land 1 year from now on…, keeping the term"), so it stays honest as soon as a dial is moved, even before the checkbox is ticked.

**Trade-offs / deviations from prompt:**
- **Out of scope, per the issue's own narrowing: the comparison output.** That's #197 (`mortgageRateRiseImpacts`' per-scenario table, `compareMortgageRateRise`'s offset rows, the two horizon bars, and the prose that keeps a `keepTerm: false` config from reading as a free choice) — nothing here beyond the two radio labels' own wording touches that framing.
- **Driven for real in a browser**, continuing the pattern from #22-#38/#98/#61-#64/#100/#111/#116/#117/#118/#127/#128/#149/#150/#157/#161/#189/#170/#172/#196. `chromium` was present in this environment, so `playwright-core` was installed ad hoc (`npm install --no-save`, in `/tmp`, not added to `package.json`/`package-lock.json`) and pointed at the system binary. Added a real mortgaged property (£180,000 balance, £900/month, 4.5%) through the Property tab's own form, opened `/forecast`, ticked "Model a mortgage rate rise", and confirmed the default-dial headline matched #158's own recorded numbers exactly (£900 → £1,064/month, £59,065 extra interest, £126,166/28% lower net worth); then moved the rate slider to 9% and the timing field to 2 years and confirmed the date shifted to Aug 2028 and every figure recomputed live (£900 → £1,415/month, £179,074 extra interest, £360,335/80% lower); then switched to "Keep the payment" and confirmed the payment held at £900 while the mortgage correctly fell into the no-finite-term case (£900/month can no longer clear the interest on £180,000 at 9%) and rendered "no fixed term to solve for" rather than a wrong number. No console or page errors throughout. The scratch driver script and its `/tmp/node_modules` were removed afterward; `git status` was clean before committing.
- **12 tests in `MortgageRateRisePanel.test.js`** — 2210 → 2215 (5 new, 2 of #158's existing tests updated for the off-state sentence's new wording). Covers a changed rate reaching the headline instead of the engine default, the years→months rounding flooring at 1 month rather than landing on the anchor, an emptied rate/timing pair falling back to the engine defaults rather than rendering `NaN`, and both `keepTerm` branches' effect on the payment sentence — exactly the issue's own minimum list. `npm test` still isn't in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (flagged since #2), so these run locally but not on the PR; `build`, `check` (0 errors, same 2 pre-existing `<slot>` warnings) and `lint` all pass.
