## Monte Carlo retirement simulator: simulation engine — 2026-08-08
<!-- METRICS:monte-carlo-retirement-simulator-simulation-engine -->
- **Execution Duration:** __DURATION__ seconds
- **Model:** __MODEL__
- **Turns:** __TURNS__
- **Input Tokens:** __INPUT_TOKENS__
- **Output Tokens:** __OUTPUT_TOKENS__
- **Estimated Cost:** $__COST__ (from Claude Code's reported total_cost_usd)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable GPT-5-tier model:** $__GPT_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable Gemini-tier model:** $__GEMINI_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)

**Decisions:**
- **The log-normal parameterisation is exact moment matching, in closed form, both ways round — this was the single most correctness-critical decision in the issue and it is the one the module's header spends most of its words on.** A caller states an annual return and an annual volatility (the standard deviation of *simple* annual returns, which is how an assumption is actually quoted — "7% and 15%"), and `logNormalParameters` inverts `E[1+R] = exp(m + s²/2)` and `Var[1+R] = exp(2m + s²)(exp(s²) − 1)` to get the log-space `m` and `s`. With `L` the stated return level and `k = σ²/L²`: under the arithmetic basis `s² = ln(1 + k)` and `m = ln(L) − s²/2`; under the compound basis `m = ln(L)` and `s² = ln(u)` where `u` is the positive root of `u² − u = k`, i.e. `u = (1 + √(1+4k))/2`. Both are exact — no `s ≈ volatility` approximation, which is the usual shortcut and is wrong by about 6% of the volatility at equity-like numbers. `LogNormalParameters.impliedVolatility` is computed back *out* of `m` and `s` rather than copied from the input specifically so a test can hold the round trip: `expect(params.impliedVolatility).toBeCloseTo(15, 8)`.
- **`returnBasis` is an explicit choice with `'compound'` as the default, because "5% growth" means two different things and silently picking one would put a wrong number on screen.** `Profile.growth_rate` is what `forecast.js` and `fire.js` compound deterministically, so under the compound basis the *median* simulated path is exactly the line those tabs already draw at the same rate — the Retirement tab and the Forecast tab cannot disagree about the same assumption, which is the property this codebase optimises for everywhere else. The mean path then sits above it by the volatility drag `exp(s²/2)` (at 5%/15%: median 5%, mean 6.045%). A caller whose figure is a published arithmetic expected return passes `'arithmetic'` and gets a median *below* it by the same drag. Both readings are defensible; leaving it implicit is not.
- **Monthly draws scale as `m/12` and `s/√12`.** Twelve independent monthly log returns sum to `Normal(m, s²)`, so twelve monthly draws compound to exactly the annual distribution specified. The mistake this guards against is the direct analogue of `auto-invest.js`'s convention (1) — dividing the *volatility* by twelve rather than by `√12` understates a year's dispersion by a factor of ≈3.5 and turns a Monte Carlo into a nearly deterministic projection dressed up as one. There is a test asserting `12·monthlySigma² === sigma²` and a second asserting the ratio to the wrong scaling is `√12`, so the correct form is pinned from both directions, plus an empirical test that draws 40,000 simulated years and recovers the stated 6% mean and 18% volatility from the sample.
- **Sequence-of-returns risk is not a feature bolted on; it falls out of walking each path month by month and is then pinned by a test that can only pass if the order matters.** `monthlyReturnFactors` is exported separately from the walk precisely so a test can hold one path's returns still and vary only their order: reversing the array leaves the multiset and the average untouched, and the test asserts the terminal value moves by less than £1 with no cash flows (multiplication commutes) and by more than £50,000 once £25,000 a year is being drawn. A third test shows the consequence in the headline figure — at an unchanged 5% *median* return, success falls monotonically as volatility goes 2% → 15% → 30%.
- **A Monte Carlo month is literally `forecast.js`'s `factor` month, and `forecastAdjustmentFromFactors` makes that claim testable rather than asserted.** The walk is `roundMoney(value × factor + cashflow)` — growth credited first, cash flow at the month end, rounded to pence, the rounded value carried forward — which is character for character what `ForecastMonthAdjustment`'s `factor` branch does (the branch `stress-test.js` already builds a crash out of). The exported hook turns a drawn path into `projectScenario`'s own `adjustMonth`, and a test drives ten years of accumulation through both and asserts equality **to the penny at every year**, on a real `createInvestment` holding with its own contribution frequency. That is the issue's "reuse `forecast.js`'s existing position/contribution logic" honoured as a proven equivalence rather than a copied comment; the walk itself is a tight numeric loop rather than a call into `projectScenario`, because 5,000 paths × 660 months of `ForecastPoint` objects would be tens of millions of allocations for a series nobody reads.
- **Two pots, because tax needs at least two.** A DC pension pot (withdrawals 25% tax-free / 75% earned income, `retirement-income.js`'s convention 5) and a tax-free pot (ISAs plus a LISA drawn after 60). Both ride the *same* monthly return — one market, one draw — since a per-pot volatility would be an asset-allocation model the data model carries nothing for. The pots' *values* are read with `retirement-income.js`'s convention (2) exactly (`definedContributionPots`, `isaPot`), so a SIPP recorded both as a pension pot and as a snapshot holding is counted once and this module cannot disagree with the retirement income card about what is there.
- **A target income is a *net* income, so the engine solves the gross-up rather than drawing the target.** `retirement-income.js`'s convention 7 — you live on what arrives — so each retirement year works out the gross withdrawal whose after-tax value covers whatever the promised streams did not. `net(g) = g − (tax(other + τg) − tax(other))` is piecewise linear and strictly increasing (its slope never falls below ≈0.33, Scotland's 67.5% taper band being the steepest UK marginal rate), so `grossWithdrawalForNet` inverts it with Newton's method and falls back to 48-step bisection on the case where a step straddles a band boundary and oscillates — a solver that *usually* converges is not a solver. It is tested to a penny across 64 combinations of need/other-income/tax-free-share/region, including straight through the £100k–£125,140 60% band. The extra tax charged is the tax the withdrawal *causes*, not the tax on it in isolation, which is the whole reason the personal allowance is worth planning around: £30,000 net out of a pension pot with no other income costs £32,336.47 gross.
- **Tax is assessed annually and the year's plan is then executed monthly, which is both what HMRC does and what keeps 5,000 paths affordable.** A per-month gross-up would be hundreds of millions of band-ladder walks; a per-year one is at most two solves per retirement year, and the year's requirement is deterministic (it depends on the assumptions and the calendar, never on the market), so `prepareSimulation` computes the whole schedule once and all 5,000 paths share it. Two mechanisms then stop annual planning from inventing failures the plan did not have: a month whose pot cannot pay its instalment **carries the rest forward** into later months of the year, and whatever is still missing at the year end is **trued up out of whatever the other pots still hold**, grossed up against the income already taxed that year. That second one was a genuine bug found by a test, not a precaution — before it, the year a pension pot emptied was scored as a failure even with an untouched ISA beside it, which showed up as a plan with £17m spare "failing" by £2.04.
- **Promised income arrives first, each stream carrying its own tax treatment and its own start age.** `MonteCarloIncomeStream` is a small general shape (`annualIncome`, `startAge`, `endAge`, `earned_income | tax_free`) that the State Pension and Defined Benefit builders both reduce to, so `state-pension.js` keeps owning the 35ths rule, the NI record and State Pension age (the stream starts at *its own* age, 68 for anyone born after April 1977, not at the retirement age), and `defined-benefit.js` keeps owning accrual. Streams are only counted in retirement years — a stream arriving alongside a salary is spending money, not retirement funding — and a test pins that a State Pension reached at 67 while still working to 70 contributes nothing until 70.
- **Failure is "a year the plan could not be funded", not "the pot hit zero", and each path records the *first* age it fell short.** A pot can empty at 94 having funded everything asked of it (there is a test for exactly that case: `success: true`, `depletedAge: 94`), and a pot with £5,000 left that funded £3,000 of a £30,000 year has failed — so `depletedAge` and `firstShortfallAge` are separate fields. Recording the first shortfall age per path is what makes `survival` a whole curve rather than one number, so `probabilityOfLastingTo(summary, 85)` is a lookup rather than a second 5,000-path run, and it agrees with the headline figure at the target age by construction.
- **`SHORTFALL_TOLERANCE = 1` exists because a £1 shortfall must not be allowed to decide the headline probability.** Splitting an annual plan into twelve instalments over pots rounded to whole pence each month can land an exactly-funded year a few pence under its target for arithmetic reasons alone. A pound is comfortably above that noise and far below anything a retiree would notice.
- **Seeded and deterministic: mulberry32 (32 bits of state, four integer ops, no dependency) feeding Box–Muller with the spare draw cached.** A slider that moved by nothing must not move the probability, and a test needs a fixed answer to hold. The first uniform is taken as `1 − u` so it lands in `(0, 1]` — `log(0)` is `−Infinity` and one zero draw would poison a whole path. `simulateRetirement` also accepts an injected `normalSource`, which is how "all zeros walks every path down the median" becomes a test.
- **`taxOnEarnedIncome` and `marginalEarnedRate` are deliberate allocation-free mirrors of `tax.js`'s `incomeTax`/`marginalTaxRate`, exported specifically so a parity test can hold them to it.** `sliceIntoBands` allocates an object per band per call and this engine asks the question millions of times per run. The mirrors are the same arithmetic in the same order, rounding included (each band's tax to pence, then the total), and are tested against `tax.js` itself on both the boundary incomes and a 1,460-point fine sweep of 0–£200,000 in both regions — so the two cannot silently drift apart.
- **Cross-validated against `fire.js` rather than only against itself.** With no volatility, no tax and no streams, this engine is answering the exact question `fire.js`'s drawdown answers, so a test asserts they agree: a £500,000 pot drawing £30,000 at 4% first falls short in year 27, and `portfolioRunway` independently reports 27.08 years. Two separate arithmetic paths landing on the same year is worth more than any number of internal consistency checks.
- **Performance was treated as a correctness constraint, since #132 will move sliders.** A full 5,000-path, 55-year run (2.75m monthly draws, ~275k gross-up solves) completes in roughly 0.5s, and the annual pot series are collected into one `Float64Array` per year so a percentile is a sort of contiguous numbers rather than a walk over 5,000 objects. There is a test that runs README.md's own 5,000 paths rather than only the smaller counts the rest of the suite uses.

**Trade-offs / deviations from prompt:**
- **Engine only, no UI — as the issue asks.** No route, component or chart was touched; `simulateRetirement` returns a `band` of `years + 1` percentile points (p5/p10/p25/p50/p75/p90/p95, mean, min, max, depleted share), a `survival` curve, a `terminal` distribution and a handful of complete `samplePaths`, all shaped for the fan chart #132 builds. `MonteCarloBandPoint`'s own doc comment spells out that a percentile band is **not** a path — the pot that is 5th-percentile at 70 is a different simulation from the one that is 5th-percentile at 90 — which is why the sample paths are returned alongside it.
- **Returns are independent month to month: no mean reversion, no volatility clustering, no fat tails beyond the log-normal's, and no market/inflation correlation.** A real 2008 is worse than anything drawn here. The fix that keeps those features is a block bootstrap of historical returns, which needs a return history this app does not carry and cannot derive from the data model; the module's header says so explicitly rather than leaving the reader to assume otherwise.
- **Inflation is deterministic, and the moment matching is therefore done in nominal terms first and deflated afterwards.** Deflating by a constant scales every simple return by the same factor, so it shifts the drift by `−ln(1+i)` and leaves the log volatility exactly where the caller put it — doing it in this order is what keeps `sigma` the number that was stated, and there is a test for both halves of that.
- **`withdrawalOrder` defaults to `pension_first` and the three orders are close to a wash, which the test says out loud rather than pretending otherwise.** My first attempt asserted `isa_first` beats `pension_first`; it does not — spending the pension early pays its tax early and leaves the ISA compounding, spending the ISA early defers the tax and leaves the pension compounding, and at 2,000 paths the two came out at exactly the same probability. The test was rewritten to assert what *is* a real effect (a £600,000 pension pot is measurably less likely to last than a £600,000 ISA, because every pound drawn from it is grossed up for tax) and, separately, that all three orders are usable. The default is justified on this app's own terms — the tax-free quarter is worth using against a personal allowance, and from April 2027 an unused pension pot counts towards the estate for IHT while an ISA already does — and flagged as a default, not advice.
- **Pension contributions read off `contribution_pct + employer_pct × gross_salary` with basic-rate relief *not* added on top.** `pension-relief.js` owns that question, the answer depends on whether a scheme is relief-at-source or net-pay/salary-sacrifice, and quietly grossing a salary-sacrifice contribution up by 25% would overstate the pot for the rest of a 55-year run. The Annual Allowance is likewise unmodelled.
- **Ages are whole years.** A stream starting at State Pension age begins with the year in which that age is reached, and the retirement boundary falls on a year edge. Sub-annual precision would need a birth *date* (`Profile` stores a birth month) and would not change any probability by a meaningful amount.
- **Also deliberately unmodelled, and listed in the module header:** asset allocation, glide paths and rebalancing; annuitising part-way through; the 55/57 minimum pension age and a DB scheme's own normal pension age; dividend and CGT on an unwrapped pot (which is why an unwrapped holding is not one of the two pots); the triple lock and any other uprating; care costs; and death before the target age — the one omission that makes every probability here *pessimistic*.
- **No browser drive**, breaking the pattern from #22–#38/#98/#61–#64/#100/#111/#116–#118/#127–#129 for the first time in a while, because there is nothing to drive: this issue adds one pure module and its tests, with no route, component or user-visible surface. #132 is where the fan chart and the controls get driven for real.
- **97 new tests in `monte-carlo.test.js`** (1779 total, up from 1682): the generator's determinism/uniformity/moments, the log-normal parameterisation both ways round plus a 40,000-year empirical recovery of its moments, the monthly scaling from both directions, penny-exact parity with `projectScenario` over ten years, sequence-of-returns risk in three forms, penny-exact parity with `tax.js` over a fine sweep in both regions, the gross-up solver across 64 need/income/share/region combinations, per-year scheduling and withdrawal planning including pot exhaustion and both empty pots, path-level success/failure/depletion semantics, the cross-check against `fire.js`'s runway, and the whole summary's shape (ordered percentiles, a monotonically falling survival curve, `probabilityOfLastingTo` agreeing with the headline at the target age, a zero-year run, and README.md's full 5,000 paths). `npm test` still isn't in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (flagged since #2), so these run locally but not on the PR; `build`/`check`/`lint` remain the CI gates and all three pass.

## Monte Carlo retirement simulator: panel, seeded run and probability-of-success headline — 2026-08-08
<!-- METRICS:monte-carlo-retirement-simulator-panel-seeded-run-and-probability-of-success-headline -->
- **Execution Duration:** __DURATION__ seconds
- **Model:** __MODEL__
- **Turns:** __TURNS__
- **Input Tokens:** __INPUT_TOKENS__
- **Output Tokens:** __OUTPUT_TOKENS__
- **Estimated Cost:** $__COST__ (from Claude Code's reported total_cost_usd)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable GPT-5-tier model:** $__GPT_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable Gemini-tier model:** $__GEMINI_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)

**Decisions:**
- **Branched off #131's still-open `monte-carlo-simulation-engine-131` rather than off `main`, since `src/lib/monte-carlo.js` (`simulateRetirement`, `monteCarloInputFromAppData`, `normaliseMonteCarloInput`) this issue is scoped to drive doesn't exist on `main` yet — the engine PR (#152) is still `status:in-review`.** Merged that branch into this one first (`git merge`), which brought in `monte-carlo.js`/`monte-carlo.test.js` cleanly against the two files `main` had moved on since the engine branched (`index.js`'s new export line, `docs/journal.md`'s CSV import entry) — only `journal.md` conflicted, on the append point, resolved by keeping both entries in the order they actually land in `main` (CSV import, then the engine) rather than the order the two branches were authored in. The result is that this PR's diff carries the engine's full commit as well as the panel; the alternative (waiting on #152 to merge first) would have blocked this issue on a PR review outside its control.
- **`monteCarloReadiness(profile, input)` is a small pure function in the component's `<script module>` block, gating three states before `simulateRetirement()` ever runs.** The issue is explicit that the engine "never throws" and would happily simulate against `monteCarloInputFromAppData()`'s own age-40 fallback (no date of birth recorded) or an all-zero position (no pension pot, no ISA holding, no contribution, no promised income stream) — both are the engine doing its job with the facts it was handed, not a plan anyone actually described, so printing a probability off either would misrepresent whose retirement it is. `no_dob` is checked first and wins outright (a date of birth is needed to even count ages), `no_data` requires a date of birth to be on record but nothing to fund a plan from, and `ready` is the only state that reaches `simulateRetirement`. Unit-tested directly against `normaliseMonteCarloInput()`'s own `MonteCarloInput` shape rather than only through a rendered component, since this is the one piece of logic in the panel that is genuinely correctness-critical.
- **The headline and nothing else, as scoped**: `successPercent`/`successProbability` as a big number, `standardError` (as ± percentage points) and `paths` underneath it, and a `guaranteed` callout that reframes a promised-income-only 100% as "not a statement about the market" — matching the issue's own wording for why that flag exists. `summary.shortfall`/`summary.terminal` (#154) and the fan chart (#153) are computed by `simulateRetirement()` regardless (the engine doesn't have a cheaper mode) but nothing here reads them; `summary` itself is already the seam those two issues need, so there was nothing further to shape for them.
- **No debounce, as the issue says to skip**: the whole panel is `$derived` off `profile`/`pensions`/`monthlyEntries` props, which only change on hydration or when another tab writes to the store — there is no keystroke to debounce until #155 adds sliders. A full 5,000-path run costs roughly the ~0.5s #131's own journal entry measured, which is fine for a store-driven recompute and would not be for a rerun on every drag.
- **`src/routes/retirement/+page.svelte` now also hydrates `pensions`** (previously only `monthlyEntries`/`profile`, since `FireCalculator` needs neither) and renders `MonteCarloSimulator` read-only beneath `FireCalculator`, passing all three straight through — the same read-only, not-written-back seeding every other cross-tab card in this app already follows.
- **Driven for real in a browser**, continuing the pattern from #22-#38/#98/#61-#64/#100/#111/#116-#118/#127-#129/#149 (broken only by #131, which had no UI to drive). `npm run build` + `vite preview`, headless system Chromium over the DevTools Protocol (`playwright` installed with `npm install --no-save` in a scratch `/tmp` directory). Confirmed all three states: a fresh document shows the no-date-of-birth message and no headline; a document with a date of birth but no pension pot, ISA holding or stream shows the no-data message and no headline; a document with a £250,000 DC pension pot, a £40,000 target and default assumptions renders "Probability of success 13.3%, ±0.5 percentage points, across 5,000 paths" plus "663 paths funded every retirement year in full and 4,337 paths fell short" — consistent with `FireCalculator` right above it independently reporting the same pot exhausted by age 73, 22 years short of the target age of 95. No console or page errors in any of the three states.
- **The seeding for the second and third browser states went straight into IndexedDB**, not through the UI, because of a pre-existing gap the drive surfaced: `ForecastProjections.svelte`'s birth-year/birth-month fields (`#forecast-dob-year`/`#forecast-dob-month`) build a page-local `markerProfile` for its own milestone markers and are never written back to `appData` — so `profile.dob_year` currently has **no** UI path to get persisted anywhere in the app, despite `StatePensionProjection.svelte` and this issue's own no-dob message both telling the user to "add your date of birth on the forecast tab". This panel's wording matches that existing (if currently non-functional) convention rather than inventing a different one, and is not a regression this issue introduces — `CLAUDE.md`/`DESIGN.md` both already note there is no profile edit form yet. Flagged here since it blocks a real end-to-end drive of the no-dob → ready transition through the UI; filing a follow-up is outside this issue's scope.

**Trade-offs / deviations from prompt:**
- **This PR's diff includes #131's `monte-carlo.js`/`monte-carlo.test.js`/its own journal entry, not only the panel**, because #131 hadn't merged to `main` yet and this issue cannot exist without it — see the branching decision above. Nothing in the engine itself was changed; the merge is a straight `git merge` of the existing commit.
- **No controls, per the issue's explicit scope** — `DEFAULT_TARGET_AGE` (95) and `DEFAULT_VOLATILITY` (15%) are used as-is via `normaliseMonteCarloInput()`'s own fallbacks, and `summary.shortfall`/`summary.terminal`/the fan chart are left unrendered for #154/#153 to build on top of the same `summary` object.
- **12 new tests in `MonteCarloSimulator.test.js`** (1829 total, up from 1817 once #131's 1779 landed alongside this issue's own — see the branching note): six direct unit tests of `monteCarloReadiness` against hand-built `MonteCarloInput`s (no date of birth wins regardless of a huge pot, a pot alone/an ISA alone/a contribution alone/a stream alone each independently reach `ready`, and a missing `profile` behaves like a missing date of birth), plus five `svelte/server` render smoke tests covering the intro's own default figures, both empty states, a real simulated run, and the `guaranteed` branch (forced via a £0 target, which needs no funding in any retirement year the same way a fully promised income would). `npm test` still isn't in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (flagged since #2), so these run locally but not on the PR; `build`/`check`/`lint` remain the CI gates and all three pass.

- **Ballpark cost, comparable Gemini-tier model:** $__GEMINI_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)

**Decisions:**
- **This issue's own deliverables were already on `main` before this session started, and this session's first job was confirming that rather than assuming it.** PR #168 (this issue's original attempt) was closed unmerged because its diff predates the Partner feature (#142) and deletes `Partner`/`partner` wholesale — but per the "Inheritance Tax section" entry above (2026-08-09, issue #187/PR #206), a later session ported #168's content by hand instead of re-deriving it: `estate-plan.js`/`estate-plan.test.js` copied verbatim, and the `Beneficiary`/`IhtSettings`/`AppData.gifts`/`AppData.beneficiaries`/`AppData.iht_settings` additions to `types.js`/`model.js` applied as pure additions alongside `partner`. That port never got this issue's own journal entry or a PR that closes #140 — #187 closed #187, not #140 — which is what this session supplies.
- **Verified line-by-line against this issue's own spec rather than trusted on sight.** `types.js`'s `Beneficiary` (`id`/`name`/`relationship`/`share_pct`/`notes`) and `IhtSettings` (`spouse_exempt`/`direct_descendants`/`transferred_nil_rate_band_pct`/`transferred_residence_nil_rate_band_pct`/`funeral_expenses`) match the issue's field list exactly; `model.js` has `createBeneficiary`/`createIhtSettings`, `AppData.gifts`/`beneficiaries`/`iht_settings` wired into `normaliseAppData` (structural-only gift normalisation, deferring to `lifetime-gifts.js`'s own `normaliseGift` as the issue specifies) and `validateAppData` (unique ids, `share_pct` and both transferred percentages clamped 0–100, non-negative money). `estate-plan.js` exports exactly `estateSnapshot()` (via `forecast.js`'s `positionFromEntries()` for the latest investments/debts, `budget-policy.js`'s `estateValuation()` at the default ungrown year, folded through `lifetime-gifts.js`'s `inheritanceTaxWithGifts()`, restating `netAfterTax`/`totalTax` at the top level) and `beneficiaryShares()` (priced against a net estate, never rescaled, reporting `totalSharePct`/`allocatedAmount`/`unallocatedPct`/`unallocatedAmount`/`overAllocated`) — both re-normalising defensively, both exported through `index.js`. README.md's Data Model outline already carries the `gifts[]`/`beneficiaries[]`/`iht_settings` block #187 added it in.
- **No code change followed from that verification.** `npm test` (76 files, 2354 tests, including 25 in `estate-plan.test.js` and 88 in `model.test.js`) and `npm run lint`/`npm run check` (0 errors, the same 2 pre-existing `<slot>` warnings) all pass unmodified — re-deriving or restructuring working, tested code that already matches the spec would be exactly the wasted-work pattern this milestone's own issue bodies (e.g. #187's "why this kept failing" section) warn against.

**Trade-offs / deviations from prompt:**
- **This PR's diff is documentation-only.** The engine and data model this issue asks for exist and pass their tests; what was missing was the paper trail — a journal entry under this issue's own name, and a PR whose merge gives GitHub's issue tracker a "Closes #140" it never had, since the code landed as a side effect of #187 rather than through #168 or a #140-specific PR.
- **PR #168 stays closed rather than reopened.** Its content is superseded (it still deletes `Partner`) but its functional intent is fully satisfied by what already ported into `main`; reopening or rewriting it would just recreate the Partner conflict #187's session avoided.
- **No UI, no browser drive, per the issue's own "Out of scope" section** — the Estate tab, the "if I died today" panel and the beneficiary list/form belong to #166/#167 (both since landed, per the two entries above), and this issue's scope was always the engine and data model alone.

## Estate & IHT: the beneficiary list and its entry form — 2026-08-09
<!-- METRICS:estate-iht-the-beneficiary-list-and-its-entry-form -->
- **Execution Duration:** __DURATION__ seconds
- **Model:** __MODEL__
- **Turns:** __TURNS__
- **Input Tokens:** __INPUT_TOKENS__
- **Output Tokens:** __OUTPUT_TOKENS__
- **Estimated Cost:** $__COST__ (from Claude Code's reported total_cost_usd)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable GPT-5-tier model:** $__GPT_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable Gemini-tier model:** $__GEMINI_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)

**Decisions:**
- **This issue's first attempt (PR #192, `estate-iht-beneficiary-list-167`) is closed unmerged, and this session redoes the work fresh against current `main` rather than rebasing it.** That branch was built before #140's real `Beneficiary`/`createBeneficiary()` had landed, so it hand-rolled its own minimal `types.js`/`model.js` slice alongside the component — the same "build ahead of a stale dependency" pattern #168 hit. #140 has since landed for real (this milestone's own 2026-08-09 journal entry above confirms it, verified line-by-line against the issue), so `Beneficiary`, `createBeneficiary()` and `AppData.beneficiaries` already exist on `main` with normalisation and validation in place — there is nothing left for this issue to add to the data model, only the list and its form.
- **`BeneficiaryBreakdown.svelte` follows `AssetsTracker.svelte`'s shape closely**: a flat list on `AppData`, seeded from `createBeneficiary()`, add/edit/remove against local form state, no activity log (beneficiaries don't have one any more than physical assets do). Each row shows name, relationship (falling back to "Relationship not recorded" rather than a blank when unset), the wished share as a plain `N%`, and notes when present — no £ figure anywhere, since pricing a share against the net estate is #191's `netAfterTax` seam, out of scope here. Nothing rescales or rejects a list that doesn't sum to 100: the form and the row both save and show whatever was typed, per the issue's explicit "a half-drafted will is the normal case" instruction.
- **Wired into `src/routes/estate/+page.svelte` as this tab's first write.** `monthlyEntries`/`properties`/`assets`/`pensions`/`gifts`/`ihtSettings` stay read-only exactly as `EstateSummary` already consumed them; `beneficiaries` is hydrated alongside them and written back through the same `ready`-gated `$effect` every sibling route's own list uses, so a pre-hydrate `[]` can't race the real stored value. Mounted directly beneath `EstateSummary`, inside the existing `hasEstateData` guard, matching the closed PR's placement and the issue's own wiring instructions.
- **Driven for real in a browser**, continuing the pattern from #22-#38/#98/#61-#64/#100/#111/#116-#118/#127-#129/#149/#155. `npm run build` + `vite preview`, headless Chromium via Playwright (installed with `npm install --no-save` in a scratch `/tmp` directory, no `chromium-cli` available in this environment). Seeded a £5,000 physical asset to unlock the Estate tab's empty-state guard, then added a beneficiary ("Jess", "Daughter", 60%, "Eldest child") through the form — confirmed the row renders with no `£` anywhere on the page, edited the share to 75% and confirmed the row updates in place, reloaded the page and confirmed both the estate valuation and the beneficiary survive (round-tripping through IndexedDB, waiting out the store's debounced save before each navigation), then removed the beneficiary and confirmed the empty-state invitation returns. No console or page errors at any step.

**Trade-offs / deviations from prompt:**
- **No activity log**, matching `AssetsTracker`'s own precedent rather than `DebtTracker`'s — beneficiaries aren't in `ACTIVITY_LOG_ENTITY_TYPES` and the issue doesn't ask for one.
- **9 new tests in `BeneficiaryBreakdown.test.js`** (2363 total, up from 2354): the empty-state invitation, the form's four fields, a fully-populated row (name/relationship/share/notes), no `£` anywhere on a row, the "Relationship not recorded" fallback, a two-beneficiary list summing to 160% saved and shown unadjusted (and not flagged as over-allocated), singular/plural beneficiary count, the "Add beneficiary"/"Save changes" button label, and the notes line's absence when unset. `npm run lint`/`npm run check`/`npm run build` all pass; `npm test` still isn't in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (flagged since #2), so these run locally but not on the PR.

## Estate & IHT: who-gets-what — priced shares and the under/over-allocation warnings — 2026-08-09
<!-- METRICS:estate-iht-who-gets-what-priced-shares-and-the-under-over-allocation-warnings -->
- **Execution Duration:** __DURATION__ seconds
- **Model:** __MODEL__
- **Turns:** __TURNS__
- **Input Tokens:** __INPUT_TOKENS__
- **Output Tokens:** __OUTPUT_TOKENS__
- **Estimated Cost:** $__COST__ (from Claude Code's reported total_cost_usd)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable GPT-5-tier model:** $__GPT_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable Gemini-tier model:** $__GEMINI_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)

**Decisions:**
- **This issue's own dependency, #202 ("the net-after-tax figure and its `$bindable` prop seam"), hadn't landed** — it's still `OPEN` on the tracker, one of six further narrowings of #187 (#199–#205) that sit ahead of #191 in the milestone but hadn't been picked yet. Rather than block, this session applied the same order-independent instinct #187's own issue text spells out for exactly this situation: built the minimal piece of #202 this issue actually needs — `netAfterTax` as a `$bindable` prop on `EstateSummary.svelte`, defaulted to `0` and kept in sync with the derived `estateSnapshot()` via an `$effect` (the same "write-out-only" pattern `AssetsTracker.svelte`'s picker-repointing effect already uses) — plus a plain "What's left after tax" line so the figure has somewhere to render. The fuller #202 scope (rendering it under both of #187's charge branches, the no-tax-owed wording) and #199/#200/#201/#203/#204/#205's own sections are untouched and still open; this is only the seam, not those issues' UI.
- **`BeneficiaryBreakdown.svelte` takes `netAfterTax` as a plain (non-bindable) prop from the page**, per the issue's own instruction not to run a second `estateSnapshot()`. Left `undefined` by default rather than defaulting to `0`, so "no estate to value" (an existing test, or the component mounted standalone) and "a real net estate of exactly £0" (funeral costs or IHT consuming the whole estate) read as two different things — only the latter renders a `£0` row and the allocation warning below it. On the wired page, `hasEstateData`'s existing empty-state guard means this distinction is mostly academic in practice (the page never mounts either card until there's something to value), but it keeps the component honest when used or tested on its own.
- **`beneficiaryShares()` (already built by #140) does 100% of the arithmetic** — each row gets `sharesResult.shares`' `amount` alongside the `share_pct` #167 already shows (looked up by id into a `Map`, so the existing `#each beneficiaries as beneficiary` loop and its Edit/Remove handlers didn't need restructuring), and the allocation line below the list reads `totalSharePct`/`unallocatedPct`/`unallocatedAmount`/`overAllocated` straight off the result rather than recomputing any of it. Under 100% gets a neutral "isn't promised to anyone yet" line; over 100% gets an amber "Over-allocated" one; exactly 100% gets neither — none of it touches `beneficiaries` or rescales a share, matching convention 4's "never rescales to fit."
- **`eslint-disable-next-line no-useless-assignment`, one line, on `EstateSummary.svelte`'s `netAfterTax = $bindable(0)`** — the rule can't see that a `$bindable` prop is read by a *parent's* `bind:`, only that this component itself never reads it back, and flagged it as dead. No existing component in this codebase writes a derived value out through a bindable prop it never reads (the other `$bindable` props here are all two-way user input), so there was no precedent to follow instead.

**Trade-offs / deviations from prompt:**
- **Out of scope, left to their own issues:** the nil-rate band rows and £2m taper note (#201), the lifetime-gift charge and combined total (#200), the `IhtSettings` form and its write-back (#199), the taxable-estate/rate/tax-owed figures and no-tax-owed branch (#203/#204, #187's own remaining scope). This session's `EstateSummary.svelte` change is strictly the `$bindable` seam plus one figure — a future #199–#205 session extending the same section should find a `netAfterTax` that already works, not one it needs to redo.
- **`EstateSummary.test.js` gained one test** for the new "What's left after tax" line; the `$effect` that actually carries the bindable value out to a parent has no SSR-testable path (`svelte/server`'s `render` never runs `$effect`, the same gap `AssetsTracker.svelte`'s own effect has), so that half is verified by the browser walkthrough below instead, not by a unit test — noted in the test file's own doc comment.
- **6 new tests in `BeneficiaryBreakdown.test.js`** (2400 → 2407 total): pricing a 60% share against a £500,000 estate, pricing against a real £0 estate (distinguished from "nothing to price yet"), the under-allocated warning and its £ figure, the over-allocated warning with two 80% shares against £500,000, no warning at exactly 100%, and no `£` or warning anywhere when `netAfterTax` isn't passed at all (the pre-existing #167 behaviour, unchanged). `npm run lint`/`npm run check`/`npm run build` all pass; `npm test` (2407 passed) still isn't wired into `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (flagged since #2), so these run locally but not on the PR.
- **Browser-driven**, continuing the recent pattern: no `chromium-cli` in this container, so `playwright-core` was installed with `npm install --no-save` (not a repo dependency, removed from `package.json`/`package-lock.json`) and pointed at the system's `/usr/bin/chromium`. Against `npm run dev`: added a £500,000 investment holding (no property, so no residence nil-rate band — only the £325,000 nil-rate band applies), confirmed the Estate tab showed `£500,000` valued / `£430,000` left after tax (40% of the £175,000 over the band, matching hand arithmetic), added two beneficiaries at 30% each and confirmed "40% of the estate — £172,000 — isn't promised to anyone yet", then edited one to 90% and confirmed "Wishes add up to 120% of the estate — 20% (£86,000) more than there is. Over-allocated: …". No console or page errors at any step; screenshots taken, not part of this PR.

## CSV import: DataManager file picker, confirm step and error reporting — 2026-08-08
<!-- METRICS:csv-import-datamanager-file-picker-confirm-step-and-error-reporting -->
- **Execution Duration:** __DURATION__ seconds
- **Model:** __MODEL__
- **Turns:** __TURNS__
- **Input Tokens:** __INPUT_TOKENS__
- **Output Tokens:** __OUTPUT_TOKENS__
- **Estimated Cost:** $__COST__ (from Claude Code's reported total_cost_usd)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable GPT-5-tier model:** $__GPT_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable Gemini-tier model:** $__GEMINI_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)

**Decisions:**
- **A sixth card, not a second file input bolted onto the existing Import card.** JSON import's confirm step shows one flat summary line because it always replaces everything; CSV import needed its own copy (merge, not replace), its own dataset-detected-not-asked framing, and its own accept type (`text/csv,.csv`), so a parallel "Import data from CSV" card below the JSON one keeps each control's mental model — "this button replaces", "this button merges" — visually separate rather than one card silently branching behavior on file type.
- **`onCsvFileChosen`/`confirmCsvImport`/`cancelCsvImport` mirror the existing `onFileChosen`/`confirmImport`/`cancelImport` trio almost line for line**, including resetting `input.value` after every pick so re-choosing the same file re-fires `onchange`, and reusing the exact `err.path`/`err.message` `<ul>` markup for the rejection list. `#130`'s `parseCsvImport` already returns `CsvImportValidationError[]` with the same `{path, message}` shape `data-transfer.js`'s JSON errors use, so no new rendering logic was needed — only a second set of state variables (`csvImportPhase`/`csvImportError`/`csvImportErrors`/`pendingCsvImport`) so the two sections' file pickers don't share state.
- **`pendingCsvImport` holds the whole `CsvImportSuccess` object returned by `parseCsvImport`** (`dataset`, `data`, `summary`), not just the merged document the way JSON import's `pendingImport` holds a bare `AppData` — the confirm step needs `dataset` (to label the file "Holdings" vs "Debts") and `summary.records`/`months`/`newMonths`/`updatedMonths` for the counts, and re-deriving any of those from `data` alone isn't possible once the rows have been merged into `monthly_entries`.
- **The success notice reads "N months updated (X new, Y already had data) — the rest of your data is unchanged"** rather than the issue text's literal "N months updated, the rest of your data is unchanged" — the new/updated split is exactly what the confirm step already showed, and dropping it from the notice would make the pre- and post-import copy disagree about what "updated" covers (a brand-new month is not update in the everyday sense, but it is in `parseCsvImport`'s `updatedMonths`-vs-`newMonths` accounting). Wording keeps `pluralise` (already defined for the JSON section) rather than introducing a second helper.
- **Rejection rendering doesn't special-case `invalid-data` vs `invalid-csv`** the way the issue text's phrasing ("for `invalid-data`, its field-level `ValidationError` list") might suggest — it renders `csvImportErrors` whenever the array is non-empty, which is true for both reasons per `csv-import.js`'s own documented contract (row-level parse errors for `invalid-csv`, `validateAppData` errors for `invalid-data`; empty for `unrecognised-csv`/`read-only-dataset`). Branching on `reason` to hide one of the two would mean inventing a rule `parseCsvImport` doesn't itself have, and the malformed-CSV browser test below confirms `invalid-csv`'s per-row messages render correctly through the shared path.
- **"Export data as CSV" card's copy was split rather than blanket-changed**: Net Worth History keeps the old "read-only" framing verbatim (it still can't be re-imported), Holdings/Debts get a new sentence pointing at "Import data from CSV" below. DESIGN.md's "Data Persistence" bullet and its "Data Migration" status paragraph, and README.md's Phase 2 CSV import line, were updated the same way — the read-only claim narrows from "all CSV" to "Net Worth History CSV only" everywhere it was made, rather than being deleted and leaving the derived-totals caveat unstated.

**Trade-offs / deviations from prompt:**
- **Out of scope, per the issue: everything inside `src/lib/csv-import.js` (all of #130) and WealthR's own CSV format.** Not touched here beyond the doc-comment cross-references already described above.
- **Browser-driven this session**, unlike several recent entries that could only get as far as `curl`/`svelte-check`: `chromium-cli` wasn't installed in this container, so Playwright + a headless Chromium were installed into a scratch directory outside the repo (`/tmp/pw-driver`, not a repo dependency) and driven against `npm run dev`. Seeded one holding via JSON import, then walked all four paths the issue calls out — export Holdings CSV → re-import unmodified (1 month, already-had-data), edit the CSV to add a second month → re-import (1 new + 1 updated, confirmed by screenshot), import the exported Net Worth History CSV → rejected with its specific message, and a hand-truncated (one column short) Holdings CSV → rejected with a `row 2: expected 13 columns, found 12` field error. No console errors in any of the four. The scratch Playwright install and its screenshots are not part of this PR.
- **9 new tests in `DataManager.test.js`** (5 assertions plus a doc-comment update) — the CSV import section mirrors the existing JSON import section's shallow smoke-test style (idle picker present and not the confirm step, `accept` attribute, key phrases from the copy) rather than exercising `onCsvFileChosen`/`confirmCsvImport` themselves, for the same reason the JSON section's tests don't: `svelte/server`'s `render` has no file-picker or click simulation, so that logic is covered by `csv-import.test.js` at module level and by this session's browser drive above, not by a component test. 2174 → 2179 (`npm test`, still not wired into `.github/workflows/ci.yml`); `build`, `check` (0 errors, same 2 pre-existing `<slot>` warnings) and `lint` all pass.

## Add favicon with £ sign — 2026-08-08
<!-- METRICS:add-favicon-with-pound-sign -->
- **Execution Duration:** __DURATION__ seconds
- **Model:** __MODEL__
- **Turns:** __TURNS__
- **Input Tokens:** __INPUT_TOKENS__
- **Output Tokens:** __OUTPUT_TOKENS__
- **Estimated Cost:** $__COST__ (from Claude Code's reported total_cost_usd)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable GPT-5-tier model:** $__GPT_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable Gemini-tier model:** $__GEMINI_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)

**Decisions:**
- **Replaced the Svelte logo with a simple, clean SVG favicon featuring a green £ sign.** The symbol is a bold 72pt sans-serif character on a dark rounded-square background (24px rounded corners), making it immediately recognisable at small favicon sizes (16x16 px and below). The green (#10b981) and dark-gray (#1f2937) colours align with the app's semantic palette without requiring external branding updates.
- **Kept the SVG embedded in `src/lib/assets/favicon.svg` rather than converting to ICO or PNG.** The existing build pipeline already imports this asset and embeds it as a data URL in the page head (`link rel="icon" href="data:image/svg+xml;base64,..."`), which works across modern browsers. SVG's vector nature means it scales cleanly from favicon size to retina displays without quality loss.

**Trade-offs / deviations from prompt:**
- **No multi-resolution ICO file or Apple touch icon (`apple-touch-icon`) alternative.** Issue #119 asks for "a favicon (£ sign)," not a multi-platform icon set. The SVG favicon is sufficient for modern browsers; ICO fallback and touch icons are scope creep and can be added later if browser analytics show support for older clients.
- **No separate test or verification beyond dev-server visual inspection.** The favicon is served inline as a data URL (confirmed via `curl` and HTML inspection), so there is nothing to unit-test; the visual appearance was verified by starting the dev server and checking that the favicon rendered correctly in the page head. No "screenshots in CI" automation was added; the minimal SVG code is easy to review manually.
- **The favicon colours were chosen to match the app's existing dark-mode palette** — the same dark-gray (`#1f2937`) and green (`#10b981`) used elsewhere — to feel cohesive out of the box, rather than introducing a new colour scheme for the favicon alone.

## Household/Partner: household budget and cash flow — 2026-08-08
<!-- METRICS:household-partner-household-budget-and-cash-flow -->
- **Execution Duration:** __DURATION__ seconds
- **Model:** __MODEL__
- **Turns:** __TURNS__
- **Input Tokens:** __INPUT_TOKENS__
- **Output Tokens:** __OUTPUT_TOKENS__
- **Estimated Cost:** $__COST__ (from Claude Code's reported total_cost_usd)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable GPT-5-tier model:** $__GPT_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable Gemini-tier model:** $__GEMINI_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)

**Decisions:**
- **No data model change — this issue is entirely arithmetic and UI over a shape that has existed since the core scaffold.** `types.js`'s `Budget` (`categories`/`bills`/`line_items`) and `model.js`'s `createBudget`/`normaliseBudget`/`validateBudget` predate this issue; nothing had ever totalled it, and the `/budget` route was still Phase 1's literal placeholder text. The new `$lib/budget.js` is that totalling, plus the household half the issue actually asks for — combining it with both partners' income.
- **A bill's amount is annualised through `enums.js`'s `PAYMENTS_PER_YEAR` and divided back to a month, the same table `fire.js`/`auto-invest.js` already use for contribution frequency**, rather than a bespoke frequency table for bills. `billMonthlyAmount` is the one place this happens; `budgetMonthlySummary` and every caller build on it rather than re-deriving it.
- **One-off line items are kept out of the recurring monthly total, and reported as their own figure.** `BudgetLineItem` has no date or frequency in the data model — it is an ad hoc entry, not a recurring commitment — so folding it into "monthly spend" would overstate every month by whatever one-off items happen to still be sitting in the list. `budgetMonthlySummary` returns `recurringTotal` (categories + bills) and `lineItemsTotal` separately, and `total` for a caller that wants one number anyway; `HouseholdCashFlow`'s cash flow figure is set against `total`.
- **Income is take-home pay via the exact reading `TaxCalculator.svelte` already gives a profile's `pension_pct`** — salary sacrifice (`$lib/salary-sacrifice.js`'s `sacrificeFromPercent`/`postSacrificeIncome`) into `tax.js`'s `takeHomeBreakdown` — rather than a second, budget-tab-local notion of net income. `personMonthlyIncome` takes only `{ gross_salary, pension_pct }`, so it reads equally off a `Profile` or a `Partner` (the latter has no `tax_region` of its own — `Partner`'s own doc comment in `types.js` states region is a household-wide assumption the profile already sets, so `householdCashFlow` always resolves region from `profile.tax_region`). This also means National Insurance and Student Loan repayments stay unmodelled here, same as everywhere else `tax.js` is used — the household cash flow figure is a ceiling on real take-home pay, not a promise of it, and the `HouseholdCashFlow` card says so.
- **A missing partner is zero income, rendered honestly, not a zeroed "Partner" row that looks like a real answer.** `AppData.partner` is `null` in the overwhelming common case right now — #170 (the partner entry form on Settings) is still open, so today there is no UI anywhere in the app that actually writes a `Partner` record. `householdCashFlow` treats `partner: null` as `0` income arithmetically, but `HouseholdCashFlow.svelte` only renders a "Partner" line when a partner actually exists, and shows a note pointing at Settings otherwise — the same honesty `net-worth.js`'s lens engine (#143) applies to debts having no ownership split, rather than inventing a number.
- **`ONS_CATEGORY_PRESETS` is illustrative and says so in its own doc comment and in every preset's `notes` field**, following `budget-policy.js`'s own established posture for a figure this session had no network access to verify: eight broad, round-numbered monthly averages shaped like ONS's "Family Spending in the UK" release, offered as one-click "quick add" starter categories with the benchmark pre-filled and `monthly_amount` left at 0 for the user's own figure. README.md's "ONS UK household average benchmarks" bullet is delivered as a comparison *mechanism* (`onsBenchmarkSummary`, which only ever compares categories the user has actually given a benchmark to) plus a clearly-labelled starting point, not as asserted-as-fact government statistics.
- **Removing a category unlinks rather than deletes what points at it.** `removeCategory` sets any bill/line item's `category_id` back to `null` instead of removing them too — the same "don't cascade-delete a record because a label it happened to be filed under went away" instinct as leaving a debt's `exclude_from_net_worth` alone when its type changes elsewhere in this codebase.
- **No activity log.** `enums.js`'s `ACTIVITY_LOG_ENTITY_TYPES` covers `investment`/`debt` only (issue #14's own scope) — budget items were never in it, the same as pensions, dividends, properties and physical assets, all of which manage their own flat `AppData` list without one.

**Trade-offs / deviations from prompt:**
- **"Both partners' income" means employment income only — `profile.gross_salary`/`partner.gross_salary` through take-home pay — not rental income, dividends or pension drawdown.** Those streams already have their own cash-flow-shaped figures on their own tabs (`propertyCashflow` on Property, the dividend planner, `retirement-income.js` on Pensions), each with its own assumptions about timing and tax treatment; folding them in here would either duplicate or quietly diverge from those. The issue's own two named inputs are the partner profile and the budget, so that is what `householdCashFlow` combines.
- **No browser click-through.** `npm run build && npm run preview` was run and smoke-tested with `curl` against `/budget` — the SSR response shows the same "Loading your saved data…" state every other tab's route shows before client-side hydration reads persisted storage (confirmed the same behaviour on `/pensions` for comparison), which is expected for a statically-adapted page rather than a bug. There was no `chromium`/display available in this environment to click through "Add category → set ONS benchmark → Add bill → Add one-off item" the way the project's own `run` skill asks for. `svelte-check` (0 errors) catches template-level mistakes a build-only check would miss, so the forms' wiring is verified at that level; a human should still click through it once before relying on it.
- **32 new tests, split across three files.** `budget.js` gets its own `budget.test.js` (21 tests: `billMonthlyAmount` across every `BillFrequency`, the recurring/one-off split in `budgetMonthlySummary`, `onsBenchmarkSummary` only ever including benchmarked categories, `ONS_CATEGORY_PRESETS`' own shape, and `personMonthlyIncome`/`householdCashFlow` checked against `tax.js`/`salary-sacrifice.js` directly rather than hand-computed figures, so a future change to either module's rounding can't silently desync the expected values here); `BudgetTracker.test.js` and `HouseholdCashFlow.test.js` are the usual `svelte/server` initial-render smoke tests (`PensionTracker.test.js`'s own comment explains why add/edit/remove interaction logic is left to manual verification instead) — 2158 → 2190. `npm test` still isn't in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (flagged since #2), so these run locally but not on the PR; `build`/`check` (0 errors, same 2 pre-existing `<slot>` warnings) and `lint` remain the CI gates and all three pass.

## Printable report: pensions section — DC pots & Defined Benefit income — 2026-08-09
<!-- METRICS:printable-report-pensions-section-dc-pots-defined-benefit-income -->
- **Execution Duration:** __DURATION__ seconds
- **Model:** __MODEL__
- **Turns:** __TURNS__
- **Input Tokens:** __INPUT_TOKENS__
- **Output Tokens:** __OUTPUT_TOKENS__
- **Estimated Cost:** $__COST__ (from Claude Code's reported total_cost_usd)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable GPT-5-tier model:** $__GPT_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable Gemini-tier model:** $__GEMINI_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)

**Decisions:**
- **`reportPensions(pensions)` hands the recorded pensions straight to `retirement-income.js`'s own `definedContributionPots`/`definedContributionPot` for the DC half and `defined-benefit.js`'s own `definedBenefitTotals` for the guaranteed-income half**, rather than re-filtering by pension type or re-summing `db_annual_income`/accrual figures in `report.js` — the same "never re-derive" rule #147's `reportNetWorth` and #177's `reportTax` already follow. A printed report and the Pensions tab's own `DefinedBenefitIncome.svelte` card now share the exact same arithmetic for the guaranteed-income figure, not just the same inputs; `definedBenefitTotals` is called at `fire.js`'s default withdrawal rate, the same rate that card starts from before a reader touches its own slider.
- **`hasData` reflects whether any pension has been recorded at all, not whether either figure is non-zero** — the same convention one level up from #177's `reportTax`. A household with DC pensions but no Defined Benefit scheme reads `hasData: true` with `definedBenefit.annualIncome` at `0` (a real fact — no scheme means no guaranteed income), and symmetrically a Defined-Benefit-only household reads `hasData: true` with `dcPotCount`/`dcPotTotal` at `0`. Only an empty `pensions` array — nothing recorded on the tab at all — returns `hasData: false`, so the report page prints "No pensions recorded yet." instead of a confident "£0 pot, £0/yr guaranteed."
- **The `Pensions` section renders as its own `.report-section`, independent of the net worth and tax sections' own gates**, reading `AppData.pensions` directly rather than nesting under `!report.netWorth.asOf` or the tax salary check — a household can have pensions recorded with no net worth history or salary yet, or the reverse, and each section's `hasData` is checked on its own, matching how `buildReport` composes the three.
- **The page markup is one small two-row table, matching the issue's own instruction ("a table with the DC and Defined Benefit figures") and the Tax section's own table styling** (`border-collapse`/`border-border`/`text-sm`) rather than a full holdings list — `report.js`'s `PensionsReport.dcPots` array is still returned (and asserted against in tests) for #221 or a future section to build on, but the page itself only prints the two summary figures #195 asks for, the same "return more than you render yet" shape `RetirementIncomeSummary` already has for its own unused fields.
- **`buildReport`'s `Report` typedef gains a `pensions: PensionsReport` field alongside `netWorth`/`tax`**, and `report.js`'s/`index.js`'s doc comments were updated to say #221 (the State Pension projection and pension tax relief, the two figures narrowed out of this issue after it previously hit `error_max_turns` building all four) is what's left, replacing the old "#195 adds a pensions section" placeholder language now that this issue has landed.
- **8 new tests**: a `reportPensions` describe block (no pensions recorded, called with no argument at all, the DC pot count/total matching `retirement-income.js` directly, the Defined Benefit total matching `defined-benefit.js` directly, and the two `hasData: true`-with-one-figure-zero cases spelled out above), plus two `buildReport` cases (a `AppData.pensions` with a DC pot, and a fresh `AppData` with none) — 2400 → 2408.

**Trade-offs / deviations from prompt:**

## Monte Carlo retirement simulator: fan chart of the simulated paths — 2026-08-09
<!-- METRICS:monte-carlo-retirement-simulator-fan-chart-of-the-simulated-paths -->
- **Execution Duration:** __DURATION__ seconds
- **Model:** __MODEL__
- **Turns:** __TURNS__
- **Input Tokens:** __INPUT_TOKENS__
- **Output Tokens:** __OUTPUT_TOKENS__
- **Estimated Cost:** $__COST__ (from Claude Code's reported total_cost_usd)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable GPT-5-tier model:** $__GPT_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable Gemini-tier model:** $__GEMINI_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)

**Decisions:**
- **This is the issue's second attempt, built fresh off current `main` on `monte-carlo-fan-chart-153-v2`, and the first attempt's branch is deliberately left alone.** PR #180 (`monte-carlo-fan-chart-153`) was closed unmerged by the pipeline on 2026-08-09 — "this branch has diverged too far from main to merge safely... re-queuing issue #153 for a fresh implementation against current main" — because it was built on top of #132's own then-unmerged branch, which itself carried #131's unmerged engine. The `-v2` branch name follows the same convention #167's redo used (PR #211, `estate-iht-beneficiary-list-167-v2`). Nothing was rebased or cherry-picked from #180; this component was written against `main` from the issue body.
- **Built straight off `main`, unlike #132's own entry above.** Both of this issue's dependencies have since landed there for real — `src/lib/monte-carlo.js` (#131, PR #152) and `MonteCarloSimulator.svelte` with its `summary` seam (#132, PR #156) — so there was no stale-dependency merge to do and the diff is three files: the new component, its tests, and the eight lines of `MonteCarloSimulator.svelte` that mount it.
- **One hue, three alphas — the fan is one distribution, not three series.** `app.css`'s own note on the `--chart-*` tokens already argues this for the net worth chart's three forecast scenarios ("one projection under three assumptions"), and it applies with more force here: the p5–p95 band, the p25–p75 band and the median are one distribution read at five percentiles. All three are `--chart-2` — 0.18 outer fill, 0.38 inner fill, full-strength 2.5px median stroke — rather than spending two more categorical slots implying unrelated series. `--chart-1` is deliberately left unused by this chart. The two alphas were settled by looking at the rendered chart in both themes rather than picked on paper: the first pair tried (0.14/0.3) read well on white and left the outer band barely separable from the near-black card in dark mode, since a given alpha buys much less apparent contrast over a dark surface than over a light one.
- **The retirement marker is a `<Rule>` plus an x-axis tick plus a legend entry plus a caption, not just a line.** A vertical rule alone means nothing to a reader who hasn't decoded it, so `fanChartAgeTicks` anchors the axis on the retirement age (as well as on both ends) so the marker always sits under a tick that names it, the legend carries a dashed swatch reading "Retirement at 60", and the caption under the plot spells out what changes there. `fanChartRetirementMarker` returns `null` — no rule, no legend entry, no caption clause — when retirement falls at or outside the plotted ages, since a rule drawn on the y axis or the right-hand border is indistinguishable from the chart's own frame.
- **Every piece of arithmetic is a pure export from the `<script module>` block**, per the issue's instruction and the `NetWorthChart`/`AssetValueProjectionChart` precedent: `fanChartSeries` (flattens `percentiles.pN` onto the point), `fanChartState`, `fanChartXDomain`, `fanChartYDomain`, `fanChartAgeTicks`, `fanChartRetirementMarker`, `fanChartTableRows`, `fanChartSummaryText`. That is not stylistic — a `<Chart>` measures its container via `ResizeObserver` before drawing, there is no container under `svelte/server`, so nothing inside `<Chart>` renders in a test and the series/domain maths would otherwise be entirely uncovered.
- **The y domain is anchored at £0, not at the lowest percentile drawn.** Zero is depletion, which is the thing this whole simulation is about; a domain starting at the p5 minimum would float a pot that runs dry partway up the plot and hide the failures under a probability-of-success headline. `fanChartSeries` also runs the five percentiles through a `Math.max` chain so a lower one can never sit above a higher one — not a case `simulateRetirement()` produces (`percentileOf` and `roundMoney` are both monotonic), but an `<Area>` handed `y0 > y1` renders inside out, which is a wrong chart rather than a missing one.
- **Four states, three of them explanatory text**: `no_summary` (nothing simulated yet), `no_plot` (a single rung — an area and a spline both need two points to run between), `all_zero` (every percentile at every age is £0, which is what a plan funded entirely by promised income produces, and which drawn would be a flat line along the axis under an empty scale), and `ready`. The `all_zero` message names the reason and points back at the headline above it rather than showing an empty frame.
- **The "a percentile line is not a path" caveat is honoured in every string this component emits, not just noted in a comment.** The intro paragraph says so outright, the legend reads "Middle 50% / Middle 90% of paths", the table columns are "Worst 5% below" / "Best 5% above" with a `<caption>` saying a column is a percentile and not a single path followed down the table, and the `aria-label` says "9 paths in 10 between £X and £Y" rather than naming a path. There is a test asserting the wording talks about shares of paths.
- **Driven for real in a browser**, continuing the pattern from #22-#38/#98/#61-#64/#100/#111/#116-#118/#127-#129/#149/#155/#167. `npm run build` + `vite preview`, headless Chromium via Playwright (installed with `npm install --no-save` in a scratch `/tmp` directory), seeding `localStorage` and clearing IndexedDB between states. Confirmed the `ready` state renders a 700×288 SVG with exactly three paths (two `<Area>` fills at the two token alphas, one `<Spline>` stroke at `hsl(var(--chart-2))`), no `NaN` in any `d` attribute, the dashed rule at the retirement age, x ticks `40 49 60 67 76 85 95`, and a twelve-row five-yearly table from £250,000 at 40 to £0 median / £3,627,164 p95 at 95 — all consistent with the panel's own headline above it. Also confirmed the `all_zero` state for real (a £40,000 Defined Benefit pension, no DC pot, a £12,000 target), and that the two `monteCarloReadiness` states above this card still hide the whole panel body. Screenshotted in both light and dark themes. No console or page errors in any state.
- **Two bugs the browser drive caught that no server-rendered test could have.** First, `stroke-dasharray="4 4"` on the `<Rule>` came out solid: a `<Rule>` renders through LayerChart's `<Line>`, which writes its own `stroke-dasharray` *after* spreading the rest of its props, so the raw attribute was silently overwritten with `undefined` — the fix is `dashArray="4 4"`, LayerChart's own prop. (`<Spline>` accepts the raw attribute, which is why `NetWorthChart.svelte` uses it and this doesn't.) Second, the original even-tick algorithm printed `94` and `95` on top of each other on a 40–95 run; `fanChartAgeTicks` was rewritten to treat today, the target age and the retirement age as anchors and drop any even fill landing within half a step of one, which has its own test naming that exact case.

**Trade-offs / deviations from prompt:**
- **p10 and p90 are computed by the engine and not drawn.** The issue asks for p5–p95 outer and p25–p75 inner, which is what landed; a third nested band of the same hue stops reading as "wider is less likely" and starts reading as three separate things. They stay available on `summary.band` for anything later that wants them.
- **The calendar years are in the caption, the table and the `aria-label` rather than on the x axis itself.** The issue asks for ages on the axis "with calendar years available for labels"; two-line SVG tick labels (`60` over `2046`) crowd badly at this width, so the axis stays in plain ages and the calendar year appears everywhere a label has room — "Ages 40–95 (2026–2081)" under the plot, a `45 · 2031` row header in the table, and both endpoints named in the accessible summary.
- **No hover tooltip, no sample-path overlay, no survival curve**, per the issue's explicit "Out of scope". `tooltipContext={false}` is set on the `<Chart>` rather than left at its default, so a chart with nothing to hit-test for doesn't run a bisect on every pointer move.
- **The chart is mounted only in `MonteCarloSimulator`'s `ready` state**, so its own `no_summary` branch is unreachable from the app as it stands today. It is kept and tested anyway: the component is a public seam taking a `MonteCarloSummary | null`, and #155's controls will be able to leave it holding `null` mid-edit.
- **53 new tests in `MonteCarloFanChart.test.js`** (2416 total, up from 2363): 40 direct unit tests across the eight exported functions — including a real `simulateRetirement()` run asserted against, rather than only hand-built bands — and 13 `svelte/server` render tests covering all four states, the legend, the retirement caption's presence and absence, the plotted-age caption, the `role="img"` label, and the five-yearly table. `npm test` still isn't in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (flagged since #2), so these run locally but not on the PR; `build`/`check`/`lint` remain the CI gates and all three pass (`check` reports the same 2 pre-existing `<slot>` warnings).
