## Estate & IHT: the IhtSettings assumptions form and the Estate tab's first write-back — 2026-08-09
<!-- METRICS:estate-iht-the-ihtsettings-assumptions-form-and-the-estate-tabs-first-write-back -->
- **Execution Duration:** 861 seconds
- **Model:** claude-sonnet-5
- **Turns:** 158
- **Input Tokens:** 18880
- **Output Tokens:** 50319
- **Estimated Cost:** $0.8114 (from Claude Code's reported total_cost_usd)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable GPT-5-tier model:** $0.7607 (illustrative -- see methodology note above)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable Gemini-tier model:** $0.4649 (illustrative -- see methodology note above)

**Decisions:**
- **The five fields commit straight into the `ihtSettings` `$bindable` prop `EstateSummary.svelte` already carries — no Save button, one edit per commit** — `PartnerProfile.svelte`'s own convention for a record that already exists rather than a list being added to, adapted for the one difference this settings object has from a `Partner`: `iht_settings` is never `null`, so there is no add/remove state and the fields are always shown once the card mounts. Booleans (`spouse_exempt`, `direct_descendants`) commit on `change`; the three numeric fields (`transferred_nil_rate_band_pct`, `transferred_residence_nil_rate_band_pct`, `funeral_expenses`) are kept in local text `$state` and commit on `input`, exactly `PartnerProfile`'s reason: a blank box has to read as "not typed" mid-keystroke rather than being coerced to `0` and briefly written into the document a parent persists. `commit()` always writes a finite, clamped number into `ihtSettings`, never `NaN` — `estate-plan.js`'s own defensive re-normalisation inside `estateSnapshot()` is a second, independent safety net for the *displayed* bill (a stored document could still reach it with a bad value some other way), not a substitute for this form doing its own job.
- **One `estateSnapshot()` call, still — editing a field never re-derives the bill.** The form only ever writes `ihtSettings`; `EstateSummary`'s existing `snapshot` `$derived` (already reading `ihtSettings` for #187/#188/#189) recomputes on its own once that prop changes, so this issue adds no second engine call and no arithmetic of its own, matching the issue's own explicit instruction to "bind through it rather than re-deriving any of the displayed figures here."
- **`ihtSettings` becomes `$bindable` on `EstateSummary.svelte`, and `routes/estate/+page.svelte` threads it through `bind:ihtSettings` into the same `$effect` that already writes `beneficiaries` back to the store**, rather than a second `$effect` — one effect, two fields, both guarded by the same `ready` flag `routes/assets/+page.svelte`'s own single-list `$effect` uses. The page's local `ihtSettings` `$state` is seeded `createIhtSettings()` rather than `undefined`, both because it now needs to type-check as a plain (non-optional) `IhtSettings` against the write-back and because `beneficiaries` above it already uses the same "typed as the real thing, never rendered before `ready`" convention rather than an `| undefined` escape hatch.
- **The section sits inside the existing card, beneath "What's left after tax" and above the illustrative-only footnote — a `<h3>Assumptions</h3>` and a one-line "changes apply straight away" caption, no separate `<Card>`**, matching the issue's "a section in `EstateSummary.svelte`, beneath #187's bill" instruction and the itemised-breakdown entry above's own precedent for extending this card in place rather than restructuring the page.
- **7 new tests in `EstateSummary.test.js`** (2476 total, up from 2470), plus a `html()` helper (`PartnerProfile.test.js`'s own pattern, since `text()`'s tag-stripping throws attribute values away along with the tags): the five field labels rendering unconditionally, a stored settings object populating the three numeric fields' `value` attributes, both checkboxes' `checked` state at true and at false, a changed `funeral_expenses` moving "What's left after tax" from £500,000 to £450,000 against the same £500,000 property, and a `NaN` `funeral_expenses` (standing in for whatever a half-typed field or a corrupted document could otherwise reach this component with) rendering neither "NaN" nor a blank total. The interactive `oninput`/`onchange`/`commit()` path itself — like `PartnerProfile`'s own — needs a real DOM and is covered by the browser drive below instead, not by `svelte/server`'s initial-render-only `render()`.

**Trade-offs / deviations from prompt:**
- **No validation-error surfacing (`PartnerProfile.svelte`'s own `validatePartnerFields`/`FIELD_LABELS` pattern) was added for `IhtSettings`.** The issue doesn't ask for it, and unlike a partner's DOB or retirement age, every field here already has a defensible fallback if left out of range: `commit()` clamps both percentages to 0–100 before they ever reach `ihtSettings`, and `estate-plan.js`'s `normaliseIhtSettingsInput` clamps again for the bill regardless. Adding a second, redundant error list would be exactly the kind of unrequested feature CLAUDE.md's build philosophy warns against; `model.js`'s `validateIhtSettings` still exists for `validateAppData` to catch anything that reaches the document some other way.
- **Out of scope, per the issue's own narrowing:** no Inheritance Tax arithmetic of any kind was touched — the bill's bands, taper, `totalTax` and the no-tax-owed wording all remain #187's/#200–#205's, unchanged. No gift entry form, no beneficiary-relationship inference of `spouse_exempt`, no property gift-on-sale — all explicitly out of scope per the issue body and untouched here.
- **Driven for real in a browser**, continuing the pattern most of this log's entries follow. `playwright-core` was installed ad hoc (`npm install --no-save`, in `/tmp`) and pointed at the system's `/usr/bin/chromium`, against `npm run build && npm run preview`. Added a £500,000 property with no mortgage through the Property tab's form, loaded `/estate`, and drove the new fields directly: typing `50000` into "Funeral expenses (£)" moved "What's left after tax" from £500,000 to £450,000 immediately; checking "Whole estate passes to a spouse or civil partner, exempt" left it at £450,000 (this estate owes no tax either side of that toggle, so the figure it changes here is the same one funeral expenses already moved); clearing the funeral expenses field back to blank returned the figure to £500,000 with no "NaN" anywhere in the page's text. Reloading the page afterwards showed the funeral expenses field back at `0` (the committed, clamped value from the cleared field, not blank) and the spouse-exempt checkbox still checked — confirming the write-back through the store's debounced sync actually persists, which `svelte/server`'s tests can't exercise. No console or page errors at any step. The scratch driver script and its `/tmp` `node_modules` were removed afterward; `git status` was clean before committing.
- **`npm test` remains local-only** — still not in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (flagged since #2). `build`, `check` (0 errors, same 2 pre-existing `<slot>` warnings) and `lint` all pass.

## Estate & IHT: the lifetime-gift charge and the combined tax total on the Estate tab — 2026-08-09
<!-- METRICS:estate-iht-gift-charge-combined-total-200 -->
- **Execution Duration:** 879 seconds
- **Model:** claude-sonnet-5
- **Turns:** 164
- **Input Tokens:** 500
- **Output Tokens:** 54088
- **Estimated Cost:** $0.8128 (from Claude Code's reported total_cost_usd)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable GPT-5-tier model:** $0.7620 (illustrative -- see methodology note above)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable Gemini-tier model:** $0.4657 (illustrative -- see methodology note above)

**Decisions:**
- **This is a from-scratch re-implementation against current `main`, not a continuation of the first attempt.** A branch for this issue (`estate-iht-gift-charge-combined-total-200`, PR #227) already existed, but the owner's pipeline had auto-closed it hours earlier — "diverged too far from main to merge safely … re-queuing issue #200 for a fresh implementation" — and `git diff origin/main...origin/estate-iht-gift-charge-combined-total-200` confirmed there was no shared history left to rebase (`no merge base`). Read #227's own PR description and test-plan numbers as a sanity check on the arithmetic (they turned out to match exactly, see below) but wrote every line fresh against `EstateSummary.svelte` as it stands on `main` today, and pushed to `-v2` rather than force-pushing over the closed branch.
- **#200's own "out of scope" list — the estate's own bands, rate and taxable estate, all `#187`'s — no longer describes a real dependency.** `#187` itself was closed and superseded by `#206` ("the Inheritance Tax section on the Estate tab", merged), but `EstateSummary.test.js`'s current top-of-file comment says outright that the estate's further bands/rate/no-tax-owed figures are `#201`–`#205`'s, still open. So on `main` today the Inheritance Tax section is genuinely minimal — a heading, the `netAfterTax` headline, and the `IhtSettings` assumptions form (#199/#226) — with no estate-tax figure of its own yet for a "combined total" to sit beside. Rather than block on four unmerged issues or invent a redundant, context-free "Estate tax: £X" row pre-empting #204's own fuller treatment, the combined total is rendered as "Total Inheritance Tax (estate + gifts)" — `snapshot.totalTax` labelled to say what it's made of — beside "Tax on gifts (payable by recipients, not the estate)" (`snapshot.giftTax`). Both are still nothing more than the two fields `estateSnapshot()` already returns, read out with a label; no Inheritance Tax arithmetic was added.
- **Everything reads off the one `estateSnapshot()` call `EstateSummary.svelte` already derives** — `snapshot.ledger.gifts` (one `GiftAssessment` per gift, oldest first), `snapshot.nilRateBandUsedByGifts`/`nilRateBand`, `snapshot.giftTax`, `snapshot.totalTax` and `snapshot.ledger.nextToFallOut`/`daysToNextFallOut` — exactly as `lifetime-gifts.js` (#139) and `estate-plan.js` (#140) already compute them. No second `estateSnapshot()` call, matching every other issue this card is built from.
- **Only a `failed` gift gets a row; `survived`/`exempt` gifts fold into one quiet count, and the whole section is gated on `gifts.length > 0`.** `lifetime-gifts.js`'s own `GiftStatus` already draws this line — rendering an `undated`/`after_death` gift would need a gift-entry form to produce one, which doesn't exist yet and is explicitly out of scope, so those two statuses are simply never rendered (nothing in the data model can currently create them). Per-gift rows show the recipient, amount, tax year, years survived and — only when non-zero — the taper relief percentage, since a gift the nil-rate band already covers gets `0%` and mentioning that would be noise (`lifetime-gifts.js`'s own convention 1: taper relief is only ever worth anything above the band).
- **The countdown renders as a date and a day count together** (`ledger.nextToFallOut` formatted `en-GB`/`timeZone: 'UTC'` to avoid a local-timezone off-by-one against the ISO calendar date `lifetime-gifts.js` computes it as, plus `daysToNextFallOut`), shown only when something is still counting — an empty ledger or one where every gift has already survived renders neither line rather than a "next: —" placeholder.
- **5 new tests in `EstateSummary.test.js`** (2481 total, up from 2476): no gift rows on a populated document with `gifts: []` and on the fully empty document; a £500,000 estate against a single £400,000 gift a year old producing £27,600 gift tax and a £227,600 combined total (re-verifying, independently, the same figures #227's now-closed PR reported for the identical scenario); 40% taper relief showing on a gift five years old; and a 26-year-old gift folding into "1 gift already out of account" without a row of its own. Gift dates are computed relative to `new Date()` at test-run time (`lifetime-gifts.test.js`'s own pattern), since `EstateSummary.svelte` never passes `estateSnapshot()` a `deathDate` override.

**Trade-offs / deviations from prompt:**
- **A minimal "Estate tax" figure was deliberately not added**, even though the issue's own body describes the combined total sitting "alongside #187's estate tax." Since no estate-tax row exists on `main` yet (see above), adding one here would either duplicate `#204`'s explicit remit ("the Inheritance Tax rate and the estate tax owed") or under-deliver it (a bare number with no rate/band context). `snapshot.totalTax` is shown with a label stating what it combines, which is enough for #200's own scope — the gift charge and the combined total — without reaching into #204's.

That matters for one line in #201's own body: "Headroom on an estate that owes nothing … belongs in #187's no-tax-owed branch" — that branch is #203, and it doesn't exist yet. Rather than block on it or invent a redundant no-tax-owed wrapper pre-empting #203's own remit, the headroom row is gated directly on `snapshot.estate.unusedAllowance > 0`, which is `0` in exactly the cases a no-tax-owed branch would gate on (`unusedAllowance` is provably non-zero iff `taxableEstate` is `0` — `estate.js`'s own arithmetic). When #203 lands its branch, this row will already read correctly inside it with no changes needed here.
- **Everything reads off `snapshot.estate`** — the after-gifts `IhtCalculation` `estate.js`'s `inheritanceTax()` returns, already carried through `estateSnapshot()` and, since #200, already read by this same card for `snapshot.nilRateBandUsedByGifts`/`nilRateBand`. No second engine call, no arithmetic of its own: `estate.allowances.nrb`/`rnrb`, `estate.nilRateBandUsed`/`residenceNilRateBandUsed`, `estate.taperApplies`, `estate.allowances.taperLoss` and `estate.unusedAllowance` are rendered exactly as `estate.js` computes them.
- **The residence nil-rate band's "no qualifying home" wording is gated on `allowances.rnrbBeforeTaper === 0`, not `allowances.rnrb === 0`.** The issue's own wording ("no qualifying home recorded, or the home is not passing to direct descendants") describes one specific case — `estate.js`'s own doc comment on `rnrbBeforeTaper` says it is `0` precisely and only when the estate has no qualifying home at all, as distinct from `rnrbEnhancement`. Gating on `rnrb === 0` instead would also catch a home that exists but has been tapered away to nothing above £2,000,000 — the exact scenario the taper note right above it exists to explain — and would show "no qualifying home recorded" on an estate that plainly has one. Caught this by hand-working a >£2,000,000 estate with a home before writing the test for it, not from the issue text, which doesn't call the distinction out.
- **4 new tests in `EstateSummary.test.js`** (2487 total, up from 2481, plus 2 more for the "no taper below the threshold" / "no headroom once bands are used in full" negative cases the issue doesn't ask for by name but which the same worked examples make free): the nil-rate band and residence nil-rate band both shown fully used on a taxed £1,500,000 estate; the no-qualifying-home wording on an estate with no property recorded, and that it never renders "£0 of £0"; the £2,000,000 taper note and its £175,000 loss on a £2,700,000 estate with a home tapered away entirely; and the unused-allowance headroom, residence-value cap included, on a £150,000 estate that owes nothing.

- **Left the `$bindable` prop's outward behaviour itself untested, and said so in the file's header comment, rather than writing a test that can't actually exercise it.** `netAfterTax`'s only write happens inside an `$effect`, and this file's own existing comment on `ihtSettings` and `BeneficiaryBreakdown.test.js`'s own header both already document that `$effect` never runs under `svelte/server`'s `render()` (initial render only, no reactivity). Rather than block on that PR or leave #218 unimplementable, this issue's session built both halves against current `main` — the shortfall section #154 owns (component, prop seam, the four-state guard, the shared formatters) and the terminal-pot section #218 actually asks for — since a terminal-pot section cannot exist without the component and shared helpers it extends. #217's own diff was read first to confirm the exact field names, degenerate cases and test shape #154's issue text specifies, then this branch's version was written independently against `main`'s current `MonteCarloSimulator.svelte` (which already has #153's fan chart wired in, something #217's branch predates) rather than cherry-picked, so the doc comments and wiring reflect what's actually on `main` today rather than the state #217 branched from. Whichever of #217 or this PR merges second will need reconciling by the pipeline's own conflict-handling step (real, non-journal conflicts get closed and requeued per `docs/journal.md`'s own commit history) — flagging that here since it's a genuine cross-issue collision, not something this session can resolve unilaterally.
- **`monteCarloTerminalRows` has no null-state branch the way `monteCarloShortfallRows` does.** Every field it reads (`mean`, `median`, `percentiles`, `depletedShare`) is a real number in every `MonteCarloSummary`, including `paths === 0` or a fully depleted run — there is no "no terminal pot to report" case the way there's a "no shortfall happened" case, so the five rows always render once there is a `summary` at all, `guaranteed` included: a guaranteed plan says nothing about what the pension pot and ISA pot themselves end up holding.
- **The depleted-majority case gets an explicit sentence, not just five rows left to speak for themselves.** The issue calls this out by name ("has to read as 'most paths ended with nothing left', not as an unexplained £0 row"), so `depletedMajority` (`terminal.depletedShare >= 0.5`) gates a one-line summary above the table — "Most paths ended with nothing left: 84.5% had a pot of £0 by age 95" in the real browser drive below — rather than relying on a reader to connect a `£0` median row to a `84.5%` depleted-share row three lines down unassisted. Half is the cutoff: below it, "most paths" would misdescribe a table the majority of paths didn't actually hit.
- **Mean-vs-median skew and the cross-path percentile caveat are stated once in the module doc comment, not repeated per-row** — matching how the shortfall section's own "conditional on failure" caveat is stated once above its table rather than in every row label. `MonteCarloBandPoint`'s docs in `monte-carlo.js` make the same "different paths, not one simulation" point for #153's fan; this file's comment cites it directly rather than re-deriving the argument.
- **`MonteCarloOutcomes` mounts as its own `Card`, sibling to the headline card, beneath the fan chart** — not nested inside the headline `Card` the way the fan chart is. The headline card's job is the single probability figure; the fan chart is the picture of the distribution behind it; this card is a third, denser reading of the same `summary` (five-plus-five numbered rows) that reads better as its own block than squeezed under an already-long card, matching #217's own placement choice.

**Trade-offs / deviations from prompt:**
- **Rebuilds #154's shortfall section and shared helpers rather than treating them as a given**, which the issue's own "Out of scope" list says are #154's alone. Unavoidable given #154 hadn't merged (see Decisions above) — the component and its module-level formatters have to exist for a terminal-pot section to extend them at all.
- **19 new tests in `MonteCarloOutcomes.test.js`** (a new file, since #154 hadn't landed to have its own), plus a one-line addition to an existing `MonteCarloSimulator.test.js` case confirming `MonteCarloOutcomes` mounts once `summary` is ready. Covers everything #154's issue text asks for (state selection, row formatting, all four component states, the null-first-shortfall-age case, a real `simulateRetirement()` render) plus #218's own additions: mean-vs-median distinctness on a right-skewed fixture, a zero-depletion fixture (no stray `0.0%` row), and both a positive and a negative assertion for the depleted-majority sentence. `npm run test`: 2516/2516 passing (2497 before this change, per the prior journal entry's own count). `npm run check`: 0 errors, the same 2 pre-existing `<slot>` warnings. `npm run lint`: clean after one `prettier --write` pass.
- **Driven for real in a browser**, continuing the pattern from prior entries. `npm run dev` against a seeded `localStorage` payload (`uk-wealth-tracker:data`, the same shape `browser-storage.js` reads/migrates) with a date of birth, a £250,000 DC pension and a £30,000 target income — `playwright` (Chromium) installed ad hoc into a scratch `/tmp` directory, not added to `package.json`/the lockfile. `/retirement` rendered the headline, the fan chart, "How the shortfalls happened" and "Pot left at age 95" all together; this particular fixture landed on an 84.5% depleted share, which exercised the depleted-majority sentence for real rather than only in a fixture test. No console or page errors. The scratch driver script, its `node_modules` and the screenshot were removed afterward; `git status` was clean before committing.

**Decisions:**
- **No new engine or arithmetic — this issue is purely markup over what #134's `mortgage-rate-rise.js` already exports.** `mortgageRateRiseImpacts` (per-scenario) and `compareMortgageRateRise` (month-by-month at `offsets`) were already fully built and tested in `mortgage-rate-rise.test.js`; `MortgageRateRisePanel.svelte` just needed to call them and render the result, the same seam #158/#184 left open (the `offsets` prop was already threaded through from `ForecastProjections` but explicitly unread, with a comment pointing at this issue).
- **Copied `StressTestPanel`/`IncomeShockPanel`'s comparison shape as closely as the data allows**, rather than inventing a new layout: two horizon bars (baseline vs risen, same inline-styled bar-and-label markup, same `barScale`/70%-width scaling), a per-scenario table (scenario + its growth rate, then figures, then a caption sentence), and a month-by-month table following `offsets`. The one deliberate divergence is the per-scenario table's "figures" column: the siblings' own figures (contributions forgone, crash cost) vary by scenario because the market event does; a mortgage rate change doesn't depend on the market at all, so `extraInterestOverRemainingTerm` is identical in all three rows — the caption sentence says so explicitly, rather than leaving three identical numbers unexplained.
- **`extraInterestOverRemainingTerm === null` (module convention 1: no finite remaining term — interest-only or underwater) renders as `—` in the per-scenario table, not `£0`**, via a small `formatOptionalMoney` helper — the same honest-non-answer standard the panel's existing headline paragraph already applies to that same field, just extended to the new table cell that reads it.
- **The `keepTerm: false` caveat the issue calls out lives as its own paragraph, gated on `!config.keepTerm`, placed immediately before the bars/tables** rather than folded into the existing headline copy from #158/#184: it explains *why* the bars will be level and every comparison-row difference will be zero (no cashflow change to project) before the reader reaches them, and the month-by-month table's own caption is branched the same way ("every row matches the baseline" instead of "rows before the change date are identical") so it doesn't imply the *later* rows should have diverged when they structurally can't.
- **The engine's other honest-empty states (`hasMortgage: false`, `occurs: false`) already gate the entire `{:else}` branch these tables live in**, inherited unchanged from #158 — no new guard was needed for the tables to "stay silent" there, since they're simply never reached.

**Trade-offs / deviations from prompt:**
- **No browser click-through.** Same constraint as the household budget entry above: no display/`chromium` available in this environment. Verified instead with `npx vitest run` (2405 passed, up from 2400), `npx eslint .` (clean), `npx prettier --check` (clean after one `--write` pass on the edited file), and `npx svelte-check` (0 errors, the same 2 pre-existing `<slot>` warnings from `ui/card.svelte`/`ui/button.svelte`). A human should click through the "tick the box → change rate/timing/keep-term → watch the tables and bars move" flow once before relying on it.
- **17 tests in `MortgageRateRisePanel.test.js`, up from 12.** The five new ones are `svelte/server` initial-render smoke tests, matching the file's existing style: the three scenario rows render, month-by-month rows honour a passed `offsets` prop (and exclude an offset not in the list), the two horizon bar labels render, the `keepTerm: false` caveat text appears alongside the unchanged `£900 to £900/month (+£0/month)` figure rather than replacing or hiding it, and an interest-only property (payment exactly equal to the interest the balance accrues) reaches the existing "no fixed term to solve for" copy instead of a table full of zeroes. No new test file for `$lib/mortgage-rate-rise.js` itself — its exports were already covered before this issue.
- **`npm test` still isn't a CI gate** (same flag as every prior entry) — the new tests above run locally and are included in the 2405-test total, but only `build`/`check`/`lint` run on the PR itself.
