## Monte Carlo retirement simulator: the six controls and the debounced re-run — 2026-08-09
<!-- METRICS:monte-carlo-retirement-simulator-the-six-controls-and-the-debounced-re-run -->
- **Execution Duration:** 884 seconds
- **Model:** claude-sonnet-5
- **Turns:** 156
- **Input Tokens:** 476
- **Output Tokens:** 46308
- **Estimated Cost:** $0.6960 (from Claude Code's reported total_cost_usd)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable GPT-5-tier model:** $0.6525 (illustrative -- see methodology note above)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable Gemini-tier model:** $0.3987 (illustrative -- see methodology note above)

**Decisions:**
- **This issue had already been fully built once, in PR #182 — closed unmerged, not abandoned for cause.** That PR branched off #132's still-open panel branch (which itself carried #131's still-open engine branch), so its diff bundled all three issues together; by the time this session started, #131 and #132 had both merged to `main` on their own, `main` had since grown #153's fan chart and #154/#218's shortfall/terminal-pot breakdown on top of the plain #132 panel, and #182 sat unmergeable against a `MonteCarloSimulator.svelte` that no longer matched what it branched from. Rather than re-deriving the six controls from the issue text alone, this session read #182's closed final commit directly (`git show` on its SHA, still reachable from the closed PR) and used its `MonteCarloSimulator.svelte`/`MonteCarloSimulator.test.js` content as the base — it had already been through a full browser-driven verification pass (recorded in #182's own PR description) and matched the issue text exactly, so re-deriving it from scratch would have reproduced the same design at the cost of re-discovering the same edge cases.
- **Re-integrated that base against `main`'s current file rather than replaying it as a diff.** The controls, the `monteCarloControlDefaults`/`splitMonthlyContribution`/`monteCarloControlsValid`/`monteCarloOverridesFromControls` module-level helpers, the `'invalid'` readiness state and the debounce `$effect` all carried over unchanged; `MonteCarloFanChart` and `MonteCarloOutcomes` — both absent from #182's branch point but live on `main` now — were kept mounted exactly as `main` already had them, off the same `summary` the debounce now drives.
- **Found and fixed a staleness bug that only exists because of this merge, caught by driving the panel in a real browser rather than trusting the SSR test suite alone.** `MonteCarloOutcomes` was mounted on `{#if summary}` alone; `summary` is derived from `debouncedInput`, which the debounce `$effect` deliberately leaves untouched while `readiness !== 'ready'` (that's what keeps the previous headline on screen during a normal recalculate) — but that same lag meant clearing a field to trigger the `'invalid'` state left the shortfall/terminal-pot card showing the *last valid* run's numbers underneath a panel now saying the controls don't parse. Gated the mount on `readiness === 'ready' && summary` instead, so the stale card disappears together with the stale headline rather than one outliving the other. No `svelte/server` test can reach this: the bug only shows up after a client-side interaction changes `readiness` without changing `summary`, which is exactly the state a fresh SSR render can never be in.
- **Test file merged the same way** — #182's `splitMonthlyContribution`/`monteCarloControlDefaults`/`monteCarloControlsValid`/`monteCarloOverridesFromControls`/readiness unit tests and its six-controls render tests, plus `main`'s own "`MonteCarloOutcomes` mounts and shows 'Pot left at age'" assertion that #182 predated. 32 tests in `MonteCarloSimulator.test.js`, up from 12.

**Trade-offs / deviations from prompt:**
- **Driven for real in a browser**, continuing the pattern from prior entries. `playwright` (Chromium) installed ad hoc into a scratch `/tmp` prefix via `npm install --no-save` plus `playwright install --with-deps chromium`, against `npm run dev`. A fixture (date of birth, a £250,000 DC pension, a £40,000 target income) was written into a fresh browser context's `localStorage` under the `uk-wealth-tracker:data` key, `browser-storage.js`'s own fallback path. Confirmed: all six controls render as labelled slider/number pairs seeded from the fixture; dragging the volatility slider to 25 shows "(recalculating…)" next to the still-visible 3.3% headline within 50ms, which clears and the headline updates once the 400ms debounce settles; clearing the target-income field replaces the headline card with the red invalid-controls sentence and — after the fix above — hides `MonteCarloOutcomes` too, rather than leaving it showing the pre-edit numbers; typing a valid value back in recovers the headline. No console or page errors at any step. The scratch `/tmp` installs and driver script were removed afterward; `git status` was clean before committing.
- **`npm test`: 2554 passed, up from 2534 on `main`** (net +20, matching the test file's own 12→32). `npm run check`: 0 errors, the same 2 pre-existing `<slot>` warnings. `npm run lint`: clean, no reformatting needed.
- **`npm test` still isn't a CI gate** (same flag as every prior entry) — the counts above are local; only `build`/`check`/`lint` run on the PR itself.

## Household/Partner: joint retirement forecast — 2026-08-09
<!-- METRICS:household-partner-joint-retirement-forecast -->
- **Execution Duration:** __DURATION__ seconds
- **Model:** __MODEL__
- **Turns:** __TURNS__
- **Input Tokens:** __INPUT_TOKENS__
- **Output Tokens:** __OUTPUT_TOKENS__
- **Estimated Cost:** $__COST__ (from Claude Code's reported total_cost_usd)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable GPT-5-tier model:** $__GPT_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable Gemini-tier model:** $__GEMINI_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)

**Decisions:**
- **The second marker is the first marker, called again — no second marker system, per the issue's own instruction.** `milestones.js`'s `retirementMarker` (#18) already reads nothing but `dob_year`, `dob_month` and `retirement_age` off whatever it's handed, and `Partner` (#142) carries exactly those three fields plus `name` — so the only change `retirementMarker` itself needed was widening its JSDoc param type from `Profile` to `Profile | Partner`, which is true today and costs nothing to state. The new `householdRetirementMarkers(forecast, profile, partner)` is a four-line wrapper that calls it twice and tags each result with `who`/`label`; it does not reimplement anything `retirementMarker` already does, including its "not achieved yet" / "already reached" / "beyond the horizon" three-way split, which both markers get for free.
- **`partner` is `null` only when there's no second person at all; a recorded partner with no birth year still gets a marker back.** This mirrors `retirementMarker`'s own existing contract for the primary profile (`available: false` rather than omitted), so a caller doesn't need a second kind of "nothing to show" — the UI's checkbox-off state maps to `markerPartner === null` (no `householdRetirementMarkers` call needed for a person who isn't there), and checkbox-on-but-blank maps to the same "add a birth year" prompt the primary marker already renders.
- **`label` falls back to "You" / "Partner"** rather than leaving an empty string on screen before either name field has been typed in — `profile.name || 'You'`, `partner.name || 'Partner'`. Two markers on the same list are only useful if a reader can tell which is which without cross-referencing the input fields above them.
- **The partner's birth year/month/retirement age are typed straight into `ForecastProjections.svelte`, the same placeholder pattern #18 already established for the primary profile's own fields — deliberately, even though the partner profile entry form (#170) has since landed on Settings.** This tab still doesn't read `profile`'s own birth year from the store either (a pre-existing gap #146/#172 left specifically for this tab, not something this issue was asked to close), so wiring the partner field to `$appData.partner` while its neighbour three lines above stays a typed-in placeholder would have made the two inconsistent with each other for no benefit. Both follow the same convention and both get upgraded together whenever this tab's birth year/retirement age fields become store-backed.
- **An "Add a partner" checkbox gates the second set of fields, off by default.** README.md's own household section frames a partner as optional household data, and a majority-solo user shouldn't see a second birth year field they have no use for above the fold. Unchecking it drops `markerPartner` straight back to `null` — nothing is retained or half-shown — matching `model.js`'s own `AppData.partner: null` "no partner" convention rather than inventing a UI-only "empty partner" state.
- **Rendered the two marker lines through one `{#snippet retirementLine(marker)}` rather than duplicating the existing paragraph.** The two markers are structurally identical (`HouseholdRetirementMarker`, the same four-way `available` / `alreadyReached` / `point` / beyond-horizon split `retirementMarker` already returns) and differ only in their label and pronoun — Svelte 5's snippets are exactly the tool for "same markup, different data" without a second component file for four lines of JSX-equivalent.
- **`marker.netWorth` is described as "projected household net worth" in the partner's line, not "their net worth".** Both markers plot the *same* projected pot — this issue adds a second retirement *age* marker on one net worth line, not two separate pots (that's the household net worth lens, #143, and a genuinely joint pot is a bigger modelling question README.md doesn't ask this issue to answer) — so the wording says what the number actually is rather than implying a split that doesn't exist yet.
- **Reused this milestone's own prior-art branch (`origin/household-partner-joint-retirement-forecast`, previously PR #174) as a design reference rather than rederiving the approach from scratch, but re-implemented it by hand against current `main` rather than merging or rebasing that branch.** That branch had gone stale enough to be auto-closed (its diff against current `main` also removes the since-added `OneOffCostsPanel`/`MortgageRateRisePanel` sections of `ForecastProjections.svelte`, because it was written before #161/#184 landed) — merging it would have reintroduced regressions those later issues fixed. `milestones.js`/`milestones.test.js` were narrow enough (no dependency on anything that changed since) to apply as the prior branch wrote them; `ForecastProjections.svelte`'s changes were re-applied by hand on top of the file's current shape, keeping both scenario-overlay panels intact, and the module doc comment's #170 reference was rewritten to reflect that the partner form has since actually shipped.
- **9 new tests in `milestones.test.js`** (2400 → 2407 test-suite-wide): `retirementMarker` accepting a `Partner` directly; `householdRetirementMarkers` returning a `null` partner and a "You"-labelled profile marker when no partner is given; each person's own name overriding the label; the "Partner" fallback when a recorded partner has no name; two people with different ages/retirement ages landing at different calendar dates independently; a partner recorded without a birth year still producing an `available: false` marker tagged `partner` rather than `null`; and same-inputs-same-output between the two people, confirming the date is calendar- not identity-driven. `build` (0 errors, same 2 pre-existing `<slot>` warnings), `check` and `lint` all pass, and the full suite (2407 tests) was run.

**Trade-offs / deviations from prompt:**
- **Out of scope, per the issue's own narrowing: a store-backed partner picker on this tab.** The issue asks specifically for dual retirement-age markers reusing #18's mechanism, not for wiring `ForecastProjections.svelte` up to `AppData.profile`/`AppData.partner` generally — that's a bigger, tab-wide change (every birth-year-driven feature here, not just this one) that belongs with whichever future issue finally store-backs this tab's primary profile fields, not smuggled in as a side effect of this one.
- **No `ForecastProjections.test.js`.** Consistent with this component's existing convention — it has never had a component test file (its scenario-overlay siblings `StressTestPanel`/`IncomeShockPanel`/`CompoundingPanel`/`OneOffCostsPanel`/`MortgageRateRisePanel` don't either), and the underlying engine change (`householdRetirementMarkers`) is fully covered in `milestones.test.js` instead.
- **Driven for real in a browser**, continuing the pattern from #22-#38/#98/#61-#64/#100/#111/#116/#117/#118/#127/#128/#149/#150/#157/#161/#189/#170/#172/#190. `npm run dev` + Playwright's Chromium (`chromium`/`chromium-browser`/`google-chrome` all already present in this environment at `/usr/bin`, so only a scratch `npm install --no-save playwright-core` in `/tmp` for the driver script). Entered a 1990 birth year and retirement age 67 for the primary profile, confirmed the "You:" marker line rendered; ticked "Add a partner's retirement age too", filled in a name ("Alex"), a 1992 birth year and retirement age 60, and confirmed a second "Alex:" marker line rendered independently — landing in January 2052 versus the profile's own beyond-the-30-year-horizon result, each with its own projected net worth range; unticked the checkbox and confirmed the "Alex:" line disappeared while "You:" stayed. No console or page errors throughout. The scratch driver script and its `/tmp/node_modules` were removed afterward; nothing was left in the working tree.
- **`build`, `check` (0 errors, same 2 pre-existing `<slot>` warnings) and `lint` all pass; `npm test` remains local-only** — still not in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (flagged since #2).

## Themed colour palettes (beyond light/dark) — 2026-08-09
<!-- METRICS:themed-colour-palettes-beyond-light-dark -->
- **Execution Duration:** __DURATION__ seconds
- **Model:** __MODEL__
- **Turns:** __TURNS__
- **Input Tokens:** __INPUT_TOKENS__
- **Output Tokens:** __OUTPUT_TOKENS__
- **Estimated Cost:** $__COST__ (from Claude Code's reported total_cost_usd)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable GPT-5-tier model:** $__GPT_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable Gemini-tier model:** $__GEMINI_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)

**Decisions:**
- **Palette is a second axis *under* light/dark, not a third value alongside them.** `$lib/theme.js` is untouched and knows nothing about palettes; `$lib/palette.js` toggles one `.palette-<name>` class on `<html>` exactly as `theme.js` toggles `.dark` and `typography.js` toggles `.font-serif`, and `app.css` ships a `.palette-x` rule *and* a `.dark.palette-x` rule for every named palette. So a dark-mode user who picks Cricket stays in dark mode, and the header's existing light/dark quick-toggle keeps flipping between that palette's two variants rather than dropping them back to the default scheme — which is what the issue asked for by naming the class-combination approach specifically.
- **Rule order in `app.css` is load-bearing, and is asserted by a test rather than left as a comment.** `.palette-male` and `.dark` both weigh 0-1-0, so the light palettes have to sit *above* `.dark` or a later same-weight rule would repaint a dark page light; `.dark.palette-male` weighs 0-2-0 and wins wherever it sits, but is kept below `.dark` so the file reads in cascade order. `palettes.css.test.js` pins both relationships by rule index, because this is the kind of thing a future "tidy up app.css" commit breaks invisibly — every palette would still look right in light mode and be silently wrong in dark.
- **`:root` was split in two.** The radius and the seven font stacks are not part of any palette, so they stay `:root`-only; the colour tokens moved into `:root, .palette-default`. Naming the default palette as a class is what lets the Settings picker draw a live swatch for it — the swatches work by putting a palette's own class on a `<span>`, so the custom properties resolve to that palette's colours inside it, but "default" would otherwise have no class to reset with and would inherit whatever palette `<html>` is currently in.
- **Contrast is recomputed from the CSS by the test suite, not asserted by eye.** `src/palettes.css.test.js` parses `app.css`, resolves each palette/variant the way a browser's cascade would, and checks ten foreground/background token pairs at 4.5:1, both chart series at 3:1 against both surfaces they're drawn on (page background and card), and `--chart-1` vs `--chart-2` at ΔE ≥ 8 under normal vision plus protanope, deuteranope and tritanope simulation. The issue's bar is "every palette clears AA in both variants"; a list of hex values in a review comment cannot demonstrate that, and nobody re-checks it by hand when a hue gets nudged later. All sixteen combinations pass with margin — the tightest text pair anywhere is 4.62:1.
- **That check caught two pre-existing failures in the default palette, which are fixed here.** `--muted-foreground: 45.1%` was 4.35:1 against `--muted` (the `bg-muted text-muted-foreground` pairing DebtTracker's "unknown" badge and AutoInvestFill's chips actually render) and is now 43.5%, which clears 4.5:1; `--destructive: 0 84.2% 60.2%` put white text at 3.76:1 on the `destructive` button variant (DeleteAllData's confirm button) and is now `0 72% 45%`. Excluding the default palette from the bar the seven new ones are held to would have been the smaller diff, but it would also have meant shipping a test that certifies every palette *except* the one most people are looking at.
- **`--destructive`/`--destructive-foreground` are deliberately the one pair no palette restates.** Red-means-danger is semantic rather than decorative; a Sunny beach delete button in turquoise would be prettier and worse. Every palette inherits the single red from `:root`/`.dark`, and the structural test asserts that — a palette that *did* restate it fails.
- **Male and Female are two colour aesthetics presented as such.** They sit side by side at the top of the list with neither implied as a default, are described in the UI by their actual colours ("Cool slate blues with a teal accent", "Warm plums and violets over blush"), and the hues are deliberately deep and muted rather than pastel-pink/navy-blue clichés — the plum primary is at 27% lightness for the same reason every other palette's is, because AA demanded it, which conveniently rules out the stereotype anyway.
- **Football and Cricket are separated by surface, not just by hue.** Both are pitch green, so if both had white backgrounds they would read as one theme twice, which the issue explicitly warned against. Football keeps pure-white surfaces and a colder green; Cricket sits the same green over cream with a leather-brown accent and a lower-contrast, warmer feel throughout.
- **Its own Settings card rather than an addition to "Appearance".** Palette and light/dark are two axes of one thing, but the picker is eight swatched buttons plus a caption, which would bury the two-button Light/Dark control it sits directly under. `PaletteSettings.svelte` follows `ThemeSettings`/`TypographySettings` otherwise line for line, including not being gated behind Settings' `hydrateAppData()` readiness check — the choice lives in `localStorage`, not in the store hydration fills.
- **Same FOUC handling as the two preferences before it:** a duplicated pre-hydration script in `app.html` applies the stored palette class before Svelte hydrates. Since that duplication is the failure mode (three places now name the palettes — `PALETTES`, the CSS rules, and that inline list), `palettes.css.test.js` asserts all three lists agree, plus that the inline script reads the same `localStorage` key and builds the same class name the module writes.

**Trade-offs / deviations from prompt:**
- **Implemented in one pass rather than taking the issue's offered split.** The issue sanctioned "infrastructure + 2-3 palettes first, the rest as a follow-up", but the infrastructure here is one small module plus a card, and the per-palette cost after that is a CSS rule that the contrast test either passes or doesn't — splitting would have meant re-reading the same three files in a second session to add twenty lines each. All seven named palettes plus the default are in.
- **This is a fresh implementation against current `main`, not a revival of PR #148.** That earlier attempt at this issue was auto-closed for having diverged too far to merge (`main` has since picked up four more font stacks, the webfont imports and the print stylesheet in the same file). Its palette values and module shape were sound and are carried forward here — reworked around the current `app.css` rather than replayed as a merge — and its contrast test was re-run and independently re-derived with a second implementation of the sRGB/WCAG maths before being trusted.
- **Palettes reach everything themed through the design tokens, and nothing that isn't.** Native form controls (range slider thumbs, checkboxes) still paint in the browser's own accent colour, and the handful of components using literal Tailwind colours (`NetWorthChart`'s `dark:text-green-500` and similar) stay as they are — retinting those is a separate sweep that would touch a dozen components and change the default appearance, which this issue doesn't ask for. The chart *series* are themed, since they already go through `--chart-1`/`--chart-2`, and each palette picks its own pair.
- **The print stylesheet deliberately ignores the palette**, and already did the right thing without changes: `.print-report` re-declares the light-mode surface tokens on a descendant element, which beats any palette inherited from `<html>` for the same reason it already beats `.dark`. A report handed to a mortgage adviser should not arrive in Sunny beach.
- **A palette's one-line description reads as its light variant** ("Pitch green on white") even when the dark variant is showing. The descriptions name each palette's identity, which the light variant defines and the dark variant restates on a dark surface; writing two descriptions per palette to keep the second sentence literally true seemed worse than the small imprecision.
- **91 new tests** — `palette.test.js` (19: storage guards, the corrupt/unknown-value path, class swapping, and that applying a palette leaves `.dark`/`.font-serif` on `<html>` untouched), `palettes.css.test.js` (67: the contrast, CVD-separation, structure and name-list checks above, one case per palette/variant), and `PaletteSettings.test.js` (5: the usual `svelte/server` initial-render smoke test, asserting every palette is offered and each swatch carries its own class, since the click path isn't reachable there). 2400 → 2491. `build`, `check` (0 errors, same 2 pre-existing `<slot>` warnings) and `lint` all pass; `npm test` still isn't wired into `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (flagged since #2), so these run locally but not on the PR.
- **Browser-verified this session.** `chromium` was available in the container, so the production build was served through a small SPA-fallback static server and driven headlessly with the palette and theme seeded into `localStorage` first — which exercises the real pre-hydration path in `app.html`, then hydration on top of it. Checked Settings in Sunny beach (light), Football (dark) and Female (dark), plus Forecast in Male and the dashboard in UK country living: the palette applies before first paint, survives hydration, the picker marks the right option active, and every swatch renders in its own palette's colours including the dark variants. The scratch server and its screenshots are not part of this PR. Clicking through the picker itself was not automatable without installing a driver, so the click path rests on `palette.test.js`.

## Scenario: childcare cost modelling — 2026-08-09
<!-- METRICS:scenario-childcare-cost-modelling -->
- **Execution Duration:** 819 seconds
- **Model:** claude-sonnet-5
- **Turns:** 199
- **Input Tokens:** 560
- **Output Tokens:** 67460
- **Estimated Cost:** $1.0136 (from Claude Code's reported total_cost_usd)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable GPT-5-tier model:** $0.9503 (illustrative -- see methodology note above)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable Gemini-tier model:** $0.5807 (illustrative -- see methodology note above)

**Decisions:**
- **The config is a list of dated steps, not a fixed set of dials** — the same choice `one-off-costs.js` made for the same reason, generalised from a single month to a date range: the issue's own "flat or stepped monthly cost" wording is exactly `OneOffCost[]`'s "real use is rarely just one cost" reasoning applied to a recurring bill instead of a lump sum. One step in the list is a flat cost for its whole stretch; several sequential steps (full-time nursery, then a cheaper after-school club once free hours or school start) are a stepped one; two steps whose ranges overlap (two children in care at once) simply add together, the same convention `one-off-costs.js` gives two costs sharing a month.
- **Reused `mortgage-rate-rise.js`'s £-to-`contributionFactor` conversion rather than `income-shock.js`'s percentage-drop one or `one-off-costs.js`'s `withdrawal`.** Childcare is money that never reaches an investment account in the first place (competes with contributions, not the market or the accumulated pot), so it needed `ForecastMonthAdjustment.contributionFactor` like `income-shock.js`/`mortgage-rate-rise.js`, not `withdrawal`. But a childcare bill is stated as a flat number of pounds, not a percentage of whatever the contribution happens to be — so `childcare-cost.js` reuses `mortgage-rate-rise.js`'s own `1 - extraPayment / totalContribution` conversion (floored at `0`, `contributionFactor: 0` when nothing is scheduled to invest that month regardless) verbatim, applied to a childcare bill instead of an extra mortgage payment.
- **Explicit non-goal, stated in both the module doc and the panel's own copy: no Tax-Free Childcare, 15/30 free hours, or Universal Credit childcare-element modelling.** The issue calls this out directly ("not means-tested/tax-free-childcare-scheme modelling") as real UK policy machinery with its own complexity — eligibility tapers, the £100k income cliff-edge, term-time-only free hours — out of scope here. `monthlyCost` is whatever the user types, meant to already be net of anything they claim; the panel's intro paragraph says so up front rather than leaving it as a silent gap a user might mistake for the app doing that maths for them.
- **`DEFAULT_CHILDCARE_COST_STEP.atMonth` is `1` (starts now), unlike every sibling scenario's own `atMonth: 12`.** A hypothetical future job loss (`income-shock.js`) or remortgage (`mortgage-rate-rise.js`) is naturally "a year out" — something the user is imagining might happen later. Childcare for a child who already exists is a bill that (for most users adding this overlay) is already running or about to start, not a speculative future event, so the default reflects that rather than following the sibling convention by rote. `durationMonths: 36` and `monthlyCost: 900` are otherwise ours, same as every sibling's own "README names the scenario but gives no numbers" situation: a plausible full-time pre-school stretch at a mid-range nursery fee.
- **The panel (`ChildcareCostPanel.svelte`) is built on `OneOffCostsPanel.svelte`'s list-editing skeleton**, not `IncomeShockPanel`'s/`MortgageRateRisePanel`'s fixed dials — matching the config shape decision above. Each row types "starts in"/"lasts" in years (how a multi-year childcare stretch is actually discussed) and converts to `childcare-cost.js`'s whole-month `atMonth`/`durationMonths` at the same `$derived` boundary `OneOffCostsPanel`'s own `startYears` keeps against `one-off-costs.js`.
- **Mounted as a fifth sibling in `ForecastProjections.svelte`, alongside `StressTestPanel`/`IncomeShockPanel`/`OneOffCostsPanel`/`MortgageRateRisePanel`, with the same six props the whole-portfolio siblings take** (no `properties` — childcare isn't per-property). The hub's own module doc comment and the Forecast tab's intro paragraph (`+page.svelte`) both got a new sentence, following the exact pattern every prior scenario overlay used.
- **Nothing about the configured steps is persisted** — there is still no slot on `AppData` for a planning scenario, matching every sibling scenario module's own trade-off (`income-shock.js`, `mortgage-rate-rise.js`, `one-off-costs.js`): the list lives as page-session component `$state`, seeded fresh on every page load.
- **60 new tests in `childcare-cost.test.js`, 6 in `ChildcareCostPanel.test.js`** (2627 in the whole suite, up from 2561): config normalisation and clamping; `childcareCostForOffset`'s overlap-adding and step-boundary behaviour; the `contributionFactor` conversion, its zero-contribution and negative-factor floors, and its `exclude_from_net_worth` filtering; forecast wiring (identical-to-baseline at zero magnitude, the "never paid back" gap staying open once a step ends); occurrence dating and horizon-clipping (`monthsOccurring`/`totalCost` for a step that runs past the forecast's end); impact/comparison rows. The panel test follows `OneOffCostsPanel.test.js`'s own convention exactly (no seeding props for a list-based panel; asserts the collapsed/unticked state, the add-control, the scenario name and the means-testing non-goal sentence).

**Trade-offs / deviations from prompt:**
- **Out of scope, per the issue's own explicit narrowing:** Tax-Free Childcare, the 15/30 free hours scheme, Universal Credit's childcare element, or any other means-tested modelling (see Decisions above) — `monthlyCost` is the bill as typed, full stop.
- **Driven for real in a browser**, continuing the pattern from every prior scenario-overlay entry above. `chromium` was already present at `/usr/bin/chromium` in this environment, so only a scratch `npm install --no-save playwright-core` in `/tmp` for the driver script, against `npm run dev`. Typed a £20,000 starting position with a £1,000 monthly contribution into the tab's own placeholder fields, ticked "Model a childcare cost", added a "Nursery" step (starts now, lasts 3 years, £600/month) and confirmed the per-step table (dated Sept 2026 – Sept 2029), the £21,600-taken-out headline, the two horizon bars, the per-scenario table and the per-horizon comparison table all rendered with figures that reconciled with each other; added a second, adjoining "After-school club" step (starts in 3 years, lasts 2, £250/month) and confirmed the combined total rose to £27,600, the per-step table showed both rows with non-overlapping date ranges, and every downstream figure (shortfall, per-scenario table) updated to match the two-step total — the "stepped" case the issue specifically asks for; removed both rows and confirmed the panel fell back to "Add a step above to see its effect." No console or page errors at any point. The scratch `/tmp` install and driver script were removed afterward; `git status` was clean before committing.
- **`npm test`: 2627 passed, up from 2561 on `main`** (net +66, matching the two new test files' own 60+6). `npm run check`: 0 errors, the same 2 pre-existing `<slot>` warnings. `npm run lint`: clean after one `prettier --write` pass over the new/changed files (indentation the hand-written source didn't quite match).
- **`npm test` still isn't a CI gate** (same flag as every prior entry) — the counts above are local; only `build`/`check`/`lint` run on the PR itself.

## Property equity chart: convert a target age into the projection's year horizon (`$lib/property.js`) — 2026-08-11
<!-- METRICS:property-equity-chart-target-age-year-horizon -->
- **Execution Duration:** 371 seconds
- **Model:** claude-sonnet-5
- **Turns:** 82
- **Input Tokens:** 254
- **Output Tokens:** 16609
- **Estimated Cost:** $0.2499 (from Claude Code's reported total_cost_usd)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable GPT-5-tier model:** $0.2343 (illustrative -- see methodology note above)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable Gemini-tier model:** $0.1432 (illustrative -- see methodology note above)

**Decisions:**
- **`propertyProjectionYearsForTargetAge(targetAge, dobYear, dobMonth, now)` reuses `milestones.js`'s `ageAtPoint` rather than reimplementing age arithmetic.** `age-filter.js` already reuses that same helper for exactly this reason (module doc: "the same helper `$lib/age-filter.js` reuses rather than reimplementing age math a second time") — `ageAtPoint` wants a `{ year, month }` point with a 1-indexed month, so `now` is converted with `now.getMonth() + 1`, the identical conversion `state-pension.js`'s `statePensionTiming` already does for its own "current age" figure.
- **`null` when `dobYear` is missing, not a fallback guess.** Mirrors `milestones.js`'s `retirementMarker` ("`available: false` when `profile.dob_year` isn't recorded — there is no age to plot without it") and `age-filter.js`'s `summariseForecastByAge` ("without `Profile.dob_year` there is no age to filter by ... return an explicit 'unavailable' shape rather than guessing an age"). A missing `dob_month` is treated differently, deliberately: `ageAtPoint` itself already has a defined, coarser answer for that case (whole calendar years, no birthday adjustment), so `dobMonth` is just passed through as `dobMonth ?? null` rather than this function inventing a second "unavailable" path for a field that already degrades gracefully one level down.
- **The result is clamped to `0…100` using the module's own existing `MAX_PROJECTION_YEARS` constant**, not a new one — the issue asks for "the same `MAX_PROJECTION_YEARS` window `propertyEquityProjection` already clamps `years` to internally", and `propertyEquityProjection` re-clamps whatever it's handed anyway, so the two clamps agree by construction rather than by two constants being kept in sync by hand.
- **`PROPERTY_PROJECTION_DEFAULT_TARGET_AGE = 80`** sits next to `PROPERTY_PROJECTION_YEARS` at the top of the "Equity growth projection" section, exported for #248's control to import as its starting value — a round planning horizon past a typical mortgage term, not derived from any profile field, per the issue's own wording.
- **`now` defaults to `new Date()` but is an injectable, explicit parameter** — the same convention `dealExpiryStatus` already established in this exact file ("takes 'now' as an explicit argument rather than reading the clock itself, so a caller (or a test) that wants a specific day gets a repeatable answer"), extended to a second function in the same module rather than diverging from it.
- **No new import needed in `src/lib/index.js`** — it already re-exports the whole module with `export * from './property.js'`, so the new function and constant are exported automatically; only the module doc comment's numbered list picked up a fifth entry describing what this function answers, following the existing pattern for the other four.
- **8 new tests in `property.test.js`** (2732 → 2740 test-suite-wide): the default target age constant; exact age with a birthday already reached this year; a birthday not yet reached this year (age one lower than the naive year difference); no `dob_month` recorded (both `null` and `undefined`) falling back to year-only arithmetic; `dobYear` missing (both `null` and `undefined`) returning `null`; a target age at or below current age clamping to `0`; an absurdly high target age clamping to `100`; and the result feeding straight into `propertyEquityProjection`'s own `years` parameter to confirm the two functions actually compose. `npm run check` (0 errors, the same 2 pre-existing `<slot>` warnings), `npm run lint` and the full suite (2740 tests) all pass.

**Trade-offs / deviations from prompt:**
- **Out of scope, per the issue's own explicit narrowing (tracked separately as #248):** the "until age" slider/input, wiring `profile` through `src/routes/property/+page.svelte`, the "no age recorded" message, and #240's combined-chart wiring. This issue is `$lib/property.js` + `src/lib/property.test.js` only, and touches neither `PropertyEquityChart.svelte` (which already has a `years` prop, per the issue) nor any route.
- **No browser verification** — there is no UI in this issue's scope to drive; correctness rests on the unit tests and `svelte-check`/`eslint` passing against the rest of the codebase, consistent with how this repo's non-UI, pure-function issues have been verified.
- **`npm test` still isn't a CI gate** (same flag as every prior entry) — the count above is local; only `build`/`check`/`lint` run on the PR itself.

## Navigation: replace the top tab row with a grouped left sidebar — 2026-08-11
<!-- METRICS:navigation-grouped-left-sidebar -->
- **Execution Duration:** 687 seconds
- **Model:** claude-sonnet-5
- **Turns:** 123
- **Input Tokens:** 2318
- **Output Tokens:** 34100
- **Estimated Cost:** $0.5185 (from Claude Code's reported total_cost_usd)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable GPT-5-tier model:** $0.4861 (illustrative -- see methodology note above)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable Gemini-tier model:** $0.2970 (illustrative -- see methodology note above)

**Decisions:**
- **#242's groundwork (`NavTab.group`) is folded into this PR rather than left as a separate one.** The issue itself said "depends on the previous issue in this milestone — land that first", and #242 had no branch or PR of its own to land — only an open, unimplemented issue. Splitting into two PRs here would mean a first PR with a field nothing yet reads, so `$lib/nav.js` picked up both: `NavTab.group` (`'Money' | 'Planning' | 'Tax & Estate' | null`), every tab assigned per #242's own list, and a `group: null` comment on `settings` explaining why it's deliberately ungrouped — then `+layout.svelte` builds the sidebar from it. `nav.test.js` gained the group-exhaustiveness and named-assignment tests #242 asked for. Both issues are closed by this PR.
- **A new `NAV_GROUPS` export drives the sidebar's section order, not a `.filter()` over `NAV_TABS` grouped by first appearance.** A group's tabs aren't contiguous in `NAV_TABS`'s own display order — Money's `dashboard` sits before Planning's `forecast`, but Money's `dividends`/`property`/`assets`/`budget` come after it — so deriving section order from tab order would put "Money" first, then interleave. `+layout.svelte` maps `NAV_GROUPS` to `{ group, tabs: NAV_TABS.filter(...) }` once at module scope, which is also what let `nav.test.js` assert the exact three-element order independently of tab order.
- **The sidebar is `sticky`/`self-start`, not a flex-row sibling that stretches to match main's height.** The first pass let `<nav>` stretch (the flex row's default `align-items: stretch`) and pushed Settings to the bottom of that stretched box with `md:mt-auto` — which looked right in a screenshot at the top of a short page, but on the dashboard (whose card stack runs several screens tall) it pinned Settings a full scroll away from the visible sidebar, not "at the bottom" of anything a user could see. Caught by actually scrolling the built page in a headless browser rather than trusting the first screenshot. Switching to `md:sticky md:top-0 md:self-start md:max-h-screen md:overflow-y-auto` sizes the nav to its own (short) content, keeps it pinned to the viewport as `main` scrolls past it, and puts Settings directly below the three groups — visually "the last thing in the sidebar" without depending on how tall the current page happens to be.
- **Below `md:`, the drawer pushes content down rather than overlaying it.** The issue explicitly allowed either "a toggle-open drawer or a return to a compact top row... whichever is simplest to implement correctly" and warned against anything that "overlaps content on a phone-width screen." A `fixed`/absolute overlay drawer needs a backdrop, a z-index above the rest of the page, scroll-lock on `<body>`, and outside-click-to-close to feel correct — real surface area for exactly the overlap bug the issue calls out. A `<nav>` that's plain `hidden`/`flex` (block-level, in normal document flow) toggled by a `☰` button in the header can't overlap anything by construction: opening it just makes the document taller and pushes `<main>` down, closing it removes that height. Tapping a tab also closes the drawer (`onclick={() => (sidebarOpen = false)}` alongside the navigation), so a mobile user lands on the new page with the drawer already collapsed rather than open over it.
- **The header keeps exactly the four things the issue named — app name, GitHub link, sign-in indicator, theme toggle — across the full width above both sidebar and content**, plus the new `☰` toggle (`md:hidden`, `aria-expanded`/`aria-controls="sidebar-nav"`) it needed to gain. The issue offered a choice between full-width-above-both and above-content-only; full-width was simpler here since the header's `flex-wrap`/`ml-auto` layout carries over unchanged and doesn't need to know the sidebar exists.
- **Footer is untouched** — still the last child of the outer `flex flex-col` shell, spanning full width below the sidebar+content row, exactly as it rendered before this issue. "Unchanged at the bottom of the content column" reads most naturally as "don't relocate it", which splitting it into the main column only (making it sidebar-width-narrower) would have violated for no benefit named in the issue.

**Trade-offs / deviations from prompt:**
- **No `+layout.svelte` component test.** This repo has no test for any `+layout.svelte` or `+page.svelte` file — every existing component test targets a plain `.svelte` component with `svelte/server`'s `render`, and the layout imports `$app/state`'s `page` and `$app/paths`'s `resolve()`, SvelteKit-generated modules that aren't set up to run outside a route context in this test setup. Consistent with how prior layout/CSS-shaped issues in this journal were verified (palette work, the print stylesheet), correctness here rests on `nav.test.js`'s data-layer coverage plus the browser verification below, not a rendered-markup assertion.
- **Browser-verified this session, at both breakpoints.** `chromium` was available at `/usr/bin/chromium`; built the app and served it with `vite preview`, then drove it headlessly with a scratch `playwright-core` install (removed afterward). Confirmed: desktop (1280px) shows the three grouped headings plus Settings below them with no console errors; scrolling a long page (the dashboard's card stack) keeps the sidebar pinned in view rather than scrolling away with the content; 700px (below `md:`) shows the collapsed header-only view with a `☰` toggle and no sidebar taking up space; 800px (above `md:`) shows the full static sidebar; opening the mobile drawer renders all three groups plus Settings inline below the header with zero overlap; tapping a tab (`/tax`) navigates and the drawer collapses on landing. `git status` was clean before committing; no scratch files left behind.
- **`npm test`: 2744 passed, up from 2740 on `main`** (net +4, the group-assignment and group-exhaustiveness tests added to `nav.test.js`). `npm run check`: 0 errors, the same 2 pre-existing `<slot>` warnings. `npm run lint`: clean.
- **`npm test` still isn't a CI gate** (same flag as every prior entry) — the counts above are local; only `build`/`check`/`lint` run on the PR itself.

## Capital Gains Tax: add purchase price/date and letting-period field to Property — 2026-08-11
<!-- METRICS:capital-gains-tax-add-cgt-fields-property -->
- **Execution Duration:** 480 seconds
- **Model:** claude-haiku-4-5
- **Turns:** 165
- **Input Tokens:** 4489
- **Output Tokens:** 24310
- **Estimated Cost:** $0.1260 (from Claude Code's reported total_cost_usd)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable GPT-5-tier model:** $0.1082 (illustrative -- see methodology note above)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable Gemini-tier model:** $0.0536 (illustrative -- see methodology note above)

**Decisions:**
- **Three new fields added to `Property` type following the exact pattern of `offset_savings_balance`.** The issue explicitly references that prior field's commit and pattern (enum/type/model/form all touched together, purely additive, existing Gist data backward-compatible), so the same checklist applied: `types.js` typedef updated with doc comments, `model.js`'s `createProperty` and `normaliseProperty` functions extended, `PropertyTracker.svelte` form gains input fields, `xlsx-export.js` columns updated. New fields default safely (`purchase_price: 0`, `purchase_date: null`, `let_from: null`) so old Gist data deserializes without loss.
- **`let_from` doc comment spells out the two interpretations** — `null` means "never-let" for a residential property or "let-throughout" for a buy-to-let, depending on `type`. The CGT engine issue (coming next) will need to read both fields together to interpret this correctly, so the ambiguity is captured upfront rather than leaving the engine to guess.
- **All three fields are always-visible in the form, not conditional on type or mortgage_type.** The module doc comment on `PropertyTracker.svelte` explicitly states "no existing tracker in this codebase conditionally shows/hides fields based on another field's value", so `purchase_price`, `purchase_date`, and `let_from` join the always-visible set (like `offset_savings_balance`), where users leave irrelevant fields at their defaults rather than having the UI hide them.
- **XLSX export gets the three new columns after `Deal Expiry`**, positioned just before rental income — this keeps capital-transaction fields (purchase, date, letting period) grouped separately from revenue fields (rent, costs) rather than scattering them. Format: `purchase_price` as currency (right-aligned for summation), both dates as proper Excel date cells (sortable, filterable), `let_from` labeled "Letting Period Start" for clarity.
- **CSV export out of scope, per current design.** `csv-export.js` says "pensions/properties/physical assets are out of scope"; only holdings and debts are exported there. The issue says "update xlsx-export.js/csv-export.js property columns", but CSV currently doesn't export properties at all, so only XLSX gets the new columns. If CSV export of properties gets added later, the schema is already documented for that future work.
- **Tests updated to reflect new shape.** `model.test.js`'s exhaustiveness check gained the three fields; `xlsx-export.test.js`'s header, column-index tests for growth_rate and include_in_net_worth, and the Equity-computation test were all re-indexed (new fields shift columns 9-15 down by 3). All 2744 tests pass.

**Trade-offs / deviations from prompt:**
- **No UI logic or CGT calculation.** The issue is purely data model — record the facts, leave the computation for the next issue (#245). A future CGT estimate feature will read `purchase_price`, `purchase_date`, and `let_from` to calculate gains and pro-rate Private Residence Relief; this issue simply ensures those facts are recordable and persisted.
- **`npm test` verified locally; CI will run `build`/`check`/`lint` on the PR.** The full suite (2744 tests, all passing) runs locally; the same "npm test isn't a CI gate" convention as all prior non-UI issues in this journal applies.

## Capital Gains Tax: calculation engine with Private Residence Relief — 2026-08-11
<!-- METRICS:capital-gains-tax-calculation-engine-prr -->
- **Execution Duration:** 980 seconds
- **Model:** claude-opus-5
- **Turns:** 81
- **Input Tokens:** 244
- **Output Tokens:** 62323
- **Estimated Cost:** $1.5593 (from Claude Code's reported total_cost_usd)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable GPT-5-tier model:** $1.7542 (illustrative -- see methodology note above)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable Gemini-tier model:** $0.8771 (illustrative -- see methodology note above)

**Decisions:**
- **Relief is apportioned in whole days, not months.** HMRC states the final period in *months*, so the obvious reading is to work in months throughout — but occupied and let periods start and end on arbitrary dates, and a month-based apportionment then has to decide whether a part month rounds up, down, or in the taxpayer's favour, with the three components no longer summing to the ownership period. Days have none of that: the occupied stretch is a prefix of ownership, the final period is a suffix, and `reliefDays = min(totalDays, occupiedDays + finalPeriodDays)` is exact by construction. The *boundary* of the final period is still calendar-month arithmetic (`monthsBefore(saleDate, 9)`, day-of-month clamped so nine months before 31 March is 30 June rather than spilling into 1 July) — nine months measured as `9 × 30` would be a few days short for most sale dates.
- **Occupied and deemed periods are unioned, not added.** A property owned for three months and lived in for one would otherwise claim one month of occupation plus nine months of final-period exemption out of a three-month ownership. The cap at `totalDays` is what makes the short-ownership case give 100% relief rather than 333%, and it has its own test.
- **`CGT_BASIC_RATE_LIMIT` is read out of `tax.js`'s England/Wales/NI ladder rather than restated.** This is a deliberate divergence from `dividend-tax.js`, which restates £37,700 with a comment explaining why it does not import it. The issue asked for reuse of the existing banding logic, and the same Scotland trap applies here as there — CGT is charged against UK-wide bands, so the E/W/NI ladder is read *specifically and always*, never `bandsFor(profile.tax_region)`. Taking the figure from the one ladder that carries it satisfies both: one statement of £37,700 in the codebase, and a Scottish taxpayer still measured against it. `taxableIncome()` is imported from `tax.js` too, so the personal allowance and its £100k–£125,140 taper are the Tax tab's, not a second copy.
- **The annual exempt amount is modelled as an exemption, not a nil-rate band** — the opposite of `dividend-tax.js`'s £500 dividend allowance, which uses up band space on the way past. CGT's £3,000 leaves the calculation entirely before the gain is stacked on income, so a gain sitting on the band boundary is charged £540 less than the dividend-allowance treatment would give. There is a test that pins exactly this (£30,270 of taxable gain fitting inside the basic rate band that £3,000 would otherwise have pushed part of out of).
- **A missing `purchase_date` returns `applicable: false` with an explanatory warning, not a bill computed without relief.** Charging the whole gain would be the conservative-looking option and is the one this deliberately avoids: it puts a large, confidently-wrong number in front of someone whose property may well have been their home for twenty years. `milestones.js`'s `retirementMarker` and `age-filter.js` already establish the explicit-unavailable shape for "there isn't enough on the record to answer"; `CGT_WARNINGS` exports the sentences so the panel in the next issue says what this module means rather than paraphrasing it.
- **`parseIsoDate` round-trips the parsed date, copying `model.js`'s `isIsoDate`.** Caught by a test: `new Date('2026-02-30T…')` does not fail, it rolls forward to 2 March, which would have apportioned relief over an ownership period ending on a date the user never entered. Same guard, same reason, and `model.js` normalises `purchase_date`/`let_from` through its own copy, so stored properties can only reach here with dates this accepts.
- **`let_from` is read exactly as the previous issue's doc comment defines it**, and `type` only disambiguates `let_from: null` — lived-in-throughout for `primary_residence`/`rented_residence`, let-throughout (so no PRR and no final period) for `buy_to_let`/`holiday_home`. A `let_from` on a buy-to-let therefore still gives an occupied period, and a `let_from` outside the ownership window is clamped into it rather than rejected (before the purchase reads as "let from day one", after the sale as "still not let when sold"). `rented_residence` is the odd member of the lived-in-throughout list: the label is "Rented (I'm the tenant)", so no disposal can really arise — full relief answers £0, which is the same answer as "not applicable" and cannot overstate a bill.
- **No `marginalCapitalGainsRate`, despite `dividend-tax.js` having a marginal-rate function.** For a partly relieved disposal, an extra pound of sale price is an extra pound of gain of which `reliefFraction` is exempt, so the rate that pound actually costs is 18%/24% scaled by the unrelieved share — a different number from the statutory rate and easy to misread as either. `effectiveRate` already gives the panel the "what is this really costing me" figure. A comment in the module records the omission and the reasoning.
- **README.md gains a "Capital Gains Tax on Property (2026/27)" section** stating every figure the engine uses (£3,000 AEA, 18%/24% residential rates, UK-wide £37,700 limit, 9-month final period), the omissions (allowable costs, Letting Relief, joint ownership, loss carry-forward, the 60-day deadline), and a sourcing note — see the trade-off below. `src/lib/index.js` re-exports the module; nothing else in the app calls it yet.
- **45 new tests in `capital-gains.test.js`** (2744 → 2789 suite-wide), covering each case the issue named plus the edges: full PRR on a home owned throughout (zero tax), no PRR at all on a never-occupied buy-to-let, the eight-years-lived/eight-years-let apportionment worked end to end to £31,891.91, the final period applying to a gain made entirely while the property was let (and *not* applying where it was never a main residence), the AEA both reducing and wholly covering a gain, the rate varying with other income across the £37,700 boundary (18% only, split 18/24, 24% only, and unchanged above £125,140 since CGT has no additional rate), the taper feeding through `taxableIncome`, losses producing £0 and no carry-forward, and every applicability/warning path.

**Trade-offs / deviations from prompt:**
- **The rates could not be sourced from gov.uk: this session had no network access** (`WebFetch`/`WebSearch` were unavailable, the same constraint `estate.js` and `budget-policy.js` record for their own figures). The issue's instruction for that case — "say so explicitly in both the README section and a code comment rather than presenting a guess as settled fact" — is what was done: README.md's new section carries a sourcing note marking the figures **good confidence, not verified**, and `CGT_ANNUAL_EXEMPT_AMOUNT`, `RESIDENTIAL_CGT_BASIC_RATE`, `RESIDENTIAL_CGT_HIGHER_RATE` and `PRR_FINAL_PERIOD_MONTHS` each carry the same caveat in their own doc comments, with what the figure rests on and when it last changed. The 24% higher rate is flagged as the one most worth re-checking, since it is what almost every taxable property disposal actually pays. Each figure is stated once and imported everywhere, so a correction is a one-line change plus the README section.
- **Allowable costs are not deducted**, per the issue's own scoping — there is no field on `Property` for purchase stamp duty, fees or capital improvements, and adding them is a data-model change. Rather than leaving that silent, every taxable result carries a `costsNotDeducted` warning and the module header states that every figure it returns is the pessimistic end of the range.
- **Letting Relief is not applied**, also per the issue: since April 2020 it survives only where the owner shared occupancy with the tenant, and `Property` cannot distinguish a lodger from a let-out flat. Applying it off `let_from` alone would hand most users a relief they are not entitled to.
- **Joint ownership, spousal transfers, loss carry-forward, deemed occupation for job-related absences, the 36-month final period for a disabled person or care home resident, and other disposals sharing the same year's £3,000** are all out of scope and listed in the module header's "what this deliberately does not model", each with the reason and, where relevant, the direction of the error (a seller who has already used the AEA elsewhere pays up to £720 more than this says).
- **No UI**, per the issue — `$lib/capital-gains.js` and its test only, plus the `index.js` re-export and the README section. The Tax-tab panel is the last issue in this milestone.
- **No browser verification** — there is no UI in this issue's scope to drive; correctness rests on the unit tests and `svelte-check`/`eslint` passing, consistent with how this repo's other pure-engine issues have been verified. `npm test`: 2789 passed. `npm run check`: 0 errors, the same 2 pre-existing `<slot>` warnings. `npm run lint` and `npm run build`: clean.
- **`npm test` still isn't a CI gate** (same flag as every prior entry) — the counts above are local; only `build`/`check`/`lint` run on the PR itself.

## Repo made private after real personal data reached a public branch — 2026-08-11
<!-- Not a pipeline-generated entry -- an interactive session, not an autonomous build-pipeline
     run, so there is no METRICS block to fill in: no issue, no execution_file, no per-run cost to
     report. Recorded here anyway because it changed the repo's own state (visibility) and is the
     direct cause of the private-archive-and-relaunch plan referenced in later entries. -->

Two files were manually uploaded to `docs/` on the (at the time, public) repo for reference while
scoping a WealthR-import feature: `docs/wealthr_export_2026-08-11.csv` (a real WealthR export,
containing actual pension policy numbers and balances) and `docs/uk-wealth-tracker-export-2026-08-11.json`
(this app's own export format). Both were read locally to confirm the WealthR CSV's section/column
structure — Monthly Summary, Investment Breakdown, Debt Breakdown, Pension Pots, each with its own
header row — then deleted in the same turn, per instruction, before either file's real values were
used in anything written down (an issue, a commit message, or this journal).

**Deleting the files from the working tree does not remove them from history.** Both remain
recoverable from the two upload commits for as long as this repo (or any clone/fork made before the
deletion) exists — a plain `git rm` + commit only stops *future* clones from getting them easily,
it does not retroactively un-expose whatever already left the repo while they were live on a public
`main`. See `docs/github.md`'s new section for the fuller writeup and the standing lesson.

**Immediate action taken:** the repo was switched to private (`gh repo edit --visibility private`).
On the GitHub Free tier this has an immediate side effect worth knowing in advance next time: GitHub
Pages requires a **public** source repo to serve on the free plan (Pro is needed for Pages from a
private repo) — the live site went down the moment visibility changed, confirmed via the Pages API
itself returning 404 straight after. This was expected and accepted, not a bug.

**Planned remediation (in progress as of this entry, not yet executed):** rather than rewrite this
repo's history in place — a `git filter-repo` + force-push, risky to do safely while an autonomous
pipeline is actively merging PRs against the same branch, and still leaves every already-existing
clone/fork untouched — the plan is a clean relaunch instead: let the current private repo's pipeline
finish its open backlog undisturbed (visibility has no effect on `claude-code-action`/`GH_PAT`
functioning normally), then rename it to a `-private-archive-<date>` name and leave its full history
intact but never public again, and stand up a **new** public repo from a single fresh commit of the
finished code — no history carried over, so there is nothing left to scrub. Secrets are reused as-is
(no evidence either was ever printed to a log); everything else (labels, milestones, any issues still
open at cutover, branch protection, Pages + the custom domain) gets recreated on the new repo.

## Estate Summary: itemise Investments by ISA wrapper, not one lump figure — 2026-08-11
<!-- METRICS:estate-summary-itemise-investments-by-wrapper -->
- **Execution Duration:** __DURATION__ seconds
- **Model:** __MODEL__
- **Turns:** __TURNS__
- **Input Tokens:** __INPUT_TOKENS__
- **Output Tokens:** __OUTPUT_TOKENS__
- **Estimated Cost:** $__COST__ (from Claude Code's reported total_cost_usd)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable GPT-5-tier model:** $__GPT_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable Gemini-tier model:** $__GEMINI_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)

**Decisions:**
- **`investmentsByWrapper` is a breakdown of `investments`, not a second figure**, the same shape decision the issue pointed at for its sibling "itemise Debts" issue in this milestone (#249, on its own unmerged branch at the time of writing, so not something this PR could import from — the pattern was read off its diff and reproduced independently, not shared code). `budget-policy.js`'s `estateValuation()` keeps computing `investments` exactly as before (`sumInvestmentValues(position.investments ?? [])`, grown at the configured rate per convention 6) and adds `investmentsByWrapper` alongside it — an array of `{ wrapper, label, amount }`, one row per `Wrapper` in `WRAPPERS` order, each row itself `sumInvestmentValues` run on that wrapper's holdings alone and grown the same way `investments` is. Two holdings in the same wrapper combine into one row; a wrapper with no holdings, or whose only holdings are `exclude_from_net_worth`, gets no row at all — the same "don't show a computed line for data that isn't there" rule this card already applies to Lifetime ISA pots and Offset mortgage savings. Every row's `amount` sums back to `investments` exactly, by construction (filter-then-sum over a partition of the same list `sumInvestmentValues(investments)` itself sums, each side grown identically).
- **Array of `{ wrapper, label, amount }`, not `Record<Wrapper, number>`** — `report.js`'s existing `holdingsByType` is the closest precedent in the codebase for "group a money total by an enum and hand a component something to `{#each}` over, empty groups omitted, enum order preserved," and `EstateSummary.svelte` never needs to look a specific wrapper up, only to iterate what's there, so a plain ordered array avoids a second hand-maintained ordering step.
- **`EstateSummary.svelte`'s single "Investments" row is replaced, not supplemented.** The card now `{#each}`es `snapshot.valuation.investmentsByWrapper`, one row per wrapper, each rendered exactly as the old lump row was (label, then `formatMoney(amount)`) — no separate "Investments" heading grouping the rows, matching how Property equity/Physical assets already sit as plain top-level rows with no group header of their own.
- **`INVESTMENT_TYPES`/`INVESTMENT_TYPE_LABELS` gain an `'other'` catch-all**, the one gap the issue named on the separate `InvestmentType` dimension (asset class, not wrapper) — appended at the end, labelled `'Other'`, the exact pattern `DEBT_TYPES`/`ASSET_CATEGORIES` already use. No component change was needed to surface it: `InvestmentHoldings.svelte`'s type `<select>` already `{#each}`es `INVESTMENT_TYPES`, so the new option appears automatically.
- **Tests added at both the calculation layer and the render layer.** `budget-policy.test.js` gained two new `estateValuation` cases (a multi-wrapper fixture asserting `WRAPPERS` order and that two same-wrapper holdings combine into one row, and an `exclude_from_net_worth` case showing a wrapper's row disappears once its only holding is excluded while an untouched wrapper alongside it still gets one) plus assertions folded into three existing cases (the single-holding fixture's `investmentsByWrapper`, the growth test's grown `amount`, and the empty-estate test's `[]`). `EstateSummary.test.js` gained a dedicated wrapper-itemisation test (three wrappers, one combined from two holdings, one excluded holding contributing nothing) and its existing itemisation test was updated for the new markup (`'General Investment Account'` in place of `'Investments'`, since `createInvestment()`'s default wrapper is `'gia'`). `model.test.js`'s `INVESTMENT_TYPES` exhaustiveness test gained the ninth value. Along the way, `EstateSummary.test.js`'s `text()` helper picked up the same `&amp;` → `&` decode `DividendTracker.test.js`/`IsaAllowanceTracker.test.js` already use, needed because `svelte/server`'s `render` HTML-escapes wrapper labels like "Stocks & Shares ISA" and the old helper only stripped tags.

**Trade-offs / deviations from prompt:**
- **No change to `total`, `withoutPensions`, or any other `EstateValuation`/`IhtLiability` field** — this is a breakdown of the existing `investments` figure, not a new one, so nothing downstream of `estateValuation()` (`estateIht`, `budgetPolicyImpact`, `estate-plan.js`'s `estateSnapshot()`) needed to change, and none of it did.
- **No `debtsByType` companion added here** — the issue scoped this PR to Investments only, and the sibling Debts breakdown (#249) already exists on its own branch; duplicating it here would create a merge conflict for no benefit, so `budget-policy.js`'s `debts` field is untouched.
- **Browser-verified this session.** `chromium` was available at `/usr/bin/chromium`; built the app, served it with `vite preview`, and drove it headlessly with a scratch `playwright-core` install (removed afterward, `node_modules` is gitignored so nothing to commit). Seeded `localStorage`'s `uk-wealth-tracker:data` key directly with a monthly entry holding four investments across three wrappers (two `isa_stocks_shares`, one `sipp`, one `gia`) and navigated to `/estate`: the card rendered "Stocks & Shares ISA £50,000", "SIPP £40,000" and "General Investment Account £10,000" as three separate rows summing to the £100,000 headline, with zero console errors. `git status` was clean of scratch files before committing.
- **`npm test`: 2792 passed, up from 2789 on `main`** (net +3 — two new `budget-policy.test.js` cases, one new `EstateSummary.test.js` case). `npm run check`: 0 errors, the same 2 pre-existing `<slot>` warnings. `npm run lint` and `npm run build`: clean.
- **`npm test` still isn't a CI gate** (same flag as every prior entry) — the counts above are local; only `build`/`check`/`lint` run on the PR itself.

## Estate Summary: show an explicit Pension pots line item when they're counted — 2026-08-11
<!-- METRICS:estate-summary-pension-pots-line-item -->
- **Execution Duration:** __DURATION__ seconds
- **Model:** __MODEL__
- **Turns:** __TURNS__
- **Input Tokens:** __INPUT_TOKENS__
- **Output Tokens:** __OUTPUT_TOKENS__
- **Estimated Cost:** $__COST__ (from Claude Code's reported total_cost_usd)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable GPT-5-tier model:** $__GPT_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable Gemini-tier model:** $__GEMINI_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)

**Decisions:**
- **One boolean pair, not a new one.** `EstateSummary.svelte` already had `showPensionExclusion` (#224), gated on `snapshot.valuation.pensionPots !== 0`. This issue adds `showPensionPots`, gated on `snapshot.valuation.pensionsCounted && pensionPots !== 0`, and narrows `showPensionExclusion` to `pensionPots !== 0 && !pensionsCounted` — the two conditions partition on `pensionsCounted` alone, so exactly one of "the itemised row" / "the exclusion note" can ever render for a given valuation, never both, matching the issue's own instruction. A zero `pensionPots` still renders neither, the same "don't show a computed £0 line" convention `showLifetimeIsaPots`/`showOffsetSavings` already follow — the issue didn't ask for that half explicitly, but leaving it out would mean a future `pensionsCounted: true` document with no DC pensions at all shows a "£0" row that reads as a bug report, the exact failure mode #224's own doc comment already named for the exclusion note.
- **New row placed between Lifetime ISA pots and Debts**, matching `budget-policy.js`'s own arithmetic order (`total = withoutPensions + (pensionsCounted ? pensionPots : 0)`, with `withoutPensions` itself built investments-then-equity-then-offset-then-assets-then-lisa) — the itemised list already mirrors that build order for every other row, so the pension pots row takes the next slot in the same sequence rather than being appended at the end.
- **No change to `estate.js`, `budget-policy.js`, or `estate-plan.js`** — `pensionsCounted`/`pensionPots` already existed and are already correct (the issue's own framing: "purely about making an already-correct number visible"). The only production change is `EstateSummary.svelte`'s derived booleans and its template.
- **The row is currently unreachable through this card in practice, and that's expected, not a gap.** `estate-plan.js`'s `estateSnapshot()` never passes a tax year to `estateValuation()` (convention 1 — always "today," no year-scenario picker on this tab), so `pensionsCounted` stays `false` under any real calendar date before 6 April 2027. The row exists so a future tax year's estate is itemised correctly the day it starts rendering, not because it renders today.

**Trade-offs / deviations from prompt:**
- **Testing the `pensionsCounted: true` branch needed a mock, not a prop.** The issue asked for "an explicit `startYear`/date override the same way `budget-policy.test.js` already does for `PENSION_IHT_TAX_YEAR`" — but unlike `budget-policy.test.js`'s direct function calls, `EstateSummary.svelte` has no prop or option that reaches `estateValuation()`'s `startYear` argument (that seam is deliberately absent per `estate-plan.js`'s convention 1, and this issue's scope explicitly excludes touching it). Reaching the branch under test therefore needed `vi.mock('$lib/budget-policy.js', …)` with `vi.importActual` for everything except a thin `estateValuation` wrapper pinning `startYear` to `PENSION_IHT_TAX_YEAR` (and `estateGrowthRate` to `0`, to keep the money figures identical to a same-inputs today's-year test rather than requiring every assertion to carry a year of compounding) whenever `estate-plan.js` doesn't supply its own. Split into a new file, `EstateSummary.pension-pots.test.js`, rather than added to the top of the existing `EstateSummary.test.js`, because `vi.mock` applies file-wide and every other test in that file depends on the real, unmocked `estateValuation` to assert `pensionsCounted: false` behaviour (#224's own exclusion-note tests).
- **`EstateSummary.test.js` gained one negative-case test** (a DC pension pot under today's real, unmocked tax year gets the exclusion note, never the itemised row) rather than moving all pension-related tests to the new file — the existing #224 tests already covered "no DC pot → no note," so the only gap was confirming the new row stays absent (not just the note staying present) once a DC pot exists.
- **Browser-verified this session.** Built, served with `vite preview`, and drove it headlessly with a scratch `playwright-core` install (removed afterward, `node_modules` is gitignored so nothing to commit). Seeded `localStorage`'s `uk-wealth-tracker:data` with a monthly entry, a £500,000 mortgage-free home and an £80,000 `dc_workplace` pension, then loaded `/estate` under today's real date: the exclusion note rendered ("£80,000 of unused Defined Contribution pension pots is left out of the total above… 6 April 2027"), no "Pension pots" row appeared, and there were zero console errors — confirming the negative case end-to-end, since today's real tax year can't reach the positive branch. `git status` was clean of scratch files before committing.
- **`npm test`: 2796 passed, up from 2792 on `main`** (net +4 — one new `EstateSummary.test.js` case, three in the new `EstateSummary.pension-pots.test.js`). `npm run check`: 0 errors, the same 2 pre-existing `<slot>` warnings. `npm run lint` and `npm run build`: clean.
- **`npm test` still isn't a CI gate** (same flag as every prior entry) — the counts above are local; only `build`/`check`/`lint` run on the PR itself.

## Property equity chart: "until age" horizon control on the Property tab — 2026-08-11
<!-- METRICS:property-equity-chart-until-age-horizon-control -->
- **Execution Duration:** __DURATION__ seconds

- **Ballpark cost, comparable Gemini-tier model:** $__GEMINI_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)

**Decisions:**
- **`PropertyTracker.svelte` gets a plain, read-only `profile` prop, not `bind:`**, seeded from `src/routes/property/+page.svelte`'s `hydrateAppData()` call exactly the way the pensions tab already seeds `StatePensionProjection`/`PensionTaxRelief` — this tab owns `properties`, not `profile`, so nothing here ever writes it back.
- **The target age lives in local `$state`, converted every render via `propertyProjectionYearsForTargetAge(targetAge, profile.dob_year, profile.dob_month, now)`** (#241), reusing the component's existing `now` prop rather than `new Date()` inline, for the same determinism reason `dealExpiryStatus` already takes one — a server-rendered test can assert on it without the answer depending on what day the suite happens to run.
- **`years={projectionYears ?? undefined}` on `PropertyEquityChart`, not a computed fallback constant.** `propertyProjectionYearsForTargetAge` already returns `null` for "no `dob_year` to convert from", and `PropertyEquityChart`'s own `years` prop already defaults to `PROPERTY_PROJECTION_YEARS` when nothing is passed — `?? undefined` lets that existing default do the fallback rather than importing `PROPERTY_PROJECTION_YEARS` a second time to restate it.
- **No `dob_year` hides the input and shows "Add your date of birth on the forecast tab to project this until a target age."** instead of a disabled control or a guessed age — the same explicit-unavailable convention `StatePensionProjection.svelte` already uses verbatim for the same missing field, rather than inventing new copy for the same situation.
- **Left a comment in `PropertyTracker.svelte` for #240** (the not-yet-landed "all properties in one chart" view) pointing whoever picks it up at this component's `profile` prop and `projectionYears` value, per the issue's instruction to avoid a second copy of the control if #240 lands first — checked `gh issue view 240`, still open, so only the comment was added, no wiring.
- **Three new tests in `PropertyTracker.test.js`**: the control renders with its default (`PROPERTY_PROJECTION_DEFAULT_TARGET_AGE`, 80) when `profile.dob_year` is set, and the muted explanatory text replaces it both when `profile.dob_year` is explicitly `null` and when no `profile` prop is passed at all (the component's own default). `npm test`: 2792 passed, up from 2789 on `main`.

**Trade-offs / deviations from prompt:**
- **Browser verification exposed a pre-existing gap this issue does not close: nothing in the running app currently writes `profile.dob_year` back into the store.** `ForecastProjections.svelte`'s own doc comment already flags this ("this tab still doesn't read `profile`/`dob_year` from the store... a pre-existing gap #146/#172 left for this tab specifically"), and `settings/+page.svelte` only wires up `partner`, not the primary profile. Driving the control through the actual forecast-tab form therefore verifies nothing, since that form's birth year never reaches `appData.profile`. Verified instead by round-tripping a JSON document through Settings' existing Export/Import (`DataManager.svelte`) with `profile.dob_year` set and then cleared, driven with a scratch `playwright-core` install against the system's `/usr/bin/chromium` (removed afterward, confirmed via `git status`/`git diff --stat` that only the intended three files changed). Confirmed by screenshot both states: the control at its default (80) showing a 44-year projection, changing it to 50 showing a 14-year projection, and the no-`dob_year` state showing the muted message with the chart still rendering its fixed 30-year default. No console errors either run.
- **The "Add your date of birth on the forecast tab" copy is accurate to the codebase's stated intent, not to what currently works.** It reuses `StatePensionProjection.svelte`'s existing sentence rather than inventing new wording that would call out the gap above, since closing that gap is #146/#172's job, not this issue's — the copy is correct once that lands, and matches what every other "no age recorded" panel in the app already says today.
- **`npm run check`: 0 errors, the same 2 pre-existing `<slot>` warnings. `npm run lint` and `npm run build`: clean.**

## Capital Gains Tax: estimate panel on the Tax tab — 2026-08-11
<!-- METRICS:capital-gains-tax-estimate-panel-tax-tab -->
- **Execution Duration:** __DURATION__ seconds
- **Model:** __MODEL__
- **Turns:** __TURNS__
- **Input Tokens:** __INPUT_TOKENS__
- **Output Tokens:** __OUTPUT_TOKENS__
- **Estimated Cost:** $__COST__ (from Claude Code's reported total_cost_usd)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable GPT-5-tier model:** $__GPT_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable Gemini-tier model:** $__GEMINI_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)

**Decisions:**
- **`CapitalGainsTaxEstimate.svelte` follows `DividendTaxSummary`'s shape, not `StressTestPanel`/`IncomeShockPanel`/`MortgageRateRisePanel`'s.** All four are "scenario tools" in README's sense, but the latter three overlay a hypothetical event onto an existing forecast (baseline anchor/horizon/spread carried in as props), where this panel has no forecast to overlay — it's a standalone calculator reading `$lib/capital-gains.js` directly, the same shape `DividendTaxSummary` already uses for its own engine (`dividend-tax.js`). So it gets a `Card`, stat tiles, a rate-by-rate table and an illustrative footnote instead of an enable-checkbox and horizon bars.
- **The property picker only lists properties with both `purchase_price` and `purchase_date` recorded**, mirroring `MortgageRateRisePanel`'s "filter the picker to properties that actually carry a mortgage" convention rather than listing every property and letting the engine's own `applicable: false` path explain itself inline. Without `purchase_date` specifically, the engine can't place an ownership period on the calendar at all — showing that property in the picker and then immediately saying "can't calculate" reads as a broken tool, where leaving it off the list and pointing at the Property tab reads as an honest "not enough recorded yet". A property with a date but no price is *not* excluded by the engine itself (it computes anyway, with a `noPurchasePrice` warning) — the picker excludes it too, on the issue's own wording ("purchase_date/purchase_price set"), for the same "don't show a half-broken figure" reason.
- **Sale date, sale price and other income are seeded once and then belong to the user** — the same idiom `DividendTaxSummary`'s `otherIncome` field uses, seeded from `profile.gross_salary` and editable without writing back. Sale price seeds from the selected property's current recorded `value` (a reasonable first guess at what it would fetch); sale date seeds from today. `initialSaleDate`/`initialSalePrice`/`initialOtherIncome` props exist for the same reason `MortgageRateRisePanel`'s dials have `initialXxx` props: there's no browser test environment for `svelte/server`'s `render` to type into a field with, so a server-rendered test needs another way to reach a non-default figure.
