- **Private Residence Relief is shown as days-converted-to-months, not raw days.** The engine apportions relief in exact days (`period.reliefDays`/`period.totalDays`), which is precise but not legible at a glance — the issue specifically asked for "the qualifying-months fraction it came from" to make the time-apportionment legible rather than a black box. `formatMonthsApprox` divides by an average 30.4368-day month purely for display; the underlying `reliefFraction` used for every money figure is untouched, so this is a presentation layer over an already-exact number, not a second calculation that could disagree with it.
- **A loss gets its own branch with no band table**, echoing convention (8) of the engine's own module doc: no tax, and an explicit sentence that this app doesn't carry the loss forward, rather than a table full of zeroes that would need reading twice to confirm nothing is owed.
- **Static "not modelled" callouts (allowable costs, Letting Relief, joint ownership, sharing the Annual Exempt Amount) live in the footnote text, not in `breakdown.warnings`.** The engine's own dynamic warnings (`costsNotDeducted`, `noPurchasePrice`, `unknownPropertyType`, the applicability failures) are rendered as-is, verbatim, per the issue's "shows the same sentence this module means rather than paraphrasing it" convention — but Letting Relief/joint ownership/other-disposals are permanent scope limits that apply to every disposal regardless of input, not conditions the engine detects, so they're stated once in the always-visible footnote instead of being invented as fake per-calculation warnings.

**Trade-offs / deviations from prompt:**
- **No changes to `$lib/capital-gains.js`**, per the issue's explicit scope — this PR is the Svelte component, the Tax route wiring (`properties` now flows into `tax/+page.svelte` read-only, the same way `monthlyEntries` already did for the ISA tracker), and its test only.
- **Browser-verified this session.** Installed a scratch Playwright/Chromium (removed nothing from the repo — no `package.json` change), ran `npm run dev`, added a property with purchase price/date/letting-period on the Property tab through the real form, then drove the Tax tab: confirmed the empty state ("no properties recorded"), the picker filtering and defaulting to the first eligible property, a live calculation reproducing the module doc's own worked example (£300,000 gain, £164,117 PRR display, band table splitting correctly), the loss state, the "sale date before purchase" state, and dark mode — with zero console errors throughout.
- **`npm test`: 2799 passed, up from 2789 on `main`** (net +10: the new component's own test file). `npm run check`: 0 errors, the same 2 pre-existing `<slot>` warnings. `npm run lint` and `npm run build`: clean.
- **`npm test` still isn't a CI gate** (same flag as every prior entry) — the counts above are local; only `build`/`check`/`lint` run on the PR itself.

## Estate Summary: itemise Debts by type instead of one lump figure — 2026-08-11
<!-- METRICS:estate-summary-itemise-debts-by-type -->
- **Execution Duration:** __DURATION__ seconds
- **Model:** __MODEL__
- **Turns:** __TURNS__
- **Input Tokens:** __INPUT_TOKENS__
- **Output Tokens:** __OUTPUT_TOKENS__
- **Estimated Cost:** $__COST__ (from Claude Code's reported total_cost_usd)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable GPT-5-tier model:** $__GPT_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable Gemini-tier model:** $__GEMINI_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)

**Decisions:**
- **`debtsByType` mirrors `investmentsByWrapper` (#250) exactly, field for field** — the issue named "Offset mortgage savings" as the precedent for granularity, but structurally the closer sibling once #250 landed is the wrapper breakdown, so this reuses its shape rather than inventing a new one. `budget-policy.js`'s `estateValuation()` keeps computing `debts` exactly as before (`roundMoney(sumDebtBalances(position.debts ?? []))`) and adds `debtsByType` alongside it: an array of `{ type, label, amount }`, one row per `DebtType` in `DEBT_TYPES` order, each row `sumDebtBalances` run on that type's debts alone. Two debts of the same type combine into one row; a type with no debts, or whose only debts are `exclude_from_net_worth`, gets no row — the same rule `investmentsByWrapper` and the Lifetime ISA pots/Offset mortgage savings rows already follow.
- **No `grown()` on the breakdown rows, matching `debts` itself** — convention 6 in `budget-policy.js`'s module comment states debts are held flat when the estate is valued for a future tax year, unlike the asset side. `investmentsByWrapper`'s rows are each `grown()`, because `investments` is; `debtsByType`'s rows are each plain `roundMoney(sumDebtBalances(...))`, because `debts` is. Getting this backwards would have made the breakdown's rows stop summing back to `debts` the moment `startYear` moved past 2026/27.
- **`EstateSummary.svelte`'s single "Debts" row is replaced, not supplemented.** The card now `{#each}`es `snapshot.valuation.debtsByType`, one row per type, each rendered exactly as the old lump row was (label, then a minus sign and `formatMoney(amount)`) — same list position, same styling, no group header, matching how the investments breakdown sits inline among Property equity/Physical assets with no heading of its own.
- **Tests added at both layers, following the wrapper breakdown's own test shape.** `budget-policy.test.js` gained two new `estateValuation` cases (a multi-type fixture asserting `DEBT_TYPES` order and that two same-type debts combine into one row, and an `exclude_from_net_worth` case showing a type's row disappears once its only debt is excluded while an untouched type alongside it still gets one), plus `debtsByType` assertions folded into three existing cases (the single-debt fixture, the growth test — asserting the breakdown stays flat while `investmentsByWrapper` grows — and the empty-estate test's `[]`). `EstateSummary.test.js` gained a dedicated debts-itemisation test (two types, one combined from two debts, one excluded debt contributing nothing) and its existing itemisation test was updated for the new markup (`'Other'` in place of `'Debts'`, since `createDebt()`'s default type is `'other'`).

**Trade-offs / deviations from prompt:**
- **No change to `total`, `withoutPensions`, or any other `EstateValuation`/`IhtLiability` field** — this is a breakdown of the existing `debts` figure, not a new one, so nothing downstream of `estateValuation()` (`estateIht`, `budgetPolicyImpact`, `estate-plan.js`'s `estateSnapshot()`) needed to change, and none of it did; `estate-plan.js` passes `valuation` straight through to the snapshot it returns, so `debtsByType` reaches the component with no extra wiring.
- **Browser-verified this session.** Built, served with `vite preview`, and drove it headlessly with a scratch `playwright-core` install (removed afterward — `node_modules` is gitignored so nothing to commit). Seeded `localStorage`'s `uk-wealth-tracker:data` key directly with a monthly entry holding one investment and three debts (a credit card, a car finance debt, and a mortgage flagged `exclude_from_net_worth`) and navigated to `/estate`: the card rendered "Credit card −£3,000" and "Car finance −£8,000" as separate rows, no "Mortgage" row (the excluded debt correctly contributed nothing) and no lump "Debts" row anywhere, with zero console errors. `git status` was clean of scratch files before committing.
- **`npm test`: 2799 passed, up from 2796 on `main`** (net +3 — two new `budget-policy.test.js` cases, one new `EstateSummary.test.js` case). `npm run check`: 0 errors, the same 2 pre-existing `<slot>` warnings. `npm run lint` and `npm run build`: clean.
- **`npm test` still isn't a CI gate** (same flag as every prior entry) — the counts above are local; only `build`/`check`/`lint` run on the PR itself.

## Widen the categorical chart palette to five series colours across every theme — 2026-08-11
<!-- METRICS:widen-categorical-chart-palette-to-five-series-colours -->
- **Execution Duration:** __DURATION__ seconds
- **Model:** __MODEL__
- **Turns:** __TURNS__
- **Input Tokens:** __INPUT_TOKENS__
- **Output Tokens:** __OUTPUT_TOKENS__
- **Estimated Cost:** $__COST__ (from Claude Code's reported total_cost_usd)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable GPT-5-tier model:** $__GPT_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable Gemini-tier model:** $__GEMINI_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)

**Decisions:**
- **The 48 new hues were searched for, not eyeballed.** Picking three more colours per theme variant by hand and then hoping they clear 3:1 on two surfaces *and* ΔE ≥ 8 across 10 pairs under four vision models is not a job for taste — with 16 variants that is 160 pairwise checks and 160 contrast checks, and the existing pair already used most of the comfortable hue space. So the values came out of a throwaway Node search (scratch, not committed — `src/palettes.css.test.js` is the permanent check) that reuses the test file's own colour maths: enumerate H/S/L candidates, hard-filter on contrast, then hill-climb the three new slots round-robin against a score that maximises the worst-case ΔE only until it is comfortably clear and spends the rest on looking deliberate.
- **The design rule behind the search is "72° apart on the wheel, anchored on `--chart-1`."** Each variant's five slots aim at the five even stops off its own `--chart-1` hue; the two stops nearest the existing slots are treated as taken and the other three become slots 3-5, searched within ±24° of their stop. That is what keeps the additions looking like the palette they joined rather than three arbitrary colours — and, because both variants of a palette derive their stops from the same hue family, `--chart-3` in `.palette-beach` and in `.dark.palette-beach` are the same green re-stepped for a dark surface, exactly the convention the existing two slots and the `.dark` comment already state.
- **Lightness varies between slots, not just hue, and the search was allowed to move it freely inside each mode's band.** ΔE76 counts the L difference, so it is the only lever left once protanope/deuteranope simulation has collapsed the red-green axis — `:root`'s new `--chart-3` sits at 20% lightness precisely because every lighter yellow-green in that hue window fell under ΔE 8 against `--chart-2`'s orange for a protanope reader. Slots that did not need it kept a preferred mid-band lightness, so the palettes don't read as arbitrarily muddy.
- **Search margins are deliberately above the test's bars: contrast ≥ 3.3 (bar: 3.0) and ΔE ≥ 9.5 (bar: 8).** A value that only just clears turns the next unrelated nudge into a red test with no headroom to absorb it. Measured worst case for a *new* slot came out at 3.30:1 contrast and ΔE 15.9; the tightest pair in the whole file is still the original `--chart-1`/`--chart-2` (ΔE 11.2 in `.palette-city` light, 12.8 in `.dark.palette-male`), untouched by this issue.
- **`CHART_SERIES_COUNT` lives in `$lib/palette.js` and is the single source of truth in both directions.** `palettes.css.test.js` builds its slot list from that constant — so the contrast and CVD checks widen with the palette automatically — and a new structural test asserts the CSS declares *exactly* those slots and no more, which is what makes "no slot can be added untested" true rather than aspirational: a `--chart-6` added to CSS alone now fails, as does a bumped constant with no CSS behind it. Verified by mutation: pointing `.palette-male`'s `--chart-4` at `--chart-1`'s value fails the CVD test with the offending pair and its ΔE named in the message.
- **`chartSeriesColor(index)` wraps rather than running out, and coerces junk input to slot 1.** A chart with six properties should draw six lines with two sharing a hue (the legend still tells them apart), not five lines and one invisible one. Non-integer/negative/`NaN` input is a caller bug, and the alternative — emitting `hsl(var(--chart-NaN))` — paints the series black or not at all with nothing in the console to explain it.

**Trade-offs / deviations from prompt:**
- **No chart was rewritten to use the new helper**, per the issue's "out of scope": nothing in `src/components/` calls `chartSeriesColor` yet, so this PR changes not one rendered pixel of the current app. #279's all-properties equity chart is its first caller. The helper is unit-tested rather than integration-tested for that reason.
- **Two stale comments were corrected as part of the change, without touching behaviour.** `PropertyEquityChart`'s module doc justified its `--destructive` mortgage line partly as a slot shortage ("this app has only `--chart-1`/`--chart-2` defined") — that reason has now evaporated, so the comment now says the line stays `--destructive` because the reason is semantic, which is the half of it the issue confirms. The palettes block comment's "the two series at ΔE ≥ 8 from each other" was likewise reworded to "every pair of slots".
- **`app.css`'s `--chart-*` comment records the measured worst-case figures, as asked, but names the *default* palette's own hex pairs only** (the pre-existing `#2a78d6`/`#eb6834` line). Writing all 48 new triples out in prose would restate the declarations three lines below them; the derivation rule plus the worst-case numbers is the part that is not readable off the values themselves.
- **Browser-verified this session.** Built, then rendered a scratch page (in `/tmp`, nothing added to the repo) against the *built* stylesheet showing all 16 theme variants — five swatches plus a five-line chart on a card each — and screenshotted it with the runner's headless Chromium. All five series are distinguishable in every variant, on both the page background and the card, in both modes; `--chart-1` … `--chart-5` are present in the built CSS asset.
- **`npm test`: 2819 passed, up from 2812 on `main`** (net +7: 5 new `chartSeriesColor`/`CHART_SERIES_COUNT` cases and 2 new structural chart-slot cases. The widened contrast/CVD coverage adds no *cases* — those two tests were already one per theme variant and now loop over 5 slots and 10 pairs inside each, rather than 2 and 1). `npm run check`: 0 errors, the same 2 pre-existing `<slot>` warnings. `npm run lint` and `npm run build`: clean.
- **`npm test` still isn't a CI gate** (same flag as every prior entry) — the counts above are local; only `build`/`check`/`lint` run on the PR itself.

## Property equity chart: combined all-properties view (one line per property) — 2026-08-11
<!-- METRICS:property-equity-chart-combined-all-properties-view -->
- **Execution Duration:** __DURATION__ seconds
- **Model:** __MODEL__
- **Turns:** __TURNS__
- **Input Tokens:** __INPUT_TOKENS__
- **Output Tokens:** __OUTPUT_TOKENS__
- **Estimated Cost:** $__COST__ (from Claude Code's reported total_cost_usd)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable GPT-5-tier model:** $__GPT_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable Gemini-tier model:** $__GEMINI_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)

**Decisions:**
- **A new `PropertyPortfolioEquityChart.svelte`, not a mode prop on `PropertyEquityChart.svelte`.** The issue's own scope calls this "additive, not a replacement", and the two charts differ enough in shape (one series with three lines vs. N series with one line each, sharing one `<Chart>` `data` array rather than each mark carrying its own) that folding both into one component would have meant two disjoint code paths behind a single `mode` prop. `PropertyEquityChart.svelte` is untouched byte-for-byte.
- **Rows, not per-property `data` props, are what every `<Spline>` reads from.** `NetWorthChart.svelte`'s own header comment documents why a per-mark `data` array breaks `bisect-x`'s `flatData` search and `<Highlight>`'s point lookup (a hit test against several differently-shaped arrays instead of one sorted series) — the same trap applies here with N properties instead of a forecast band. `portfolioEquityRows(series)` (module block) pivots each property's `propertyEquityProjection(...)` output into one row per year, `{ year, [property.id]: equity, ... }`, and `<Chart data={rows}>` is the only thing handed to LayerChart; every `<Spline>` reads its own property's key off that one shared array via its `y` accessor. That is also what makes the tooltip trivial: `bisect-x` hands the module-block `propertyPortfolioTooltipReading` one row already carrying every property's figure for that year, no per-series lookup needed.
- **Only `equity` is plotted, not `value`/`mortgageBalance` too.** The single-property chart draws all three because there is exactly one property to describe in full; N properties already means N lines here, and N-times-three would stop being legible past a handful of properties. A reader who wants a given property's value/mortgage split still has the single-property view the toggle switches back to — the combined chart's own intro paragraph says so.
- **No per-point highlight markers, only the `<Highlight lines>` crosshair.** LayerChart's `<Highlight points>` can produce one dot per series if `<Chart>`'s `y` prop is given as an array of accessors (traced through `Highlight.shared.svelte.js`'s `Array.isArray(this.yCoord)` branch), but that path hands the dot's `fill` from `ctx.cGet(...)`, which is `null` without a `c` scale configured — every dot would render in the same default colour, not matched to its line, which is worse than no dot at all given the legend and tooltip already carry that job. `<Chart>` still needs *a* `y` accessor for its own scale plumbing even with `yDomain` given explicitly, so it gets the first series' equity purely as a placeholder (`primaryY`, instance script) — nothing renders off it directly.
- **Colour comes from #240's `chartSeriesColor(index)`, cycling through the five `--chart-*` tokens in property-list order** — this chart is that helper's first real caller (#240's own PR left it uncalled by design). The legend, the `<Spline>` strokes, the tooltip's dots and the table's header row all resolve colour/name once per property (`series`, instance script) rather than recomputing it in four places.
- **`PropertyTracker.svelte`'s toggle is a `role="radiogroup"` of two radios (`chartMode`, `$state`), seeded from an `initialChartMode` prop the same way `NetWorthChart.svelte` seeds `lens` from `initialLens`** — purely so a server-rendered test can assert what "All properties" mode renders without a pointer to click with; every real caller leaves it unset and gets "Single property", unchanged from before this issue. The property picker (`<select>`) is only rendered in single mode, per the issue's "the dropdown is only meaningful in single mode"; the existing "Equity growth projection for" label text and the "until age" control are untouched and still present in both modes, so #248/#274's control drives whichever chart is on screen through the same `projectionYears` value.
- **The accessible `<details>` table fallback uses one column per property**, not a per-property section — with the year as the shared row key across every property's series, a column-per-property table is the same information a reader gets from the line chart's x-axis alignment (which properties were converging or diverging in which year), where per-property sections would have scattered that comparison across N separate tables.

**Trade-offs / deviations from prompt:**
- **Depended on #240, which had not merged to `main` when this session started (PR #280 was open, not yet merged).** Rather than build a parallel, throwaway five-slot palette or block on an issue outside this one's scope, this session waited for #280 to merge (it did, mid-session — `chartSeriesColor`/`CHART_SERIES_COUNT` landed on `main` at `cb0fb39`), then rebased this branch's start point onto that commit before writing any of #279's own code. No palette or `chartSeriesColor` code in this PR — it is entirely #280's, imported unchanged.
- **No change to `$lib/property.js`'s maths**, per the issue's explicit out-of-scope note — `propertyEquityProjection` is called once per property, exactly as `PropertyEquityChart.svelte` already calls it once for its own property, and every figure on the combined chart traces back to that one unmodified function.
- **Browser-verified this session.** Installed a scratch Playwright + Chromium (`npm install --no-save playwright`, `npx playwright install chromium`; `package.json`/`package-lock.json` untouched, `node_modules` gitignored, confirmed via `git status` before finishing), ran `npm run preview` against the production build, and drove the Property tab through the real form: added three properties with different values/mortgages/growth rates, confirmed the toggle defaults to "Single property" with the picker and existing single-property chart unchanged, switched to "All properties" and confirmed three distinctly-coloured lines with a matching legend, hovered the chart and confirmed the tooltip named all three properties with colour-matched dots and the correct year/figures, and expanded "Show as a table" and confirmed one column per property with figures matching the tooltip. Zero console errors throughout.
- **`npm test`: 2839 passed, up from 2819 on `main`** (net +20: `PropertyPortfolioEquityChart.test.js` is new — module-block formatter/`portfolioEquityRows`/`propertyPortfolioTooltipReading` coverage plus server-rendered legend/table/summary assertions — and `PropertyTracker.test.js` gained toggle-wiring cases). `npm run check`: 0 errors, the same 2 pre-existing `<slot>` warnings. `npm run lint` and `npm run build`: clean.
- **`npm test` still isn't a CI gate** (same flag as every prior entry) — the counts above are local; only `build`/`check`/`lint` run on the PR itself.

## Life insurance: tracker UI on the Estate tab — 2026-08-12
<!-- METRICS:life-insurance-tracker-ui-on-the-estate-tab -->
- **Execution Duration:** __DURATION__ seconds
- **Model:** __MODEL__
- **Turns:** __TURNS__
- **Input Tokens:** __INPUT_TOKENS__
- **Output Tokens:** __OUTPUT_TOKENS__
- **Estimated Cost:** $__COST__ (from Claude Code's reported total_cost_usd)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable GPT-5-tier model:** $__GPT_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable Gemini-tier model:** $__GEMINI_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)

**Decisions:**
- **New `LifeInsuranceTracker.svelte`, following `PensionTracker.svelte`'s add/edit/remove-a-row shape exactly.** Same flat-list-on-`AppData` model (a policy is a point-in-time fact, not a monthly snapshot line), same `formFields()`/`startEdit()`/`addPolicy()`/`saveEdit()`/`submitForm()`/`removePolicy()` split, same list-item-plus-form layout. Kept deliberately close to that precedent rather than inventing a new shape, since the issue named `PensionTracker.svelte`/`AssetsTracker.svelte` explicitly as the pattern to follow.
- **Form scope is exactly the four facts the issue names — name, provider, sum assured, in-trust — not the full `LifeInsurance` record.** `current_value` (surrender value) and `notes` already exist on the data model from #252 but have no form field here; they sit at their factory defaults (`0` / `''`) for policies added through this UI. Matching the issue's stated scope over "use every field since it exists" avoids guessing at a surrender-value UX nobody asked for yet in this issue.
- **"In trust" is a checkbox inside the add/edit form, not a row-level instant toggle.** `AssetsTracker`'s `include_in_net_worth` toggles instantly outside the form because it is meant to be flipped often as a what-if. In-trust status is a one-time fact about how a policy is legally written, set once when the policy is added — closer to `PensionTracker`'s type select than to a frequently-toggled switch — so it goes through the same add/edit flow as the other fields, per the issue's own field list ("...and an 'In trust' checkbox").
- **One sentence of inline help directly under the checkbox, in the form itself.** "A policy written in trust pays out to your beneficiaries directly and sits outside your estate for Inheritance Tax." Matches the issue's explicit instruction on how much explanation to give ("a sentence, not a full explainer") and its own row summary echoes the state (`· in trust`) so the fact is visible without reopening the form to edit.
- **Wired `life_insurance` into the Estate tab's existing hydrate/write-back pattern**, alongside `beneficiaries`/`iht_settings` in `routes/estate/+page.svelte` — same `onMount` read off `appData`, same `$effect` write-back gated on `ready`. `LifeInsuranceTracker` renders as a third card after `EstateSummary`/`BeneficiaryBreakdown`, inside the same `{:else}` branch so it only mounts once hydration has completed.

**Trade-offs / deviations from prompt:**
- **No Estate valuation change, as the issue specifies.** `life_insurance` policies are entered and persisted but do not yet count toward `estateValuation()`/`EstateSummary.svelte`'s totals — the issue is explicit that this lands in the next issue in the milestone. `hasEstateData` (the Estate tab's empty-state gate) is also left untouched, so a user with only life insurance policies and nothing else still sees the "nothing to value yet" empty state rather than the tracker; that gate is scoped to the valuation lists it already checks (`monthlyEntries`/`properties`/`assets`) and changing it wasn't asked for here.
- **`current_value` and `notes` have no form field.** Both exist on the data model already (#252) for future use — the surrender-value field would matter once a "cash in this policy instead" scenario exists, and `notes` for free text — but neither is part of what this issue names, so they're left for whichever future issue needs them.
- **Test coverage is server-rendered smoke tests only, matching `PensionTracker.test.js`'s own stated rationale.** No browser test environment is set up in this repo, so `svelte/server`'s `render` covers the initial render against a given `policies` list (empty state, row summary incl. the in-trust marker, pluralisation, sum-assured total, inline IHT help text, submit-button label) rather than add/edit/remove interactions, which are left to `npm run build && npm run preview` manual verification — the same trade-off every other flat-list tracker in this codebase already makes.
- **`npm test`: 2831 passed (2823 existing + 8 new), all green.** No change to `npm run lint` or `npm run check` — both remain clean with the same 2 pre-existing `<slot>` warnings. `npm run build` also succeeds unchanged.

## Life insurance: wire into Estate valuation, respecting the in-trust exclusion — 2026-08-12
<!-- METRICS:life-insurance-wire-into-estate-valuation -->
- **Execution Duration:** __DURATION__ seconds
- **Model:** __MODEL__
- **Turns:** __TURNS__
- **Input Tokens:** __INPUT_TOKENS__
- **Output Tokens:** __OUTPUT_TOKENS__
- **Estimated Cost:** $__COST__ (from Claude Code's reported total_cost_usd)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable GPT-5-tier model:** $__GPT_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable Gemini-tier model:** $__GEMINI_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)

**Decisions:**
- **The in-trust rule lives in exactly one function, `payableIntoEstate(policy)` in `budget-policy.js`, and is keyed on `in_trust !== true` rather than `=== false`.** A missing, `undefined` or malformed flag therefore counts the cover *into* the estate — the same reading `model.js`'s own `asBoolean(source.in_trust, false)` already takes on load, and the safer of the two errors available here: an overstated estate quotes a bill that turns out not to be owed, an understated one hides a real 40% charge on a figure that is typically six digits. This is the whole reason the issue routed itself to the higher-scrutiny model, so it is stated in the function's own doc comment rather than left to be inferred from the operator.
- **Two fields come out of `estateValuation()`, not one: `lifeInsurance` (counted) and `lifeInsuranceInTrust` (reported, never counted).** The issue asks the card to say how much cover is being excluded and why, and the only alternative to a second field is `EstateSummary.svelte` re-summing the raw policy list and re-applying the in-trust test itself — two implementations of the one rule that must not disagree. Modelled on the `pensionPots`/`pensionsCounted` pair this function already has: always report, sometimes count. The counted half is summed into `withoutPensions` alongside `investments`/`equity`/`offsetSavings`/`physicalAssets`, exactly as `propertyOffsetSavings` was added, and both halves are grown by `estateGrowthRate` like every other asset-side component (convention 6).
- **Sum assured, not `current_value`.** The estate is credited with what a policy *pays out* on death, which is the question this valuation asks; the surrender value is what it would fetch if cashed in while alive, a different question nothing in the app asks yet. `current_value` stays untouched at its factory default for policies added through #253's form.
- **`qualifyingResidenceValue()` is deliberately untouched, for the same reason offset savings stay out of it.** Cover is money in the estate, but it is not part of *the home*, so letting it near the residence nil-rate band's own figure would inflate an allowance that is capped at what the home is worth. Two tests pin this — one on `estateValuation().residence`, one on `estateSnapshot().estateInput.residenceValue` — because it is the kind of thing a later "just add it to the total" change would silently break.
- **The card's two new pieces are independently gated, unlike #251's mutually-exclusive pension pair.** A document can hold one policy in trust and one not, and both readings are then true at once: the "Life insurance" row shows the counted £200,000 while the note names the excluded £300,000. The note fires exactly when the recorded sum assured is *not* fully counted (`lifeInsuranceInTrust !== 0`), which is the condition the issue asked for, and says why in the same sentence — a policy in trust pays the beneficiaries directly, so no Inheritance Tax is charged on it.
- **`hasEstateData` on the Estate tab now counts `lifeInsurance.length` too.** Once cover is part of the valuation, a document holding only policies has an estate worth showing, so the "Nothing to value yet" empty state would have been untrue — and, since #253 put the tracker itself behind that gate, a user who recorded policies and later removed their last property or asset would have lost access to their own policy list. Verified in a real browser: added an asset, added a policy, removed the asset, and confirmed the Estate tab still values £300,000 and still renders the tracker.
- **README documents the rule as prose under the Estate & IHT Calculator section, not just as a feature bullet** — which side of the trust a policy sits on, that sum assured is the figure used, that a missing flag is treated as not-in-trust and why, and that cover never touches the residence nil-rate band. `life_insurance[]` was also missing from README's Data Model outline (#252 added the model without it); added here, since this issue makes README the stated source of truth for the rule.

**Trade-offs / deviations from prompt:**
- **Growing a sum assured by `estateGrowthRate` is coarse, and is done anyway because the issue asks for it.** A level-term policy's death benefit does not compound at 5% a year — a fixed cash sum is not an asset that grows, and a decreasing-term policy moves the other way. But convention 6 is one nominal rate applied to the whole asset side, the issue explicitly asks for "growing the figure by `estateGrowthRate` the same way every other component does", and a component that alone stayed flat would be a silent exception to a documented convention. It only bites on a future-tax-year valuation; `EstateSummary.svelte`'s own call is always today's ungrown year, so nothing on the card is affected. Flagged here rather than fixed unilaterally.
- **Two files the issue did not name had to change for the wiring to reach the card.** `estate-plan.js` gains a `life_insurance` key on `EstateSnapshotInput` and passes the whole recorded list through to `estateValuation()` (it applies no rule of its own), and `routes/estate/+page.svelte` passes the already-hydrated `lifeInsurance` list into `EstateSummary`. Without both, the new fields would compute correctly and always be zero on screen.
- **Both intro paragraphs — the Estate tab's and the card's — were reworded to name life insurance as an input.** Each previously listed the tabs an estate is valued from and claimed "nothing here is entered separately"; life insurance is recorded on this same tab, so both now read "…plus any life insurance recorded below". Small, but leaving them would have made the card's own provenance claim wrong.
- **The tracker is still only reachable once the Estate tab has something to value.** `hasEstateData` counting policies fixes the case where policies already exist, but a brand-new document still cannot reach the life insurance form without first recording a monthly entry, property or asset — the tracker sits inside the same `{:else}` branch #253 put it in. Moving it outside that gate is a layout change this issue does not ask for.
- **Browser-verified this session.** Installed a scratch Playwright + Chromium (`npm install --no-save playwright`; `package.json`/`package-lock.json` confirmed untouched afterwards via `git status`), ran `npm run preview` against the production build, and drove the real forms: added a £100,000 asset, then a £300,000 policy not in trust and a £500,000 policy in trust. The card showed a "Life insurance £300,000" row, a £400,000 total, "£500,000 of life insurance cover is written in trust…" beneath it, and £30,000 of tax (40% of £400,000 − £325,000). Zero console errors, and the figures survived a reload.
- **`npm test`: 2872 passed, up from 2857 on `main`** (net +15: 9 new `budget-policy.test.js` cases — the in-trust split, a malformed flag, surrender value ignored, growth, the residence band left alone, the empty case and a bill-level worked example — plus 4 new `EstateSummary.test.js` cases and 2 new `estate-plan.test.js` cases). `npm run check`: 0 errors, the same 2 pre-existing `<slot>` warnings. `npm run lint` and `npm run build`: clean.
- **`npm test` still isn't a CI gate** (same flag as every prior entry) — the counts above are local; only `build`/`check`/`lint` run on the PR itself.

## Dividends/Investments tab: inline ISA guidance and non-ISA CGT explanation — 2026-08-12
<!-- METRICS:dividends-investments-tab-inline-isa-guidance-and-non-isa-cgt-explanation -->
- **Execution Duration:** __DURATION__ seconds
- **Model:** __MODEL__
- **Turns:** __TURNS__
- **Input Tokens:** __INPUT_TOKENS__
- **Output Tokens:** __OUTPUT_TOKENS__
- **Estimated Cost:** $__COST__ (from Claude Code's reported total_cost_usd)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable GPT-5-tier model:** $__GPT_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable Gemini-tier model:** $__GEMINI_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)

**Decisions:**
- **New `InvestmentGuidance.svelte`, mounted from `InvestmentHoldings.svelte` rather than written inline into it.** The issue named both `InvestmentHoldings.svelte` and `src/routes/dividends/+page.svelte` as candidate homes and left the choice to whichever "reads more naturally", pointing at the wrapper picker living in the former as the deciding factor. A separate component keeps the four reference sections independently testable via `svelte/server` and keeps `InvestmentHoldings.svelte`'s own state (month selection, the add/edit form) untouched by content that never changes at runtime.
- **Collapsed behind a native `<details>`, no open-by-default state.** Matches the disclosure pattern `StudentLoanRepayment.svelte`'s "All five plan types" and every chart's "Show as a table" already use in this codebase, and satisfies the issue's explicit ask that a returning user who already knows this content isn't made to scroll past it.
- **Every number quoted is imported from the module that already owns it, not restated as a literal.** `$lib/isa.js` supplies `ADULT_ISA_ALLOWANCE`, `JISA_ALLOWANCE` and `LISA_ANNUAL_SUBLIMIT`; `$lib/capital-gains.js` supplies `CGT_ANNUAL_EXEMPT_AMOUNT`, `RESIDENTIAL_CGT_BASIC_RATE` and `RESIDENTIAL_CGT_HIGHER_RATE`. This is what makes the CGT section possible at all without inventing a second set of figures: `capital-gains.js`'s own header already states the property and "other assets" CGT rates coincided from 30 October 2024, so the non-ISA CGT explanation the issue asks for is the property tool's existing rate ladder, read and reused, not a new one.
- **"UK ISA Options Explained" iterates `ISA_WRAPPERS` from `$lib/enums.js`** rather than a hand-written list, so the six wrappers and their order can never drift from what the ISA Allowance Tracker (Tax tab) already shows. Help to Buy's "closed to new applicants since 30 November 2019" line matches the fact `$lib/isa.js`'s `closedToNewAccounts` already asserts, rather than restating a second, possibly-differing date.
- **README.md's existing property CGT section gets one new bullet, not a duplicate figures block.** The issue explicitly warns against stating the same tax-figures twice where they could drift; since the Annual Exempt Amount and 18%/24% rates are already documented there for property, the new bullet says only that the same numbers apply to shares/funds/GIA gains too (aligned in the October 2024 Budget) and points at this issue's inline guidance as the place that reads them.

**Trade-offs / deviations from prompt:**
- **"One ISA of each type per tax year" is stated as no longer true, not restated as given.** The issue's Key Rules list names this as one of the rules to include, but HMRC removed that restriction for Cash, Stocks & Shares and Innovative Finance ISAs from 6 April 2024 — savers can now hold and pay into more than one of the same type in a tax year, with the £20,000 cap the only real constraint. Stating the pre-2024 rule as current would have been a factual error the app would be repeating knowingly, so the inline text gives the current rule instead. This session had no network access to re-confirm the change against gov.uk (the same constraint every prior tax-figure addition in this codebase records for its own numbers), so it carries the same "good confidence, not verified" posture as `capital-gains.js`'s own figures rather than being presented as settled.
- **Help to Buy ISA's bonus percentage and cap are described qualitatively ("a 25% government bonus, paid out when you complete on a home") rather than with the specific £1,600/£12,000/£3,000 thresholds.** Those figures are well outside what any file in this codebase already states, and with no network access to check them this session, adding new unverified numbers felt like a worse trade than a slightly less specific but safely correct sentence — the closure date and the shared-allowance fact, which are already asserted elsewhere in the codebase, are stated with the specific figures they already carry.
- **No new calculation, per the issue's explicit scope.** The CGT section explains the same rate ladder `capital-gains.js` already implements for property, but nothing here computes a non-property gain — the property scenario tool on the Tax tab remains the only CGT calculator in the app, and the guidance text says so.
- **Browser-verified this session.** Installed a scratch Playwright + Chromium (`npm install --no-save playwright`; `package.json`/`package-lock.json` confirmed untouched afterwards via `git status`), ran `npm run preview` against the production build, added a monthly snapshot on the Net Worth tab, and drove the real card: confirmed it renders collapsed by default below the investment holdings form, expands on click to show all four sections with the expected headings, and closed with zero console errors.
- **`npm test`: 2884 passed, up from 2872 on `main`** (net +12: 11 new `InvestmentGuidance.test.js` cases covering the four section headings, the shared-allowance wording, all six wrapper labels, the Help to Buy closure date, the Lifetime ISA figures and Pensions-tab pointer, the Innovative Finance risk caveat, and the CGT figures/scope statement — plus 1 new `InvestmentHoldings.test.js` case confirming the card is wired in). `npm run check`: 0 errors, the same 2 pre-existing `<slot>` warnings. `npm run lint` and `npm run build`: clean.
- **`npm test` still isn't a CI gate** (same flag as every prior entry) — the counts above are local; only `build`/`check`/`lint` run on the PR itself.

## Net Worth chart: month selector control + headline figure follows the selected month — 2026-08-12
<!-- METRICS:net-worth-chart-month-selector-control-headline-figure-follows-the-selected-month -->
- **Execution Duration:** __DURATION__ seconds
- **Model:** __MODEL__
- **Turns:** __TURNS__
- **Input Tokens:** __INPUT_TOKENS__
- **Output Tokens:** __OUTPUT_TOKENS__
- **Estimated Cost:** $__COST__ (from Claude Code's reported total_cost_usd)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable GPT-5-tier model:** $__GPT_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable Gemini-tier model:** $__GEMINI_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)

**Decisions:**
- **Scoped strictly to the issue's own narrowed description, not the prior max-turns attempt it points at.** This issue records that an earlier run hit `error_max_turns` (100) and left a pushed-but-unmerged branch (`net-worth-chart-month-selector`, commit `d1691b9`) implementing both this issue and parts of its follow-up, #288. That branch's diff moves the month-on-month change line, the "at `<month>`" caption and the first-snapshot caption onto the selected month too — all three are on this issue's own "Out of scope (→ #288)" list. Read as reference only, per the issue's own instruction; none of it was merged. This session's diff touches exactly one more thing than `latest.net_worth` was: the headline figure. `monthChange`, both captions and the accessible summary all still read `latest`, unchanged.
- **A `<select>` above the headline, not click-to-pin on the chart.** The chart's pointer is already spoken for by #87's `bisect-x` hover tooltip; a second, different meaning for a click on the same plot area would fight it rather than compose with it — a dropdown needs no new hit-testing and sits naturally beside the lens toggle it is stacked under. Options are most-recent-first (`[...points].reverse()`) so the default (top) option is the same month the headline already showed before this issue existed. Rendered only when `points.length > 1`, per spec.
- **`selectedMonthKey` is a nullable sentinel (`null` = "follow the latest month"), not seeded from `latest` at construction the way `forecastOn`/`horizon`/`lens` seed from their props.** The dashboard route (`+page.svelte`) constructs `NetWorthChart` with `monthlyEntries={[]}` and only fills it in after an async `onMount` (`hydrateAppData`); a selection captured eagerly at construction would freeze on "nothing recorded yet" and never see the real months once they load a moment later. Staying `null` sidesteps that race entirely — `effectiveMonthKey` re-resolves `selectedMonthKey ?? latest?.date.getTime()` against whatever `points` currently is, on every render, so it is correct the instant real data arrives regardless of when that is. The issue's own scope notes flagged this exact race up front, so it was designed around rather than discovered.
- **The `<select>` is bound with a plain one-way `value={effectiveMonthKey}` plus its own `onchange`, not `bind:value`.** A two-way binding would need `selectedMonthKey` itself to already equal the latest month's key to render pre-selected — exactly the mount-time assumption the point above rules out. Svelte still marks the matching server-rendered `<option>` `selected` for a plain one-way `value` (asserted directly in the new tests, the same mechanism #81's `bind:value`-based horizon picker already relies on for its own SSR test). `onchange` resets `selectedMonthKey` to `null` when the option picked is the current latest month, rather than pinning to that month's key, so re-selecting "the latest" from the dropdown resumes auto-following it and a month recorded afterwards is picked up without the user having to re-select it.
- **`selected` falls back to `latest` when `effectiveMonthKey` names no point in the current `points` array**, the same "degrade to today's behaviour rather than to nothing plotted" rule the mount-time race above already relies on. Nothing in this app deletes a recorded month out from under a mounted chart today, so this is defensive rather than reachable, but it costs nothing and keeps `selected` from ever being `undefined`.
- **`initialSelectedMonth` prop, following `initialLens`'s own precedent exactly** — a `{ month, year } | null` that seeds `selectedMonthKey` synchronously, so a server-rendered test can assert an earlier month's headline without a pointer to drive the `<select>` with. Every real caller leaves it unset.
- **Forecast anchor, month-on-month change, both captions and the accessible summary needed no code changes at all.** `forecastFromEntries` is still only ever called with `monthlyEntries` itself and has no concept of a selection; `monthChange`, the captions and `summary` still read `latest` untouched. This held automatically rather than needing a guard, and the new tests pin it down explicitly so a future #288 change that starts threading `selected` through the rest of the summary block does not silently drag the forecast anchor along with it.

**Trade-offs / deviations from prompt:**
- **Browser-verified this session.** Installed a scratch Playwright + Chromium (`npm install --no-save playwright`; confirmed via `git status` that `package.json`/`package-lock.json` were untouched afterwards, and the scratch driver script was deleted before finishing), built and ran `npm run preview`, then drove the real dashboard through its actual UI — the `+ Add month` / holding forms, not a seeded storage blob — to record three months (Jan/Feb/Mar 2026, each carrying forward the prior month's holdings per #285's pre-fill plus one new holding). Confirmed: the selector renders with `Mar 2026`, `Feb 2026`, `Jan 2026` in that order and `Mar 2026` pre-selected; selecting `Feb 2026` moves the headline from £96,000 to £50,000 (Feb's own carried-forward total); the month-on-month line stays `↑ £46,000 (+92%) month-on-month` (Mar vs Feb, unmoved by the selection); the caption stays `at Mar 2026 · 3 months recorded since Jan 2026`; the forecast line stays `Projected from your Mar 2026 snapshot`; and the chart's hover tooltip still opens on pointer-over. Zero console errors throughout.
- **`npm test`: 2892 passed, up from 2884 on `main`** (net +8, all in `NetWorthChart.test.js`: the selector's absence with one recorded month, the most-recent-first option order and default `selected` marking, the unchanged default headline, `initialSelectedMonth` moving the headline and the `selected` marking together, an explicit regression test that the month-on-month change/caption/summary stay on `latest` under a selection, and the forecast anchor staying on the true latest month regardless of selection). `npm run check`: 0 errors, the same 2 pre-existing `<slot>` warnings. `npm run lint` and `npm run build`: clean.
- **`npm test` still isn't a CI gate** (same flag as every prior entry) — the counts above are local; only `build`/`check`/`lint` run on the PR itself.

## Financial Headlines: month-over-month deltas and FIRE progress engine — 2026-08-12
<!-- METRICS:financial-headlines-deltas-and-fire-progress-engine -->
- **Execution Duration:** __DURATION__ seconds
- **Model:** __MODEL__
- **Turns:** __TURNS__
- **Input Tokens:** __INPUT_TOKENS__
- **Output Tokens:** __OUTPUT_TOKENS__
- **Estimated Cost:** $__COST__ (from Claude Code's reported total_cost_usd)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable GPT-5-tier model:** $__GPT_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable Gemini-tier model:** $__GEMINI_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)

**Decisions:**
- **New `$lib/headlines.js`, exported through `index.js` like every other feature module, rather than folding this into `net-worth.js` or `fire.js`.** The issue frames this as the first of a four-issue milestone (#262 smart insights, #263 fun content, #264 the dashboard card), and #262's own description says it "extends the same module, doesn't replace it" and "appends its own headline-generating rules" to this one's output — so the module needed to exist as its own thing from the start, with a combining function (`financialHeadlines`) shaped to be extended rather than rewritten.
- **Arithmetic and prose kept in separate functions.** `monthOverMonthDeltas(entries)` returns plain numbers (`{ current, previous, absolute, percentage }` per metric) with no wording baked in; five small `*Headline` functions (`netWorthDeltaHeadline`, `investmentsDeltaHeadline`, `debtsDeltaHeadline`, `fireProgressHeadline`, plus the `financialHeadlines` combiner) turn those numbers into the `{ id, text, tone }` sentences. The issue's own closing line — "this is arithmetic dressed as prose, get the arithmetic right first" — reads as a design instruction as much as a testing one, so the two are independently unit-tested rather than only asserting on final sentence strings.
- **Reused `net-worth.js`'s `netWorthSeries`/`netWorthPoint` for every figure rather than re-summing `investments[]`/`debts[]` by hand**, per the issue's explicit instruction. `monthOverMonthDeltas` calls `netWorthSeries(entries)` once (which already sorts by `compareMonthlyEntries` and runs every holding through `debt.js`'s `sumInvestmentValues`/`sumDebtBalances`, respecting `exclude_from_net_worth`) and diffs its last two points — so this module can never disagree with the net worth chart about what a month's investments, debts or net worth add up to.
- **FI number target field confirmed by grep, not assumed:** `profile.retirement_target` is what `retirement/+page.svelte`'s own doc comment and `FireCalculator.svelte` both read as "target annual retirement income," so `fireProgressHeadline` reads that field and feeds it straight into `fire.js`'s `fireNumber()`. The withdrawal rate has no equivalent home on `Profile` — `FireCalculator.svelte` keeps it as a page-session-only slider defaulting to `fire.js`'s own `DEFAULT_WITHDRAWAL_RATE` — so this headline defaults to the same constant, with an optional override parameter for a future caller that has a better number.
- **Delta headlines suppress themselves when there's nothing real to say, not just when there's nothing to compare against.** `investmentsDeltaHeadline`/`debtsDeltaHeadline` return `null` when both the current and previous month's figure are £0 (never held any investments, never carried any debt) — otherwise a debt-free household would see "Debts are unchanged this month." every single month forever, which is noise dressed as an observation. `netWorthDeltaHeadline` has no such guard since net worth £0 in both months is itself worth saying.
- **Tone is direction-of-change, computed once per headline and carried on the object**, not re-derived from the sentence text later — matches the issue's own reasoning for including `tone` at all ("so the UI issue can colour them without re-deriving sentiment from the text"). Debts flips the polarity from investments/net worth (falling debt is `positive`) since less debt is the good outcome; `fireProgressHeadline`'s tone follows this month's percentage-point movement toward the target, not whether the target has been reached, for consistency with how every other headline in this engine assigns tone.
- **Percentage precision matches the user's own three examples exactly, not one shared format**: net worth's month-on-month percentage and the FIRE headline's "% of the way" figure are both one decimal place (`9.6%`, `152.5%`), but the FIRE headline's percentage-*point* change since last month is two decimals (`13.33%`) — copied verbatim from the issue body's own wording rather than picking a single convention and forcing all three figures through it.

**Trade-offs / deviations from prompt:**
- **No UI added**, per the issue's own explicit scope ("this issue is the calculation engine only... no UI") — `financialHeadlines()` is not wired into any route yet; #264 does that once #262/#263 also exist.
- **`fireProgressHeadline`'s signature is `(profile, entries, { withdrawalRate })`** rather than accepting a pre-computed current net worth, so it independently derives "current net worth" from `entries` via the same `netWorthSeries` path `monthOverMonthDeltas` uses — the two engines can be called separately (e.g. a future caller with only a profile and no monthly history yet) without one having to thread its intermediate result into the other.
- **`npm test`: 2916 passed** (up 24 from the pre-existing baseline, all in the new `headlines.test.js`; every other suite unchanged). `npm run check`: 0 errors, the same 2 pre-existing `<slot>` warnings from `card.svelte`/`button.svelte`. `npm run lint` and `npx prettier --check .`: clean.
- **`npm test` still isn't a CI gate** (same flag as every prior entry) — the counts above are local; only `build`/`check`/`lint` run on the PR itself.

## Financial Headlines: dad joke, a nugget to ponder, and curated learning resources — 2026-08-12
<!-- METRICS:financial-headlines-dad-joke-nugget-and-curated-resources -->
- **Execution Duration:** __DURATION__ seconds
- **Model:** __MODEL__
- **Turns:** __TURNS__
- **Input Tokens:** __INPUT_TOKENS__
- **Output Tokens:** __OUTPUT_TOKENS__
- **Estimated Cost:** $__COST__ (from Claude Code's reported total_cost_usd)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable GPT-5-tier model:** $__GPT_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable Gemini-tier model:** $__GEMINI_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)

**Decisions:**
- **New `$lib/worth-knowing.js`, not folded into `headlines.js`.** The issue is explicitly "independent of the two calculation-engine issues elsewhere in this milestone, no dependency either way" — this content has nothing to calculate from the household's own data, unlike `headlines.js`'s deltas/FIRE progress or #262's smart insights. Exported through `index.js` immediately after `headlines.js`'s own export, matching that module's precedent of "engine only, no UI, here" — #264 is still the one issue in this milestone that touches a route or component.
- **Deterministic monthly rotation re-derives the same FNV-1a hash `version.js`'s `codenameForSha` uses, rather than importing it.** The issue names that function as the model to follow ("hashed the same way… so it's stable within a session/month rather than re-rolling on every render, but still varies month to month"), but `version.js`'s `hash` isn't exported and the two modules hash unrelated inputs (a commit SHA vs. a `YYYY-M` month key) for unrelated reasons — re-deriving the ~10-line algorithm locally seemed a smaller cost than either exporting an internal of a build-versioning module or adding a cross-import that would only ever be used once. `dadJokeOfTheMonth`/`nuggetToPonder` both default to `forecast.js`'s existing `currentCalendarMonth()` (the same "today's calendar month" helper the forecast anchor already uses) but accept an explicit `{ month, year }` override, so tests can pin specific months without depending on wall-clock time.
- **Separate hash salts (`'dad-joke:'` / `'nugget:'`) prepended to the month key for each bank**, not one shared hash reused as an index into both — otherwise the joke and the nugget would always land on the same relative position in their respective banks every month, a correlation with no reason to exist.
- **Curated resources are a fixed list (`curatedResources()`), not rotated monthly like the joke/nugget.** The issue's own framing — "a short, real list" the user asked for both video and reading formats — reads as a reference list meant to be seen in full, not a monthly-varying nugget; hiding five of six entries behind a rotation every month would work against the point of curating them. Six entries (3 websites, 3 YouTube channels): Monevator, MoneySavingExpert and MoneyHelper for websites; Damien Talks Money, The Humble Penny and James Shack for YouTube — chosen as long-standing, UK-specific, non-product-selling resources across a spread of angles (passive investing/FIRE, consumer money guides, official impartial guidance, mainstream accessible video, FIRE-from-a-couple's-perspective, and chartered-planner retirement/drawdown content) rather than six variations on the same niche.
- **Both content banks are sized well past the issue's 20–30 floor with real, distinct entries (27 jokes, 24 nuggets) rather than one bank stretched thin to hit both counts** — the issue explicitly warns against padding with weak filler, and a shared theme (e.g. "compound interest") appears at most once per bank rather than being reworded three times to inflate the count.
- **Nuggets mix short attributed quotes with original observations, and only quote where the wording is common-knowledge-famous enough to be confident in without a source to check against this session** (Buffett's "spend what is left after saving," Franklin's "investment in knowledge," Housel's "wealth is what you don't see") — see the trade-off below on why nothing more obscure got a quotation mark and a name attached to it.

**Trade-offs / deviations from prompt:**
- **No live web research for the curated resources list — `WebSearch`/`WebFetch` were both unavailable this session** (permission requests for both were declined/unavailable in this run's environment), despite the issue's explicit instruction to "verify these are real, currently-active, and genuinely well-regarded at implementation time rather than trusting any specific names suggested here." The six names picked are long-established (Monevator has run since 2007; MoneySavingExpert and MoneyHelper are large, well-known UK institutions unlikely to have disappeared; the three YouTube channels are recalled as active, UK-focused and well-regarded as of this model's training data) but that is "good confidence, not verified" — the same posture prior entries in this journal have recorded for tax-figure claims made without network access (see the ISA/CGT entries above). If live web access is available in a future session, spot-checking that all six URLs/handles still resolve and still match this description would be worth doing before this list is trusted as current.
- **No UI added**, per the issue's own framing that the dashboard card is "built in the final issue of this milestone" (#264) — `worthKnowing()` is not wired into any route yet, matching `headlines.js`'s own "no UI, no dependency" precedent from #261.
- **No display-tone/colour concept attached to this content**, unlike `headlines.js`'s `{ id, text, tone }` shape. A dad joke or a Buffett quote has no positive/negative direction the way a net-worth delta does — the issue's own display note asks for a section "distinct in tone" from the numeric headlines by being visually separate, not by carrying a sentiment value, so `worthKnowing()`'s return shape (`{ dadJoke, nugget, resources }`) is deliberately unlike `financialHeadlines()`'s array-of-uniform-objects rather than forcing a `tone: 'neutral'` field that would mean nothing.
- **`npm test`: 2930 passed, up from 2916 on `main`** (net +14, all in the new `worth-knowing.test.js`: determinism and spread-across-a-year checks for both the joke and the nugget, non-empty-string checks, year (not just month-number) sensitivity, and the curated resources list's shape — non-empty, fixed across calls, both `youtube`/`website` types present, well-formed ids/urls/names/descriptions, no duplicate ids). `npm run check`: 0 errors, the same 2 pre-existing `<slot>` warnings. `npm run lint`, `npx prettier --check .` and `npm run build`: clean.
- **`npm test` still isn't a CI gate** (same flag as every prior entry) — the counts above are local; only `build`/`check`/`lint` run on the PR itself.

## Financial Headlines: smart insights (new holdings, concentration, milestones, composition) — 2026-08-12
<!-- METRICS:financial-headlines-smart-insights -->
- **Execution Duration:** __DURATION__ seconds
- **Model:** __MODEL__
- **Turns:** __TURNS__
- **Input Tokens:** __INPUT_TOKENS__
- **Output Tokens:** __OUTPUT_TOKENS__
- **Estimated Cost:** $__COST__ (from Claude Code's reported total_cost_usd)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable GPT-5-tier model:** $__GPT_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable Gemini-tier model:** $__GEMINI_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)

**Decisions:**
- **Extended the existing `$lib/headlines.js`/`headlines.test.js` pair rather than adding a new module**, per the issue's own opening line ("extends the same module, doesn't replace it") and per #261's own doc comment naming this exact split. The four new rules (`newHoldingHeadlines`, `concentrationHeadline`, `milestoneHeadlines`, `pensionCompositionHeadline`) are appended to `financialHeadlines`'s output after the existing four, in a fixed order; #264's dashboard card still doesn't need to know which rule produced which entry.
- **Holding identity across months is name + wrapper, not `id`, confirmed by grep rather than assumed from the `Investment` typedef's own claim that `id` is "stable across months for the same holding."** `model.js`'s `createNextMonthlyEntry` — the function that pre-fills a new month from the last one — mints a fresh `id` for every copied holding (`createInvestment({ ...investment, id: createId('inv') })`), so `id` is not actually stable in practice. This matches the issue's own explicit instruction for the new-holding rule ("a name/wrapper combination not present in the previous month's"), and the same `holdingKey` helper is reused for the milestone rule's per-holding tracking for consistency.
- **A shared `countedHoldings` helper filters `exclude_from_net_worth` uniformly across all four new rules**, matching what `netWorthSeries`/`sumInvestmentValues` already exclude from the totals these rules read from — so a holding the user has explicitly said shouldn't count towards net worth (e.g. a house deposit tracked on the property tab) can't trigger a "new holding," "milestone" or "concentrated" headline either.
- **Milestone bands are a 1/2.5/5 geometric sequence (`£1,000` … `£10,000,000`)**, not the user's own `£5,000` example hardcoded as the one figure this rule can ever report — the same shape `milestones.js`'s own `£100k/£250k/£500k/£1M` forecast pills use one order of magnitude up. Only the *highest* newly-crossed band is reported per total per month (not every band passed through), so a big first recorded month doesn't fire ten headlines at once. "First month crossing," not "above it this month," is enforced by comparing against the *maximum* of every prior recorded month rather than just the previous one, per the issue's explicit instruction — since that maximum only ever grows, a value that dips back under a band and re-crosses it can never re-announce it.
- **Milestone crossings are checked for all three totals the issue names**: net worth, total counted investments, and each individual counted holding (by `holdingKey`) — not just the one concrete investments example given. The very first recorded month is treated as eligible to cross a band (prior maximum `-Infinity`), since "first month above £X" is true whether or not there's history to compare it against.
- **Concentration checks two independent groupings, not "whichever holding is biggest within its own wrapper."** A single holding's value can never exceed its own wrapper's total, so grouping "by holding" using the same per-wrapper total as the denominator would make the wrapper check always win except at exact ties — not a meaningful "or" per the issue's "one wrapper or holding" wording. Instead, holdings are also grouped **by name, summed across every wrapper they appear in** (the same fund split between an ISA and a SIPP is one concentration risk, not two small ones below the threshold) — a genuinely independent signal that can exceed any single wrapper's share. Threshold is 65%, the middle of the issue's suggested 60–70% range. A wrapper/holding tie (the ordinary case — a wrapper holding exactly one thing) resolves to the wrapper label, matching the user's own example naming "Cash ISA" rather than whatever fund sits inside it.
- **Composition reports the pension pot as a share of (net worth + the pension pot itself), while still calling that combined figure "your net worth" in the sentence, matching the user's own wording.** This app's own `netWorthSeries` deliberately excludes pensions from "net worth" (documented on `report.js` too) — so read literally, a pension can never be "N% of net worth" without N being unbounded. Reuses `retirement-income.js`'s own `definedContributionPot` (DC workplace pensions and SIPPs — the only pension types with a monetary pot to report at all; Defined Benefit and State pensions pay an income, not a pot) rather than re-summing `pensions[]` here, per the issue's explicit instruction. Threshold is a plain majority (50%), per the issue's own "pensions being the majority of net worth" framing.
- **`financialHeadlines`'s signature grew an optional `pensions = []` parameter** rather than a breaking required one, so every existing caller (and every pre-existing test) keeps working unchanged; only a caller that wants the composition rule needs to pass pensions in.
- **Two of the pre-existing `financialHeadlines` tests needed new fixtures, not just new assertions.** Their old single-holding, unnamed, default-wrapper (`gia`) fixtures are now, correctly, 100% concentrated in one wrapper by the new concentration rule, and a bare `£1,000` first month now correctly crosses this rule's smallest milestone band — so "nothing to report" was no longer true of those specific numbers once the new rules exist. Rewrote both fixtures to be genuinely diversified across four wrappers (so concentration stays quiet) and below the smallest milestone band (so milestones stay quiet too), which is what the tests were actually trying to assert in the first place.

**Trade-offs / deviations from prompt:**
- **No UI added**, per the same "calculation engine only" scope #261 established — none of the four new functions are wired into any route; #264 still owns the dashboard card.
- **Per-holding milestone tracking is a design extrapolation, not a literal instruction.** The issue's parenthetical — "a recorded total (net worth, investments, a specific holding)" — names three totals to watch but gives a concrete example for only one (investments crossing £5,000). Implemented all three for completeness rather than only the one with an example, reusing the same `highestNewlyCrossedBand` helper for all three.
- **`npm test`: 2945 passed** (up 29 from the 2916 baseline: 53 tests now in `headlines.test.js`, up from 24, plus the two rewritten `financialHeadlines` fixtures counted as changes not additions; every other suite unchanged). `npm run check`: 0 errors, the same 2 pre-existing `<slot>` warnings from `card.svelte`/`button.svelte` (required one explicit `@type` annotation on `milestoneHeadlines`' local `headlines` array to stop TypeScript widening `tone` to `string`). `npm run lint` and `npx prettier --check .`: clean.
- **`npm test` still isn't a CI gate** (same flag as every prior entry) — the counts above are local; only `build`/`check`/`lint` run on the PR itself.

## Financial Headlines: dashboard card combining deltas, insights and the fun content — 2026-08-12
<!-- METRICS:financial-headlines-dashboard-card-combining-deltas-insights-and-fun-content -->
- **Execution Duration:** __DURATION__ seconds
- **Model:** __MODEL__
- **Turns:** __TURNS__
- **Input Tokens:** __INPUT_TOKENS__
- **Output Tokens:** __OUTPUT_TOKENS__
- **Estimated Cost:** $__COST__ (from Claude Code's reported total_cost_usd)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable GPT-5-tier model:** $__GPT_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable Gemini-tier model:** $__GEMINI_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)

**Decisions:**
- **Built strictly against what `financialHeadlines()` returns today, without special-casing #262.** The issue's dependency line says all three prior Financial Headlines issues should land first, but a `gh issue`/`gh pr` check at the start of this session showed #262 ("smart insights") is still open — its PR (`#291`, branch `financial-headlines-smart-insights`) exists but was never merged, and a since-merged dad-joke PR (`#292`) even clobbered a since-abandoned direct-to-main commit of #262's work back out of `headlines.js` along the way, so `main` currently has only the delta/FIRE-progress engine from #261. Re-implementing #262 was out of scope for this issue and risked conflicting with an open PR possibly still being worked; instead this card renders whatever `financialHeadlines()`'s array contains, in order, with no knowledge of which rule produced which entry — exactly the contract both #261's and #262's own doc comments already promise ("the display issue later in this milestone doesn't need to know which engine-half produced which headline"). Once #262 merges, its new headlines will appear in this card with no code change here.
- **New `FinancialHeadlines.svelte`, placed above `NetWorthChart` on `+page.svelte`.** The issue leaves placement to judgement once both are on screen together; headlines read naturally as the opening summary of the month before the chart that backs them up, the way a news front page runs headlines before the detail.
- **`profile` added to `+page.svelte`'s own state, read-only, following the exact convention `growthRate`/`partner` already use there** (hydrated once from the store on mount, never written back — entering/editing a profile stays the Settings tab's job). Needed because `fireProgressHeadline` reads `profile.retirement_target`, and the dashboard route previously only pulled `profile.growth_rate` out into a local variable, never the whole `Profile` object.
- **Tone shown as both colour and an `aria-hidden` glyph (▲/▼/–) on a left-border-accented row, mirroring `DebtTracker.svelte`'s D/I-ratio status-chip classes** (`bg-*-50`/`text-*-700ish`/`border-*` triples) rather than inventing a new colour language for this card. Every headline's own sentence already states its direction in words ("up", "down", "unchanged") independently of both the colour and the glyph, so colour is never the only signal reaching the user, per the issue's accessibility requirement — a `sr-only` "Positive:"/"Negative:"/"Neutral:" label was added on top of that as an explicit third channel for a screen reader that skips straight to the text.
- **Two independent empty-state messages, not one.** `monthlyEntries.length === 0` gets "no snapshot recorded yet" (the whole numeric section is skipped, matching `EstateSummary.svelte`'s "don't show £0 as if it were real" convention); a single recorded month gets "only one month recorded, no month-over-month change yet" — computed from `monthOverMonthDeltas(monthlyEntries) !== null` directly, not from `headlines.length`, because a single month can still carry a valid FIRE-progress headline (it needs no prior month to compare against) — showing both the "record a second month" note and a FIRE headline together at once, rather than one silently suppressing the other, is what stays honest about both.
- **"Worth knowing" always renders**, unconditionally beneath the numeric section (inside the same `Card`, separated by a border and its own `h3`), regardless of whether `monthlyEntries` is empty or `headlines` came back `[]` — matching #263's own framing that the fun content has "no dependency either way" on the calculation engines.
- **External resource links carry `rel="external"` alongside `rel="noopener noreferrer"`.** `eslint-plugin-svelte`'s `svelte/no-navigation-without-resolve` flags any dynamic `<a href={...}>` by default (it can't tell a data-driven external URL from an internal route needing SvelteKit's `resolve()`), and Prettier reformats the `href="{resource.url}"` string-literal dodge straight back into `href={resource.url}`. `rel="external"` is the rule's own documented escape hatch for exactly this case (a purpose-built check in its source, `hasRelExternal`), so that's what's used rather than suppressing the rule with an eslint-disable comment.
- **Browser-verified this session**, beyond the server-rendered test suite: installed a scratch Playwright (`npx playwright install chromium`; nothing added to `package.json`/`package-lock.json`, confirmed via `git status`), ran `npm run dev`, and drove the real page twice — once against a genuinely empty store (confirms the "no snapshot recorded yet" message and the Worth Knowing section with no numeric headlines above it) and once after seeding two months of investments/debts directly into IndexedDB's `app-data` store the same way `browser-storage.js` itself writes it (confirms all four headline kinds render with the correct green/positive styling, left border and glyph, and that the rest of the dashboard — chart, holdings, debt ratio — reflects the same seeded data). Zero console errors in either pass.

**Trade-offs / deviations from prompt:**
- **The card cannot show any #262 smart-insight headline yet, since #262 itself hasn't merged to `main`** (see the first Decisions bullet) — today it only ever renders the net-worth/investments/debts deltas and the FIRE-progress headline from #261's engine. This is a consequence of milestone sequencing outside this issue's control, not a gap in this issue's own implementation; the card was written to need no change once #262 lands.
- **No project `run` skill existed for this app** (checked per the `run` skill's own instructions before falling back), so the browser verification above used an ad hoc Playwright script written to a temp path outside the repo and deleted afterwards, rather than a reusable one. Worth a `/run-skill-generator` pass in a future session if browser verification becomes routine for this repo.
- **`npm test`: 2938 passed, up from 2930 on `main`** (net +8, all in the new `FinancialHeadlines.test.js`: heading/structure smoke test, every headline kind present in the populated case, the two-months-recorded case saying nothing about needing a second month, both empty-state messages (zero months, exactly one month, one month with and without a FIRE target), and the Worth Knowing section rendering identically whether or not any numeric headlines exist). `npm run check`: 0 errors, the same 2 pre-existing `<slot>` warnings. `npm run lint`, `npx prettier --check .` and `npm run build`: clean.
- **`npm test` still isn't a CI gate** (same flag as every prior entry) — the counts above are local; only `build`/`check`/`lint` run on the PR itself.

## Net Worth chart: make the remaining headline readouts follow the selected month — 2026-08-12
<!-- METRICS:net-worth-chart-remaining-headline-readouts-follow-selected-month -->
- **Execution Duration:** __DURATION__ seconds
- **Model:** __MODEL__
- **Turns:** __TURNS__
- **Input Tokens:** __INPUT_TOKENS__
- **Output Tokens:** __OUTPUT_TOKENS__
- **Estimated Cost:** $__COST__ (from Claude Code's reported total_cost_usd)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable GPT-5-tier model:** $__GPT_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable Gemini-tier model:** $__GEMINI_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)

**Decisions:**
- **`monthChange` now derives from `selected` rather than `points` directly, but `$lib/net-worth.js`'s `monthOnMonthChange(points)` keeps its one signature.** It still just compares the last two entries of whatever array it is handed; the component finds `selected`'s index in `points` with `points.indexOf` (safe because `selected` is always either a member of `points` or the `latest` fallback, both drawn from the same array) and hands `monthOnMonthChange` the prefix `points.slice(0, index + 1)`. Picking March compares March to February by construction, not because the function learned what "March" means — exactly the reuse the issue asked for over teaching it an index-aware second signature.
- **The two headline captions (`at {month} · N months recorded since {first}` and the single-snapshot `{month} — your first snapshot.`) both switched their leading `formatMonth(latest)` to `formatMonth(selected)`, but `points.length` and `formatMonth(first)` stayed put.** The issue draws this line explicitly — "N months recorded since {first} stays a fact about the whole series, not about the selection" — and it was worth calling out in code, not just in the diff, since a later "helpfully" pass is exactly the kind of change likely to blur it back together.
- **The accessible summary gets one new conditional sentence — `Showing {selected}, not the latest recorded month.` — appended only when `selected !== latest`, after the existing shape-of-the-line sentence and before the forecast sentence.** The issue's third bullet left the exact wording open ("if it reads better there" for the selector's own label instead); a sentence in the summary was simpler than restructuring the `<select>`'s `<label>`, and it is the one place already responsible for telling a screen reader user things the visual layout communicates for free — here, that the number they are about to hear is not the latest month's.
- **Forecast anchoring was untouched by design, and the existing test asserting it (`keeps the forecast anchored on the actual latest month regardless of selection`) needed no change** — `forecastFromEntries` still takes `monthlyEntries` whole, never a slice, and the "Projected from your {month} snapshot…" caption still reads `latest`. Re-verified rather than assumed: ran the full suite with the new `monthChange`/caption/summary changes in place and confirmed that test still passes unmodified.
- **Replaced the one test that documented #288 as future work** (`leaves the month-on-month change, caption and accessible summary on the latest month — out of scope for #260, picked up by #288`) **with six narrower ones**: the caption moving to the selected month while the "recorded since" fact stays put, the month-on-month figure comparing the selected month to its predecessor, the month-on-month line disappearing entirely when the first recorded month is selected (nothing to compare it against — the same `< 2 points` null `monthOnMonthChange` already returns), and a pair covering the new summary sentence's presence/absence.

**Trade-offs / deviations from prompt:**
- **No on-screen or `<select>`-label change beyond the accessible summary sentence.** The issue offered two places to name the selected month for assistive tech — the summary or the selector's own labelling — "if it reads better there." The summary already carries the full shape-of-the-line sentence and is read as one block, so adding the clarifying sentence there rather than editing the `<label for="{uid}-month">` avoided two half-answers to the same question competing for a screen reader's attention.
