- **`npm test`: 3006 passed, up from 3002 on `main`** (net +4: five new/rewritten cases in the `NetWorthChart month selector` describe block minus the one removed placeholder test). `npm run check`: 0 errors, the same 2 pre-existing `<slot>` warnings from `card.svelte`/`button.svelte`. `npm run lint` (`prettier --check .` + `eslint .`) and `npm run build`: clean.
- **No browser verification this session.** The change is confined to text nodes already covered by the server-rendered test suite (headline figure, caption, month-on-month line, accessible summary) — no new interactive control, no `<Chart>` internals touched, and #260's own prior entry already covered the `<select>`'s browser behaviour. `npm test`/`npm run check`/`npm run lint`/`npm run build` above are the verification for this change.
- **`npm test` still isn't a CI gate** (same flag as every prior entry) — the counts above are local; only `build`/`check`/`lint` run on the PR itself.

## Budget: import bank statement CSVs via a generic column-mapper — 2026-08-12
<!-- METRICS:budget-import-bank-statement-csvs-via-a-generic-column-mapper -->
- **Execution Duration:** __DURATION__ seconds
- **Model:** __MODEL__
- **Turns:** __TURNS__
- **Input Tokens:** __INPUT_TOKENS__
- **Output Tokens:** __OUTPUT_TOKENS__
- **Estimated Cost:** $__COST__ (from Claude Code's reported total_cost_usd)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable GPT-5-tier model:** $__GPT_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable Gemini-tier model:** $__GEMINI_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)

**Decisions:**
- **New `$lib/bank-csv-import.js`, deliberately not touching the whole `AppData` document.** `csv-import.js`'s `parseCsvImport` validates its merged result through `model.js`'s `validateAppData` because it writes into `monthly_entries`, a structure with many cross-field invariants. This module only ever produces fresh `BudgetLineItem`s (via `createBudgetLineItem`, always a new id, an `Math.abs`-clamped non-negative amount, and a `category_id` that is either `null` or an id read straight off the caller's own `budget.categories`), so the result is valid by construction — the same guarantee `BudgetTracker.svelte`'s existing manual "Add item" form already relies on without a validation pass of its own. The module hands back `lineItems`/`summary` only, not a document; `BankCsvImport.svelte` appends them the same way `submitLineItem` already does: `budget = { ...budget, line_items: [...budget.line_items, ...added] }`.
- **The mapped date is parsed, validated and reported per-row like every other cell — then stored in `notes`, not a new field.** `BudgetLineItem` is `id`/`name`/`amount`/`category_id`/`notes` only (the issue quotes this shape verbatim), and adding a `date` field was out of scope. A bad date is still a specific, row-numbered rejection (`row 4 (Date): "31/02/2026" is not a valid calendar date for DD/MM/YYYY`) rather than silently dropped or defaulted, matching `csv-import.js`'s own "tell the user exactly what's wrong" standard — the parsed ISO date just lands in `notes` afterwards rather than nowhere.
- **Category matching, not category entry.** The mapping screen has an optional "Category column" select, but never asks the user to pick a `BudgetCategory` per row. A mapped column's text is matched case-insensitively against existing `budget.categories[].name`; anything that doesn't match (or no column mapped at all) imports with `category_id: null`, exactly the issue's "leave unmapped/unset rather than forcing a category-per-row" instruction. Browser-verified: importing a "Groceries"-tagged row against an existing "Groceries" category attached it correctly; a same-file row with no matching category text came through uncategorised.
- **Amount handling covers both real-world shapes as two named modes on one `BankCsvMapping` object** (`'signed'` with one amount column, `'debit-credit'` with two) rather than two separate mapping types, so the mapping form is one `<fieldset>` with a radio toggle rather than two different screens. Both currency-symbol/thousands-separator stripping and parenthesised-negative reading happen in one shared cell parser (`parseBankAmountCell`); `debit-credit` mode additionally rejects a row carrying both a debit and a credit value (ambiguous — which one is the transaction?) or neither (nothing to import), rather than guessing. Locale-aware decimal commas (`1.234,56`) are explicitly out of scope per this module's own header comment — comma is always read as a thousands separator, matching a UK-first app and the issue's literal "currency symbol/thousands-separator" wording rather than a full locale parser.
- **`guessBankCsvMapping(header)` pre-fills the mapping form from the header row's own text** (keyword matching for Date/Description/Amount/Debit/Credit/Category column names) so a well-labelled bank export needs no changes before clicking Continue — confirmed in the browser pass below, where a `Date,Description,Amount,Category` header was mapped correctly with zero manual selection. This is a UX nicety beyond the issue's literal ask, not a requirement, but keeps the "one-time mapping step per upload" from being tedious for the common case; every field is still just a plain `<select>` the user can override.
- **Three-phase UI (`idle` → `mapping` → `confirming`) in a new `BankCsvImport.svelte`**, wired into `BudgetTracker.svelte` as a fourth card between "Recurring bills" and "One-off items" — the same upload/mapping-or-confirm/confirm split `DataManager.svelte` already uses for JSON and Holdings/Debts CSV import, reused here rather than inventing a different shape. A row-level parse failure at the mapping step (bad date, non-numeric amount) reports every problem and *stays* on the mapping screen rather than advancing, so the user can fix the mapping (or re-upload a corrected file) and retry without starting over.
- **No `flushAppDataSync()` call anywhere in this feature**, unlike `DataManager.svelte`'s imports. `BudgetTracker.svelte` only ever mutates its bindable `budget` prop; the `/budget` page's own `$effect` already writes `budget` changes back into the shared `appData` store, whose own debounced sync persists them. Adding a second persistence path here would have been redundant with, and risked racing, that existing one.
- **Browser-verified this session** (same approach the previous Financial Headlines entry took, since no project `run` skill exists yet — see Trade-offs): installed a scratch Playwright, ran `npm run dev`, and drove `/budget` end to end — added a "Groceries" category, uploaded a 3-row CSV where one row had a non-numeric amount, confirmed the mapping auto-guessed all four columns correctly, clicked Continue and saw the exact row-numbered error for the bad row with the file unchanged, re-uploaded a 2-row clean version of the same file, clicked through to the confirm summary ("2 transactions will be added... 1 matched an existing category by name, 1 will be uncategorised"), confirmed, and verified both transactions appeared in One-off items with the right amount, category and the imported date in Notes, with the monthly totals recalculating correctly. Zero console errors throughout.

**Trade-offs / deviations from prompt:**
- **No project `run` skill existed for this app** (checked per the `run` skill's own instructions before falling back, same as the last session that needed browser verification), so the pass above used an ad hoc Playwright script at a temp path outside the repo, deleted afterwards rather than kept as a reusable skill.
- **Date format support is five common layouts (`DD/MM/YYYY`, `YYYY-MM-DD`, `MM/DD/YYYY`, `DD-MM-YYYY`, `DD.MM.YYYY`), not an open-ended parser.** The issue asks for "a date format picker (UK DD/MM/YYYY vs YYYY-MM-DD vs others — banks vary)" without an exhaustive list; these five cover every UK/US bank-export layout encountered in practice (the `-`/`.` variants are what a spreadsheet re-save of the `/` layout commonly produces). Adding another format later is a one-line addition to `BANK_CSV_DATE_FORMATS`.
- **`npm test`: 3002 passed, up from 2967 on `main`** (net +35: 30 in the new `bank-csv-import.test.js` covering every date format, both amount modes, category matching, and edge cases like an incomplete mapping or a too-short row; 4 in `BankCsvImport.test.js`'s server-rendered idle-state smoke test, following every other component test in this repo's "no browser test environment, initial render only" convention; 1 in `BudgetTracker.test.js` confirming the new card renders). `npm run check`: 0 errors, the same 2 pre-existing `<slot>` warnings from `card.svelte`/`button.svelte` (one JSDoc fix was needed: `BankCsvDateFormat.order` had to be typed `readonly string[]` rather than a narrow `('day'|'month'|'year')[]` union, which `Object.freeze(['day','month','year'])` widened to plain `string[]` and failed to satisfy). `npm run lint`, `npx prettier --check .` and `npm run build`: clean.
- **`npm test` still isn't a CI gate** (same flag as every prior entry) — the counts above are local; only `build`/`check`/`lint` run on the PR itself.

## Live share/fund prices: provider research + single-ticker quote fetch — 2026-08-12
<!-- METRICS:live-share-fund-prices-provider-research-single-ticker-quote-fetch -->
- **Execution Duration:** __DURATION__ seconds
- **Model:** __MODEL__
- **Turns:** __TURNS__
- **Input Tokens:** __INPUT_TOKENS__
- **Output Tokens:** __OUTPUT_TOKENS__
- **Estimated Cost:** $__COST__ (from Claude Code's reported total_cost_usd)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable GPT-5-tier model:** $__GPT_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable Gemini-tier model:** $__GEMINI_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)

**Decisions:**
- **Re-verified the provider live rather than trusting the prior session's notes, and it changed the answer.** #266's own issue body flags Twelve Data as a provisional pick from an earlier session that ran out of turns before committing anything. `curl`-ing Twelve Data's actual `/exchanges` page this session (its `Min. individual plan` column, checked against the real live markup, not the marketing copy) shows the free "Basic" plan's coverage is Crypto, Forex and US stocks only — the pricing page's own "3 Markets" stat for that tier — and the London Stock Exchange sits behind the paid "Grow" plan ($29/mo). The free-tier `symbol_search` endpoint does find LSE tickers (it works off the whole instrument database, not the plan-gated pricing data), which is almost certainly what fooled the earlier session into thinking the free tier covered UK listings when the pricing endpoint itself does not. Financial Modeling Prep's current pricing page was checked the same way and states outright that "UK and Canada Coverage" needs its $59/mo Premium plan. **Alpha Vantage** was the only one of the three whose own documentation demonstrates London Stock Exchange coverage on the free tier (a `TSCO.LON` example sitting right next to the US one) — confirmed live for CORS too (`Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *` on every response, checked with `curl -H "Origin: ..."` against all three providers). Its trade-off is a hard 25-requests/day cap (verified against its current pricing page, not assumed from memory), which is what makes this feature manual/on-demand-only rather than a per-holding auto-refresh.
- **`mapTickerToQuery` only translates the one suffix actually verified — `.L` (Yahoo-style, this app's convention per #265) to Alpha Vantage's `.LON`** — and returns `null` for every other suffix (`.DE`, `.PA`, `.HK`, ...) rather than guessing at an exchange code, per the issue's explicit instruction. A bare ticker with no suffix is treated as a US symbol and passed through upper-cased unchanged, matching Alpha Vantage's own default-market behaviour.
- **`fetchQuote` classifies failures by inspecting the documented Alpha Vantage response shapes rather than the demo API key's live behaviour**, since the public `demo` key only actually resolves for one hard-coded symbol (`AAPL` on `GLOBAL_QUOTE`) and 401s on everything else, including other real US tickers — there was no way to observe a genuine unrecognised-ticker or rate-limit response live without spending part of the 25/day budget on a throwaway key mid-session. The classification (`Note`/`Information`-with-rate-limit-wording → `rate-limited`; missing or empty `Global Quote` / missing `05. price` → `unrecognised-ticker`; thrown fetch → `network-error`; non-OK HTTP status → `network-error`; JSON parse failure → `unparseable-response`) follows Alpha Vantage's own documented and widely-observed response shapes for `GLOBAL_QUOTE`, and is exercised in `price-feed.test.js` against hand-built mock bodies of each shape rather than live traffic.
- **`fetchQuote` never throws, by construction**: both the `fetch()` call and the `response.json()` parse are wrapped in their own `try`/`catch`, each returning a typed failure result rather than letting a rejected promise or a `SyntaxError` propagate — the one hard requirement from the issue, since #298 will loop this over a whole portfolio and can't afford to guard every call itself.
- **`VITE_ALPHA_VANTAGE_API_KEY` documented in `.env.example` with the same "inlined into the client bundle" caveat `VITE_GITHUB_TOKEN` already carries, plus an honest note on why it matters less here** — this key only ever reads public market quotes, never anything the user owns, unlike a `gist`-scoped GitHub token. Provider research (chosen and both rejected candidates, with reasons) is documented twice, matching how this app already documents its other external-service integrations: once at the top of `price-feed.js` for anyone reading the code, once in a new README.md "Live share/fund prices (optional)" section for anyone reading the docs.
- **No `Investment.last_price` field and no batch/loop over holdings added here** — #298 owns both, per the issue's explicit split. This module's public surface (`isPriceFeedAvailable`, `mapTickerToQuery`, `fetchQuote`) is the whole of what #298 and #295 need to build on.

**Trade-offs / deviations from prompt:**
- **Currency/unit of the returned price is passed through unlabelled and unverified.** Alpha Vantage's `GLOBAL_QUOTE` response carries no currency field, and confirming live whether an LSE quote comes back in pounds or pence wasn't possible without a real API key (the `demo` key only resolves `AAPL`). This module's header comment flags the gap explicitly rather than silently assuming one or the other, since #298's price-to-value ratio maths will need a real answer before it can use this module's output for anything beyond display.
- **No live end-to-end request against Alpha Vantage with a real key** — signing up for a personal key wasn't done this session (the issue's own scope is the module and its tests, not a live demo), so `fetchQuote`'s success path is verified against `price-feed.test.js`'s mocked `fetch` and Alpha Vantage's documented response shape, not an actual network round trip. CORS and the free-tier rate limit *were* verified live, via unauthenticated `curl` requests that don't need a key.
- **`npm test`: 3026 passed, up from 3006 on `main`** (net +20, all in the new `price-feed.test.js`: `isPriceFeedAvailable` with/without a key including the empty-string case, `mapTickerToQuery` covering the bare-ticker, `.L`, unverified-suffix and malformed-input cases, and `fetchQuote` covering success, every failure reason, and never-throws on a non-`Error` rejection). `npm run check`: 0 errors, the same 2 pre-existing `<slot>` warnings from `card.svelte`/`button.svelte`. `npm run lint` (`prettier --check .` + `eslint .`) and `npm run build`: clean.
- **`npm test` still isn't a CI gate** (same flag as every prior entry) — the counts above are local; only `build`/`check`/`lint` run on the PR itself.

## Live share/fund prices: per-holding refresh feedback panel (PriceRefreshResults.svelte) — 2026-08-12
<!-- METRICS:live-share-fund-prices-per-holding-refresh-feedback-panel -->
- **Execution Duration:** __DURATION__ seconds
- **Model:** __MODEL__
- **Turns:** __TURNS__
- **Input Tokens:** __INPUT_TOKENS__
- **Output Tokens:** __OUTPUT_TOKENS__
- **Estimated Cost:** $__COST__ (from Claude Code's reported total_cost_usd)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable GPT-5-tier model:** $__GPT_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable Gemini-tier model:** $__GEMINI_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)

**Decisions:**
- **`PriceRefreshResult` is defined as a local JSDoc typedef inside `PriceRefreshResults.svelte` itself, not imported from anywhere.** This is the load-bearing constraint the issue was narrowed down to: the previous attempt at #295 burned its 100-turn budget because it found no `refreshInvestmentPrices` in `$lib/price-feed.js` (that's #298, which sorts after this issue in `claude-build.yml`'s `[milestone, issue number]` pick order and so can never be relied on here) and started building #298's whole batch-refresh engine before it could even begin the UI. Keeping the type local — matching the three-state table in the issue body field-for-field — means this component only depends on merged code (#265's `Investment.ticker`, #266's `price-feed.js` module, referenced only in prose comments, never `import`ed) and is fully buildable and testable today.
- **One `<li>` per result, rendered in whatever order the `results` array arrives in, switched on `status` inside a single `{#each}`** rather than three separate grouped lists (all updated, then all baseline, then all failed). The issue's ordering requirement is only "one entry per holding the refresh attempted", not a grouping; preserving array order keeps the panel's order matching whatever order #298/#300 process holdings in, and each `{#if result.status === ...}` branch only ever destructures the fields that status's row in the issue's table lists — e.g. the `'failed'` branch never touches `value`/`price`, which is what `PriceRefreshResults.test.js`'s "never shows a failed holding as updated" test asserts directly.
- **A holding's quoted `price`/`previousPrice` is formatted with a plain `toLocaleString`, not the same `Intl.NumberFormat('en-GB', { style: 'currency', currency: 'GBP' })` formatter used for `value`/`previousValue`.** `price-feed.js`'s own header comment is explicit that Alpha Vantage's `GLOBAL_QUOTE` response carries no currency field and the raw price is passed through unlabelled — printing a `£` in front of a number that might actually be in pence, or a foreign currency for a non-UK ticker, would be a fabricated claim this component has no basis for. `value`/`previousValue` are safe to format as GBP because they're this app's own money fields, not the provider's raw quote.
- **Single generic empty state, not two separate messages for "no refresh run yet" vs. "no holding this month has a ticker".** The issue calls out both cases, but this component only receives `results`/`holdingNames` — it has no prop that would let it distinguish "haven't run a refresh" from "ran one against zero eligible holdings" (both arrive as `results: []`), and inventing one now would be speculating about a prop #300 hasn't been specified to pass. The empty-state copy ("a price refresh only covers holdings with a ticker symbol recorded, and results appear here once one has been run") is worded to read sensibly under either cause.
- **`holdingLabel` falls back to `result.ticker` via `??`, not `||`**, so a `holdingNames` entry that happens to be an empty string still counts as "given" rather than silently being treated the same as "missing" — matches this codebase's general preference (seen in `InvestmentHoldings.svelte`'s own null-vs-empty handling for `bought_for`/`year_purchased`) for treating "explicitly present" and "absent" as different states rather than collapsing them.
- **Tests are server-rendered smoke tests via `svelte/server`'s `render`, following `InvestmentGuidance.test.js`'s exact pattern** (issue #255's own precedent, which this issue's body names directly) — this repo has no browser test environment, and a purely presentational component with no internal state has nothing an interaction test would add over asserting the rendered text for each `status` value and prop combination.

**Trade-offs / deviations from prompt:**
- **Not rendered anywhere in the app.** Per the issue's explicit "Out of scope" list, wiring this into `InvestmentHoldings.svelte` is #300's job; this component is exercised only by its own test file, the same state #266's `price-feed.js` module landed in.
- **No live browser verification** — since nothing imports or renders this component yet, there is no page to open in a browser to see it; correctness here rests on the server-rendered test suite plus `npm run check`/`npm run build`, not a visual pass.
- **`npm test`: 3035 passed, up from 3026 on `main`** (net +9, all in the new `PriceRefreshResults.test.js`: the empty state with an empty array and with the prop omitted entirely, the status-count summary line, an updated holding's increase and decrease formatting, the ticker fallback when `holdingNames` has no entry, the baseline wording, a failed result's message, and a failed result never reading as updated alongside a real update). `npm run check`: 0 errors, the same 2 pre-existing `<slot>` warnings from `card.svelte`/`button.svelte`. `npm run lint` (`prettier --check .` + `eslint .`) and `npm run build`: clean.
- **`npm test` still isn't a CI gate** (same flag as every prior entry) — the counts above are local; only `build`/`check`/`lint` run on the PR itself.

## Live share/fund prices: portfolio batch refresh + Investment.last_price ratio scaling — 2026-08-12
<!-- METRICS:live-share-fund-prices-portfolio-batch-refresh-last-price-ratio-scaling -->
- **Execution Duration:** __DURATION__ seconds
- **Model:** __MODEL__
- **Turns:** __TURNS__
- **Input Tokens:** __INPUT_TOKENS__
- **Output Tokens:** __OUTPUT_TOKENS__
- **Estimated Cost:** $__COST__ (from Claude Code's reported total_cost_usd)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable GPT-5-tier model:** $__GPT_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable Gemini-tier model:** $__GEMINI_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)

**Decisions:**
- **Kept the issue's `last_price` ratio design rather than adding a share count, and the result shape is what makes it safe.** `value` moves by `newPrice / last_price` — unit-free, so nothing in the codebase ever has to decide whether an LSE quote came back in pounds or pence (which #266 established Alpha Vantage's `GLOBAL_QUOTE` doesn't say). The cost is that the first fetch can't move anything, which is why `'baseline'` is a distinct status rather than a silent no-op.
- **The return type is a three-way discriminated union whose failure arm carries no numbers at all** — `'updated'` has `previousValue`/`value`/`previousPrice`/`price`, `'baseline'` has only `price`, `'failed'` has only `reason`/`message`. The issue's honesty requirement ("design it so a caller *can't* accidentally apply a failed holding") is met structurally rather than by convention: there is literally no field on a failed result for #300 to write back, so a stale value cannot be dressed up as a fresh one. This matches, field for field, the `PriceRefreshResult` contract #295 already pinned down in `PriceRefreshResults.svelte` (#301) — that component defined the shape first and asked whoever landed #298 to either match it or note the divergence; it's matched exactly, so no divergence note is needed and #300 can pass results straight through.
- **Pacing is derived from the one number #266 documented (25 requests/day), and the comment is honest that a per-day cap implies no useful *spacing* on its own** — actually staying under 25/day across a day is one request every ~58 minutes, which is not a pause an interactive refresh can take. `PRICE_REFRESH_REQUEST_INTERVAL_MS` is instead one minute ÷ 25 = 2,400ms ("spend the whole day's budget and it still takes a minute"), and what the pause really buys is *sequencing*: each response's rate-limit signal arrives before the next request goes out, so the run can stop at the first refusal instead of firing a burst that comes back half-rejected.
- **Three request-saving behaviours on top of plain sequential looping**, all of them about a 25/day budget being genuinely tight: one request per *distinct* ticker (the same fund in an ISA and a GIA is two holdings but one quote, and both get their own result from it); a ticker `mapTickerToQuery` can't map never reaches the network, so it costs neither a request nor a pause; and a hard `PRICE_REFRESH_MAX_REQUESTS` cap at the provider's whole daily allowance, past which holdings come back `'failed'` with "try the rest tomorrow" rather than going silently missing from the results.
- **A quote of zero or less is a failure, not a value.** `fetchQuote` guarantees a finite number but not a *plausible* one, and the one thing a ratio must never do is multiply a holding by nothing — so a non-positive quote returns `'failed'`/`'unparseable-response'` instead of wiping the holding out. Symmetrically, a `last_price` of zero or less is treated as no baseline at all (a fresh `'baseline'`), and `validateAppData` rejects a stored `last_price` of `0` outright, since the field is only ever a ratio's denominator.
- **`carryLastPrice(before, fields)` in `model.js`, called by `InvestmentHoldings.svelte`'s `saveEdit`.** A baseline is only meaningful as one half of a pair — the price that was current when `value` was last known to be right — so two manual edits invalidate it: changing the `ticker` (the recorded price belongs to the *old* instrument) and retyping `value` (a figure copied from a broker already reflects today's price, so scaling it by the move since the last fetch would count that move twice). Both drop the baseline to `null`, costing one refresh cycle rather than producing a confidently wrong number. Putting the rule in `model.js` rather than inline in the component is what makes it unit-testable, given this repo has no browser test environment for interaction tests.
- **Pacing is tested for real, not stubbed out.** `price-feed.test.js`'s new block runs under `vi.useFakeTimers()` and advances through the pauses (the `store.test.js` precedent), so the sequencing assertions — second request not issued until an interval has elapsed, exactly one request per distinct ticker, no further requests after a rate-limit refusal, never more than the daily cap — exercise the real `setTimeout` path rather than an injected no-op clock.

**Trade-offs / deviations from prompt:**
- **Holdings past the daily cap are reported as `'failed'`/`'rate-limited'`, which stretches "one result per *attempted* holding".** They were never sent to the network, so on a strict reading they're closer to the untickered holdings the issue says to skip. Reporting them is the more honest of the two: a holding that silently vanishes from the results of a refresh the user just ran reads as a bug, whereas "one update can look up at most 25 different tickers" explains itself. Untickered holdings still get no result at all, as specified.
- **The issue frames pacing as making a large portfolio "take a while" rather than come back half rate-limited; with a 25/day tier that isn't fully achievable and the cap says so instead.** No spacing can make a 40-ticker portfolio fit in a 25-request day, so the module degrades to "25 refreshed, the rest tomorrow" rather than to "this takes a while". The early-abort on the first rate-limit refusal is the other half of that: continuing would spend a minute of pauses to collect identical failures.
- **`last_price` is deliberately absent from the CSV/XLSX exports**, matching how `ticker` (#265) was left out of them — those exports are a human-facing holdings summary, and a bare per-share number in an unknown currency unit is not something a user could interpret in a spreadsheet. It is in the JSON document, so it round-trips through backup/restore and Gist sync.
- **No live end-to-end refresh against Alpha Vantage** — same position as #266's entry: no real API key was obtained this session, so the batch path is verified against mocked `fetch` responses in the documented `GLOBAL_QUOTE` shapes. No browser pass either: nothing renders `refreshInvestmentPrices` yet (#300 wires the button, #301's panel is still unmounted), so there is no page on which its behaviour would be visible.
- **`npm test`: 3062 passed, up from 3035 on `main`** (net +27: 18 in `price-feed.test.js` covering baseline/updated/failed results, ratio scaling up and down with penny rounding, unit-blindness, the no-ticker skip, the not-configured path, unmappable tickers, per-distinct-ticker deduplication, request pacing under fake timers, the rate-limit early abort, the daily cap, and never-throws for garbage input and a misbehaving feed; 9 in `model.test.js` covering `last_price`'s default, normalisation, round trip, validation of zero/negative, and all four `carryLastPrice` outcomes). `npm run check`: 0 errors, the same 2 pre-existing `<slot>` warnings from `card.svelte`/`button.svelte` (one fix was needed en route: a local `@typedef {import('./types.js').Investment} Investment` in `price-feed.js` collided with `model.js`'s through `index.js`'s `export *`, so the type is referenced inline the way `debt.js`/`fire.js` already do). `npm run lint` (`prettier --check .` + `eslint .`) and `npm run build`: clean.
- **`npm test` still isn't a CI gate** (same flag as every prior entry) — the counts above are local; only `build`/`check`/`lint` run on the PR itself.

- **Output Tokens:** __OUTPUT_TOKENS__
- **Estimated Cost:** $__COST__ (from Claude Code's reported total_cost_usd)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable GPT-5-tier model:** $__GPT_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable Gemini-tier model:** $__GEMINI_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)

**Decisions:**
- **The write-back rules live in a new pure function, `applyPriceRefreshResults(investments, results)` in `model.js`, not inline in `InvestmentHoldings.svelte`.** Same reasoning `createNextMonthlyEntry` (#259) already documents at the top of this component: this repo has no browser test environment, so anything worth unit-testing has to be extractable from a click handler. `refreshPrices()` in the component is now three lines — call `refreshInvestmentPrices`, map the results through `applyPriceRefreshResults`, reassign `monthlyEntries` — and every one of the three per-status write-back rules (`'updated'` writes `value`+`last_price`, `'baseline'` writes `last_price` only, `'failed'`/no-match write nothing) is covered directly in `model.test.js` without touching Svelte at all.
- **Deliberately not logged to `activityLog`, and said so in the component's own header comment** (the issue's explicit instruction to make this call and justify it). The log is a record of discrete per-holding decisions with a revert affordance; one click can touch every holding in a month, and logging each as its own entry would both drown a log meant for deliberate edits and offer a "revert" that has nothing sensible to revert *to* other than running another refresh. The refreshed figures stay visible and further hand-editable (which *does* log) in the holdings list itself, and #295's results panel is the record of what a given refresh did.
- **The button is gated on `isPriceFeedAvailable() && selectedEntry`, computed once as a plain `const` rather than a `$derived`** — the API key is a build-time env var (`import.meta.env`), not something that can change while the app is running, so there's nothing for reactivity to track. `PriceRefreshResults` itself, however, renders unconditionally whenever a feed is configured (button state aside), showing its own "no results yet" empty state — that's what makes it reachable at all from this repo's SSR-only test harness, since the button (nested under the `$effect`-gated `selectedEntry` branch) is not.
- **`priceRefreshResults` is cleared whenever the selected month changes** (both the month `<select>`'s `onchange` and `addMonth()`), so switching months can't leave stale per-holding results on screen next to a different month's holdings, keyed by ids that no longer mean the same thing.
- **Ran a real end-to-end pass against the live Alpha Vantage feed**, not just mocks — `.env.local` with a real free-tier key (obtained this session, not committed), `npm run dev`, and a Playwright script driving a real Chromium: added a month, added a `VWRL.L` holding, clicked "Update prices", and watched a genuine `'baseline'` result render (real quoted price, £1,000 left unchanged, correct copy). A second click landed on a genuine `'rate-limited'` `'failed'` result (the free tier is 25/day and this session's #298 work had already spent some of today's budget) and rendered *that* correctly too — value still £1,000, red "Couldn't fetch a price" row, no `last_price` clobbered. Both paths verified against the real provider in a real browser, which #298's own entry flagged as not done for the underlying engine.

**Trade-offs / deviations from prompt:**
- **`InvestmentHoldings.test.js` still can't assert the button itself present, even with a feed mocked available**, for the same `$effect`-doesn't-run-in-`svelte/server` reason the file's own header comment already gives for edit/remove interactions — the button only renders once `selectedEntryId` is set, which no server render reaches regardless of props. What the new tests do cover: the whole price-refresh area (button *and* panel) is absent with no feed configured, and `PriceRefreshResults` renders its empty state once a feed is configured, since that panel — unlike the button — renders unconditionally outside the month-selected branch. The interactive path (click → apply → panel updates) is instead what the live Playwright pass above exercised.
- **The results panel is placed directly below the holdings form's `Card`, above `InvestmentGuidance`**, rather than literally inside the same card as the form — the issue says "below the form" without pinning down which container, and keeping it a sibling card matches how `InvestmentGuidance` (#255) already sits below this same form as its own standalone card rather than being folded into it.
- **`npm test`: 3070 passed, up from 3062 on `main`** (net +8: 6 in `model.test.js`'s new `applyPriceRefreshResults` suite covering the updated/baseline/failed/no-match write-back rules, applying a mixed batch by `investmentId` rather than array position, and non-mutation of the input; 2 in `InvestmentHoldings.test.js` covering the price-refresh area's presence/absence against a mocked `isPriceFeedAvailable`). `npm run check`: 0 errors, the same 2 pre-existing `<slot>` warnings from `card.svelte`/`button.svelte`. `npm run lint` (`prettier --check .` + `eslint .`) and `npm run build`: clean.
- **`npm test` still isn't a CI gate** (same flag as every prior entry) — the counts above are local; only `build`/`check`/`lint` run on the PR itself.

## Footer: show the last-updated date and time next to the commit link — 2026-08-12
<!-- METRICS:footer-last-updated-commit-timestamp -->
- **Execution Duration:** __DURATION__ seconds
- **Model:** __MODEL__
- **Turns:** __TURNS__
- **Input Tokens:** __INPUT_TOKENS__
- **Output Tokens:** __OUTPUT_TOKENS__
- **Estimated Cost:** $__COST__ (from Claude Code's reported total_cost_usd)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable GPT-5-tier model:** $__GPT_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)
- **Ballpark cost, comparable Gemini-tier model:** $__GEMINI_BALLPARK__ (illustrative -- see methodology note above)

**Decisions:**
- **New `commitDate()` in `vite.config.js` mirrors `commitSha()` exactly**: `git log -1 --format=%cI` for the commit's own ISO 8601 timestamp (with its original timezone), not the CI pipeline's build/deploy wall-clock time — per the issue's own rationale, a reader asking "when was this last updated" wants the commit time, and the deploy run can lag it by minutes. Same try/catch-to-`''` fallback as `commitSha()`, for the same reasons (local dev without git, a shallow clone).
- **Wired the new `__COMMIT_DATE__` define through all four places `__COMMIT_SHA__` already touches**, per the issue's explicit checklist: `vite.config.js`'s `define` block, `app.d.ts`'s ambient `declare global` (so `svelte-check` knows the global exists), `eslint.config.js`'s `globals` entry (so the linter doesn't flag it as undefined), and a new `COMMIT_DATE` export from `$lib/version.js` using the same `typeof __COMMIT_DATE__ !== 'undefined' ? __COMMIT_DATE__ : ''` guard `COMMIT_SHA` uses (empty string rather than `COMMIT_SHA`'s `'dev'` fallback, since there's no sensible placeholder date and every caller already has to handle "missing").
- **Formatting stayed inline in `+layout.svelte`, no new `$lib` date-formatting helper** — reused the exact `Intl.DateTimeFormat('en-GB', { dateStyle: 'medium', timeStyle: 'short' })` convention `ActivityLog.svelte` and `GitHubSignIn.svelte` already use, as the issue explicitly asked to check for and match rather than introducing a new one.
- **`formattedCommitDate` is a `$derived.by` that returns `''` for a falsy `COMMIT_DATE` or a `Date` that fails to parse** (`Number.isNaN(parsed.getTime())`), and the footer only renders the `· updated …` fragment inside `{#if formattedCommitDate}` — mirrors the existing `{#if COMMIT_SHA}` guard around the commit link one line above it, so a dev/shallow-clone build just omits the fragment instead of crashing or showing "Invalid Date", per the issue's explicit instruction to mirror that fallback.

**Trade-offs / deviations from prompt:**
- **No new test file.** `+layout.svelte`, like every other file under `src/routes/`, has zero existing unit test coverage in this repo — routes aren't unit-tested here at all (`find src/routes -iname '*.test.js'` returns nothing), and `COMMIT_SHA`/`CODENAME` are themselves untested constants in `version.test.js` (only the pure `codenameForSha()` helper is covered). Adding a bespoke test harness for this one component would be inconsistent with that precedent. Verified instead via the full existing pipeline — `npm test` (3070 passed, unchanged from `main`), `npm run check` (0 errors, same 2 pre-existing `<slot>` warnings), `npm run lint` (clean) — plus a real `npm run build`, then grepping the compiled client bundle to confirm `__COMMIT_DATE__` was replaced with this repo's actual `git log -1 --format=%cI` output and that the `updated ${...}` template plus the `dateStyle:medium,timeStyle:short` formatter config both compiled into the layout chunk as expected.
- **No live browser/Playwright pass**, unlike some recent entries in this log — this is a purely presentational, build-time-constant addition with no interactive state, network call, or user input to exercise, so static verification of the compiled output plus the unaffected test suite was judged sufficient.
